uses this extraction on the continuous restrictions of the branch witnesses and
then applies the PR \#2693 subsequence Vitali bridge.  Thus compactness of the
local branch family in \(C(B(h_0,r),\mathbb C)\) yields a subsequence whose
limit is holomorphic on \(B(h_0,r)\).

At a real centre, the theorem ending in \texttt{\_ball\_real} additionally
identifies the centre value with the real infinite-volume free energy:
\[
  f(p.h)=\texttt{freeEnergyInfinite}\,G\,\Lambda\,p .
\]
The concrete \(\mathbb Z^d\) wrappers now live in
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
SubseqCompactOpen/CompactOpenVitali/Ball.lean
\end{Verbatim}
under \texttt{Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex}, and are
re-exported by the old
\texttt{SubseqCompactOpen/CompactOpenVitali.lean} path.

This result still does not prove Montel compactness of the branch family, nor
does it perform the diagonal extraction needed for a coherent local cover of
\(\texttt{leeYangDomain}\).  It isolates the compactness-to-subsequence step
that the remaining normal-family argument must feed.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Two-ball compact-open diagonal handoff; PR \#2695]
The first finite-diagonal step is formalised by
\begin{Verbatim}
exists_subseq_two_tendstoLocallyUniformlyOn_of_isCompact_compactOpen
\end{Verbatim}
and its Lee--Yang wrapper
\begin{Verbatim}
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_branchFamily_compactOpen_vitali_two_ball
\end{Verbatim}
Given two open sets \(s_1,s_2\subset\mathbb C\), two compact subsets of
\(C(s_i,\mathbb C)\), and two branch families whose continuous restrictions
lie in those compact subsets, one first extracts a subsequence on \(s_1\), then
extracts a further subsequence on \(s_2\).  The auxiliary lemma
\begin{Verbatim}
tendstoLocallyUniformlyOn_subseq_of_strictMono
\end{Verbatim}
shows that locally uniform convergence on \(s_1\) is preserved by the second
strictly increasing reindexing.  Thus a single strictly increasing
\(\sigma:\mathbb N\to\mathbb N\) works on both balls.

For two Lee--Yang balls the ambient theorem applies the PR \#2693 subsequence
Vitali bridge on each ball, producing holomorphic local limits on both balls.
The concrete \(\mathbb Z^d\) wrapper now lives in
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
SubseqCompactOpen/CompactOpenVitali/FiniteDiagonal.lean
\end{Verbatim}
under \texttt{Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex}, and is
re-exported by the old
\texttt{SubseqCompactOpen/CompactOpenVitali.lean} path.

This is the two-ball base case for the finite diagonal extraction theorem.  By
itself it does not prove Montel compactness, compatibility of the two limits on
overlaps, or global patching on \(\texttt{leeYangDomain}\).
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Finite compact-open diagonal handoff; PR \#2696]
The arbitrary finite extraction step is formalised by
\begin{Verbatim}
exists_subseq_fin_tendstoLocallyUniformlyOn_of_isCompact_compactOpen
\end{Verbatim}
and its Lee--Yang wrapper
\begin{Verbatim}
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_branchFamily_compactOpen_vitali_fin_ball
\end{Verbatim}
For a finite family \(i:\mathrm{Fin}\,n\) of open sets \(s_i\subset\mathbb C\),
compact subsets of \(C(s_i,\mathbb C)\), and total branch families whose
continuous restrictions lie in those compact subsets, the theorem produces one
strictly increasing \(\sigma:\mathbb N\to\mathbb N\) such that every
\(F_{i,\sigma(m)}\) converges locally uniformly on \(s_i\).

The proof is induction over \(n\).  At the successor step one extracts on the
head open set, applies the induction hypothesis to the tail families already
restricted to that head subsequence, and uses
\begin{Verbatim}
tendstoLocallyUniformlyOn_subseq_of_strictMono
\end{Verbatim}
to preserve the head convergence under the final tail refinement.  For finitely
many Lee--Yang balls the ambient theorem then applies the PR \#2693 subsequence
Vitali bridge on every ball, producing holomorphic local limits for the whole
finite family.  The concrete \(\mathbb Z^d\) wrapper now lives in
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
SubseqCompactOpen/CompactOpenVitali/FiniteDiagonal.lean
\end{Verbatim}
under \texttt{Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex}, and is
re-exported by the old
\texttt{SubseqCompactOpen/CompactOpenVitali.lean} path.

This result still assumes compact-open compactness for each branch family.  It
does not prove Montel compactness, compatibility of arbitrary branch choices on
overlaps, coherent local-cover extraction, or global patching on
\(\texttt{leeYangDomain}\).
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Finite overlap-compatibility handoff; PR \#2697]
The generic overlap uniqueness step is formalised by
\begin{Verbatim}
eqOn_of_tendstoLocallyUniformlyOn_of_eventuallyEqOn
pairwise_eqOn_of_tendstoLocallyUniformlyOn_of_eventuallyEqOn
\end{Verbatim}
and its Lee--Yang finite-ball wrapper
\begin{Verbatim}
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_branchFamily_compactOpen_vitali_fin_ball_overlap
\end{Verbatim}
If two stage families converge locally uniformly on open sets \(s\) and \(t\),
and the stage functions are eventually equal on \(s\cap t\), then Hausdorff
uniqueness of pointwise limits gives equality of the two limiting functions on
the overlap.  The finite-family theorem applies this argument pairwise over
\(\mathrm{Fin}\,n\).

For finitely many Lee--Yang balls, the ambient theorem combines this overlap
lemma with the PR \#2696 compact-open finite diagonal extraction.  It returns a
single strictly increasing subsequence \(\sigma:\mathbb N\to\mathbb N\), a
family of holomorphic local limits \(f_i\), and pairwise equalities
\[
  f_i = f_j \quad\text{on}\quad
  B(h_i,r_i)\cap B(h_j,r_j)
\]
whenever the selected stage branches are eventually equal on the same
overlaps.  The concrete \(\mathbb Z^d\) wrapper now lives in
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
SubseqCompactOpen/CompactOpenVitali/Overlap.lean
\end{Verbatim}
under \texttt{Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex}, and is
re-exported by the old
\texttt{SubseqCompactOpen/CompactOpenVitali.lean} path.

This is an explicit-hypothesis compatibility handoff.  It does not construct
coherent branch choices, prove that arbitrary local logarithm branches agree on
overlaps, prove Montel compactness, or patch the resulting local limits to a
global function on \(\texttt{leeYangDomain}\).
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Packaged finite subsequence branch-limit family; PR \#2705]
The structure
\begin{Verbatim}
Ambient.LeeYangFiniteSubseqBranchLimitFamily
\end{Verbatim}
packages the finite compact-open diagonal and overlap output.  For a finite
family of balls \(B(h_i,r_i)\), it contains:
\begin{itemize}
\item a strictly increasing stage map
  \(\sigma:\mathbb N\to\mathbb N\);
\item per-ball branch families \(F_{i,m}\) indexed by the selected stages;
\item local limits \(f_i\);
\item the stage-\(\sigma(m)\) exponential partition-function identity on each
  ball;
\item centre normalisation
  \(F_{i,m}(h_i)=f_{\Lambda_{\sigma(m)}}(J,h_i,\beta)\);
\item locally uniform convergence \(F_{i,m}\to f_i\), differentiability of
  \(f_i\), and pairwise compatibility on ball overlaps.
\end{itemize}

The theorem
\begin{Verbatim}
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_finiteSubseqBranchLimitFamily_compactOpen
\end{Verbatim}
constructs such a package from the PR \#2697 finite compact-open/overlap
handoff.  The theorem
\begin{Verbatim}
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_finiteSubseqBranchLimitFamily_patch
\end{Verbatim}
then consumes the packaged compatibility and patches the local limits to a
single function differentiable on the finite union of balls.  The concrete
\(\mathbb Z^d\) wrappers are
\begin{Verbatim}
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_finiteSubseqBranchLimitFamily_compactOpen_latticeGraph
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_finiteSubseqBranchLimitFamily_patch_latticeGraph
\end{Verbatim}
The compact-open package wrapper now lives in
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
SubseqCompactOpen/FiniteSubseqCompactOpen/CompactOpen.lean
\end{Verbatim}
The patch wrapper now lives in
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
SubseqCompactOpen/FiniteSubseqCompactOpen/Patch.lean
\end{Verbatim}
under \texttt{Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex}; both are
re-exported by the old
\texttt{SubseqCompactOpen/FiniteSubseqCompactOpen.lean} path.

This is still a finite, explicit-hypothesis handoff.  It does not prove Montel
compactness or construct a coherent \(\texttt{leeYangDomain}\)-cover; it only
records the finite post-extraction output in the same structured style as the
later local-cover packages.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Packaged finite subsequence branch-limit real-centre
identification; PR \#2706]
The theorem
\begin{Verbatim}
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_finiteSubseqBranchLimitFamily_patch_real
\end{Verbatim}
adds the real-centre conclusion to the PR \#2705 finite package.  Its input is
a compatible \texttt{Ambient.LeeYangFiniteSubseqBranchLimitFamily} for real
parameters \((p.J,p.\beta)\), an index \(i_0:\mathrm{Fin}\,n\), the centre
identity \(h_{i_0}=p.h\), and \(0<r_{i_0}\).  The theorem first patches the
finite family on \(\bigcup_i B(h_i,r_i)\).  It then applies the existing
subsequence real-centre identification theorem to the selected component
\(i_0\): the branch identities are indexed by the package's strict stage map,
and centre normalisation rewrites the selected branch values as the real
finite-volume free-energy sequence along that subsequence.  Therefore the
patched function satisfies
\[
  g(p.h)=\texttt{freeEnergyInfinite}.
\]
The concrete \(\mathbb Z^d\) wrapper is
\begin{Verbatim}
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_finiteSubseqBranchLimitFamily_patch_real_latticeGraph
\end{Verbatim}
It now lives in
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
SubseqCompactOpen/FiniteSubseqCompactOpen/Patch.lean
\end{Verbatim}
under \texttt{Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex}, and is
re-exported by the old
\texttt{SubseqCompactOpen/FiniteSubseqCompactOpen.lean} path.

This remains a finite, conditional handoff.  It identifies the real centre for
a selected ball in a packaged finite family, but it does not construct Montel
compactness, coherent branch choices, or a full
\(\texttt{leeYangDomain}\)-cover.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Finite compact-open extraction to a real-centre patch; PR
\#2707]
The theorem
\begin{Verbatim}
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_finiteSubseqBranchLimitFamily_compactOpen_patch_real
\end{Verbatim}
composes the PR \#2705 finite compact-open package extraction with the PR
\#2706 finite real-centre patch.  Its hypotheses include compactness of each
branch family in \(C(B(h_i,r_i),\mathbb C)\), the restriction identity between
the total stage functions and their continuous restrictions, the Lee--Yang
stage branch identities, eventual equality on every pairwise ball overlap,
bounded edge density, disjoint-tower hypotheses for the real parameter, and a
selected ball \(i_0\) with \(h_{i_0}=p.h\) and \(0<r_{i_0}\).  The conclusion
simultaneously returns the extracted
\texttt{Ambient.LeeYangFiniteSubseqBranchLimitFamily} and a patched function
on \(\bigcup_i B(h_i,r_i)\) satisfying
\[
  g(p.h)=\texttt{freeEnergyInfinite}.
\]
The concrete \(\mathbb Z^d\) wrapper is
\begin{Verbatim}
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_finiteCompactOpenBranchLimitFamily_realPatch_latticeGraph
\end{Verbatim}
in
\begin{Verbatim}
Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex/
  BranchAscoliCompactOpen/RealCoverPatches/RealPatch.lean
\end{Verbatim}
and is re-exported through the legacy real-cover path.

This remains a finite, explicit-hypothesis handoff.  It eliminates the
separate manual step of first extracting a finite package and then applying
the real-centre patch, but it does not prove Montel compactness, coherent
branch choices, or a full \(\texttt{leeYangDomain}\)-cover.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Finite compact-open extraction to a patched finite family; PR
\#2708]
The theorem
\begin{Verbatim}
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_finiteSubseqBranchLimitFamily_compactOpen_patch
\end{Verbatim}
composes the PR \#2705 finite compact-open package extraction with the packaged
finite patch.  Its hypotheses are compactness of each branch family in
\(C(B(h_i,r_i),\mathbb C)\), the restriction identity between total stage
functions and their continuous restrictions, the Lee--Yang stage branch
identities, and eventual equality on every pairwise ball overlap.  The
conclusion returns both the extracted
\texttt{Ambient.LeeYangFiniteSubseqBranchLimitFamily} and a patched function on
\(\bigcup_i B(h_i,r_i)\) that agrees with every packaged local limit and is
differentiable on that finite union.  The concrete \(\mathbb Z^d\) wrapper is
\begin{Verbatim}
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_finiteCompactOpenBranchLimitFamily_patch_latticeGraph
\end{Verbatim}
It now lives in
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
SubseqCompactOpen/FiniteCoverPatches/CompactOpen.lean
\end{Verbatim}
under \texttt{Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex}, and is
re-exported by the old
\texttt{SubseqCompactOpen/FiniteCoverPatches.lean} path.

This remains a finite, explicit-hypothesis handoff.  It removes the separate
manual step of first extracting a packaged finite family and then applying the
finite patch, but it does not prove Montel compactness, coherent branch
choices, or a full \(\texttt{leeYangDomain}\)-cover.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Finite Lee--Yang cover branch-limit package; PR \#2709]
The structure
\begin{Verbatim}
Ambient.LeeYangFiniteCoverBranchLimitFamily
\end{Verbatim}
records the finite-cover geometry needed by the later diagonal local-cover
extraction: centres \(h_i\in\mathcal D_{\mathrm{LY}}\), positive radii
\(r_i\), ball containment \(B(h_i,r_i)\subseteq\mathcal D_{\mathrm{LY}}\),
and the compatible finite subsequence branch-limit family on those balls.
The wrapper
\begin{Verbatim}
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_finiteCoverBranchLimitFamily_compactOpen
\end{Verbatim}
packages the finite compact-open extraction into this Lee--Yang-cover shape
under the same compactness, restriction, branch-identity, and eventual
overlap-equality hypotheses as the finite compact-open handoff.

The patching wrappers
\begin{Verbatim}
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_finiteCoverBranchLimitFamily_patch
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_finiteCoverBranchLimitFamily_patch_real
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_finiteCoverBranchLimitFamily_compactOpen_patch
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_finiteCoverBranchLimitFamily_compactOpen_patch_real
\end{Verbatim}
then patch the finite Lee--Yang cover and, when one selected centre is the real
field \(p.h\), identify the patched value with
\(\texttt{freeEnergyInfinite}\).  The concrete \(\mathbb Z^d\) compact-open
package and patch wrappers are implemented in
\begin{Verbatim}
Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex/
  BranchAscoliCompactOpen/RealCoverPatches/FiniteCover.lean
Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex/
  BranchAscoliCompactOpen/RealCoverPatches/Patch.lean
\end{Verbatim}
and are re-exported through the legacy real-cover path.  The compact-open
real-centre wrapper is implemented in
\begin{Verbatim}
Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex/
  BranchAscoliCompactOpen/CompactCoverPatches/FiniteCover.lean
\end{Verbatim}
including
\begin{Verbatim}
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_finiteCoverBranchLimitFamily_realPatch_latticeGraph
\end{Verbatim}
for the real-centre compact-open conclusion.

This remains a finite, post-Montel handoff.  It aligns the finite extraction
output with the eventual Lee--Yang local-cover endpoint, but it does not prove
the compact-open compactness of the branch family or construct coherent
stage-level branch choices.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Finite real-centred Lee--Yang cover package; PR \#2710]
The structure
\begin{Verbatim}
Ambient.LeeYangFiniteRealCoverBranchLimitFamily
\end{Verbatim}
packages the PR \#2709 finite Lee--Yang cover branch-limit family together
with the selected finite-cover index whose centre is the real field \(p.h\).
Thus the real-centre witness needed by the finite-union patch is carried by a
single data object rather than by separate arguments.

The wrapper
\begin{Verbatim}
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_finiteRealCoverFamily_patch
\end{Verbatim}
patches such a bundled finite real cover and identifies the selected
real-centre value with \(\texttt{freeEnergyInfinite}\).  The compact-open
wrapper
\begin{Verbatim}
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_finiteRealCoverFamily_compactOpen_patch
\end{Verbatim}
combines compact-open extraction, eventual stage-level overlap equality, the
finite Lee--Yang cover package, and the finite real-centre patch into one
real-centred finite-cover endpoint.  The concrete \(\mathbb Z^d\) wrappers are
\begin{Verbatim}
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_finiteRealCoverFamily_patch_latticeGraph
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_finiteRealCoverFamily_compactOpen_patch_latticeGraph
\end{Verbatim}
The first wrapper lives in
\begin{Verbatim}
Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex/
  SubseqCompactOpen/FiniteCoverPatches/Patch.lean
\end{Verbatim}
and is re-exported by the old
\texttt{SubseqCompactOpen/FiniteCoverPatches.lean} path.  The compact-open
wrapper lives in
\begin{Verbatim}
Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex/
  BranchAscoliCompactOpen/CompactCoverPatches/RealCover.lean
\end{Verbatim}
and is re-exported through the legacy compact-cover path.

This remains a finite, conditional endpoint.  It records the real-centred
finite-cover shape expected from the later local-cover extraction, but it does
not prove Montel compactness, coherent branch selection, or the diagonal
coherent local-cover construction.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Compact finite real-centred Lee--Yang cover package; PR \#2711]
The structure
\begin{Verbatim}
Ambient.LeeYangCompactFiniteRealCoverBranchLimitFamily
\end{Verbatim}
extends the PR \#2710 finite real-centred cover by a compact target
\(K\subset\mathcal D_{\mathrm{LY}}\), a proof that the real field \(p.h\)
belongs to \(K\), and a proof that \(K\) is covered by the finite Lee--Yang
balls.  This is the compact-target finite-cover shape needed before a later
finite-subcover extraction from a genuine local cover.

The wrapper
\begin{Verbatim}
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_compactFiniteRealCover_patch
\end{Verbatim}
patches the underlying finite real cover and then restricts differentiability
from the finite union of balls to \(K\).  It still identifies the selected real
centre with \(\texttt{freeEnergyInfinite}\).  The compact-open wrapper
\begin{Verbatim}
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_compactFiniteRealCover_cOpenPatch
\end{Verbatim}
also packages compact-open extraction and eventual stage-level overlap
equality into the compact finite real-cover object.  The concrete
\(\mathbb Z^d\) wrappers are
\begin{Verbatim}
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_compactFiniteRealCover_patch_latticeGraph
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_compactFiniteRealCover_cOpenPatch_latticeGraph
\end{Verbatim}
The first wrapper lives in
\begin{Verbatim}
Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex/
  SubseqCompactOpen/FiniteCoverPatches/Patch.lean
\end{Verbatim}
and is re-exported by the old
\texttt{SubseqCompactOpen/FiniteCoverPatches.lean} path.  The compact-open
wrapper lives in
\begin{Verbatim}
Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex/
  BranchAscoliCompactOpen/CompactCoverPatches/CompactTarget.lean
\end{Verbatim}
and is re-exported through the legacy compact-cover path.

This remains a finite, explicit-hypothesis handoff.  It does not prove Montel
compactness, coherent branch choices, finite-subcover extraction from an
arbitrary local cover, or diagonal coherent local-cover extraction.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Finite local-patching handoff; PR \#2698]
The finite differentiable patching step is formalised by
\begin{Verbatim}
exists_differentiableOn_iUnion_of_finite_eqOn
\end{Verbatim}
and its Lee--Yang finite-ball wrapper
\begin{Verbatim}
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_branchFamily_compactOpen_vitali_fin_ball_patch
\end{Verbatim}
Suppose \(s_i\subset\mathbb C\) is a finite family of open sets and
\(f_i\) is differentiable on \(s_i\).  If the local functions are pairwise
compatible,
\[
  f_i=f_j \quad\text{on}\quad s_i\cap s_j ,
\]
then there is a single function \(g\) on \(\mathbb C\) such that
\[
  g=f_i \quad\text{on}\quad s_i
\]
for every \(i\), and \(g\) is differentiable on the finite union
\(\bigcup_i s_i\).

The proof is induction over the finite index.  At the successor step, patch the
tail by the induction hypothesis, define the new function by
\texttt{Set.piecewise} using the head open set, and use pairwise compatibility
to show that this piecewise function agrees with the head branch on the head
and with the tail patch on the tail union.  Differentiability on the final
union follows from \texttt{DifferentiableOn.union\_of\_isOpen}.

For finitely many Lee--Yang balls, the ambient theorem composes this patching
lemma with the PR \#2697 overlap handoff.  It produces the extracted local
limits \(f_i\), a patched function \(g\) on the finite union of balls, local
agreement \(g=f_i\) on each ball, and differentiability of \(g\) on that finite
union.  The concrete \(\mathbb Z^d\) wrapper is in
\begin{Verbatim}
Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex/
  BranchAscoliCompactOpen/EventualPatches/FiniteBallPatch.lean
\end{Verbatim}
and is re-exported through the legacy eventual-patches path.

This is still a finite, explicit-hypothesis handoff.  It does not construct the
coherent stage branches that supply overlap equality, prove Montel compactness,
perform diagonal coherent local-cover extraction, or patch a full
\(\texttt{leeYangDomain}\)-cover.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Open-cover local-patching handoff; PR \#2699]
The open-cover differentiable patching step is formalised by
\begin{Verbatim}
exists_differentiableOn_iUnion_of_eqOn
\end{Verbatim}
and its Lee--Yang open-cover wrapper
\begin{Verbatim}
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_branchFamily_openCover_patch
\end{Verbatim}
Let \(s_i\subset\mathbb C\) be an arbitrary family of open sets and let
\(f_i\) be differentiable on \(s_i\).  If the local functions are pairwise
compatible,
\[
  f_i=f_j \quad\text{on}\quad s_i\cap s_j ,
\]
then there is a single function \(g\) on \(\mathbb C\) such that \(g=f_i\) on
each \(s_i\), and \(g\) is differentiable on \(\bigcup_i s_i\).

The proof defines \(g(z)\) by choosing one index \(i\) with \(z\in s_i\) when
\(z\) lies in the union, and using \(0\) outside the union.  Pairwise
compatibility proves that this choice agrees with every local function on its
own open set.  For differentiability, if \(z\in s_i\), then \(s_i\) is a
neighbourhood of \(z\); on that neighbourhood the patched function is
eventually equal to \(f_i\), so differentiability transfers from \(f_i\) to
\(g\).

For a Lee--Yang open cover, the ambient theorem first applies the Vitali
handoff on each cover member to obtain differentiable local limits, then
applies the open-cover patching theorem.  If the cover contains
\(\texttt{leeYangDomain}\), the resulting patched function is differentiable on
\(\texttt{leeYangDomain}\).  The concrete \(\mathbb Z^d\) wrapper is in the
\texttt{OpenPatch.lean} child, re-exported by \texttt{LocalCoverPatch.lean}.

This is still a conditional handoff.  It assumes the compatible local-cover
limits explicitly, and does not prove coherent branch selection, Montel
compactness, or diagonal coherent local-cover extraction.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Pointed Lee--Yang local-cover patching assembly; PR \#2700]
The theorem
\begin{Verbatim}
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_branchFamily_localCover_patch
\end{Verbatim}
specialises the open-cover patching handoff to the canonical cover of
\(\texttt{leeYangDomain}\) by balls centred at its own points.  For each
point \(h_0\in\texttt{leeYangDomain}\), assume a positive radius \(r(h_0)\)
whose ball is contained in \(\texttt{leeYangDomain}\), a branch family
\(F_{h_0,n}\), and a local limit \(f_{h_0}\).  If \(F_{h_0,n}\) is
holomorphic on the ball, satisfies the finite-stage exponential partition
function identity there, and converges locally uniformly to \(f_{h_0}\), then
the PR \#2699 open-cover theorem supplies differentiability of each local
limit.

The remaining hypothesis is pairwise compatibility:
\[
  f_{h_0}=f_{h_1}
  \quad\text{on}\quad
  B(h_0,r(h_0))\cap B(h_1,r(h_1)).
\]
Under this condition the local limits patch to a single function \(g\) on
\(\mathbb C\), agreeing with each \(f_{h_0}\) on its ball, and \(g\) is
differentiable on \(\texttt{leeYangDomain}\).  The proof uses the subtype of
Lee--Yang points as the open-cover index and covers a point \(z\) by the ball
centred at \(z\) itself.  The concrete \(\mathbb Z^d\) wrapper is
\begin{Verbatim}
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_branchFamily_localCover_patch_latticeGraph
\end{Verbatim}
in \texttt{Branches/LocalCoverPatch/OpenPatch.lean}, re-exported by
\texttt{Branches/LocalCoverPatch.lean}.

This is still a conditional post-extraction handoff.  It packages the pointed
Lee--Yang ball cover, but it does not prove coherent branch selection, Montel
compactness, or diagonal coherent local-cover extraction.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Structured Lee--Yang local-cover data patching; PR \#2701]
The structure
\begin{Verbatim}
Ambient.LeeYangLocalBranchLimit
\end{Verbatim}
packages the data attached to one point of the Lee--Yang domain: a positive
radius, containment of the corresponding ball in \(\texttt{leeYangDomain}\), a
per-stage branch family, a local limit, the finite-stage exponential
partition-function identity on the ball, and locally uniform convergence to
that limit.

The theorem
\begin{Verbatim}
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_branchLimitData_localCover_patch
\end{Verbatim}
takes a point-indexed family of such data and a pairwise compatibility
hypothesis for the packaged local limits on ball overlaps.  It extracts the
radius, branch family, limit, and convergence fields and feeds them into the
PR \#2700 pointed local-cover patching assembly.  Consequently there is a
single function \(g\) on \(\mathbb C\), agreeing with every packaged local
limit on its own ball, and \(g\) is differentiable on
\(\texttt{leeYangDomain}\).  The concrete \(\mathbb Z^d\) wrapper is
\begin{Verbatim}
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_branchLimitData_localCover_patch_latticeGraph
\end{Verbatim}
in the \texttt{StructuredPatch.lean} child, re-exported by
\texttt{LocalCoverPatch.lean}.

This is still a conditional post-extraction handoff.  It makes the local-cover
input easier to pass around, but it does not prove coherent branch selection,
Montel compactness, or diagonal coherent local-cover extraction.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Structured Lee--Yang local-cover data with real-axis
identification; PR \#2702]
The theorem
\begin{Verbatim}
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_branchLimitData_localCover_real
\end{Verbatim}
combines the PR \#2701 structured data patching theorem with the real-axis
Fekete convergence theorem.  Its input is a compatible point-indexed family of
\texttt{Ambient.LeeYangLocalBranchLimit} data for the real parameters
\((p.J,p.\beta)\), together with a real Lee--Yang centre \(p.h\) and a centre
normalisation hypothesis
\[
  F_n(p.h)=f_{\Lambda_n}(p.J,p.h,p.\beta).
\]
The PR \#2701 theorem first patches the packaged local limits to a function
\(g\) differentiable on \(\texttt{leeYangDomain}\).  The locally uniform
convergence field in the package centred at \(p.h\) gives pointwise convergence
of \(F_n(p.h)\).  The centre normalisation rewrites this sequence as the real
finite-volume free-energy sequence, and the existing real-axis Fekete theorem
identifies the limit with \(\texttt{freeEnergyInfinite}\).  Since \(g\) agrees
with the packaged local limit on the centre ball, the theorem concludes
\[
  g(p.h)=\texttt{freeEnergyInfinite}.
\]
The concrete \(\mathbb Z^d\) wrapper is
\begin{Verbatim}
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_branchLimitData_localCover_real_latticeGraph
\end{Verbatim}
in the \texttt{StructuredPatch.lean} child, re-exported by
\texttt{LocalCoverPatch.lean}.

This is still a conditional post-extraction handoff: compatibility of the
packaged local limits and the coherent local-cover extraction remain external
inputs.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Packaged Lee--Yang local-cover branch-limit family; PR \#2703]
The structure
\begin{Verbatim}
Ambient.LeeYangLocalBranchLimitFamily
\end{Verbatim}
bundles the full compatible local-cover endpoint expected from the later
coherent extraction: for each point of \(\texttt{leeYangDomain}\) it contains a
\texttt{LeeYangLocalBranchLimit} package, and it also contains the pairwise
compatibility proof that the packaged local limits agree on all corresponding
ball overlaps.

The theorem
\begin{Verbatim}
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_branchLimitFamily_localCover_patch
\end{Verbatim}
is the one-argument version of the PR \#2701 data patching theorem: from a
compatible family it obtains a function \(g\) agreeing with every packaged
local limit on its ball and differentiable on \(\texttt{leeYangDomain}\).  The
real-centre wrapper
\begin{Verbatim}
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_branchLimitFamily_localCover_real
\end{Verbatim}
adds the PR \#2702 conclusion: if the package at a real Lee--Yang centre is
normalised to the finite-volume free-energy sequence, then
\[
  g(p.h)=\texttt{freeEnergyInfinite}.
\]
The concrete \(\mathbb Z^d\) wrappers are
\begin{Verbatim}
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_branchLimitFamily_localCover_patch_latticeGraph
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_branchLimitFamily_localCover_real_latticeGraph
\end{Verbatim}
in the \texttt{StructuredPatch.lean} child, re-exported by
\texttt{LocalCoverPatch.lean}.

This remains conditional and post-extraction.  The new structure removes the
last loose compatibility argument from downstream APIs, but it does not prove
Montel compactness or construct the coherent compatible family.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Real-centred packaged Lee--Yang local-cover family; PR \#2704]
The structure
\begin{Verbatim}
Ambient.LeeYangRealBranchLimitFamily
\end{Verbatim}
adds the real-axis endpoint data to the compatible family package.  For real
parameters \(p\), it contains:
\begin{itemize}
\item the membership \((p.h:\mathbb C)\in\texttt{leeYangDomain}\);
\item a compatible \texttt{LeeYangLocalBranchLimitFamily} at
  \((p.J,p.\beta)\);
\item the centre normalisation
\[
  F_n(p.h)=f_{\Lambda_n}(p.J,p.h,p.\beta)
\]
for the branch family attached to the ball centred at \(p.h\).
\end{itemize}

The theorem
\begin{Verbatim}
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_realBranchLimitFamily_localCover_real
\end{Verbatim}
feeds this package into the PR \#2703 real wrapper.  Therefore a single
real-centred family package gives a function \(g\) which agrees with every
packaged local limit on its Lee--Yang ball, is differentiable on
\(\texttt{leeYangDomain}\), and satisfies
\[
  g(p.h)=\texttt{freeEnergyInfinite}.
\]
The concrete \(\mathbb Z^d\) wrapper is
\begin{Verbatim}
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_realBranchLimitFamily_localCover_real_latticeGraph
\end{Verbatim}
in the \texttt{StructuredPatch.lean} child, re-exported by
\texttt{LocalCoverPatch.lean}.

This is still a conditional post-extraction handoff.  It does not prove
Montel compactness or construct the family; it names the exact real-centred
object that a later coherent local-cover extraction should provide.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Pre-Montel all-stage Lee--Yang branch-choice package; PR \#2727]
The structures
\begin{Verbatim}
Ambient.LeeYangAllStageBranchData
Ambient.LeeYangPointwiseNormalisedAllStageBranchData
\end{Verbatim}
record the finite-stage branch-choice data before the Montel and diagonal
steps.  For every point \(h_0\in\texttt{leeYangDomain}\) they store a radius
\(r(h_0)>0\) with
\[
  B(h_0,r(h_0))\subset\texttt{leeYangDomain},
\]
and for every stage \(n\) a selected analytic logarithm branch
\(F_{h_0,n}\) on that ball satisfying
\[
  \exp(|\Lambda_n|F_{h_0,n}(z))
    = Z_{\Lambda_n}(J,z,\beta).
\]
The pointwise-normalised structure additionally records
\[
  F_{h_0,n}(h_0)=f_{\Lambda_n}(J,h_0,\beta)
\]
at every Lee--Yang centre and every stage.

The constructor
\begin{Verbatim}
Ambient.exists_leeYangPointwiseNormalisedAllStageBranchData_of_positive_real
\end{Verbatim}
builds this package for positive real \(\beta,J\).  It combines openness of
\(\texttt{leeYangDomain}\) with the strong all-stage finite Lee--Yang branch
witness, and uses choice only to select one ball and one branch witness for
each centre and stage.  The concrete \(\mathbb Z^d\) wrapper is
\begin{Verbatim}
exists_leeYangPointwiseNormalisedAllStageBranchData_of_positive_real_latticeGraph
\end{Verbatim}
in \texttt{Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex.lean}.

This is an unconditional pre-Montel package.  It still does not provide
locally uniform subsequential limits, compactness of the branch families, or
coherent eventual equality on overlaps.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Pointwise-normalised all-stage finite compact-open bridge; PR
\#2728]
The theorems
\begin{Verbatim}
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_pointwiseNormAllStageData_finCompactOpen
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_pointwiseNormAllStageData_finCompactOpen_patch
\end{Verbatim}
restrict a \texttt{LeeYangPointwiseNormalisedAllStageBranchData} package to
finitely many selected Lee--Yang centres and feed those selected branch
families into the finite compact-open diagonal/overlap extraction.

The first theorem returns a
\begin{Verbatim}
Ambient.LeeYangFiniteSubseqBranchLimitFamily
\end{Verbatim}
for the selected centres.  The patched variant immediately composes this
package with the finite local patching theorem, producing one differentiable
function on the finite union of the selected balls.

The concrete \(\mathbb Z^d\) wrappers are
\begin{Verbatim}
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_pointwiseNormAllStageData_finCompactOpen_latticeGraph
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_pointwiseNormAllStageData_finCompactOpen_patch_latticeGraph
\end{Verbatim}
The package wrapper lives in
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
SubseqCompactOpen/FiniteSubseqCompactOpen/CompactOpen.lean
\end{Verbatim}
and is re-exported by the old
\texttt{SubseqCompactOpen/FiniteSubseqCompactOpen.lean} path.  The patch
wrapper lives in
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
SubseqCompactOpen/FiniteCoverPatches/CompactOpen.lean
\end{Verbatim}
under \texttt{Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex}, and is
re-exported by the old
\texttt{SubseqCompactOpen/FiniteCoverPatches.lean} path.

This bridge remains finite and conditional.  It assumes compact-open
compactness of the selected continuous restrictions and explicit eventual
stage-level equality on pairwise overlaps.  It does not prove Montel
compactness or construct coherent branch choices.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Pointwise-normalised all-stage finite Lee--Yang cover bridge;
PR \#2729]
The theorems
\begin{Verbatim}
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_pointwiseNormAllStageData_finCoverCOpen
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_pointwiseNormAllStageData_finCoverCOpen_patch
\end{Verbatim}
upgrade the PR \#2728 finite compact-open bridge to the finite Lee--Yang cover
package.  The extra geometric data is already stored in the all-stage package:
each selected centre has a positive radius and its ball is contained in
\texttt{leeYangDomain}.

The first theorem returns a
\begin{Verbatim}
Ambient.LeeYangFiniteCoverBranchLimitFamily
\end{Verbatim}
for the selected centres.  The patched variant then applies the finite
Lee--Yang cover patching theorem, producing one differentiable function on the
finite union of the selected Lee--Yang balls.

The concrete \(\mathbb Z^d\) wrappers are
\begin{Verbatim}
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_pointwiseNormAllStageData_finCoverCOpen_latticeGraph
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_pointwiseNormAllStageData_finCoverCOpen_patch_latticeGraph
\end{Verbatim}
in
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex/
  SubseqCompactOpen/PointwiseAllStage/FinCover.lean
\end{Verbatim}
and are re-exported through the legacy
\texttt{SubseqCompactOpen/PointwiseAllStage.lean} path.

This bridge remains finite and conditional.  It assumes compact-open
compactness of the selected continuous restrictions and explicit eventual
stage-level equality on pairwise overlaps.  It does not prove Montel
compactness or construct coherent branch choices.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Pointwise-normalised all-stage real-centred compact cover
patch; PR \#2730]
The theorems
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_pointwiseNormAllStageData_finRealCoverCOpen_patch
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_pointwiseNormAllStageData_compactRealCoverCOpen_patch
\end{Verbatim}
add the real-centred and compact-target layers to the PR \#2729 all-stage
finite Lee--Yang cover bridge.  The finite real-centred wrapper uses a
selected index whose centre is the real field \(p.h\), producing a
\begin{Verbatim}
Ambient.LeeYangFiniteRealCoverBranchLimitFamily
\end{Verbatim}
and a patched function on the finite union of selected balls whose value at
\(p.h\) is \(\uparrow\)\texttt{freeEnergyInfinite}.  The compact wrapper adds
a compact target \(K\), the hypotheses \(K\subseteq\texttt{leeYangDomain}\) and
\(p.h\in K\), and a supplied cover of \(K\) by the same finite selected balls.
It returns a
\begin{Verbatim}
Ambient.LeeYangCompactFiniteRealCoverBranchLimitFamily
\end{Verbatim}
and restricts differentiability of the patch to \(K\).

The concrete \(\mathbb Z^d\) wrappers are
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_pointwiseNormAllStageData_finRealCOpen_patch_latticeGraph
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_pointwiseNormAllStageData_compactRealCOpen_patch_latticeGraph
\end{Verbatim}
in
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex/
  SubseqCompactOpen/PointwiseAllStage/RealCover/FinReal.lean
\end{Verbatim}
and
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex/
  SubseqCompactOpen/PointwiseAllStage/RealCover/CompactReal.lean
\end{Verbatim}
and are re-exported through the legacy
\texttt{SubseqCompactOpen/PointwiseAllStage/RealCover.lean} and
\texttt{SubseqCompactOpen/PointwiseAllStage.lean} paths.

This bridge remains finite and conditional.  It assumes compact-open
compactness of the selected continuous restrictions, explicit eventual
stage-level equality on pairwise overlaps, a selected real-centre index, and a
finite selected-ball cover of \(K\).  It does not prove Montel compactness,
construct coherent branch choices, or extract a compact finite subcover from
the point-indexed all-stage cover.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Pointwise-normalised all-stage compact finite subcover
geometry; PR \#2731]
The structure and theorems
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Ambient.LeeYangPointwiseNormAllStageCompactRealFinGeometry
Ambient.exists_finset_cover_of_isCompact_pointwiseNormAllStageData
Ambient.exists_finset_cover_of_isCompact_pointwiseNormAllStageData_real
Ambient.exists_pointwiseNormAllStageCompactRealFinGeometry_of_isCompact
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_pointwiseNormAllStageData_compactRealCOpen_patch_geom
\end{Verbatim}
extract the finite compact geometry that PR \#2730 previously required as an
explicit input.  If \(K\) is compact, \(K\subseteq\texttt{leeYangDomain}\), and
\(p.h\in K\), the point-indexed all-stage Lee--Yang balls stored in
\texttt{LeeYangPointwiseNormalisedAllStageBranchData} have a finite subcover
of \(K\).  The real-centred variant inserts the centre \(p.h\), enumerates the
finite set by \(\texttt{Fin}\ n\), records positivity and Lee--Yang-domain
containment of the selected radii, and stores the real-centre index.

The final wrapper takes this compact finite geometry, compact-open compactness
for the selected continuous branch restrictions, and explicit eventual
stage-level equality on pairwise selected overlaps, then feeds the data to the
PR \#2730 compact real-cover patch bridge.

The concrete \(\mathbb Z^d\) wrappers are
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
exists_pointwiseNormAllStageCompactRealFinGeometry_of_isCompact_latticeGraph
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_pointwiseNormAllStage_compactRealCOpen_patch_geom_latticeGraph
\end{Verbatim}
in
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex/
  SubseqCompactOpen/PointwiseAllStage/CompactGeometry/Geometry.lean
\end{Verbatim}
and
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex/
  SubseqCompactOpen/PointwiseAllStage/CompactGeometry/Patch.lean
\end{Verbatim}
and are re-exported through the legacy paths
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
SubseqCompactOpen/PointwiseAllStage/CompactGeometry.lean
\end{Verbatim}
and
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
SubseqCompactOpen/PointwiseAllStage.lean
\end{Verbatim}

This closes the compact finite-subcover extraction layer for all-stage
point-indexed data.  It still does not prove Montel compactness, locally
uniform subsequential convergence, or coherent branch selection.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Pointwise-normalised all-stage compact-open data package;
PR \#2732]
The structure and theorems
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Ambient.LeeYangPointwiseNormAllStageCompactRealCOpenData
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_allStageCOpenData_patch
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_allStageCOpenData_patch_of_isCompact
\end{Verbatim}
package the compact-open data expected from a Montel extraction over the
selected all-stage compact finite geometry of PR \#2731.  For each selected
Lee--Yang ball the package records a compact set of continuous restrictions, a
continuous restriction for every stage, membership of each restriction in that
compact set, agreement with the original pointwise-normalised branch family,
and eventual stage-level equality on pairwise selected overlaps.

The first wrapper consumes this packaged compact-open data directly and invokes
the compact real-cover patch endpoint.  The second wrapper starts only from a
compact target \(K\subseteq\texttt{leeYangDomain}\) with \(p.h\in K\), extracts
the finite all-stage geometry, and then takes the packaged compact-open data
for that geometry as the remaining input.

The concrete \(\mathbb Z^d\) wrappers are
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_allStageCOpenData_patch_latticeGraph
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_allStageCOpenData_patch_of_isCompact_latticeGraph
\end{Verbatim}
in
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex/
  SubseqCompactOpen/PointwiseAllStage/COpenData.lean
\end{Verbatim}
and are re-exported through the legacy
\texttt{SubseqCompactOpen/PointwiseAllStage.lean} path.

This packages the compact-target Montel/coherence output shape but still does
not prove Montel compactness, locally uniform subsequential convergence, or
coherent branch selection.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[All-stage range-closure compact-open bridge; PR \#2740]
The structure and conversion
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Ambient.LeeYangPointwiseNormAllStageCompactRealRangeClosureCOpenData
Ambient.LeeYangPointwiseNormAllStageCompactRealRangeClosureCOpenData.toCOpenData
\end{Verbatim}
specialise the PR \#2732 compact-open package to carriers of the form
\[
  \overline{\operatorname{range}(\widetilde F_i)}
\]
in the compact-open topology on continuous maps over each selected
Lee--Yang ball.  For each selected ball the input records only the continuous
stage restrictions, compactness of this range closure, the restriction
identity back to the original branch family, and eventual stage-level equality
on pairwise selected overlaps.  The conversion sets the compact-open carrier
to the closure of the range and supplies stage membership by the elementary
\texttt{subset\_closure} inclusion.

The ambient patch endpoints are
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_allStageRangeClosureCOpenData_patch
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_allStageRangeClosureCOpenData_patch_of_isCompact
\end{Verbatim}
and the concrete \(\mathbb Z^d\) wrappers are
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_allStageRangeClosureCOpenData_patch_latticeGraph
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_allStageRangeClosureCOpenData_patch_isCompact_latticeGraph
\end{Verbatim}
in the \texttt{RangeClosure.lean} child module, re-exported by the
\texttt{RangeCompactOpen.lean} umbrella path.

This matches the relative-compactness form naturally expected from a Montel
argument, but it still assumes compactness of the range closure and coherent
eventual overlap equality.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[All-stage relatively compact range bridge; PR \#2741]
The structure and conversion
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Ambient.LeeYangPointwiseNormAllStageCompactRealRangeRelCompactCOpenData
Ambient.LeeYangPointwiseNormAllStageCompactRealRangeRelCompactCOpenData.toRangeClosureData
\end{Verbatim}
replace the direct compactness assumption on
\[
  \overline{\operatorname{range}(\widetilde F_i)}
\]
from PR \#2740 by a compact compact-open carrier \(C_i\) containing the actual
stage-restriction range.  The conversion proves compactness of the range
closure as a closed subset of \(C_i\): the range inclusion gives
\(\overline{\operatorname{range}(\widetilde F_i)}\subseteq C_i\) by
\texttt{closure\_minimal}, while compact subsets of the continuous-map space
are closed.  The resulting range-closure data then feeds the PR \#2740 bridge.

The ambient patch endpoints are
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_allStageRangeRelCompactCOpenData_patch
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_allStageRangeRelCompactCOpenData_patch_of_isCompact
\end{Verbatim}
and the concrete \(\mathbb Z^d\) wrappers are
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_allStageRangeRelCompactCOpen_patch_latticeGraph
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_allStageRangeRelCompact_patch_isCompact_latticeGraph
\end{Verbatim}
in the \texttt{RelCompact.lean} child module, re-exported by the
\texttt{RangeCompactOpen.lean} umbrella path.

This is the standard relative-compactness input shape for the Montel layer:
the compact carrier can be supplied by a later normal-family or Ascoli
argument, while coherent eventual overlap equality remains a separate
hypothesis.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Range norm-bounded relatively compact bridge; PR \#2742]
The conversion
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Ambient.LeeYangPointwiseNormAllStageCompactRealRangeNormBoundedAscoliData.toRangeRelCompactData
\end{Verbatim}
turns the range norm-bounded Ascoli input into the relative-compactness
package of PR \#2741.  For each selected Lee--Yang ball it takes
\[
  C_i=\operatorname{range}(\widetilde F_i)
\]
as the compact carrier.  Compactness of this carrier in the compact-open
topology is supplied by the existing range Ascoli helper from PR \#2736, using
closedness of the pointwise image
\(\texttt{ContinuousMap.toFun}'' C_i\), the stagewise pointwise norm bounds,
and equicontinuity of the range carrier.

The ambient patch endpoints are
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_allStageRangeNormBoundedRelCompact_patch
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_allStageRangeNormBoundedRelCompact_patch_of_isCompact
\end{Verbatim}
and the concrete \(\mathbb Z^d\) wrappers are
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_allStageRangeNormBoundedRelCompact_patch_latticeGraph
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_allStageRangeNormBoundedRelCompact_patch_isCompact_latticeGraph
\end{Verbatim}
in
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex/
  RangeAscoliPatches/AllStageAscoli/RangeNormBounded.lean
\end{Verbatim}
and are re-exported through the legacy
\texttt{RangeAscoliPatches/AllStageAscoli.lean} path.

This exposes the compact carrier produced by the range norm-bounded Ascoli
layer in the relative-compactness shape used by Montel.  It still leaves
closedness of the pointwise range image, the stage-restriction norm bounds,
equicontinuity, and coherent overlap equality as hypotheses.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Branch bounded relatively compact bridge; PR \#2743]
The conversions
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Ambient.LeeYangPointwiseNormAllStageCompactRealBranchNormBoundedAscoliData.toRangeRelCompactData
Ambient.LeeYangPointwiseNormAllStageCompactRealBranchConstNormBoundedAscoliData.toRangeRelCompactData
Ambient.LeeYangPointwiseNormAllStageCompactRealBranchLocallyBoundedAscoliData.toRangeRelCompactData
\end{Verbatim}
compose the branch-family bounded Ascoli layers with the range
relative-compactness package of PR \#2742.  The first conversion transports
branch norm bounds to the selected restriction ranges, the second reduces
ballwise constant bounds to branch norm bounds, and the third chooses those
constants from ballwise local boundedness witnesses.

The ambient patch endpoints are
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_branchNormBoundedRelCompact_patch
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_branchNormBoundedRelCompact_patch_of_isCompact
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_branchConstNormBoundedRelCompact_patch
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_branchConstNormBoundedRelCompact_patch_of_isCompact
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_branchLocallyBoundedRelCompact_patch
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_branchLocallyBoundedRelCompact_patch_of_isCompact
\end{Verbatim}
and the concrete \(\mathbb Z^d\) wrappers now live in the
\texttt{NormBounded.lean}, \texttt{ConstNormBounded.lean}, and
\texttt{LocallyBounded.lean} child modules, re-exported by the
\texttt{BranchRelCompact.lean} umbrella path.

This exposes the final relative-compactness input directly at the
branch-family boundedness boundary used in Glimm--Jaffe's Vitali argument.  It
still leaves local boundedness, closedness of the pointwise range image,
equicontinuity, and coherent overlap equality as hypotheses.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Branch-local direct range patch bridge; PR \#2754]
The direct-range patch endpoints
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_branchLocallyBoundedRelCompact_directRange_patch
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_branchLocallyBoundedRelCompact_directRange_patch_of_isCompact
\end{Verbatim}
expose the existing branch locally bounded Ascoli data-to-relatively compact
range conversion at the compact patch boundary.  Given
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Ambient.LeeYangPointwiseNormAllStageCompactRealBranchLocallyBoundedAscoliData
\end{Verbatim}
the endpoint first constructs the relatively compact range data directly, then
applies the all-stage relatively compact range patch theorem.

The concrete \(\mathbb Z^d\) wrappers are
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_branchLocallyBoundedRelCompact_directRange_patch_latticeGraph
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_branchLocallyBoundedRelCompact_directRange_patch_isCompact_latticeGraph
\end{Verbatim}
in
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex/
  RangeAscoliPatches/BranchLocalDirectRange/LocallyBounded.lean
\end{Verbatim}
and are re-exported through the legacy
\texttt{RangeAscoliPatches/BranchLocalDirectRange.lean} path.  This is a
composition bridge; branch local bounds, closedness, equicontinuity, and
coherent overlap inputs remain explicit.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70.
\end{theorem}

\end{Verbatim}
package the branch locally bounded Ascoli inputs with no separate selected

first extract the compact all-stage geometry from the forgotten all-stage

freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_eventualOverlapBranchLocallyBoundedRelCompact_directRange_patch_isCompact_latticeGraph
\end{Verbatim}
in

Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex/
  RangeAscoliPatches/BranchLocalDirectRange/EventualOverlap.lean
\end{Verbatim}
and are re-exported through the legacy
\texttt{RangeAscoliPatches/BranchLocalDirectRange.lean} path.  This removes

\begin{theorem}[Branch-deviation locally bounded bridge; PR \#2744]
The structure
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Ambient.LeeYangPointwiseNormAllStageCompactRealBranchDeviationAscoliData
\end{Verbatim}
splits the remaining branch-family local boundedness input into two pieces:
local boundedness of the principal finite-volume free energy on each selected
Lee--Yang ball, and a uniform bound on the deviation between the selected
logarithm branch and that principal value.  The conversion
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Ambient.LeeYangPointwiseNormAllStageCompactRealBranchDeviationAscoliData.toBranchLocallyBoundedData
\end{Verbatim}
combines these two estimates by the triangle inequality, producing the branch
locally bounded Ascoli package of PR \#2739.  The further conversion
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Ambient.LeeYangPointwiseNormAllStageCompactRealBranchDeviationAscoliData.toRangeRelCompactData
\end{Verbatim}
then feeds the PR \#2743 relative-compactness bridge.

The ambient patch endpoints are
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_branchDeviationRelCompact_patch
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_branchDeviationRelCompact_patch_of_isCompact
\end{Verbatim}
and the concrete \(\mathbb Z^d\) wrappers are
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\scriptsize]
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_branchDeviationRelCompact_patch_latticeGraph
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_branchDeviationRelCompact_patch_isCompact_latticeGraph
\end{Verbatim}
in the \texttt{RelCompact.lean} child module, re-exported by the umbrella
module.

This isolates the branch-choice deviation estimate needed to turn the existing
Lee--Yang local bounded free-energy bounds into branch-family Montel input.
Closedness, equicontinuity, branch-deviation bounds, and coherent overlap
equality remain explicit hypotheses.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70.
\end{theorem}

The direct-range patch endpoints

Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_branchDeviationRelCompact_directRange_patch_of_isCompact

branch-deviation bounds, closedness, equicontinuity, and coherent overlap inputs

\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Branch-deviation named via-local direct range route; PR \#2767]
The conversion
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\scriptsize]
Ambient.LeeYangPointwiseNormAllStageCompactRealBranchDeviationAscoliData.toRangeRelCompactData_viaLocal
\end{Verbatim}
names the ordinary all-stage route that first turns branch-deviation Ascoli
data into branch-local bounded Ascoli data, then feeds the branch-local
direct-range relatively compact conversion.  This is a named restatement of
the existing branch-deviation range conversion, with the branch-local
intermediate exposed at the same directRange boundary as the direct
branch-deviation endpoint of PR \#2755.

The ambient patch endpoints are
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\scriptsize]
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_branchDeviationViaLocalRelCompact_directRange_patch
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_branchDeviationViaLocalRelCompact_directRange_patch_of_isCompact
\end{Verbatim}
and the concrete \(\mathbb Z^d\) wrappers are
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\scriptsize]
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_branchDeviationViaLocalRelCompact_directRange_patch_latticeGraph
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_branchDeviationViaLocalRelCompact_directRange_patch_isCompact_latticeGraph
\end{Verbatim}
in the \texttt{ViaLocal.lean} child module, re-exported by the umbrella module.

The named route is a composition result: the principal finite-volume
free-energy bound and branch-deviation bound are combined by the triangle
inequality to recover branch-local boundedness, after which the direct
branch-local range conversion supplies relatively compact range data.
Principal free-energy bounds, branch-deviation bounds, closedness,
equicontinuity, restriction identities, and ordinary coherent overlap equality
remain explicit inputs.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Positive-real ordinary branch-deviation compact-target wrappers; PR \#2770]
The positive-real compact-target endpoints
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_posRealBranchDeviationRelCompact_patch_of_isCompact

\end{Verbatim}
construct pointwise-normalised all-stage branch data from positive real
ferromagnetic parameters, extract the compact finite all-stage geometry, and

\(\mathbb Z^d\) wrappers are
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\scriptsize]

freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_posRealBranchDeviation_directRange_patch_isCompact_latticeGraph
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_posRealBranchDeviationViaLocal_directRange_patch_isCompact_latticeGraph
\end{Verbatim}
respectively in the \texttt{RelCompact.lean}, \texttt{DirectRange.lean}, and
\texttt{ViaLocal.lean} child modules, re-exported by the
\texttt{PositiveReal.lean} umbrella path.

These wrappers add no compactness step.  Principal free-energy bounds,
branch-deviation bounds, closedness, equicontinuity, restriction identities,
and ordinary coherent overlap equality remain explicit through the supplied
branch-deviation Ascoli data.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70.
\end{theorem}

in \texttt{Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex.lean}.  This

\begin{theorem}[Eventual-overlap branch-deviation via-local bridge; PR \#2764]
The conversions
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]

free-energy bound and the branch-deviation bound already carried by
\texttt{LeeYangPointwiseNormAllStageCompactRealEventualOverlapBranchDeviationAscoliData}
with the triangle inequality.  The selected-overlap field remains supplied by
\texttt{LeeYangRealPointwiseNormalisedEventualOverlapBranchData}.

The patch endpoints
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_eventualOverlapBranchDeviationViaLocalRelCompact_directRange_patch
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_eventualOverlapBranchDeviationViaLocalRelCompact_directRange_patch_of_isCompact
\end{Verbatim}
then reuse the PR \#2759 direct eventual-overlap branch-local relatively
compact range route.

\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_eventualOverlapBranchDeviationViaLocalRelCompact_directRange_patch_latticeGraph
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_eventualOverlapBranchDeviationViaLocalRelCompact_directRange_patch_isCompact_latticeGraph
\end{Verbatim}
in \texttt{Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex.lean}.  This
records the non-closed-ball eventual-overlap analogue of the

\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Eventual-overlap branch-local via-deviation bridge; PR \#2765]
The non-closed-ball branch-local input can also be routed through the
branch-deviation boundary when the principal finite-volume free energy is
locally bounded on every selected Lee--Yang ball.  The all-stage conversion
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Ambient.LeeYangPointwiseNormAllStageCompactRealBranchLocallyBoundedAscoliData.toBranchDeviationData
Ambient.LeeYangPointwiseNormAllStageCompactRealBranchLocallyBoundedAscoliData.toRangeRelCompactData_viaDeviation
\end{Verbatim}
takes the usual branch-local Ascoli data together with an explicit
\texttt{freeEnergy\_bound} field.  On a selected ball the triangle inequality
gives
\[
  \|b_{i,m}(z)-F_m(z)\|
  \le \|b_{i,m}(z)\|+\|F_m(z)\|,
\]
so a branch-local bound and the principal free-energy bound produce the
branch-deviation estimate required by the PR \#2744 branch-deviation package.
Closedness, equicontinuity, restrictions, and overlap equality are preserved
from the branch-local input.

For pointwise-normalised eventual-overlap data, the parallel conversion
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Ambient.LeeYangPointwiseNormAllStageCompactRealEventualOverlapBranchLocallyBoundedAscoliData.toBranchDeviationData
Ambient.LeeYangPointwiseNormAllStageCompactRealEventualOverlapBranchLocallyBoundedAscoliData.toRangeRelCompactData_viaDeviation
\end{Verbatim}
preserves the same branch-local Ascoli fields and records the derived
branch-deviation bound.  The coherent selected-overlap input is still supplied
by the eventual-overlap package when the downstream ordinary branch-deviation
route is formed.

The resulting abstract patch endpoints are
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_branchLocalViaDeviationRelCompact_directRange_patch
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_branchLocalViaDeviationRelCompact_directRange_patch_of_isCompact
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_eventualOverlapBranchLocalViaDeviationRelCompact_directRange_patch
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_eventualOverlapBranchLocalViaDeviationRelCompact_directRange_patch_of_isCompact
\end{Verbatim}
and the concrete \(\mathbb Z^d\) wrappers are
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_branchLocalViaDeviationRelCompact_directRange_patch_latticeGraph
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_branchLocalViaDeviationRelCompact_directRange_patch_isCompact_latticeGraph
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_eventualOverlapBranchLocalViaDeviationRelCompact_directRange_patch_latticeGraph
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_eventualOverlapBranchLocalViaDeviationRelCompact_directRange_patch_isCompact_latticeGraph
\end{Verbatim}
in
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex/
  RangeAscoliPatches/BranchLocalDirectRange/ViaDeviation.lean
Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex/
  RangeAscoliPatches/BranchLocalDirectRange/EventualOverlapViaDeviation.lean
\end{Verbatim}
and are re-exported through the legacy
\texttt{RangeAscoliPatches/BranchLocalDirectRange.lean} path.  This records
the non-closed-ball analogue of the PR \#2762 closed-ball
branch-local to branch-deviation route, with the principal free-energy bound
kept as an explicit input rather than supplied automatically by closed-ball
containment.  The all-stage endpoint keeps coherent overlap equality as an
ordinary branch-local input, while the eventual-overlap endpoint fills selected
overlap from eventual-overlap data.  It still does not construct
eventual-overlap data or prove principal free-energy bounds, branch-local
bounds, closedness, equicontinuity, restriction identities, or ordinary
all-stage coherent overlap equality where eventual-overlap data is not used.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe
\cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Closed-ball free-energy bound bridge; PR \#2745]
The structure
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Ambient.LeeYangClosedBallPointwiseNormalisedAllStageBranchData
\end{Verbatim}
is a closed-ball variant of the pre-Montel all-stage branch-choice package.
It carries the usual pointwise-normalised branch family and also records that
each selected radius has its closed ball contained in
\texttt{leeYangDomain}.  For positive real ferromagnetic parameters, the
constructor
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Ambient.exists_leeYangClosedBallPointwiseNormalisedAllStageBranchData_of_positive_real
\end{Verbatim}
chooses those radii from the closed-ball Lee--Yang domain lemma and then uses
the corresponding open balls for the finite-stage logarithm branches.

The structure
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Ambient.LeeYangPointwiseNormAllStageCompactRealClosedBallBranchDeviationAscoliData
\end{Verbatim}
keeps the closed-ball all-stage data and the branch-deviation, closedness,
equicontinuity, restriction, and overlap inputs.  Its conversion
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Ambient.LeeYangPointwiseNormAllStageCompactRealClosedBallBranchDeviationAscoliData.toDeviationData
\end{Verbatim}
fills the principal finite-volume free-energy bound required by PR \#2744
using the closed-ball Lee--Yang locally bounded free-energy theorem.  The
ambient patch endpoints are
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_closedBallBranchDeviationRelCompact_patch
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_closedBallBranchDeviationRelCompact_patch_of_isCompact
\end{Verbatim}
with concrete \(\mathbb Z^d\) wrappers in
\texttt{Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex.lean}, including
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
exists_leeYangClosedBallPointwiseNormalisedAllStageBranchData_of_positive_real_latticeGraph
\end{Verbatim}
for the closed-ball data constructor.

Thus the principal free-energy boundedness input is no longer separate once
closed-ball branch radii are available.  The remaining analytic inputs are the
branch-deviation bound, closedness, equicontinuity, and coherent overlap
equality.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Positive-real closed-ball branch-data bridge; PR \#2746]
The compact-target endpoint
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_posRealClosedBallDeviation_patch_of_isCompact
\end{Verbatim}
packages the positive-real branch-data construction from PR \#2745 into the
closed-ball branch-deviation patch.  Under positive real ferromagnetic
parameters, compactness of \(K\), \(K\subseteq\texttt{leeYangDomain}\), and
membership of the real field in \(K\), the theorem first constructs
\[
  \texttt{LeeYangClosedBallPointwiseNormalisedAllStageBranchData}
\]
and then extracts the finite compact all-stage geometry from that branch data.
The resulting existential package consumes only the remaining closed-ball
branch-deviation Ascoli data and returns the same compact finite-cover patch
conclusion as PR \#2745.

The concrete \(\mathbb Z^d\) wrapper is
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_posRealClosedBallDeviation_patch_isCompact_latticeGraph
\end{Verbatim}
in \texttt{Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex.lean}.  This
removes the separate caller-supplied closed-ball branch-data input at the
compact-target boundary.  Closedness, equicontinuity, branch-deviation bounds,
and coherent overlap equality remain explicit hypotheses.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Closed-ball branch local-boundedness bridge; PR \#2747]
The structure
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Ambient.LeeYangClosedBallBranchLocallyBoundedAscoliData
\end{Verbatim}
replaces the explicit closed-ball branch-deviation estimate by local
boundedness of the selected branch family on each selected Lee--Yang ball.
Its conversion
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Ambient.LeeYangClosedBallBranchLocallyBoundedAscoliData.toClosedBallDeviationData
\end{Verbatim}
uses the closed-ball Lee--Yang principal free-energy bound and the triangle
inequality to produce the PR \#2745 closed-ball branch-deviation package.
The compact-target endpoint
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_posRealClosedBallBranchLocallyBounded_patch_of_isCompact
\end{Verbatim}
also constructs the closed-ball all-stage branch data from positive real
ferromagnetic parameters before extracting the finite compact geometry.

The concrete \(\mathbb Z^d\) wrappers include
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_closedBallBranchLocallyBoundedRelCompact_patch_latticeGraph
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_closedBallBranchLocallyBoundedRelCompact_patch_isCompact_latticeGraph
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_posRealClosedBallBranchLocallyBounded_patch_isCompact_latticeGraph
\end{Verbatim}
in \texttt{Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex.lean}.  The
remaining inputs are branch local bounds, closedness, equicontinuity, and
coherent overlap equality.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Direct closed-ball branch local-boundedness bridge; PR \#2748]
The conversion
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Ambient.LeeYangClosedBallBranchLocallyBoundedAscoliData.toBranchLocallyBoundedData
\end{Verbatim}
forgets only the closed-ball containment data and produces the underlying
pointwise-normalised branch locally bounded Ascoli package.  Therefore the
direct endpoint
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_posRealClosedBallBranchLocallyBounded_direct_patch_of_isCompact
\end{Verbatim}
feeds PR \#2743's branch locally bounded relative-compactness bridge without
first building a closed-ball branch-deviation package.

The concrete \(\mathbb Z^d\) wrappers include
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_closedBallBranchLocallyBoundedRelCompact_direct_patch_latticeGraph
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_closedBallBranchLocallyBoundedRelCompact_direct_patch_isCompact_latticeGraph
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_posRealClosedBallBranchLocallyBounded_direct_patch_isCompact_latticeGraph
\end{Verbatim}
in \texttt{Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex.lean}.  The
remaining inputs are branch local bounds, closedness, equicontinuity, and
coherent overlap equality.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Direct closed-ball branch range relative-compact conversion; PR \#2749]
The conversion
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Ambient.LeeYangClosedBallBranchLocallyBoundedAscoliData.toRangeRelCompactData_direct
\end{Verbatim}
composes the PR \#2748 direct closed-ball-to-branch-locally-bounded
conversion with PR \#2743's branch locally bounded relative-compactness data
conversion.  It therefore produces the relative-compact range package directly
from closed-ball branch locally bounded Ascoli data, without first constructing
closed-ball branch-deviation data.

This is a data-level bridge, so it avoids the auxiliary positivity and
nonempty-volume hypotheses that were needed only by the older route through the
closed-ball Lee--Yang principal free-energy bound.  The remaining inputs are
branch local bounds, closedness, equicontinuity, and coherent overlap equality.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Closed-ball branch-deviation to branch-local bridge; PR \#2750]
The conversion
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Ambient.LeeYangPointwiseNormAllStageCompactRealClosedBallBranchDeviationAscoliData.toClosedBallBranchLocallyBoundedData
\end{Verbatim}
recovers closed-ball branch locally bounded Ascoli data from closed-ball
branch-deviation Ascoli data.  On each selected Lee--Yang ball, the
closed-ball Lee--Yang principal free-energy bound controls the principal
finite-volume free energy, while the supplied deviation bound controls the
difference between the selected branch and that principal value.  The triangle
inequality gives a branch-family local bound.

The conversion preserves the same restriction, closedness, equicontinuity, and
coherent overlap fields.  It does not prove the branch-deviation estimate or
the Ascoli side conditions; it only exposes the automatic passage from
closed-ball deviation data to the branch-local boundary.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Closed-ball branch-deviation direct range bridge; PR \#2751]
The conversion
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Ambient.LeeYangPointwiseNormAllStageCompactRealClosedBallBranchDeviationAscoliData.toRangeRelCompactData_closedBallLocal_direct
Ambient.LeeYangPointwiseNormAllStageCompactRealClosedBallBranchDeviationAscoliData.toRangeRelCompactData_direct
\end{Verbatim}
maps closed-ball branch-deviation Ascoli data directly to relatively compact
range data through the branch-local boundary.  It first applies the PR \#2750
conversion from closed-ball branch-deviation data to closed-ball branch
locally bounded Ascoli data, then applies the PR \#2749 direct conversion from
closed-ball branch locally bounded Ascoli data to relatively compact range
data.  PR \#2757 adds the direct-route alias
\texttt{toRangeRelCompactData\_direct} so this data boundary matches the
closed-ball branch-local direct conversion naming.

This is a composition bridge.  It adds no new analytic side condition beyond
the closed-ball Lee--Yang principal free-energy hypotheses already needed for
PR \#2750.  Restriction, closedness, equicontinuity, branch-deviation, and
coherent overlap inputs remain explicit.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Closed-ball branch-deviation named via-local direct range route; PR \#2768]
The conversion
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Ambient.LeeYangPointwiseNormAllStageCompactRealClosedBallBranchDeviationAscoliData.toRangeRelCompactData_viaLocal_direct
\end{Verbatim}
names the ordinary closed-ball route that first turns closed-ball
branch-deviation Ascoli data into closed-ball branch-local bounded Ascoli data,
then feeds the direct closed-ball branch-local relatively compact conversion.
This is a named restatement of the PR \#2751/PR \#2757 closed-ball
branch-deviation range conversion, with the branch-local intermediate exposed
under the same via-local naming used by the eventual-overlap closed-ball route.

The ambient patch endpoints are
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_closedBallBranchDeviationViaLocalRelCompact_directRange_patch
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_closedBallBranchDeviationViaLocalRelCompact_directRange_patch_of_isCompact
\end{Verbatim}
and the concrete \(\mathbb Z^d\) wrappers are
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_closedBallBranchDeviationViaLocalRelCompact_directRange_patch_latticeGraph
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_closedBallBranchDeviationViaLocalRelCompact_directRange_patch_isCompact_latticeGraph
\end{Verbatim}
in \texttt{Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex.lean}.

The named route adds no new compactness step.  Closed-ball containment and
branch-deviation bounds recover branch-local boundedness through the existing
closed-ball triangle-inequality conversion, after which the direct closed-ball
branch-local range conversion supplies relatively compact range data.
Closedness, equicontinuity, restriction identities, and ordinary coherent
overlap equality remain explicit inputs.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70.
\end{theorem}

Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70.
