\begin{theorem}[All-stage Arzelà--Ascoli compact-open bridge; PR \#2733]
The generic theorem
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
isCompact_compactOpen_complex_of_isCompact_toFun_image_equicontinuous
\end{Verbatim}
wraps mathlib's Arzelà--Ascoli theorem for complex-valued continuous maps:
compactness of the pointwise function-space image, together with
equicontinuity, implies compactness in the compact-open topology.

The ambient structure and wrappers
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Ambient.LeeYangPointwiseNormAllStageCompactRealAscoliData
Ambient.LeeYangPointwiseNormAllStageCompactRealAscoliData.toCOpenData
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_allStageAscoliData_patch
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_allStageAscoliData_patch_of_isCompact
\end{Verbatim}
apply this theorem to the selected all-stage compact finite geometry from
PR \#2731.  For each selected Lee--Yang ball, the Ascoli data records a carrier
of continuous restrictions, compactness of its image under
\(\texttt{ContinuousMap.toFun}\), equicontinuity, membership of every stage
restriction, agreement with the original branch family, and eventual equality
on pairwise selected overlaps.  The conversion theorem supplies the compact
open data of PR \#2732, after which the compact real-cover patch endpoint is
invoked.

The concrete \(\mathbb Z^d\) wrappers are
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_allStageAscoliData_patch_latticeGraph
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_allStageAscoliData_patch_of_isCompact_latticeGraph
\end{Verbatim}
in
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex/
  RangeAscoliPatches/AllStageAscoli/Ascoli.lean
\end{Verbatim}
and are re-exported through the legacy
\texttt{RangeAscoliPatches/AllStageAscoli.lean} path.

This narrows the remaining Montel input to compactness of the selected
\(\texttt{ContinuousMap.toFun}\) images in the pointwise function-space
topology, equicontinuity of the selected branch restrictions, and coherent
overlap equality.  It still does not derive those inputs from holomorphy and
local boundedness.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[All-stage closed-product Arzelà--Ascoli bridge; PR \#2734]
The generic theorems
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
isCompact_toFun_image_complex_of_isClosed_subset_pi_compacts
isCompact_compactOpen_complex_of_isClosed_subset_pi_compacts_equicontinuous
\end{Verbatim}
wrap Tychonoff compactness for complex-valued function spaces.  If the
\(\texttt{ContinuousMap.toFun}\) image of a carrier is closed in the pointwise
function space and every point evaluation lands in a compact target set, then
the image is compact.  With equicontinuity, the PR \#2733 Arzelà--Ascoli bridge
then gives compactness in the compact-open topology.

The ambient structure and wrappers
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Ambient.LeeYangPointwiseNormAllStageCompactRealClosedProductAscoliData
Ambient.LeeYangPointwiseNormAllStageCompactRealClosedProductAscoliData.toAscoliData
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_allStageClosedProductAscoliData_patch
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_allStageClosedProductAscoliData_patch_of_isCompact
\end{Verbatim}
apply this closed-product input to the selected all-stage compact finite
geometry from PR \#2731.  For each selected Lee--Yang ball, the data records a
carrier of continuous restrictions, compact pointwise target sets, closedness
of the pointwise function-space image, membership of every carrier element in
the pointwise targets, equicontinuity, stage membership, restriction
identities, and eventual equality on pairwise selected overlaps.  The
conversion supplies the PR \#2733 Ascoli data, then the PR \#2732 compact-open
data, and finally the compact real-cover patch endpoint.

The concrete \(\mathbb Z^d\) wrappers are
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_allStageClosedProductAscoliData_patch_latticeGraph
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_allStageClosedProductAscoliData_patch_of_isCompact_latticeGraph
\end{Verbatim}
in
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex/
  RangeAscoliPatches/AllStageAscoli/ClosedProduct.lean
\end{Verbatim}
and are re-exported through the legacy
\texttt{RangeAscoliPatches/AllStageAscoli.lean} path.

This splits the remaining normal-family input into closedness of the
pointwise function-space image, compact pointwise target bounds,
equicontinuity, and coherent overlap equality.  It still does not derive those
inputs from holomorphy and local boundedness.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[All-stage norm-bounded Arzelà--Ascoli bridge; PR \#2735]
The generic theorems
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
isCompact_toFun_image_complex_of_isClosed_norm_le
isCompact_compactOpen_complex_of_isClosed_norm_le_equicontinuous
\end{Verbatim}
specialise the PR \#2734 closed-product compactness bridge.  If the
\(\texttt{ContinuousMap.toFun}\) image of a carrier is closed in the pointwise
function space and every carrier element satisfies a pointwise norm bound
\(\|f(x)\|\le R(x)\), then the compact pointwise target at \(x\) is taken to
be the closed complex ball \(\overline B(0,R(x))\).  Tychonoff compactness
gives compactness of the pointwise image, and equicontinuity then gives
compactness in the compact-open topology by Arzelà--Ascoli.

The ambient structure and wrappers
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Ambient.LeeYangPointwiseNormAllStageCompactRealNormBoundedAscoliData
Ambient.LeeYangPointwiseNormAllStageCompactRealNormBoundedAscoliData.toClosedProductData
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_allStageNormBoundedAscoliData_patch
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_allStageNormBoundedAscoliData_patch_of_isCompact
\end{Verbatim}
apply this norm-bounded input to the selected all-stage compact finite
geometry from PR \#2731.  The conversion builds the PR \#2734
closed-product data by setting each pointwise compact target to
\(\overline B(0,R_i(x))\), then reuses the PR \#2734, PR \#2733, and
PR \#2732 bridges to reach the compact real-cover patch endpoint.

The concrete \(\mathbb Z^d\) wrappers are
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_allStageNormBoundedAscoliData_patch_latticeGraph
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_allStageNormBoundedAscoliData_patch_of_isCompact_latticeGraph
\end{Verbatim}
in
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex/
  RangeAscoliPatches/AllStageAscoli/NormBounded.lean
\end{Verbatim}
and are re-exported through the legacy
\texttt{RangeAscoliPatches/AllStageAscoli.lean} path.

This removes the separately supplied compact pointwise target sets from the
closed-product input.  It still does not derive the closedness, pointwise norm
bounds, equicontinuity, or coherent overlap equality from holomorphy and local
boundedness.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[All-stage range norm-bounded Arzelà--Ascoli bridge; PR \#2736]
The generic theorems
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
isCompact_toFun_range_complex_of_isClosed_norm_le
isCompact_compactOpen_range_complex_of_isClosed_norm_le_equicontinuous
\end{Verbatim}
specialise the PR \#2735 norm-bounded bridge to carriers of the form
\(\operatorname{range}(F)\), where \(F : \alpha \to C(X,\mathbb C)\).
If the \(\texttt{ContinuousMap.toFun}\) image of this range is closed and
the stagewise bounds \(\|F(a)(x)\|\le R(x)\) hold, then every carrier element
satisfies the carrier-wide norm bound by range membership.  The previous
closed-ball and Arzelà--Ascoli bridges then give compactness of the pointwise
image, and compact-open compactness under equicontinuity.

The ambient structure and conversion
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Ambient.LeeYangPointwiseNormAllStageCompactRealRangeNormBoundedAscoliData
Ambient.LeeYangPointwiseNormAllStageCompactRealRangeNormBoundedAscoliData.toNormBoundedData
\end{Verbatim}
fix each selected carrier to be the range of the continuous stage
restrictions.  The conversion produces
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Ambient.LeeYangPointwiseNormAllStageCompactRealNormBoundedAscoliData
\end{Verbatim}
by using the range witness for membership and then reuses the PR \#2735,
PR \#2734, PR \#2733, and PR \#2732 bridges.

The ambient patch endpoints are
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_allStageRangeNormBoundedAscoliData_patch
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_allStageRangeNormBoundedAscoliData_patch_of_isCompact
\end{Verbatim}
and the concrete \(\mathbb Z^d\) wrappers are
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_allStageRangeNormBoundedAscoliData_patch_latticeGraph
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_allStageRangeNormBoundedAscoliData_patch_isCompact_latticeGraph
\end{Verbatim}
in
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex/
  RangeAscoliPatches/AllStageAscoli/RangeNormBounded.lean
\end{Verbatim}
and are re-exported through the legacy
\texttt{RangeAscoliPatches/AllStageAscoli.lean} path.

This removes the separate carrier and membership bookkeeping from the
norm-bounded Ascoli input.  It still does not derive closedness of the range
pointwise images, stage restriction norm bounds, equicontinuity, or coherent
overlap equality from holomorphy and local boundedness.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[All-stage branch norm-bounded Arzelà--Ascoli bridge; PR \#2737]
The ambient structure and conversion
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Ambient.LeeYangPointwiseNormAllStageCompactRealBranchNormBoundedAscoliData
Ambient.LeeYangPointwiseNormAllStageCompactRealBranchNormBoundedAscoliData.toRangeNormBoundedData
\end{Verbatim}
move the pointwise norm-bound input one step closer to the original
Glimm--Jaffe branch family.  Instead of requiring
\[
  \|\widetilde F_{i,m}(x)\| \le R_i(x)
\]
for the auxiliary continuous restriction, the branch package assumes
\[
  \|F_{i,m}(z)\| \le R_i(z)
\]
for the selected branch function
\(\texttt{data.branchData.branchFamily}(\texttt{geom.center}\ i)\ m\).
The recorded identity \(F_{i,m}(z)=\widetilde F_{i,m}(z)\) on the selected
ball transports this estimate to the PR \#2736 range norm-bounded package.

The ambient patch endpoints are
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_allStageBranchNormBoundedAscoliData_patch
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_allStageBranchNormBoundedAscoliData_patch_of_isCompact
\end{Verbatim}
and the concrete \(\mathbb Z^d\) wrappers are
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_allStageBranchNormBoundedAscoliData_patch_latticeGraph
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_allStageBranchNormBoundedAscoliData_patch_of_isCompact_latticeGraph
\end{Verbatim}
in
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex/
  BranchAscoliCompactOpen/AscoliPatches/NormBounded.lean
Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex/
  BranchAscoliCompactOpen/AscoliPatches/CompactTarget.lean
\end{Verbatim}
and are re-exported through the legacy AscoliPatches path.

This removes the need to state norm bounds directly on the continuous
restrictions.  It still does not derive the branch-family norm bounds,
closedness of the range pointwise images, equicontinuity, or coherent overlap
equality from holomorphy and local boundedness.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[All-stage branch constant norm-bounded Arzelà--Ascoli bridge; PR \#2738]
The ambient structure and conversion
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Ambient.LeeYangPointwiseNormAllStageCompactRealBranchConstNormBoundedAscoliData
Ambient.LeeYangPointwiseNormAllStageCompactRealBranchConstNormBoundedAscoliData.toBranchNormBoundedData
\end{Verbatim}
specialise the PR \#2737 branch norm-bounded package to the case where each
selected Lee--Yang ball has a single real norm bound \(C_i\).  The package
assumes
\[
  \|F_{i,m}(z)\| \le C_i
\]
for every stage \(m\) and every \(z\) in the selected ball.  The conversion
uses the pointwise constant function \(x\mapsto C_i\) as the bound required by
the branch norm-bounded Ascoli package.

The ambient patch endpoints are
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_allStageBranchConstNormBoundedAscoliData_patch
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_allStageBranchConstNormBoundedAscoliData_patch_of_isCompact
\end{Verbatim}
and the concrete \(\mathbb Z^d\) wrappers are
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_allStageBranchConstNormBoundedAscoliData_patch_latticeGraph
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_allStageBranchConstAscoli_patch_isCompact_latticeGraph
\end{Verbatim}
The first lives in
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex/
  BranchAscoliCompactOpen/AscoliPatches/ConstNormBounded.lean
\end{Verbatim}
and is re-exported through the legacy AscoliPatches path; the compact-target
cover wrapper lives in
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex/
  BranchAscoliCompactOpen/CoverPatches/ConstCompactTarget.lean
\end{Verbatim}
and is re-exported through the legacy CoverPatches path.

This narrows the branch-family norm-bound input to ballwise uniform estimates.
It still does not derive those estimates, closedness of the range pointwise
images, equicontinuity, or coherent overlap equality from holomorphy and local
boundedness.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[All-stage branch locally bounded Ascoli bridge; PR \#2739]
The structure
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Ambient.LeeYangPointwiseNormAllStageCompactRealBranchLocallyBoundedAscoliData
\end{Verbatim}
is the locally bounded variant of the PR \#2738 branch constant norm-bounded
Ascoli input.  For each selected Lee--Yang ball it assumes
\[
  \exists C_i,\ \forall m,\ \forall z\in B_i,\quad
    \|F_{i,m}(z)\| \le C_i .
\]
The conversion
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Ambient.LeeYangPointwiseNormAllStageCompactRealBranchLocallyBoundedAscoliData.toConstData
\end{Verbatim}
chooses these constants and feeds them to the PR \#2738 constant norm-bounded
package.

The ambient patch endpoints are
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_allStageBranchLocallyBoundedAscoliData_patch
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_allStageBranchLocallyBoundedAscoliData_patch_of_isCompact
\end{Verbatim}
and the concrete \(\mathbb Z^d\) wrappers are
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_allStageBranchLocallyBoundedAscoliData_patch_latticeGraph
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_allStageBranchLocallyBoundedAscoli_patch_isCompact_latticeGraph
\end{Verbatim}
in
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex/
  BranchAscoliCompactOpen/CoverPatches/LocallyBounded.lean
Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex/
  BranchAscoliCompactOpen/CoverPatches/LocallyBoundedCompactTarget.lean
\end{Verbatim}
and are re-exported through the legacy CoverPatches path.

This narrows the branch-family norm-bound input to local boundedness witnesses
on each selected ball.  It still does not prove those witnesses, closedness of
the range pointwise images, equicontinuity, or coherent overlap equality.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Raw Lee--Yang local-cover branch-data packaging; PR \#2715]
The theorems
\begin{Verbatim}
Ambient.exists_leeYangLocalBranchLimitFamily_of_branchData
Ambient.exists_leeYangRealBranchLimitFamily_of_branchData
\end{Verbatim}
turn the raw data expected from a coherent local-cover Montel extraction into
the packaged endpoint.  The input consists, for each
\(h_0\in\texttt{leeYangDomain}\), of a radius \(r(h_0)>0\), containment
\[
  B(h_0,r(h_0))\subset\texttt{leeYangDomain},
\]
branch functions \(F_{h_0,n}\), local limits \(f_{h_0}\), branch identities
\[
  \exp(|\Lambda_n|F_{h_0,n}(z))
    = Z_{\Lambda_n}(p.J,z,p.\beta),
\]
locally uniform convergence \(F_{h_0,n}\to f_{h_0}\) on the ball, and
compatibility
\[
  f_{h_0}=f_{h_1}
  \quad\hbox{on}\quad
  B(h_0,r(h_0))\cap B(h_1,r(h_1)).
\]
The real-centred constructor additionally assumes
\((p.h:\mathbb C)\in\texttt{leeYangDomain}\) and the normalisation
\[
  F_{p.h,n}(p.h)
    = f_{\Lambda_n}(p.J,p.h,p.\beta).
\]

The wrapper
\begin{Verbatim}
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_branchData_localCover_real
\end{Verbatim}
then composes this packaging with the PR \#2704 real-centred endpoint.  It
returns a \texttt{LeeYangRealBranchLimitFamily} and a differentiable global
patch on \texttt{leeYangDomain}; the patch agrees directly with each raw
local limit \(f_{h_0}\) on \(B(h_0,r(h_0))\), agrees with the packaged
local limits, and has value \texttt{freeEnergyInfinite} at \(p.h\).  The
concrete \(\mathbb Z^d\) wrappers are
\begin{Verbatim}
exists_leeYangLocalBranchLimitFamily_of_branchData_latticeGraph
exists_leeYangRealBranchLimitFamily_of_branchData_latticeGraph
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_branchData_localCover_real_latticeGraph
\end{Verbatim}
The first two branch-family wrappers now live in
\begin{Verbatim}
Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex/Branches/EventualOverlap/
  RawBranchData.lean
\end{Verbatim}
and are re-exported by the old
\begin{Verbatim}
Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex/Branches/EventualOverlap.lean
\end{Verbatim}
path.  The real local-cover patch wrapper now lives in
\begin{Verbatim}
Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex/Branches/RealCompact/
  RawPatch.lean
\end{Verbatim}
and is re-exported by the old
\begin{Verbatim}
Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex/Branches/RealCompact.lean
\end{Verbatim}
.

This is still post-Montel.  It does not prove compactness, diagonal
extraction, or coherent branch selection; it records the exact raw-data shape
that suffices for the real-centred packaged local-cover endpoint.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Eventual-overlap Lee--Yang local-cover packaging; PR \#2716]
The generic theorem
\begin{Verbatim}
pairwise_eqOn_of_tendstoLocallyUniformlyOn_of_eventuallyEqOn_indexed
\end{Verbatim}
extends the finite overlap-compatibility handoff to arbitrary index types.  If
\(F_{i,n}\to f_i\) locally uniformly on open sets \(U_i\), and for every pair
\(i,j\)
\[
  F_{i,n}=F_{j,n}
  \quad\hbox{eventually on}\quad U_i\cap U_j,
\]
then \(f_i=f_j\) on \(U_i\cap U_j\).

The ambient constructors
\begin{Verbatim}
Ambient.exists_leeYangLocalBranchLimitFamily_of_branchData_eventuallyEqOn
Ambient.exists_leeYangRealBranchLimitFamily_of_branchData_eventuallyEqOn
\end{Verbatim}
apply this theorem to the full point-indexed Lee--Yang ball cover.  Thus the
raw branch-data package no longer needs limit compatibility as a separate
input; it is derived from locally uniform convergence and eventual
stage-level overlap equality.  The real patching wrapper
\begin{Verbatim}
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_branchData_eventuallyEqOn_localCover_real
\end{Verbatim}
then invokes the PR \#2715 raw-data endpoint and returns the same real-centred
global patch conclusion.

The branch-family concrete \(\mathbb Z^d\) wrappers now live in
\begin{Verbatim}
Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex/Branches/EventualOverlap/
  EventuallyEqOn.lean
\end{Verbatim}
and are re-exported by the old
\begin{Verbatim}
Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex/Branches/EventualOverlap.lean
\end{Verbatim}
path.  The real local-cover patch wrapper now lives in
\begin{Verbatim}
Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex/Branches/RealCompact/
  RawPatch.lean
\end{Verbatim}
and is re-exported by the old
\begin{Verbatim}
Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex/Branches/RealCompact.lean
\end{Verbatim}
.

This is still a conditional coherent-overlap handoff.  It does not construct
the coherent branch choices, prove Montel compactness, or perform the
diagonal local-cover extraction.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Structured eventual-overlap branch data; PR \#2717]
The structures
\begin{Verbatim}
Ambient.LeeYangEventualOverlapBranchData
Ambient.LeeYangRealEventualOverlapBranchData
\end{Verbatim}
bundle the full point-indexed Lee--Yang local-cover input expected after a
coherent extraction: the radii, containment of the balls in
\texttt{leeYangDomain}, the selected per-stage branches, their local limits,
locally uniform convergence, coherent eventual stage-level equality on
overlaps, and, in the real-centred structure, centre membership and
normalisation to the finite-volume free energy sequence.

The constructors
\begin{Verbatim}
Ambient.exists_leeYangLocalBranchLimitFamily_of_eventualOverlapBranchData
Ambient.exists_leeYangRealBranchLimitFamily_of_realEventualOverlapBranchData
\end{Verbatim}
consume these structures directly and produce the packaged
\texttt{LeeYangLocalBranchLimitFamily} and
\texttt{LeeYangRealBranchLimitFamily} endpoints.  The real patching wrapper
\begin{Verbatim}
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_realEventualOverlapBranchData_localCover_real
\end{Verbatim}
then invokes the PR \#2716 eventual-overlap endpoint and returns a global
patch differentiable on \texttt{leeYangDomain}, together with the real-centre
identification.

The branch-family concrete \(\mathbb Z^d\) wrappers now live in
\begin{Verbatim}
Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex/Branches/EventualOverlap/
  StructuredFamily.lean
\end{Verbatim}
and are re-exported by the old
\begin{Verbatim}
Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex/Branches/EventualOverlap.lean
\end{Verbatim}
path.  The real local-cover patch wrapper now lives in
\begin{Verbatim}
Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex/Branches/RealCompact/
  StructuredPatch.lean
\end{Verbatim}
and is re-exported by the old
\begin{Verbatim}
Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex/Branches/RealCompact.lean
\end{Verbatim}
.

This is still a conditional post-Montel package.  It records the coherent
data shape without constructing coherent branches, proving Montel compactness,
or performing the diagonal local-cover extraction.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Pointwise-normalised eventual-overlap local-family package; PR \#2724]
The theorem

Ambient.exists_leeYangLocalBranchLimitFamily_of_pointwiseNormEventualData
\end{Verbatim}
exposes the non-real-centred packaged local-cover family endpoint for the

\begin{Verbatim}

in

\begin{Verbatim}

path.

path.

This remains conditional and post-Montel.  It patches already supplied

prove compactness of branch families, or perform diagonal extraction.

\begin{theorem}[Structured eventual-overlap compact-open patch; PR \#2719]
The theorem
\begin{Verbatim}
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_realEventualOverlapBranchData_cOpenPatch
\end{Verbatim}
composes the PR \#2718 compact-geometry extraction with the PR \#2714
compact local-cover \texttt{Fin n} compact-open patch endpoint.  It first
extracts a
\begin{Verbatim}
Ambient.LeeYangCompactLocalCoverFinGeometry
\end{Verbatim}
from structured real eventual-overlap data over a compact
\(K\subset\texttt{leeYangDomain}\) containing the real centre.  For the
selected centres of that geometry, compact-open compactness of the data's
branch-family restrictions plus an explicit selected-centre normalisation
hypothesis then produce a compact finite real-centred Lee--Yang cover package
and a patch differentiable on \(K\), with the real-centre value identified as
\(\texttt{freeEnergyInfinite}\).  The structured data supplies the stage-level
eventual overlap equality.

The concrete \(\mathbb Z^d\) wrapper is
\begin{Verbatim}
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_realEventualOverlapBranchData_cOpenPatch_latticeGraph
\end{Verbatim}
in
\begin{Verbatim}
Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex/
  BranchAscoliCompactOpen/EventualPatches/RealEventualOverlap.lean
\end{Verbatim}
and is re-exported through the legacy eventual-patches path.

This remains conditional and post-Montel.  It still assumes coherent
eventual-overlap data, compact-open compactness of the selected branch-family
restrictions, and the extra selected-centre normalisation; it does not
construct coherent branches, prove compactness of branch families, or perform
the diagonal extraction.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Pointwise-normalised eventual-overlap compact-open patch; PR \#2720]
The structures
\begin{Verbatim}
Ambient.LeeYangPointwiseNormalisedEventualOverlapBranchData
Ambient.LeeYangRealPointwiseNormalisedEventualOverlapBranchData
\end{Verbatim}
strengthen the structured eventual-overlap input by requiring branch
normalisation at every Lee--Yang centre:
the selected branch value at the centre equals the finite-volume
\texttt{freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion} value there.  The real pointwise
package projects to the real-centred structured data used in PR \#2719.

The theorem
\begin{Verbatim}
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_pointwiseNormEventualData_cOpenPatch
\end{Verbatim}
then composes PR \#2719 while discharging its selected-centre normalisation
hypothesis from the pointwise data.  The compact-open compactness of selected
continuous restrictions remains an explicit hypothesis.

The concrete \(\mathbb Z^d\) wrapper is
\begin{Verbatim}
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_pointwiseNormEventualData_cOpenPatch_latticeGraph
\end{Verbatim}
in
\begin{Verbatim}
Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex/
  BranchAscoliCompactOpen/EventualPatches/PointwiseNorm.lean
\end{Verbatim}
and is re-exported through the legacy eventual-patches path.

This remains conditional and post-Montel.  It packages a stronger coherent
data shape, but does not construct coherent branches, prove compactness of
branch families, or perform diagonal extraction.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Pointwise-normalised eventual-overlap local-cover patch; PR \#2721]
The theorem
\begin{Verbatim}
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_pointwiseNormEventualData_localCover_real
\end{Verbatim}
uses the pointwise-normalised real eventual-overlap package before any compact
target or compact-open extraction is selected.  It projects the pointwise data
to the structured real eventual-overlap package and invokes the local-cover
patch endpoint, returning a real-centred packaged local-cover family and a
single function \(g\) differentiable on \(\mathtt{leeYangDomain}\).  The
patched function agrees with the pointwise data's local limits on each
Lee--Yang ball and satisfies
\[
  g(p.h) = \texttt{freeEnergyInfinite}.
\]

The concrete \(\mathbb Z^d\) wrapper is
\begin{Verbatim}
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_pointwiseNormEventualData_localCover_real_latticeGraph
\end{Verbatim}
in
\begin{Verbatim}
Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex/Branches/RealCompact/
  StructuredPatch.lean
\end{Verbatim}
and is re-exported by the old
\begin{Verbatim}
Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex/Branches/RealCompact.lean
\end{Verbatim}
path.

This remains conditional and post-Montel.  It narrows the local-cover endpoint
for future pointwise-normalised extraction output, but does not construct
coherent branches, prove compactness of branch families, or perform diagonal
extraction.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Compact local-cover finite subcover extraction; PR \#2712]
The theorems
\begin{Verbatim}
Ambient.exists_finset_cover_of_isCompact_leeYangLocalBranchLimitFamily
Ambient.exists_finset_cover_of_isCompact_leeYangRealBranchLimitFamily
\end{Verbatim}
extract finite subcovers from the packaged Lee--Yang local-cover endpoint.  If
\(K\) is compact and \(K\subset\texttt{leeYangDomain}\), then the balls carried
by a compatible \texttt{LeeYangLocalBranchLimitFamily} have a finite
\texttt{Finset} of centres \(t\) such that
\[
  K \subset \bigcup_{h_0\in t} B(h_0,r(h_0)).
\]
For a real-centred package, when \((p.h:\mathbb C)\in K\), the finite set is
chosen after inserting the real centre
\(\langle p.h,\texttt{centre\_mem}\rangle\).  Thus the compact target is
covered by finitely many packaged local-cover balls and the real-centre witness
is retained.

The concrete \(\mathbb Z^d\) wrappers are
\begin{Verbatim}
exists_finset_cover_of_isCompact_leeYangLocalBranchLimitFamily_latticeGraph
exists_finset_cover_of_isCompact_leeYangRealBranchLimitFamily_latticeGraph
\end{Verbatim}
in
\begin{Verbatim}
Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex/Branches/RealCompact/
  CompactGeometry.lean
\end{Verbatim}
and are re-exported by the old
\begin{Verbatim}
Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex/Branches/RealCompact.lean
\end{Verbatim}
path.

This is the compactness finite-subcover step for an already packaged local
cover.  It does not prove Montel compactness, coherent branch choices,
stage-level overlap equality, or conversion of the \texttt{Finset} subcover
into the existing \texttt{Fin n} compact finite real-cover package.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Compact local-cover \texttt{Fin n} geometry enumeration; PR
\#2713]
The structure
\begin{Verbatim}
Ambient.LeeYangCompactLocalCoverFinGeometry
\end{Verbatim}
records the \(\texttt{Fin n}\)-indexed geometry obtained from a compact
finite subcover of a real-centred packaged Lee--Yang local-cover family.  It
contains the compact target \(K\), the source
\texttt{LeeYangRealBranchLimitFamily}, an integer \(n\), selected centres
\[
  c : \texttt{Fin n}\to\texttt{leeYangDomain},
\]
radii \(r_i\) equal to the radii in the source local-cover data, positive
radius and ball-containment proofs, a cover
\[
  K \subset \bigcup_{i:\texttt{Fin n}} B(c_i,r_i),
\]
and a selected real-centre index \(i_0\) with \(c_{i_0}=p.h\).

The theorem
\begin{Verbatim}
Ambient.exists_compactLocalCoverFinGeometry_of_leeYangRealBranchLimitFamily
\end{Verbatim}
constructs this package by applying PR \#2712 to obtain a finite
\texttt{Finset} \(t\), then enumerating \(t\) using
\(\texttt{t.equivFin}\).  The real-centre index is the image of
\(\langle p.h,\texttt{centre\_mem}\rangle\) under this equivalence.  The
concrete \(\mathbb Z^d\) wrapper is
\begin{Verbatim}
exists_compactLocalCoverFinGeometry_of_leeYangRealBranchLimitFamily_latticeGraph
\end{Verbatim}
in
\begin{Verbatim}
Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex/Branches/RealCompact/
  CompactGeometry.lean
\end{Verbatim}
and is re-exported by the old
\begin{Verbatim}
Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex/Branches/RealCompact.lean
\end{Verbatim}
path.

This remains a geometry-only enumeration step.  It does not construct the
finite subsequence branch-limit family needed by
\texttt{LeeYangCompactFiniteRealCoverBranchLimitFamily}, nor does it prove
Montel compactness, coherent branch choices, or stage-level overlap equality.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Compact local-cover \texttt{Fin n} compact-open patch; PR
\#2714]
The theorem
\begin{Verbatim}
Ambient.freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_compactLocalCoverFinGeometry_cOpenPatch
\end{Verbatim}
uses a package
\[
  \mathcal G :
  \texttt{LeeYangCompactLocalCoverFinGeometry}
\]
as the finite geometry input for the compact finite real-cover compact-open
patch endpoint.  Its fields supply compactness of \(K\), containment
\(K\subset\Omega_{\mathrm{LY}}\), real-centre membership, the finite
\(\texttt{Fin n}\)-indexed centres and radii, positive-radius and
ball-containment proofs, the cover
\[
  K\subset \bigcup_{i:\texttt{Fin n}} B(c_i,r_i),
\]
and the selected real-centre index \(i_0\) with \(c_{i_0}=p.h\).

Given the same finite compact-open compactness data and eventual stage-level
overlap equality used by the earlier compact finite real-cover endpoint, the
wrapper returns
\begin{Verbatim}
LeeYangCompactFiniteRealCoverBranchLimitFamily
\end{Verbatim}
and a patch differentiable on \(K\), with value
\[
  g(p.h)=\texttt{freeEnergyInfinite}(G,\Lambda,p).
\]
The concrete \(\mathbb Z^d\) wrapper is
\begin{Verbatim}
freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_compactLocalCoverFinGeometry_cOpenPatch_latticeGraph
\end{Verbatim}
in
\begin{Verbatim}

  BranchAscoliCompactOpen/CompactCoverPatches/LocalCover.lean
\end{Verbatim}
and is re-exported through the legacy compact-cover path.

This is a packaging bridge from compact local-cover finite geometry to the
compact finite real-cover compact-open patch theorem.  It does not prove
Montel compactness, coherent branch choices, diagonal extraction, or
stage-level overlap equality.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Compact Lee--Yang fugacity gap; PR \#2684]
The theorems
\texttt{exists\_leeYangFugacity\_norm\_le\_lt\_one\_on\_isCompact}
and
\texttt{exists\_leeYangFugacityVec\_norm\_le\_lt\_one\_on\_isCompact}
record the compactness gap for the Lee--Yang fugacity.  If $\beta>0$,
$K$ is compact, and $K\subseteq\texttt{leeYangDomain}$, then there is
$r<1$ such that
\[
  |\exp(-2\beta h)|\le r \qquad(h\in K).
\]
The vector form gives the same bound for every coordinate of
\texttt{leeYangFugacityVec}.

The proof uses continuity of $h\mapsto |\exp(-2\beta h)|$ and the extreme
value theorem on the compact set $K$.  At a maximiser $h_0$, the pointwise
Lee--Yang bound gives $|\exp(-2\beta h_0)|<1$; if $K$ is empty one takes
$r=0$.

This is preparatory for quantitative Lee--Yang polynomial lower bounds.
It does not prove lower normalised-log control or Montel convergence.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70, and
Friedli--Velenik \cite{friedli-velenik}, Theorem 3.43, pp.~122--127.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Finite-stage compact Lee--Yang polynomial lower bound; PR \#2685]
The theorem
\texttt{exists\_pos\_le\_norm\_isingEdgePoly\_eval\_leeYangFugacityVec\_on\_isCompact}
records the finite-volume compact lower bound for the Lee--Yang polynomial
factor.  If $\beta>0$, $0\le t<1$, $K$ is compact, and
$K\subseteq\texttt{leeYangDomain}$, then there is $\epsilon>0$ such that
\[
  \epsilon \le
  \left\|P_E\bigl(\texttt{leeYangFugacityVec}(\beta,h)\bigr)\right\|
  \qquad(h\in K).
\]
The proof first records the general continuity theorem
\texttt{MultilinPoly.continuous\_eval}.  The composed map
$h\mapsto P_E(z(h))$ is continuous, hence its norm has a minimum on compact
$K$.  At a minimiser the finite-volume Lee--Yang theorem gives nonvanishing,
so the minimum is positive.  If $K$ is empty one takes $\epsilon=1$.

This is only a finite-volume compact lower bound: the constant depends on the
finite graph.  It is not lower normalised-log control uniform in an exhaustion.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70, and
Friedli--Velenik \cite{friedli-velenik}, Theorem 3.43, pp.~122--127.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Finite-stage Lee--Yang \(Z_{\mathbb C}\) lower bound; PR \#2686]
The theorem
\texttt{norm\_partitionFunctionComplex\_ge\_exp\_mul\_isingEdgePoly\_lower}
records the finite-volume bridge from a Lee--Yang polynomial lower bound to a
lower bound on the complex partition function.  If \(0\le \beta\), \(0\le J\),
\(|\Re h|\le R\), and
\[
  \epsilon \le
  \left\|P_E\bigl(\texttt{leeYangFugacityVec}(\beta,h)\bigr)\right\|,
\]
then
\[
  \exp(-\beta R |V|)\epsilon
    \le \left\|Z_G(J,h,\beta)\right\|.
\]

The proof rewrites \(Z_G\) by the Friedli--Velenik factorisation
\[
  Z_G(J,h,\beta)=
  \texttt{leeYangNormalization}(\beta,J,h,|E|,|V|)
  \cdot P_E(\exp(-2\beta h)).
\]
The normalisation has modulus
\(\exp(\beta(J|E|+\Re(h)|V|))\), which is at least
\(\exp(-\beta R|V|)\) under the displayed hypotheses; multiplying by the
polynomial lower bound gives the result.

This remains a finite-volume lower bound.  The parameter \(\epsilon\) may
depend on the finite graph, so the theorem does not prove lower
normalised-log control uniform in an exhaustion.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70, and
Friedli--Velenik \cite{friedli-velenik}, (3.63)--(3.65), pp.~122--123.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Polynomial lower witnesses to lower normalised-log bridge; PR \#2687]
The theorem
\texttt{real\_log\_norm\_partitionFunctionComplexAlongExhaustion\_div\_card\_ge\_of\_poly\_lower\_stage}
records the stagewise logarithmic consequence of the preceding lower bound.
If \(0\le\beta\), \(0\le J\), \(|\Re h|\le R\), and
\[
  \epsilon \le
  \left\|P_{E_n}\bigl(\texttt{leeYangFugacityVec}(\beta,h)\bigr)\right\|,
\]
then
\[
  \frac{\log \epsilon}{|\Lambda_n|}-\beta R
    \le
  \frac{\log \left\|Z_{\Lambda_n}(J,h,\beta)\right\|}{|\Lambda_n|}.
\]

The existential handoff
\texttt{exists\_lower\_log\_norm\_partitionFunctionComplexAlongExhaustion\_of\_poly\_lower}
says that if these polynomial lower witnesses satisfy a uniform lower
normalised-log estimate, then the partition functions satisfy the lower
normalised-log hypothesis consumed by the Lee--Yang locally bounded family
handoff of PR \#2683.  The concrete
\(\mathbb Z^d\) wrapper is
\texttt{exists\_lower\_log\_norm\_partitionFunctionComplexAlongExhaustion\_of\_poly\_lower\_latticeGraph}.

This is still conditional.  It moves the missing lower bound from
\(\left\|Z_{\Lambda_n}\right\|\) to the polynomial witnesses
\(\epsilon_n(h)\), but it does not prove a stage-uniform lower
normalised-log bound for those witnesses.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70, and
Friedli--Velenik \cite{friedli-velenik}, (3.63)--(3.65), pp.~122--123.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Locally bounded family from polynomial lower witnesses; PR \#2688]
The theorem
\texttt{exists\_norm\_freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion\_le\_poly\_lower\_leeYang}
composes the polynomial-witness lower-log bridge of PR \#2687 with the
Lee--Yang upper/lower-log handoff of PR \#2683.  On a compact
\(K\subseteq\texttt{leeYangDomain}\), assume bounded edge density, nonempty
stages, \(\beta>0\), \(J>0\), a real-part bound \(|\Re h|\le R\), and a
stage-uniform lower normalised-log estimate for positive polynomial witnesses
\(\epsilon_n(h)\).  Then there is \(C\) such that
\[
  \left\|f_n(h)\right\| \le C+\pi
  \qquad(n\in\mathbb N,\ h\in K).
\]
The \(\mathbb Z^d\) wrapper is
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
exists_norm_freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_le_poly_lower_ly_latticeGraph
\end{Verbatim}
It lives in
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex/Bounds/
  PolynomialLower.lean
\end{Verbatim}
and is re-exported through the legacy
\texttt{PerStageComplex/Bounds.lean} path.

This theorem still does not prove the lower normalised-log estimate for the
polynomial witnesses.  It packages the exact route from that remaining input
to the locally bounded family condition needed by the later normal-family or
Vitali construction.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70, and
Friedli--Velenik \cite{friedli-velenik}, (3.63)--(3.65), pp.~122--123.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Compact locally bounded family from polynomial witnesses; PR \#2689]
The theorem
\texttt{exists\_norm\_freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion\_le\_poly\_lower\_leeYang\_of\_isCompact}
is the compact-set wrapper for PR \#2688.  Since every compact
\(K\subseteq\mathbb C\) has a uniform real-part bound
\(|\Re h|\le R\), the explicit \(R\)-hypothesis in the PR \#2688 handoff can
be discharged from compactness.  Thus, on compact
\(K\subseteq\texttt{leeYangDomain}\), bounded edge density, nonempty stages,
\(\beta>0\), \(J>0\), and a stage-uniform lower normalised-log estimate for
positive polynomial witnesses \(\epsilon_n(h)\) imply
\[
  \left\|f_n(h)\right\| \le C+\pi
  \qquad(n\in\mathbb N,\ h\in K).
\]
The \(\mathbb Z^d\) wrapper is
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
exists_norm_freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_le_poly_lower_ly_latticeGraph_of_isCompact
\end{Verbatim}
It lives in
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex/Bounds/
  PolynomialLower.lean
\end{Verbatim}
and is re-exported through the legacy
\texttt{PerStageComplex/Bounds.lean} path.

The polynomial-witness lower normalised-log estimate remains an explicit
hypothesis.  The wrapper only removes the bookkeeping real-part bound from the
statement consumed by later normal-family or Vitali inputs.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70, and
Friedli--Velenik \cite{friedli-velenik}, (3.63)--(3.65), pp.~122--123.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Ball-local bound from polynomial witnesses; PR \#2690]
The theorem
\texttt{exists\_norm\_freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion\_le\_poly\_lower\_leeYang\_on\_ball}
puts the compact wrapper of PR \#2689 into the local-cover form.  Assume that
the closed ball \(\overline B(h_0,\rho)\) is contained in
\texttt{leeYangDomain} and that the positive polynomial witnesses on this
closed ball satisfy a stage-uniform lower normalised-log estimate.  Since
\(\overline B(h_0,\rho)\) is compact, PR \#2689 gives a constant \(C\) with
\[
  \left\|f_n(h)\right\| \le C+\pi
  \qquad(n\in\mathbb N,\ h\in\overline B(h_0,\rho)).
\]
Restricting along \(B(h_0,\rho)\subseteq\overline B(h_0,\rho)\) yields the
same estimate on the open ball.  The \(\mathbb Z^d\) wrapper is
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
exists_norm_freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_le_poly_lower_ly_latticeGraph_on_ball
\end{Verbatim}
It lives in
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex/Bounds/
  PolynomialLower.lean
\end{Verbatim}
and is re-exported through the legacy
\texttt{PerStageComplex/Bounds.lean} path.

This is still conditional on the polynomial-witness lower normalised-log
estimate.  It does not construct the Montel subsequence or coherent
locally-uniform branch family; it only gives the ball-local bounded-family
input those later steps consume.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70, and
Friedli--Velenik \cite{friedli-velenik}, (3.63)--(3.65), pp.~122--123.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Point-local bound from polynomial witnesses; PR \#2691]
The theorem \texttt{leeYangDomain\_closedBall\_subset} records the elementary
local compactness fact used by the Lee--Yang cover: every
\(h_0\in\texttt{leeYangDomain}\) admits a radius \(\rho>0\) such that
\[
  \overline B(h_0,\rho)\subseteq\texttt{leeYangDomain}.
\]
It follows from the openness of \texttt{leeYangDomain} by first taking an open
ball \(B(h_0,r)\subseteq\texttt{leeYangDomain}\) and then using the half-radius
closed ball \(\overline B(h_0,r/2)\subseteq B(h_0,r)\).

The theorem
\texttt{exists\_norm\_freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion\_le\_poly\_lower\_leeYang\_around}
combines this radius choice with PR \#2690.  If the polynomial witnesses have a
stage-uniform lower normalised-log estimate on each closed Lee--Yang ball
chosen around \(h_0\), then for some \(\rho>0\) there is a constant \(C\) with
\[
  \left\|f_n(h)\right\| \le C+\pi
  \qquad(n\in\mathbb N,\ h\in B(h_0,\rho)).
\]
The \(\mathbb Z^d\) wrapper is
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
exists_norm_freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_le_poly_lower_ly_latticeGraph_around
\end{Verbatim}
It lives in
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex/Bounds/
  PolynomialLower.lean
\end{Verbatim}
and is re-exported through the legacy
\texttt{PerStageComplex/Bounds.lean} path.

This remains conditional on the polynomial-witness lower normalised-log
estimate.  The wrapper supplies the point-local bounded-family shape needed by
later local-cover arguments; it does not prove the normal-family extraction or
global branch patching.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70, and
Friedli--Velenik \cite{friedli-velenik}, (3.63)--(3.65), pp.~122--123.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Root-product Lee--Yang polynomial lower-log bound; PR \#2692]
The definitions and theorems
\texttt{MultilinPoly.uniformPolynomial},
\texttt{MultilinPoly.uniformPolynomial\_eval}, and
\texttt{MultilinPoly.uniformPolynomial\_natDegree\_le\_card} specialize a
multilinear polynomial \(P\) to the one-variable polynomial
\[
  q(w)=P(w,\ldots,w),
  \qquad \deg q\le |V|.
\]
The general root-product estimate
\path|Polynomial.one_sub_radius_pow_natDegree_le_norm_eval_of_roots_norm_ge_one|
says that if \(q(0)=1\) and every root \(a\) of \(q\) satisfies
\(|a|\ge 1\), then for \(0\le r<1\) and \(|w|\le r\),
\[
  (1-r)^{\deg q} \le |q(w)|.
\]
Indeed, writing \(q(w)=c\prod_a (w-a)\), the identity \(q(0)=1\) gives
\(|c|\prod_a |a|=1\).  Since \(|a|\ge 1\) and \(|w|\le r\),
\[
  |w-a|\ge |a|-|w|\ge |a|-r\ge (1-r)|a|,
\]
and multiplying the factors proves the displayed bound.

For the Ising Lee--Yang polynomial the theorem
\texttt{isingEdgePoly\_uniformPolynomial\_roots\_norm\_ge\_one} supplies the
root condition.  If a root had \(|a|<1\), evaluating the multivariable
Lee--Yang polynomial at the constant vector \(z_i=a\) would contradict the
finite-volume Lee--Yang nonvanishing theorem.  The constant term theorem
\texttt{isingEdgePoly\_uniformPolynomial\_eval\_zero} gives \(q(0)=1\).
Consequently
\path|one_sub_radius_pow_card_le_norm_isingEdgePoly_eval_leeYangFugacityVec|
proves
\[
  (1-r)^{|V|}
    \le
  \left|P_E\bigl(\texttt{leeYangFugacityVec}(\beta,h)\bigr)\right|
  \qquad
  (|\exp(-2\beta h)|\le r<1).
\]
The exponent is \(|V|\), not \(\deg q\), because
\(\deg q\le |V|\) and \(0\le 1-r\le 1\), so
\((1-r)^{|V|}\le (1-r)^{\deg q}\).

On a compact \(K\subseteq\texttt{leeYangDomain}\), the compact fugacity gap
provides \(r<1\) such that \(|\exp(-2\beta h)|\le r\) for every \(h\in K\).
The theorem
\path|Ambient.exists_poly_lower_norm_isingEdgePoly_eval_leeYangFugacityVec_on_isCompact|
therefore takes
\[
  \epsilon_n=(1-r)^{|\Lambda_n|}.
\]
Since
\[
  \frac{\log \epsilon_n}{|\Lambda_n|}=\log(1-r),
\]
these witnesses have a stage-uniform lower normalised-log bound.  Composing
with the PR \#2687 polynomial-to-\(Z\) bridge yields the compact lower-log
theorem, and the locally bounded wrappers remove the former polynomial-witness
hypothesis from the compact, ball-local, and point-local statements:
\begin{Verbatim}[breaklines=true,fontsize=\small]
Ambient.exists_lower_log_norm_partitionFunctionComplexAlongExhaustion_leeYang_of_isCompact
Ambient.exists_norm_freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_le_leeYang_of_isCompact
Ambient.exists_norm_freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_le_leeYang_on_ball
Ambient.exists_norm_freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_le_leeYang_around
\end{Verbatim}
The corresponding \(\mathbb Z^d\) wrappers live in
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex/Bounds/
  LeeYangLower.lean
\end{Verbatim}
and are re-exported through the legacy
\texttt{PerStageComplex/Bounds.lean} path.

This completes the lower normalised-log side of the Lee--Yang Vitali input.
The remaining task for GJ \S 4.6 is the coherent locally-uniform branch-family
or Montel construction and the global branch patching.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70, and
Friedli--Velenik \cite{friedli-velenik}, Theorem 3.43, pp.~122--127 and
(3.63)--(3.65), pp.~122--123.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Compact-uniform complex partition-function bound; PR \#2678]
The theorem
\texttt{exists\_abs\_re\_le\_on\_isCompact} records the elementary
compactness input needed for the Montel side of the Lee--Yang argument: for
every compact set $K\subseteq\mathbb C$ there is a real number $R\ge 0$ such
that
\[
  |\mathrm{Re}\,h|\le R\qquad (h\in K).
\]
Combining this with the stage-wise estimate from PR \#630 gives
\[
  \|Z_{\Lambda_n}(J,h,\beta)\|
  \le
  2^{|\Lambda_n|}
  \exp\!\left(|\beta|\left(|J|\,|E_{\Lambda_n}|+R|\Lambda_n|\right)\right)
\]
for all stages $n$ and all $h\in K$.  In Lean this packaged form is
\[
  \texttt{norm\_partitionFunctionComplexAlongExhaustion\_le\_on\_isCompact\_stage}.
\]
The concrete $\mathbb Z^d$ wrapper
\texttt{norm\_partitionFunctionComplexAlongExhaustion\_le\_on\_isCompact\_stage\_latticeGraph}
lives in
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex/Bounds/
  PerStage.lean
\end{Verbatim}
and is re-exported through the legacy
\texttt{PerStageComplex/Bounds.lean} path.

This is a compact-field envelope for the partition functions themselves: it is
uniform in $h\in K$, but the right-hand side still depends on $|\Lambda_n|$ and
$|E_{\Lambda_n}|$.  It is therefore an input to later normalised logarithmic
or free-energy estimates, not a standalone stage-uniform Montel bound and not
yet a construction of coherent locally uniformly convergent branches.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Upper normalised log bound from compact envelope; PR \#2681]
The theorem
\texttt{exists\_real\_log\_norm\_partitionFunctionComplexAlongExhaustion\_div\_card\_le\_on\_isCompact}
records the upper half of the normalised-log input.  Assume that $K$ is compact,
all stages are nonempty, the exhaustion has bounded edge density
$|E_{\Lambda_n}|\le c|\Lambda_n|$, and
$Z_{\Lambda_n}(J,h,\beta)\ne 0$ for $h\in K$.  Then there is a constant $C$
such that
\[
  \frac{\log\|Z_{\Lambda_n}(J,h,\beta)\|}{|\Lambda_n|}\le C
  \qquad(n\in\mathbb N,\ h\in K).
\]
The proof first uses the compact real-part bound $|\mathrm{Re}\,h|\le R$ and
the envelope of PR \#2678 to get the per-stage estimate
\[
  \frac{\log\|Z_{\Lambda_n}(J,h,\beta)\|}{|\Lambda_n|}
  \le
  \log 2+
  |\beta|\left(|J|\,\frac{|E_{\Lambda_n}|}{|\Lambda_n|}+R\right).
\]
Bounded edge density replaces the edge ratio by the single constant $c$.
The concrete $\mathbb Z^d$ wrappers live in
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex/Bounds/
  UpperLog.lean
\end{Verbatim}
and are re-exported through the legacy
\texttt{PerStageComplex/Bounds.lean} path.

This is not yet the absolute normalised-log bound used by PR \#2679 and
PR \#2680: it supplies only an upper bound for
$\log\|Z_{\Lambda_n}\|$.  The matching compact Lee--Yang lower bound is supplied
later by PR \#2692 through the root-product polynomial estimate.  Reference:
Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Lee--Yang compact upper normalised log bound; PR \#2682]
The theorem
\texttt{exists\_log\_norm\_partitionFunctionComplexAlongExhaustion\_div\_card\_le\_leeYangDomain}
specialises PR \#2681 to compact subsets of the Lee--Yang domain.  Assume
$\beta>0$, $J>0$, all stages are nonempty, the exhaustion has bounded edge
density, and $K\subseteq\texttt{leeYangDomain}$ is compact.  Then there is a
constant $C$ such that
\[
  \frac{\log\|Z_{\Lambda_n}(J,h,\beta)\|}{|\Lambda_n|}\le C
  \qquad(n\in\mathbb N,\ h\in K).
\]
The only new step beyond PR \#2681 is to discharge the nonvanishing hypothesis:
Lee--Yang gives
\[
  Z_{\Lambda_n}(J,h,\beta)\ne 0
  \qquad(h\in K\subseteq\texttt{leeYangDomain})
\]
at every finite stage.  The concrete $\mathbb Z^d$ wrapper lives in
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex/Bounds/
  PolynomialLower.lean
\end{Verbatim}
and is re-exported through the legacy
\texttt{PerStageComplex/Bounds.lean} path.

This is an upper normalised-log estimate only.  PR \#2692 supplies the matching
compact Lee--Yang lower control needed to turn this into the absolute
normalised-log hypothesis used by PR \#2679, PR \#2680, and PR \#2683.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Lee--Yang lower log to locally bounded handoff; PR \#2683]
The theorem
\texttt{exists\_abs\_log\_norm\_partitionFunctionComplexAlongExhaustion\_div\_card\_le\_lower\_leeYang}
combines the compact Lee--Yang upper normalised-log bound of PR \#2682 with a
remaining lower normalised-log hypothesis.  Assume $\beta>0$, $J>0$, all
stages are nonempty, the exhaustion has bounded edge density, and
$K\subseteq\texttt{leeYangDomain}$ is compact.  If there is a constant $L$
such that
\[
  -L \le
  \frac{\log\|Z_{\Lambda_n}(J,h,\beta)\|}{|\Lambda_n|}
  \qquad(n\in\mathbb N,\ h\in K),
\]
then there is a constant $C$ such that
\[
  \frac{\left|\log\|Z_{\Lambda_n}(J,h,\beta)\|\right|}{|\Lambda_n|}
  \le C
  \qquad(n\in\mathbb N,\ h\in K).
\]
The proof takes the maximum of the lower-control constant and the automatic
Lee--Yang upper-control constant, then applies the elementary two-sided
absolute-value bridge
\texttt{abs\_log\_norm\_partitionFunctionComplexAlongExhaustion\_div\_card\_le\_of\_two\_sided\_on\_set}.

The companion theorem
\texttt{exists\_norm\_freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion\_le\_lower\_log\_leeYang}
immediately feeds this absolute normalised-log bound into PR \#2680, giving a
constant $C$ with
\[
  \|f_n(h)\|\le C+\pi
  \qquad(n\in\mathbb N,\ h\in K).
\]
The concrete $\mathbb Z^d$ wrappers are
\texttt{exists\_abs\_log\_norm\_div\_card\_le\_lower\_ly\_latticeGraph}
and
\texttt{exists\_norm\_freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion\_le\_lower\_log\_ly\_latticeGraph}.
They live in
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex/Bounds/
  PolynomialLower.lean
\end{Verbatim}
and are re-exported through the legacy
\texttt{PerStageComplex/Bounds.lean} path.

In this handoff theorem the lower normalised-log control is isolated as an
explicit hypothesis; PR \#2692 supplies that hypothesis on compact Lee--Yang
sets via the root-product polynomial lower bound.  Reference: Glimm--Jaffe
\cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Normalised log bound handoff; PR \#2679]
The theorem
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\small, breaklines=true]
norm_freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion_le_of_abs_log_norm_bound_stage
\end{Verbatim}
records the exact elementary step from normalised logarithmic control to a
finite-stage complex free-energy bound.  If the stage $\Lambda_n$ is nonempty
and
\[
  \frac{\left|\log\|Z_{\Lambda_n}(J,h,\beta)\|\right|}{|\Lambda_n|}
  \le C,
\]
then
\[
  \|f_n(h)\|
  \le
  C+\frac{\pi}{|\Lambda_n|},
\]
where $f_n$ is the principal complex free energy
\texttt{freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion}.  The proof is a direct
specialisation of
\texttt{norm\_freeEnergyComplex\_le\_trivial\_bound} to the induced graph on
$\Lambda_n$, using the principal-log estimate
\[
  \|\log z\|\le |\log\|z\||+\pi .
\]
The setwise companion
\texttt{norm\_freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion\_le\_of\_abs\_log\_norm\_bound\_on\_set}
packages one constant $C$ for all stages and all fields in a set $K$.  The
concrete $\mathbb Z^d$ wrappers live in
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex/Bounds/
  UpperLog.lean
\end{Verbatim}
and are re-exported through the legacy
\texttt{PerStageComplex/Bounds.lean} path.

This is intentionally a conditional handoff.  It identifies the missing
normal-family input after the compact $Z$ envelope of PR \#2678: one still
needs control of the absolute logarithm, including lower control on
\(\|Z_{\Lambda_n}\|\), not merely an upper envelope for
\(\|Z_{\Lambda_n}\|\).  Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe},
\S 4.6, pp.~67--70.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Locally bounded family from normalised log control; PR \#2680]
The theorem
\texttt{norm\_freeEnergyComplexAlongExhaustion\_le\_of\_abs\_log\_norm\_bound\_on\_set\_uniform}
turns the PR \#2679 stagewise estimate into a stage-independent local bound.
Assume that all stages are nonempty and that a set $K\subseteq\mathbb C$ has a
common normalised-log bound
\[
  \frac{\left|\log\|Z_{\Lambda_n}(J,h,\beta)\|\right|}{|\Lambda_n|}
  \le C
  \qquad (n\in\mathbb N,\ h\in K).
\]
Then, for every stage and every $h\in K$,
\[
  \|f_n(h)\|\le C+\pi .
\]
The proof composes PR \#2679 with the elementary consequence of nonemptiness
\[
  \frac{\pi}{|\Lambda_n|}\le \pi ,
\]
using \texttt{Fintype.card\_pos} in Lean.  The concrete $\mathbb Z^d$ wrapper
lives in
\begin{Verbatim}[fontsize=\footnotesize]
Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/PerStageComplex/Bounds/
  UpperLog.lean
\end{Verbatim}
and is re-exported through the legacy
\texttt{PerStageComplex/Bounds.lean} path.

This theorem packages the exact locally bounded family shape that a later
Montel/normal-family argument can consume.  It remains conditional on the
normalised-log estimate; no lower control on $Z_{\Lambda_n}$ is proved here.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe}, \S 4.6, pp.~67--70.
\end{theorem}

\begin{remark}[Status of PR \#200 — merged 2026-04-19, \texttt{52ea2f1}]
PR \#200 was squash-merged into \texttt{main} on 2026-04-19 as
commit \texttt{52ea2f1}. Items now present in \texttt{main}:
Morera-based pointwise local analytic log-$Z$ branch on the Lee-Yang
domain (headline
\texttt{exists\_freeEnergyComplex\_analyticAt\_branch\_of\_leeYangDomain}),
the subdomain result in \texttt{leeYangSubdomain}, the Vitali bridge
wrapping mathlib's
\texttt{TendstoLocallyUniformlyOn.differentiableOn}, and modulus bounds
$|Z(J,h,\beta)|\le Z(J,\mathrm{Re}\,h,\beta)$, trivial upper bound,
$\|\log z\|\le|\log\|z\||+\pi$, and the derived
$\|f_{\text{complex}}\|\le(|\log\|Z\||+\pi)/|\iota|$.
Still not formalised (future PRs): Montel-style locally uniform
convergence of $f_\Lambda$ on Lee-Yang (mathlib lacks Montel;
self-implementation required). Note that
\texttt{vitali\_bridge\_leeYangDomain} itself \emph{is} already in
\texttt{main}; what remains is the locally uniform convergence
\emph{input} to the bridge, after which the bridge immediately
yields analyticity of the infinite-volume limit $f_\infty$ on
Lee-Yang. Separately, a global branch patched from the local
pieces via simple connectivity is also not yet formalised.
The principal-branch \texttt{Complex.log} of $Z$ may itself be
discontinuous where $Z$ crosses the negative real axis; the
local-branch statement is the mathematically correct form of GJ
Thm 4.6.2.
\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}[Subdomain of Lee-Yang on which \texttt{freeEnergyComplex} is analytic; PR \#200]
For real $\beta > 0$, real $J$, and complex $h$ with
$\beta \cdot |\mathrm{Im}\,h| \cdot |\iota| < \pi / 2$ (a Lee-Yang
subdomain),
\[
  \mathrm{Re}\,(\texttt{partitionFunctionComplex}\,G\,(J:\mathbb C)\,h\,(\beta:\mathbb C)) > 0,
\]
so the value lies in \texttt{slitPlane}, and
$\texttt{freeEnergyComplex}$ is analytic in $h$.
Proof: for each $\sigma$, the real part of $\exp(-\beta H(\sigma))$
equals $\exp(a) \cos(b)$
(with $a$ the real part of the exponent and
$b = \beta \cdot \mathrm{Im}\,h \cdot \sum_i \sigma_i$,
$|b| \le \beta |\mathrm{Im}\,h| |\iota| < \pi/2$), hence $\cos > 0$ and
the term is positive. Summing over $\sigma$ gives positivity of
$\mathrm{Re}\,Z$. Lean name:
\texttt{freeEnergyComplex\_analyticAt\_h\_of\_leeYangSubdomain}.

Note: the subdomain shrinks as $\beta |\iota|$ grows, so it is not
directly usable at the infinite volume limit. Extension to the full
Lee-Yang domain requires branch selection and is a TODO of this PR.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{freeEnergyInfinite\_of\_eventually\_const}; PR \#171]
If $\forall^\infty n, f_n = c$, then
$\texttt{freeEnergyInfinite}\,G\,\Lambda\,p = c$.
\texttt{tendsto\_const\_nhds.congr'} turns eventual constancy into a
\texttt{Tendsto} statement, and PR \#170 turns the limsup into the limit.
This generalizes the existing \texttt{\_beta\_zero} and
\texttt{\_zero\_params} results, which assumed every $n$ was nonempty.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{freeEnergy\_J\_zero} family; PR \#174]
At $J = 0$, the interaction term is zero and $Z_G = Z_\bot$, independently
of the graph. For $|\iota| > 0$ and arbitrary $h, \beta$,
\[
  Z_G(\langle 0, h, \beta\rangle) = (2 \cosh(\beta h))^{|\iota|},
  \quad
  f_G(\langle 0, h, \beta\rangle) = \log(2 \cosh(\beta h)).
\]
The same PR also provides the along-exhaustion and infinite-volume lifts
under eventual nonemptiness. This is the fourth zero-parameter closed form
alongside the existing $\beta = 0$, $J = h = 0$, and $\bot$-graph families.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{\_eq\_bot\_at\_J\_zero} along-exhaustion / $\infty$-vol lift; PR \#176]
The base-layer $\bot$-equivalence identities from PR \#175 are extended to
the along-exhaustion and infinite-volume layers:
\[
  \texttt{freeEnergyAlongExhaustion}\,G\,\Lambda\,\langle 0,h,\beta\rangle\,n
    = \texttt{freeEnergyAlongExhaustion}\,\bot\,\Lambda\,\langle 0,h,\beta\rangle\,n,
\]
\[
  \texttt{freeEnergyInfinite}\,G\,\Lambda\,\langle 0,h,\beta\rangle
    = \texttt{freeEnergyInfinite}\,\bot\,\Lambda\,\langle 0,h,\beta\rangle.
\]
The along-exhaustion version uses \texttt{change} and
\texttt{freeEnergy\_eq\_bot\_at\_J\_zero}. The infinite-volume version uses
functoriality of \texttt{Filter.limsup}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{correlation\_eq\_bot\_at\_J\_zero}; PR \#190]
The $\bot$-equivalence identity chain from PR \#175 is extended to the
correlation layer. For arbitrary $G, A, h, \beta$,
\[
  \texttt{correlation}\,G\,\langle 0, h, \beta\rangle\,A
    = \texttt{correlation}\,\bot\,\langle 0, h, \beta\rangle\,A.
\]
The denominator $Z$ is graph-independent
(\texttt{partitionFunction\_eq\_bot\_at\_J\_zero}),
and the Boltzmann weight in the numerator integral is also graph-independent
(\texttt{hamiltonian\_eq\_bot\_at\_J\_zero}).
Taking the ratio gives graph-independence.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{correlation\_bot\_closed} / \texttt{correlation\_J\_zero}; PR \#191]
This records the closed form for correlations on the $\bot$-graph and lifts
it to arbitrary graphs at $J=0$. For arbitrary $p, A$,
\[
  \texttt{correlation}\,\bot\,p\,A = \tanh(p.\beta \cdot p.h)^{|A|},
\]
Combining with PR \#190 gives, for arbitrary $G, A, h, \beta$,
\[
  \texttt{correlation}\,G\,\langle 0, h, \beta\rangle\,A
    = \tanh(\beta h)^{|A|}.
\]
Proof: on $\bot$, the Boltzmann weight factors site-wise as
$\prod_i \exp(\beta h\,\mathrm{sign}(\sigma_i))$. On the
\texttt{spinProduct} side, \texttt{Finset.prod\_filter} rewrites it as
$\prod_{i \in \iota}\, (\mathrm{if}\ i \in A\ \mathrm{then}\ \mathrm{sign}(\sigma_i)\ \mathrm{else}\ 1)$
Then \texttt{Fintype.sum\_prod\_piFinset} swaps
$\sum_\sigma \prod_i$ to $\prod_i \sum_s$. The site sum is $2\sinh$ when
$i \in A$ (\texttt{sum\_spin\_sign\_exp\_sign}) and $2\cosh$ when
$i \notin A$ (\texttt{sum\_exp\_spin\_sign}). Dividing by
$Z = (2\cosh)^{|\iota|}$ yields
$\prod_i (\mathrm{if}\ i \in A\ \mathrm{then}\ \tanh\ \mathrm{else}\ 1) = \tanh^{|A|}$.
This is the fifth zero-parameter closed form paired with the $Z$/free-energy
results from PR \#175.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[$\bot$-equivalence core identities at $J=0$; PR \#175]
Refactor the separate ``reduction to $\bot$'' arguments from each layer of
PR \#174 into a single base identity chain:
\begin{align*}
  H_G(\langle 0, h, \beta\rangle, \sigma) &= H_\bot(\langle 0, h, \beta\rangle, \sigma), \\
  Z_G(\langle 0, h, \beta\rangle) &= Z_\bot(\langle 0, h, \beta\rangle), \\
  f_G(\langle 0, h, \beta\rangle) &= f_\bot(\langle 0, h, \beta\rangle).
\end{align*}
(\texttt{hamiltonian\_eq\_bot\_at\_J\_zero} /
\texttt{partitionFunction\_eq\_bot\_at\_J\_zero} /
\texttt{freeEnergy\_eq\_bot\_at\_J\_zero}, with names unified under the
\texttt{\_eq\_bot\_at\_J\_zero} suffix). The main \texttt{\_J\_zero}
statements reduce to these identities composed by \texttt{.trans} with
\texttt{partitionFunction\_bot} and \texttt{freeEnergy\_bot}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{freeEnergyInfinite\_\{beta\_zero,zero\_params\}\_of\_eventually\_nonempty}; PR \#172]
Under the assumption $\forall^\infty n, \Lambda_n$ is nonempty,
\[
  \texttt{freeEnergyInfinite}\,G\,\Lambda\,\langle J, h, 0\rangle
    \;=\; \texttt{freeEnergyInfinite}\,G\,\Lambda\,\langle 0, 0, \beta\rangle
    \;=\; \log 2.
\]
Feed the finite versions
\texttt{freeEnergyAlongExhaustion\_\{beta\_zero,zero\_params\}}
into PR \#171 \texttt{\_of\_eventually\_const} using
\texttt{filter\_upwards}. Under \texttt{[Nonempty V]}, this holds
automatically by \texttt{Exhaustion.eventually\_volume\_nonempty}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{freeEnergy\_nonneg\_of\_ferromagnetic}; PR \#169]
For $0 < |\iota|$ and ferromagnetic $p$,
\[
  0 \;\le\; f_G(p).
\]
This follows by transitivity from PR \#168 and \texttt{Real.log\_pos}
($0 < \log 2$). The existing $\Lambda$ version
\texttt{freeEnergy\LeanLambda\_nonneg\_of\_ferromagnetic} (PR \#125) is refactored
here into a thin wrapper around the base result.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{card\_mul\_freeEnergy\_eq\_log\_partitionFunction}; PR \#167]
For $|\iota| > 0$,
\[
  |\iota| \cdot f_G(p) = \log Z_G(p).
\]
Unfold the definition $f_G(p) = |\iota|^{-1} \cdot \log Z_G(p)$
and use \texttt{field\_simp} to cancel $|\iota|$. This is the base layer for
the existing $\Lambda$ version
\texttt{card\_mul\_freeEnergy\LeanLambda\_eq\_log\_partitionFunction\LeanLambda\_of\_nonempty}
(PR \#119); that $\Lambda$ version is refactored here into a thin wrapper.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{log\_partitionFunction\_ge\_card\_mul\_log\_two\_cosh\_of\_ferromagnetic} family; PR \#173]
For ferromagnetic $p$,
\[
  |\iota| \cdot \log(2 \cosh(\beta h)) \;\le\; \log Z_G(p).
\]
This sharpens PR \#165 \texttt{\_ge\_card\_mul\_log\_two}. It provides
three-layer lifts: base, $\Lambda$, and along-exhaustion. Proof:
$Z_G \ge Z_\bot = (2 \cosh(\beta h))^{|\iota|}$, followed by $\log$ and
\texttt{Real.log\_pow}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{magnetization\_beta\_zero}; PR \#187]
Finite-volume magnetization vanishes at $\beta = 0$:
$\texttt{magnetization}\,G\,\langle J, h, 0\rangle\,i = 0$.
Specialize PR \#182 to a singleton. This is the finite-volume companion to
PR \#186 (\texttt{magnetizationInfinite\_beta\_zero}).
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{magnetizationInfinite\_beta\_zero}; PR \#186]
Infinite-volume magnetization vanishes at $\beta = 0$:
$\texttt{magnetizationInfinite}\,G\,\Lambda\,\langle J, h, 0\rangle\,i = 0$.
Specialize PR \#183 \texttt{correlationInfinite\_beta\_zero\_vanish} to the
singleton $A = \{i\}$. In addition to the existing
\texttt{magnetizationInfinite\_zero\_at\_h\_zero} from $h=0$ symmetry, this
formalizes vanishing at $\beta = 0$ (infinite temperature).
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{correlation\_empty}; PR \#185]
Normalization of the Gibbs measure:
$\texttt{correlation}\,G\,p\,\emptyset = 1$
holds in all four layers: base, $\Lambda$, along-exhaustion, and
infinite-volume. \texttt{spinProduct\_empty} gives $\sigma^\emptyset = 1$;
unfolding the correlation definition gives
$Z^{-1} \sum_\sigma 1 \cdot w = Z^{-1} \cdot Z = 1$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{correlation\LeanLambda / correlationAlongExhaustion / correlationInfinite\_beta\_zero\_vanish}; PR \#183]
PR \#182 is lifted through three layers:
\begin{itemize}
\item $\Lambda$ layer: for $A : \texttt{Finset}\,\uparrow\Lambda$ with
  $A.\texttt{Nonempty}$, $\texttt{correlation\LeanLambda} = 0$.
\item Along-exhaustion: both branches, $A \subseteq \Lambda_n$ and its
  negation, give 0.
\item Infinite-volume: \texttt{ciSup\_const} gives the supremum of the
  zero sequence as 0.
\end{itemize}
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{correlation\LeanLambda / correlationAlongExhaustion / correlationInfinite\_zero\_params\_vanish}; PR \#189]
PR \#188 is lifted through three layers, as the $J=h=0$ counterpart of
PR \#183.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{correlation\_zero\_params\_vanish\_of\_nonempty\_A}; PR \#188]
Correlation vanishes on the $J = h = 0$ slice: if $A.\texttt{Nonempty}$, then
$\texttt{correlation}\,G\,\langle 0, 0, \beta\rangle\,A = 0$.
\texttt{hamiltonian\_zero\_params} gives Boltzmann weight $= 1$, and
PR \#148 \texttt{sum\_config\_spinProduct\_eq\_zero} makes the numerator
vanish.
This is the companion to PR \#182 ($\beta = 0$).
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{correlation\_beta\_zero\_vanish\_of\_nonempty\_A}; PR \#182]
At $\beta = 0$, correlations vanish for nonempty $A$:
\[
  A.\texttt{Nonempty} \Rightarrow
    \texttt{correlation}\,G\,\langle J, h, 0\rangle\,A = 0.
\]
This is the infinite-temperature ($\beta = 0$) slice of the Glimm--Jaffe
\S 4.1 correlation definition. Since the Boltzmann weight is 1, the
numerator is $\sum_\sigma \prod_{i \in A} \sigma_i$, which is 0 by the
existing \texttt{sum\_config\_spinProduct\_eq\_zero}
(NonnegCorrelations.lean, via the \texttt{flipAt} involution). Alongside the
existing $\beta = 0$ closed forms
(\texttt{freeEnergy\_beta\_zero} = $\log 2$, PR \#131;
\texttt{partitionFunction\_beta\_zero}), this completes the correlation
slice.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{freeEnergyInfinite\_\{J\_zero,beta\_zero,zero\_params\}\_of\_nonempty}; PR \#181]
Under \texttt{[Nonempty V]}, these are assumption-free versions. The values
are $\log(2 \cosh(\beta h))$ for $J=0$ and $\log 2$ for $\beta=0$ or
$J=h=0$. They directly combine the existing
\texttt{\_of\_eventually\_nonempty} results with
\texttt{\LeanLambda.eventually\_volume\_nonempty}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{Ambient.Finset.Nonempty.fintype\_card\_coe\_pos}; PR \#180]
Helper extraction refactor: the repeated pattern in 21+ locations,
$\Lambda.\texttt{Nonempty} \Rightarrow 0 < \texttt{Fintype.card}\,\uparrow\Lambda$
is made into a theorem. The chain
\texttt{rw [Fintype.card\_coe]; exact Finset.card\_pos.mpr hne} is replaced
by one call. The mathematical content is unchanged, and readability improves
in AmbientLattice and AmbientLatticeSum.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{freeEnergyAlongExhaustion\_\{beta\_zero,zero\_params\}\_tendsto\_of\_eventually\_nonempty}; PR \#179]
This is the $\beta=0$ and $J=h=0$ parallel version of the $J=0$ Tendsto
statement from PR \#178:
\[
  \texttt{Tendsto}\,(\cdots \langle J, h, 0\rangle)\,\texttt{atTop}\,(\mathcal N(\log 2))
\]
\[
  \texttt{Tendsto}\,(\cdots \langle 0, 0, \beta\rangle)\,\texttt{atTop}\,(\mathcal N(\log 2))
\]
It uses the same eventually-constant plus
\texttt{tendsto\_const\_nhds.congr'} pattern. This completes the
infinite-volume Tendsto results for all zero-parameter slices.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{freeEnergyAlongExhaustion\_J\_zero\_tendsto\_of\_eventually\_nonempty}; PR \#178]
This is the first nontrivial infinite-volume \texttt{Tendsto} convergence
after the CLAUDE.local.md update bringing infinite systems into scope. Under
$\forall^\infty n, \Lambda_n$ nonempty,
\[
  \texttt{Tendsto}\,(\texttt{freeEnergyAlongExhaustion}\,G\,\Lambda\,\langle 0, h, \beta\rangle)\,
    \texttt{atTop}\,(\mathcal N(\log(2 \cosh(\beta h)))).
\]
The $J = 0$ slice is stagewise constant with value
$\log(2 \cosh(\beta h))$, so it avoids the Fekete step and does not require
translation invariance. Transport by \texttt{tendsto\_const\_nhds.congr'}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{partitionFunction\_ge\_two\_cosh\_pow\_card\_of\_ferromagnetic} family; PR \#177]
For ferromagnetic $p$, the non-logarithmic form is
\[
  (2 \cosh(\beta h))^{|\iota|} \;\le\; Z_G(p).
\]
This is the exponential image of the log form from PR \#173. Proof:
\texttt{partitionFunction\_bot} plus
\texttt{partitionFunction\_monotone\_subgraph}. It provides three-layer
lifts: base, $\Lambda$, and along-exhaustion.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{partitionFunction\_ge\_two\_pow\_card\_of\_ferromagnetic} family; PR \#165]
For ferromagnetic $p$,
\[
  2^{|\iota|} \;\le\; Z_G(p).
\]
\texttt{partitionFunction\_bot\_ge\_two\_pow\_card}
This combines the $\bot$-graph bound with $\cosh \ge 1$ and
\texttt{partitionFunction\_monotone\_subgraph}. The log form
\texttt{log\_partitionFunction\_ge\_card\_mul\_log\_two\_of\_ferromagnetic}
states $|\iota| \cdot \log 2 \le \log Z_G$, and the same PR also provides
the $\Lambda$ and along-exhaustion lifts.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{inducedGraph\_bot}; PR \#129]
$\texttt{inducedGraph}\,(\bot : \texttt{SimpleGraph}\,V)\,\Lambda
  = (\bot : \texttt{SimpleGraph}\,\uparrow\Lambda)$.
\texttt{induce = comap} + \texttt{SimpleGraph.comap\_bot}.
It is marked \texttt{@[simp]}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{freeEnergy\_ge\_log\_two\_cosh}]
For a ferromagnetic system with $J \ge 0, h \ge 0, \beta > 0$ and
$|\iota| > 0$,
\[
  \log(2 \cosh(\beta h)) \le \texttt{freeEnergy}\,G\,p.
\]
\texttt{freeEnergy\_bot} identifies the left-hand side with $f(\bot, p)$,
then \texttt{freeEnergy\_monotone\_subgraph} is applied to \texttt{bot\_le}.
This sharpens the uniform $\log 2$ lower bound from PR \#121 using
$\cosh \ge 1$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{freeEnergyAlongExhaustion\_ge\_log\_two\_cosh}]
The same lower bound holds at each nonempty stage.
Specialization via \texttt{change} + definitional unfolding.
\end{theorem}

\subsection{Spin flip symmetry / \texorpdfstring{$h$}{h}-even (PR \#126)}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{hamiltonian\_neg\_h}]
\[
  H_{G, (J, -h, \beta)}(\sigma)
    = H_{G, (J, h, \beta)}(\sigma.\text{flip}).
\]
The $J$-term is invariant by \texttt{interactionEnergy\_flip}; the external
field satisfies
$-(-h) \sum \text{sign}(\sigma_i) = h \sum \text{sign}(\sigma_i)
  = -h \sum \text{sign}(\sigma.\text{flip}_i)$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{partitionFunction\_neg\_h}]
$Z(J, -h, \beta) = Z(J, h, \beta)$.
\texttt{hamiltonian\_neg\_h} plus reindexing along the \texttt{flipEquiv}
bijection.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{freeEnergy\_neg\_h}]
$f(J, -h, \beta) = f(J, h, \beta)$.
The free-energy density is an even function of $h$.
\end{theorem}

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{$|h|$}{|h|}-monotonicity (PR \#127)}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{freeEnergy\_eq\_abs\_h}]
$f(J, h, \beta) = f(J, |h|, \beta)$.
Split into the cases $|h| = h$ and $|h| = -h$, then use
\texttt{freeEnergy\_neg\_h}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{freeEnergy\_monotone\_abs\_h}]
For ferromagnetic systems,
$|h_1| \le |h_2| \Rightarrow f(J, h_1, \beta) \le f(J, h_2, \beta)$.
Rewrite both sides to $|h_i|$ using \texttt{freeEnergy\_eq\_abs\_h}, then
apply \texttt{freeEnergy\_monotone\_h} (monotone on \texttt{Ici 0}) from
$|h_i| \ge 0$.
\end{theorem}

\subsubsection{Along-exhaustion specializations (PR \#128)}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{freeEnergyAlongExhaustion\_neg\_h} /
\texttt{\_eq\_abs\_h} / \texttt{\_monotone\_abs\_h}]
For each $n$, specialize the finite-volume versions directly:
\begin{itemize}
  \item $f_n(J, -h, \beta) = f_n(J, h, \beta)$.
  \item $f_n(J, h, \beta) = f_n(J, |h|, \beta)$.
  \item For ferromagnetic systems with $J \ge 0, \beta > 0$,
        $|h_1| \le |h_2| \Rightarrow f_n(h_1) \le f_n(h_2)$.
\end{itemize}
Each proof uses \texttt{change} and definitional unfolding to apply
\texttt{IsingModel.freeEnergy\_*}.
\end{theorem}

\subsection{Reflection positivity (\S10.4)}

\begin{definition}[\texttt{ReflectionPositive}]
A bilinear form $b$ is reflection-positive if $b(x, x) \ge 0$ for all $x$.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{discriminant\_nonneg}]
If $a t^2 + 2bt + c \ge 0$ for all $t \in \mathbb{R}$, then $b^2 \le ac$.
This is the algebraic core of the Schwarz inequality (10.4.2).
Proved using \texttt{discrim\_le\_zero} from Mathlib.
\end{theorem}

\subsection{Multiple reflections (\S10.5--10.6)}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{iterated\_schwarz\_sq}; Prop.~10.5.2 core]
If $0 \le x$, $0 \le a$, and $x^2 \le ax$, then $x \le a$.
This is the algebraic core of the iterated Schwarz inequality
for multiple reflection bounds.
\end{theorem}

\S10.6 extends the Schwarz inequality to nonsymmetric reflections,
needed only for regularity of $P(\varphi)_2$ fields (not existence).

\subsubsection{Nonsymmetric reflections (\S10.6 algebraic core)}

For a possibly non-symmetric bilinear form $b$ with
$b(x, y) \ne b(y, x)$, expanding $b(x + ty, x + ty)$ gives
$b(y, y)\,t^2 + \bigl(b(x, y) + b(y, x)\bigr)\,t + b(x, x) \ge 0$
for all $t \in \mathbb{R}$, i.e.\ a quadratic with asymmetric linear
coefficient $b_1 + b_2$.

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{nonsymmetric\_discriminant\_mean}]
If $a t^2 + (b_1 + b_2)\,t + c \ge 0$ for all $t$, then
$\bigl((b_1 + b_2) / 2\bigr)^2 \le a \cdot c$.
Proved by reducing to \texttt{discriminant\_nonneg} at
$b := (b_1 + b_2) / 2$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{nonsymmetric\_mean\_le\_geom\_mean};
\texttt{nonsymmetric\_sum\_abs\_bound}]
$|(b_1 + b_2) / 2| \le \sqrt{a \cdot c}$, equivalently
$|b_1 + b_2| \le 2\sqrt{a \cdot c}$ (under $a, c \ge 0$).
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{schwarz\_abs\_bound}]
Symmetric counterpart: $|b| \le \sqrt{a \cdot c}$ from the standard
quadratic $a t^2 + 2bt + c \ge 0$ with $a, c \ge 0$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{discriminant\_nonneg\_converse};
\texttt{discriminant\_nonneg\_iff}]
Converse of \texttt{discriminant\_nonneg} for $a > 0$:
$b^2 \le a\,c$ implies $a t^2 + 2bt + c \ge 0$ for all $t$.
Proved by completing the square
$a t^2 + 2bt + c = a(t + b/a)^2 + (c - b^2/a)$.
Combined biconditional as \texttt{discriminant\_nonneg\_iff}.
\end{theorem}

Two-variable iteration of the non-symmetric Schwarz gives a
Cauchy-Schwarz-in-product form and cross-variable cube bounds:

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{nonsymmetric\_iterated\_schwarz};
\texttt{nonsymmetric\_two\_le\_sum}]
From $x^2 \le a \cdot b$ with $x, a, b \ge 0$:
$x \le \sqrt{a \cdot b}$ (GM bound) and $2x \le a + b$ (AM bound,
via $(a - b)^2 \ge 0$).
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{nonsymmetric\_product\_bound}]
Under $x^2 \le a$, $y^2 \le b$ with $xy \ge 0$:
$xy \le \sqrt{a \cdot b}$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{nonsymmetric\_cross\_iteration\_x},
\texttt{\_y}; \texttt{nonsymmetric\_cube\_bound\_x}, \texttt{\_y}]
From the cross-bounds $x^2 \le a \cdot y$ and $y^2 \le b \cdot x$:
$x^4 \le a^2 \cdot b \cdot x$ and $y^4 \le a \cdot b^2 \cdot y$.
When $x > 0$ (resp.\ $y > 0$): $x^3 \le a^2 \cdot b$ (resp.\
$y^3 \le a \cdot b^2$).
\end{theorem}

These algebraic pieces are the foundation for non-symmetric reflection
positivity; combined with a bilinear-form expansion hypothesis, they
yield Schwarz-type bounds without requiring $b(x, y) = b(y, x)$.

\paragraph{Capstone: reflection-positive Schwarz.}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{polarization\_identity}]
For a bilinear form $b : \alpha \to \alpha \to \mathbb{R}$ on an
additive commutative group $\alpha$ (with bilinearity given as four
elementary hypotheses),
\[
  b(x + y, x + y) - b(x - y, x - y) = 2 \cdot (b(x, y) + b(y, x)).
\]
Exposes the symmetrized off-diagonal $b(x, y) + b(y, x)$ as a difference
of diagonal entries, useful even when $b$ is non-symmetric.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{schwarz\_of\_reflection\_positive}; \S10.6 main]
For a bilinear $b : \alpha \to \alpha \to \mathbb{R}$ on an $\mathbb{R}$-module $\alpha$
with \texttt{ReflectionPositive b} ($b(x, x) \ge 0$),
\[
  \left(\frac{b(x, y) + b(y, x)}{2}\right)^2 \le b(x, x) \cdot b(y, y).
\]
Proof: bilinearity expands
$b(x + t\cdot y, x + t\cdot y) = b(y, y)\,t^2 + (b(x, y) + b(y, x))\,t
+ b(x, x)$;
reflection positivity gives this quadratic $\ge 0$ for all $t$;
\texttt{nonsymmetric\_discriminant\_mean} yields the Schwarz bound.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Corollaries]
AM-GM form \texttt{reflection\_positive\_mean\_le\_geom\_mean}:
$|(b(x, y) + b(y, x)) / 2| \le \sqrt{b(x, x) \cdot b(y, y)}$,
equivalently \texttt{\_sum\_abs\_bound}:
$|b(x, y) + b(y, x)| \le 2 \sqrt{b(x, x) \cdot b(y, y)}$.

Degenerate case \texttt{\_off\_diag\_zero\_of\_diag\_zero}:
$b(x, x) = 0 \Rightarrow b(x, y) + b(y, x) = 0$
(and the symmetric $\texttt{\_right}$ variant for $b(y, y) = 0$).
