\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{classical\_schwarz\_of\_symmetric\_reflection\_positive}]
Under the additional symmetry $b(x, y) = b(y, x)$, the non-symmetric
bound reduces to the classical Cauchy-Schwarz inequality:
$(b(x, y))^2 \le b(x, x) \cdot b(y, y)$, with abs form
$|b(x, y)| \le \sqrt{b(x, x) \cdot b(y, y)}$, and the sharper
degenerate case $b(x, x) = 0 \Rightarrow b(x, y) = 0$ (for the
individual entry, not just the sum).
\end{theorem}

This completes the §10.6 algebraic core required for non-symmetric
reflection-positivity Schwarz bounds.

\paragraph{Closure operations on ReflectionPositive.}

The class of reflection-positive bilinear forms is closed under
standard cone operations:

\begin{itemize}
\item \texttt{ReflectionPositive.zero}: the zero form is RP.
\item \texttt{ReflectionPositive.const}: the constant form $b(x, y) = c$ with $c \ge 0$ is RP.
\item \texttt{ReflectionPositive.of\_diag\_nonneg}: a form depending only on the first argument, with nonneg values, is RP.
\item \texttt{ReflectionPositive.add}: the sum of two RP forms is RP.
\item \texttt{ReflectionPositive.smul\_nonneg}: a nonneg scalar multiple of an RP form is RP.
\item \texttt{ReflectionPositive.comp}: reparametrization $b'(x, y) := b(g(x), g(y))$ under $g : \beta \to \alpha$ inherits RP.
\item \texttt{ReflectionPositive.sum}: a finite sum $\sum_i b_i$ of RP forms is RP.
\item \texttt{ReflectionPositive.weighted\_sum}: a nonneg-weighted sum $\sum_i c_i b_i$ is RP.
\item \texttt{ReflectionPositive.of\_diag\_eq}: RP depends only on diagonal values (transfer lemma).
\end{itemize}

Together these show that RP bilinear forms form a convex cone closed
under reparametrization and finite sums, supplying building blocks
for constructing concrete RP witnesses in applications.

\section{Phase transitions (\S16.1)}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{magnetization\_monotone\_h}; \S16.1, p.~283]
The magnetization $M(i) = \langle\sigma_i\rangle$ is monotone increasing
in $h$ on $[0, \infty)$ for ferromagnetic parameters.
Follows from \texttt{correlation\_monotone\_h}.
\end{theorem}

In finite volume, $f(h)$ is real-analytic (\texttt{freeEnergyH\_analyticOn}),
so $M(h)$ is smooth and no phase transitions occur.
Phase transitions appear only in the infinite volume limit:
a first-order transition at $h_0$ corresponds to a jump discontinuity
of $M(h)$ at $h_0$ (\S16.1, p.~283).

\section{Critical exponents (\S17.5, \S17.7)}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{eta\_nonneg\_finite\_vol}; Thm~17.7.1]
The critical exponent $\eta \ge 0$: in finite volume,
$\langle\sigma_i;\sigma_j\rangle \ge 0$ by GKS-II
(\texttt{truncated2\_nonneg}).
\end{theorem}

The bound $\zeta \ge 0$ follows from $U_4 \le 0$ (\texttt{cor\_4\_3\_3}).
The correlation length definition (inverse mass, \S17.5)
and the continuity theorem (Thm~17.5.1) require the infinite
volume limit and transfer matrix spectral theory.

\section{Cluster expansion (\S18.1--18.3)}

The lattice Ising analogue of the cluster expansion is the
\textbf{high-temperature expansion}, decomposing each Boltzmann factor:
$\exp(\beta J \sigma_i\sigma_j) = \cosh(\beta J)(1 + \tanh(\beta J)\sigma_i\sigma_j)$.
This is already proved as \texttt{exp\_edgeSpin\_decomp}.

\begin{definition}[\texttt{highTempParam}]
$t = \tanh(\beta J)$, the high-temperature expansion parameter.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{abs\_highTempParam\_lt\_one}]
$|t| = |\tanh(\beta J)| < 1$ for all finite $\beta J$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{highTempParam\_lt\_one}]
$t < 1$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{partitionFunction\_high\_temp\_expansion}; GJ~\S18.3,
cf.\ FV~\S3.7.3 eq.\,(3.42)]
For any Ising parameter $p = (J, h, \beta)$ on a finite simple graph
$G = (\iota, E)$,
\[
Z(G; p) = (\cosh(\beta J))^{|E|}
\sum_{\sigma}
\Bigl(\prod_{\{i,j\} \in E} (1 + \tanh(\beta J)\,\sigma_i\sigma_j)\Bigr)
\exp\!\Bigl(\beta h \sum_i \sigma_i\Bigr).
\]
\end{theorem}

\textit{Proof.} The Hamiltonian factorises as
$-\beta H = \beta J \sum_e \sigma_i\sigma_j + \beta h \sum_i \sigma_i$,
so $e^{-\beta H} = \prod_e e^{\beta J \sigma_i\sigma_j} \cdot
e^{\beta h \sum_i \sigma_i}$.
Since $\sigma_i\sigma_j \in \{\pm 1\}$,
$e^{\beta J \sigma_i\sigma_j} = \cosh(\beta J) + \sinh(\beta J)\sigma_i\sigma_j
= \cosh(\beta J)(1 + \tanh(\beta J)\sigma_i\sigma_j)$
by \texttt{exp\_edgeSpin\_decomp} and $\cosh(\beta J) > 0$.
Multiplying over edges and pulling out $\cosh(\beta J)^{|E|}$ gives the claim.
\hfill$\square$

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{partitionFunction\_high\_temp\_expansion\_h\_zero}; GJ~\S18.3 / FV~\S3.7.3 eq.\,(3.42)]
At zero external field, the high-temperature expansion specialises to
\[
Z(G; J, 0, \beta) = (\cosh(\beta J))^{|E|}
\sum_{\sigma}
\prod_{\{i,j\} \in E} (1 + \tanh(\beta J)\,\sigma_i\sigma_j).
\]
\end{theorem}

\textit{Proof.} Direct specialisation of the previous theorem at $h = 0$,
where the field factor $\exp(\beta h \sum_i \sigma_i) = 1$.
\hfill$\square$

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{partitionFunction\_high\_temp\_expansion\_h\_zero\_closed}; FV~\S3.7.3 eq.\,(3.45)]
For any Ising parameter $(J, 0, \beta)$ on a finite simple graph
$G = (\iota, E)$,
\[
Z(G; J, 0, \beta) = 2^{|\iota|} \cdot (\cosh(\beta J))^{|E|}
\sum_{\substack{X \subseteq E \\ \text{every $v$ has even $X$-degree}}}
  \tanh(\beta J)^{|X|}.
\]
\end{theorem}

\textit{Proof.} Starting from the zero-field expansion
$Z = (\cosh \beta J)^{|E|} \sum_\sigma \prod_e (1 + \tanh(\beta J)\sigma_i\sigma_j)$,
expand each edge product via
$\prod_{e \in E}(1 + f_e) = \sum_{X \subseteq E} \prod_{e \in X} f_e$
(\texttt{Finset.prod\_one\_add}); pull out $\tanh(\beta J)^{|X|}$ and
swap the $\sigma$- and $X$-sums. The inner $\sigma$-sum
$\sum_\sigma \prod_{e \in X} \sigma_i\sigma_j = \prod_v \sum_{s_v}(s_v)^{d_X(v)}$
factorises by Fubini and equals $2^{|\iota|}$ when every
$d_X(v) := |\{e \in X : v \in e\}|$ is even, else $0$. The terms with
odd-degree vertices vanish, leaving the sum over even-degree
subgraphs.
\hfill$\square$

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{correlation\_high\_temp\_expansion\_h\_zero\_closed}; FV~\S3.7.3 eq.\,(3.46)]
For any $A \subseteq \iota$ on a finite simple graph $G = (\iota, E)$,
\[
\langle \sigma_A \rangle_{\beta, 0} =
  \frac{\sum_{X \subseteq E,\, \partial X = A} \tanh(\beta J)^{|X|}}
       {\sum_{X \subseteq E,\, \partial X = \emptyset} \tanh(\beta J)^{|X|}},
\]
where $\partial X := \{ v : \iota \mid d_X(v) \text{ is odd}\}$ is the
boundary of the edge set $X$.
\end{theorem}

\textit{Proof.} The correlation function is
$\langle \sigma_A \rangle = N / Z$ with
$N := \sum_\sigma \sigma_A \cdot e^{-\beta H(\sigma)}$. Repeating the
high-temperature expansion of the previous theorem with the extra
factor $\sigma_A = \prod_{v \in A} (\sigma_v)^{1}$ gives
\[
N = 2^{|\iota|} \cdot (\cosh \beta J)^{|E|}
   \sum_{X \subseteq E,\, \partial X = A} \tanh(\beta J)^{|X|};
\]
the parity step now requires
$d_X(v) + \mathbf{1}_{v \in A}$ to be even at every $v$, i.e.\
$d_X(v) \equiv \mathbf{1}_{v \in A} \pmod{2}$, equivalently
$\partial X = A$. Dividing by $Z$ from the previous theorem
cancels the prefactor $2^{|\iota|} (\cosh \beta J)^{|E|}$.
\hfill$\square$

\paragraph{Wrappers.} The two closed forms are lifted to the
\(\Lambda\)-layer (\texttt{partitionFunction\LeanLambda\_high\_temp\_expansion\_h\_zero\_closed},
\texttt{correlation\LeanLambda\_high\_temp\_expansion\_h\_zero\_closed} in
\texttt{AmbientLattice/Defs.lean}) and to the concrete
\(\mathbb{Z}^d\) lattice
(\texttt{partitionFunction\LeanLambda\_latticeGraph\_high\_temp\_expansion\_h\_zero\_closed},
\texttt{correlation\LeanLambda\_latticeGraph\_high\_temp\_expansion\_h\_zero\_closed}
in \texttt{Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/HighTemperatureBounds.lean}).

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{partitionFunction\_high\_temp\_expansion\_h\_zero\_lower\_bound}]
For \(0 \le \beta J\),
\(Z(G; J, 0, \beta) \ge 2^{|\iota|} \cdot (\cosh(\beta J))^{|E|}.\)
\end{theorem}

\textit{Proof.} In FV (3.45) the empty edge set \(X = \emptyset\) is
trivially even-degree (every vertex has degree \(0\)) and contributes
\(\tanh(\beta J)^0 = 1\) to the sum; every other even-degree subset
contributes a nonneg amount once \(\tanh(\beta J) \ge 0\) is known
(equivalent to \(0 \le \beta J\)). Multiplying the inequality
\(\sum_X \tanh(\beta J)^{|X|} \ge 1\) by the nonneg prefactor
\(2^{|\iota|} (\cosh \beta J)^{|E|}\) gives the claim.
\hfill$\square$

\section{Cluster (polymer / Mayer) expansion (\S18.4--\S18.7)}

\paragraph{Polymer-family identity (\S18.4).} The
even-subgraph sum in FV~(3.45) is reorganised into a sum over
\textbf{vertex-disjoint compatible polymer families}. A
\emph{polymer} is a non-empty connected even-degree edge subset
(\texttt{IsPolymer}); compatibility is vertex-disjointness
(\texttt{IsPolymerVertexDisjoint}). Each even-degree edge set $X$
decomposes uniquely into its connected components
(\texttt{polymerDecomposition}), giving a bijection between
$\{X : \partial X = \emptyset\}$ and
$\mathrm{vdCompatiblePolymerFamilies}\,G$. The capstone identity is
\[
Z(G; J, 0, \beta) = 2^{|\iota|} \cdot (\cosh \beta J)^{|E|}
  \sum_{\Gamma \in \mathrm{vdCompatiblePolymerFamilies}(G)}
    \prod_{P \in \Gamma} \tanh(\beta J)^{|P|}
\]
(\texttt{partitionFunction\_high\_temp\_expansion\_h\_zero\_polymer\_family}).

\paragraph{Mayer / Ursell infrastructure (Steps~576--615).}
The exponential identity $\log \Xi = \sum_{n \ge 1} \frac{1}{n!}
\sum_{\omega} \phi^T(\omega)\, z(t,\omega)$ is formalised via
\texttt{ursellCoefficient} (\(\phi^T(\omega)\),
Step~583), \texttt{clusterSeqActivity} (\(z(t,\omega)\),
Step~581), \texttt{mayerExpansionTerm} (the $n$-th term,
Step~587), and \texttt{mayerPartialSum} (truncation through
cluster size $N$, Step~591). Pair-Ursell formula
$\phi^T(\omega) = -\tfrac{1}{2}$ for incompatible pairs,
$0$ otherwise (Steps~585--586); uniform absolute bound
$|\phi^T(\omega)| \le 2^{\binom{n}{2}}/n!$ (Step~615) via
$|\mathrm{connectedSpanningEdgeSubsets}\,G| \le 2^{|G.\mathrm{edgeFinset}|}$
(Step~602) plus
\texttt{SimpleGraph.card\_edgeFinset\_le\_card\_choose\_two}.
Polymer free energy
$\mathrm{polymerFreeEnergy}\,G\,t := \log(\sum_\Gamma \prod_P t^{|P|})$
(Step~610) is real-analytic on $[0,\infty)$ and connects to the
Ising free energy via
$f(J,0,\beta) = \log 2 + (|E|/|\iota|) \log\cosh(\beta J)
  + \mathrm{polymerFreeEnergy}\,G\,(\tanh \beta J)/|\iota|$
under $0 < |\iota|$, $0 \le \beta J$ (Step~612).

\paragraph{Phase~B base cases (PRs~\#1518--\#1546, \#1553).}
Explicit values of the alternating connected-subgraph sum
$\mathrm{alternatingConnectedSubgraphSum}\,G
:= \sum_{S \in \mathrm{connectedSpanningEdgeSubsets}\,G} (-1)^{|S|}$
were computed by \texttt{decide} (with
\texttt{set\_option maxRecDepth} / \texttt{maxHeartbeats} raised) on:
\begin{itemize}
  \item Complete graphs \(K_3 = 2\), \(K_4 = -6\)
    (PRs~\#1518, \#1519);
  \item Path graphs \(P_n\) for \(n = 3,\dots,8\) with values
    \(1, -1, 1, -1, 1, -1\) (PRs~\#1542--\#1544; \(P_8\) requires
    \texttt{maxRecDepth 8000} + \texttt{maxHeartbeats 1000000});
  \item Cycle graphs \(C_n\) for \(n = 3,\dots,7\) with values
    \(2, -3, 4, -5, 6\) (PRs~\#1545, \#1546, \#1553).
\end{itemize}
The general formula
$\mathrm{alternatingConnectedSubgraphSum}(K_n) = (-1)^{n-1}(n-1)!$
and any case with $n \ge 9$ require chromatic-polynomial /
matrix-tree-theorem machinery not yet in Mathlib, and remain
deferred (Phase~B blocker).

\paragraph{Polymer free energy bounds and characterisations
(PRs~\#1517--\#1566).} A coordinated set of sharpenings was added
in this batch:
\begin{itemize}
  \item \emph{Convergence radius via $\log$ \texttt{HasSum}}
    (\#1517): \texttt{polymerFreeEnergy\_hasSum\_via\_log\_of\_pow\_lt\_two}
    expresses $\mathrm{polymerFreeEnergy}\,G\,t$ as a `HasSum` of
    the natural log expansion under the explicit radius
    $(1+t)^{|E|} < 2$.
  \item \emph{Filter forms} (\#1521, \#1522): the
    \texttt{mayerExpansionTerm} / \texttt{mayerPartialSum} sums
    can be restricted to connected polymer-sequence supports
    (\texttt{\_filter\_connected}) without changing the value.
  \item \emph{Upper bounds} (\#1523--\#1527):
    $\mathrm{polymerFreeEnergy}\,G\,t \le \varepsilon(G,t)$
    (where $\varepsilon := \sum_\Gamma \prod_P t^{|P|} - 1$),
    $\le (1+t)^{|E|} - 1$, $< \log 2$ under
    $(1+t)^{|E|} < 2$, the
    \texttt{polymerFreeEnergy\_high\_temp\_sandwich} 5-tuple, and
    the explicit free-energy correction
    \texttt{freeEnergy\_lt\_log\_two\_plus\_high\_temp\_correction}.
  \item \emph{Strict / iff characterisations}
    (\#1547--\#1550, \#1554--\#1560): under $\varepsilon > 0$,
    $\mathrm{polymerFreeEnergy} < \varepsilon$ (strict; \#1547),
    $= 0 \iff \varepsilon = 0$, $> 0 \iff \varepsilon > 0$
    (\#1548), strict $< (1+t)^{|E|} - 1$ (\#1554, \#1555 tanh
    form), \texttt{vdPolymerFamilies\_sum\_eq\_one\_iff\_eps\_eq\_zero}
    and \texttt{\_gt\_one\_iff\_eps\_pos} (\#1557), bundles of
    strict-mono / strict-pos (\#1559, \#1560)
    including \texttt{StrictMonoOn (Set.Ioi 0)}.
  \item \emph{Pair connectivity} (\#1551, \#1552):
    \texttt{polymerSeqIncompatibilityGraph\_two\_connected\_iff\_incompatible}
    and the connected-restricted form of the $n=2$ Mayer term
    equate to incompatibility.
  \item \emph{$\Lambda$-layer wrappers} (\#1563, \#1564):
    $10 + 8$ theorems lifting the abstract API to the
    $\Lambda$ layer in
    \texttt{IsingModel/AmbientLattice/Analyticity.lean}.
  \item \emph{Ferromagnetic / tanh-monotonicity} (\#1565, \#1566):
    $0 \le J,\, 0 < \beta$ versions of the strict / iff bundle, and
    $\mathrm{polymerFreeEnergy}(\tanh(\beta J))$ monotone (resp.\
    strictly monotone) in $\beta$ and $J$ under the appropriate
    sign assumptions; \#1566 introduces the local helper
    \texttt{real\_tanh\_strictMono}.
\end{itemize}

\paragraph{General-$h$ analyticity capstone (\S18.6,
PRs~\#1528--\#1541).} Per-direction and joint analyticity of the
free energy at \emph{general} external field $h$:
\begin{itemize}
  \item \texttt{freeEnergy\_analyticAt\_\{beta,J,h\}\_general\_h}
    (\#1528--\#1530) and matching
    \texttt{\_analyticOnNhd\_\{beta,J,h\}\_general\_h}: the partial
    real-analyticity of $f(J,h,\beta)$ is now available without
    the $h = 0$ restriction.
  \item \texttt{freeEnergy\_analyticAt\_joint} (\#1531) and
    \texttt{\_analyticOnNhd\_joint} (\#1532) over $\mathrm{Set.univ}$:
    joint analyticity in $(\beta, J, h)$.
  \item \texttt{freeEnergy\_continuous\_joint} +
    \texttt{\_differentiable\_joint} (\#1533).
  \item Lambda-layer joint analyticity in
    \texttt{IsingModel/AmbientLattice/Analyticity.lean} (\#1535).
  \item \texttt{correlation\_analyticAt\_joint} +
    full hierarchy at the Lambda layer (\#1536, \#1537).
  \item \texttt{gibbsExpectation\_analyticAt\_joint} for an arbitrary
    observable $F: \mathrm{Configuration} \to \mathbb{R}$ (\#1539).
  \item \texttt{partitionFunction} continuity / differentiability per
    direction at general $h$ (\#1540) plus Lambda-layer wrappers
    (\#1541).
\end{itemize}

\paragraph{Ferromagnetic forms of \texttt{polymerFreeEnergy}
sandwich + hasSum, all volume layers (\S18.5, PR~\#1573).} Under
$0 \le J,\, 0 < \beta$ each of the §18.5 PR~\#1569 statements
(\texttt{polymerFreeEnergy\_tanh\_high\_temp\_sandwich} and
\texttt{polymerFreeEnergy\_tanh\_hasSum\_via\_log\_of\_pow\_lt\_two})
admits a ferromagnetic version, derived through
\texttt{mul\_nonneg h$\beta$.le hJ} and matched at each of the five
layers (abstract / $\Lambda$-direct / along-exhaustion /
$\mathbb{Z}^d$ $\Lambda$-direct / $\mathbb{Z}^d$ along-exhaustion).
The four $\mathbb{Z}^d$ wrappers use the abbreviated suffix
\texttt{\_ferro} (instead of \texttt{\_ferromagnetic}) so each
declaration fits the linter's 100-character budget. The along-exhaustion
and $\mathbb{Z}^d$ high-temperature wrappers from this family now live in
\texttt{AmbientLattice/SpecialCases/HighTemperature.lean} and
\texttt{Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/HighTemperature.lean};
PR~\#1864 moved only those non-$\Lambda$ wrappers out of the legacy
modules. The $\Lambda$-layer wrappers were already in
\texttt{IsingModel/AmbientLattice/Analyticity.lean} and remain there.

\paragraph{\texttt{vdPolymerFamilies\_sum} bound family completion,
all volume layers (\S18.5, PR~\#1595).} Adds Lambda, along-ex, and
$\mathbb{Z}^d$ wraps for the three abstract bound theorems
\texttt{\_le\_two\_pow},
\texttt{\_le\_one\_plus\_tanh\_pow}, and
\texttt{one\_le\_vdPolymerFamilies\_sum} (which had no wrappers
before). The Lambda wrappers for the four sandwich abstracts already
existed; this PR fills the bare-bound and \(\geq 1\) gaps. 12 new
thin direct-instantiation wrappers (3 abstracts $\times$ 4
layers). The along-exhaustion and concrete $\mathbb{Z}^d$ wrappers
now live in the matching \texttt{MayerVdBounds.lean} child modules;
the $\Lambda$-layer wrappers live in
\texttt{IsingModel/AmbientLattice/AnalyticityLambdaBasicIdentities.lean}
(narrowed out of \texttt{Analyticity.lean} in PR~\#1986).

\paragraph{\S17.5 Lemma 17.5.2 stub honest naming + docs cleanup
(PR~\#1644).} The previously-named
\texttt{latticeMass\_ge\_pseudoMass} and
\texttt{latticeMass\_le\_constant\_mul\_pseudoMass} in
\texttt{PseudoMass.lean} were misleading aliases of
\texttt{pseudoMass\_pos} and \texttt{discrete\_hls\_constant}
respectively (their conclusions did not mention
\texttt{latticeMass}). They have been renamed to
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_pseudoMass\_pos} and
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_constant\_exists} to honestly reflect
their content as \emph{helpers} toward the full Lemma 17.5.2,
not the lemma itself. \texttt{docs/index.md} updated: §17.5
Lemma 17.5.2 (full) is now marked as \textbf{Partial}, with the
gap explicitly identified as the missing concrete-to-abstract map
$(d, \Lambda, p) \to (\alpha, r, c)$ and the exponential decay
bounds on $\mathbb{Z}^d$ (Step 117h+).

\paragraph{\S17.5 Lemma 17.5.2 conditional sandwich capstone
(Step 117l, PRs~\#2663--\#2672).} The new module
\texttt{Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/Lemma\_17\_5\_2.lean}
introduces the named capstone interface for
Glimm--Jaffe \S17.5 Lemma~17.5.2 (2nd ed., pp.~311--312):
\[
m^-(\beta) \le m(\beta) \le C \cdot m^-(\beta),
\]
where $m^- := \mathrm{pseudoMassFromParamsAtPair}$ and
$m := \mathrm{latticeMass}$.  The capstone module exposes the following named
declarations:
\begin{itemize}
\item \texttt{Lemma\_17\_5\_2\_UpperBound} -- the \texttt{Prop}-valued
  predicate naming the upper-bound side
  $m \le C \cdot \mathrm{ofReal}\,m^-$ as a hypothesis.  Future
  Lipschitz + HLS work (cf.\ \texttt{pseudoMass\_pow\_succ\_lipschitz}
  and \texttt{discrete\_hls\_constant}) is intended to discharge it
  unconditionally.
\item \texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_lower\_bound\_of\_decay} -- the
  Lemma~17.5.2 lower-bound side, a renaming of the existing decay-based
  bridge \texttt{latticeMass\_ge\_pseudoMassFromParamsAtPair\_of\_decay}
  under the GJ-named identifier.
\item \texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_sandwich\_of\_decay\_and\_upper} -- the
  conditional sandwich capstone, returning both inequalities once a
  validating exponential decay rate and an upper-bound hypothesis are
  supplied.
\item \texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_upper\_bound\_of\_all\_decay\_rates\_le} --
  the final order-theoretic upper-bound assembly: since
  \texttt{latticeMass} is an \texttt{sSup} over validating decay rates, a
  uniform bound on every admissible rate by $C\,\mathrm{ofReal}(m^-)$ gives
  the named \texttt{Lemma\_17\_5\_2\_UpperBound}.
\item \texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_sandwich\_of\_decay\_and\_all\_decay\_rates\_le}
  -- the corresponding sandwich wrapper combining the lower validating
  pseudo-mass decay input with the all-decay-rate upper assembly.
\item \texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_cubic\_high\_temp\_lower\_capstone} and
  \texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_cubic\_high\_temp\_latticeMass\_pos} -- the
  Lemma~17.5.2 lower-bound side specialised to the cubic exhaustion at
  high temperature $\beta J (2d) < 1$, derived as direct projections
  of the existing
  \texttt{cubicNamedRate\_capstone\_bundle\_of\_cubic\_corr\_mem\_Ioo\_and\_profile}
  bundle.  These do not require any upper-bound hypothesis, but they
  remain conditional on active-range membership of the anchored cubic
  pair correlation $\langle\sigma_0\sigma_z\rangle_\infty$ in $(0,2)$
  together with the tanh-power profile lower bound
  $g_\alpha(-\log(\beta J\,2d)) \le \langle\sigma_0\sigma_z\rangle_\infty$.
\item \texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_constant\_existence} -- a strict-positivity
  alias of \texttt{discrete\_hls\_constant} ($2\alpha > d$).
\item \texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_hls\_convolution\_constant} -- the
  positive constant form of the polynomial HLS convolution estimate.
\item \texttt{Lemma\_17\_5\_2\_HLSDenominatorComparison} -- the named
  finite-stage scalar comparison between the high-temperature
  Lebowitz/susceptibility derivative bound and the HLS denominator
  $Kc(\beta)/(h(\beta))^{2\alpha}$.
\item \texttt{Lemma\_17\_5\_2\_InfiniteHLSDenominatorComparison} -- the
  infinite-volume derivative comparison
  $|c'(\beta)|\le Kc(\beta)/(h(\beta))^{2\alpha}$ for
  $c(\beta)=\langle\sigma_x\sigma_z\rangle_\infty$.
\item \texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_beta\_deriv\_abs\_le\_high\_temp} -- the
  finite-stage high-temperature beta-derivative absolute bound
  $|c'|\le J M^2 + 4dJ$, exposing the concrete Lebowitz/susceptibility
  input that must be compared with the HLS pseudo-mass denominator.
\item \texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_hls\_derivative\_hypothesis\_of\_abs\_bound}
  -- the scalar comparison bridge from a concrete absolute derivative bound
  to the HLS denominator hypothesis
  $|c'|\le Kc(\beta)/(h(\beta))^{2\alpha}$.
\item \texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_beta\_hls\_derivative\_hypothesis\_of\_high\_temp}
  -- the finite-stage high-temperature package combining the preceding
  beta-derivative bound with that HLS denominator comparison.
\item \texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_beta\_pseudoMass\_power\_deriv\_le\_of\_high\_temp\_bound}
  -- the finite-stage concrete handoff from the HLS derivative hypothesis to
  \texttt{pseudoMass\_power\_deriv\_le}, giving
  $(h(\beta))^{2\alpha}|h'(\beta)|\le K/\rho$.
\item \texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_beta\_pseudoMass\_power\_deriv\_le\_of\_hls\_constant}
  -- the existential HLS-constant version of the preceding power-derivative
  estimate.
\item \texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_beta\_pseudoMass\_pow\_succ\_deriv\_bound\_of\_high\_temp\_bound}
  -- the corresponding finite-stage derivative bound for
  $\beta\mapsto(h(\beta))^{2\alpha+1}$.
\item \texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_beta\_pseudoMass\_pow\_succ\_deriv\_bound\_of\_hls\_constant}
  -- the HLS-constant existential version of the pointwise
  $(h(\beta))^{2\alpha+1}$ derivative bound.
\item \texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_beta\_pseudoMass\_pow\_succ\_lipschitz\_of\_high\_temp\_bound}
  -- the finite-stage interval Lipschitz estimate for
  $\beta\mapsto(h(\beta))^{2\alpha+1}$, obtained by applying the MVT to the
  pointwise concrete pseudo-mass power derivative bound.
\item \texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_beta\_pseudoMass\_pow\_succ\_lipschitz\_of\_hls\_constant}
  -- the corresponding HLS-constant existential interval estimate, still
  conditional on the pointwise denominator comparison.
\item \texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_infinite\_pseudoMass\_pow\_succ\_deriv\_bound\_of\_hls\_constant}
  and
  \texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_infinite\_pseudoMass\_pow\_succ\_lipschitz\_of\_hls\_constant}
  -- the corresponding infinite-volume pointwise derivative and interval
  Lipschitz handoffs, retaining the HLS convolution inequality for the chosen
  constant and using the exact infinite-volume denominator comparison.
\end{itemize}
PR~\#2664 adds a finite high-temperature bridge discharging the upper-bound
predicate in the cubic active-range setting without adding an axiom.
The theorem
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_cubic\_high\_temp\_upper\_bound\_of\_active\_range}
uses the already-proved Step~115 bound
\[
  \mathrm{latticeMass}(d,\Lambda_{\mathrm{cubic}},\langle J,0,\beta\rangle)
  \leq
  \mathrm{ofReal}\bigl(-\log(\tanh(\beta J))\bigr)
\]
together with exhaustion-independence of \texttt{latticeMass} and the
active-range positivity of
\[
  m^- :=
  \texttt{cubicOriginPseudoMassFromParamsAtPair}\,\alpha\,r\,\beta\,J\,z.
\]
Since $m^- > 0$, the finite constant
\[
  C_{\mathrm{fin}} =
  \frac{-\log(\tanh(\beta J))}{m^-}
\]
satisfies
\[
  \mathrm{latticeMass}(d,\Lambda,\langle J,0,\beta\rangle)
  \leq C_{\mathrm{fin}}\,\mathrm{ofReal}(m^-).
\]
In Lean this last algebraic step is
\texttt{ENNReal.ofReal\_div\_of\_pos} followed by
\texttt{ENNReal.div\_mul\_cancel}.  The theorem
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_cubic\_high\_temp\_sandwich\_capstone}
then combines this finite upper bridge with
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_cubic\_high\_temp\_lower\_capstone}, returning
\texttt{HasExponentialDecay}, the lower inequality
\[
  \mathrm{ofReal}(m^-) \leq
  \mathrm{latticeMass}(d,\Lambda,\langle J,0,\beta\rangle),
\]
and the finite upper inequality above.

This finite constant is not the HLS-uniform constant from the proof of
GJ~Theorem~17.5.1 (p.~312).  It closes the existing Lean upper-bound
predicate in the high-temperature active-range regime using Step~115; the
sharper HLS-uniform upper-bound proof remains a future refinement.

\paragraph{\S17.5 Lemma 17.5.2 all-rate Step~115 and enlarged HLS finite
sandwich (Issue~\#1645).} The Step~115 upper bound is now also exposed in the
all-admissible-rate form
\[
  a \le \mathrm{ofReal}\bigl(-\log(\tanh(\beta J))\bigr)
\]
for every nonnegative validating rate
\[
  \texttt{HasExponentialDecay}\;
    d\;\Lambda_{\mathrm{cubic}}\;\langle J,0,\beta\rangle\;a.
\]
In Lean this is
\texttt{HasExponentialDecay\_rate\_le\_neg\_log\_tanh\_betaJ}.  Its proof is
the contradiction argument formerly embedded in
\texttt{latticeMass\_le\_neg\_log\_tanh\_betaJ}: choose an axis point
\(r_n=(n,0,\ldots,0)\), combine the path lower bound
\(\tanh(\beta J)^n\le \langle\sigma_0\sigma_{r_n}\rangle\) with the assumed
exponential-decay upper bound, and let \(n\) dominate the witness constant.
The lattice-mass theorem is then just the \texttt{sSup} wrapper over this
all-rate estimate.

This all-rate form lets the cubic high-temperature all-rate bridge avoid a
round trip through the named upper-bound predicate.  Given a target exhaustion
\(\Lambda\), the validating rate is first transferred to
\(\Lambda_{\mathrm{cubic}}\) by exhaustion-independence of
\texttt{truncated2Infinite}, then Step~115 bounds it by the path rate.  The
active-range positivity of
\[
  m^-=\texttt{cubicOriginPseudoMassFromParamsAtPair}\,\alpha\,r\,\beta\,J\,z
\]
rewrites the finite constant
\((-\log(\tanh(\beta J))/m^-)\,\mathrm{ofReal}(m^-)\) back to the path rate.

The new HLS-style finite capstone
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_cubic\_high\_temp\_enlarged\_hls\_sandwich}
chooses an enlarged constant
\[
  K=\max\left\{K_0,\,
    \frac{(-\log(\tanh(\beta J)))\,r}{(2\alpha+1)m^-}\right\},
\]
where \(K_0\) is any positive HLS convolution constant from
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_hls\_convolution\_constant}.  The inequality
\(K_0\le K\) preserves the HLS convolution estimate, while the second
inequality in the maximum gives
\[
  -\log(\tanh(\beta J))
  \le \frac{(2\alpha+1)K}{r}\,m^-.
\]
Composing this scalar comparison with the all-rate Step~115 bound yields
\[
  \mathrm{latticeMass}(d,\Lambda,\langle J,0,\beta\rangle)
  \le
  \mathrm{ofReal}\!\left(\frac{(2\alpha+1)K}{r}\right)
  \mathrm{ofReal}(m^-),
\]
and the lower side is supplied by
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_cubic\_high\_temp\_lower\_capstone}.  The result is a
complete finite high-temperature sandwich carrying an HLS convolution
inequality, but the enlarged constant may depend on the present
high-temperature parameters and the anchored pair.  It is therefore not the
final uniform HLS constant claimed in the book's proof of
Theorem~17.5.1.

The same finite package is also available for an arbitrary cubic pair
\(\{x,z\}\).  The theorem
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_cubic\_pair\_high\_temp\_lower\_capstone} first uses
the reference-exhaustion pseudo-mass transfer to show that
\[
  m^-_{\Lambda_{\mathrm{cubic}}}(x,z)
  := \texttt{pseudoMassFromParamsAtPair}\,
      \alpha\,r\,d\,\Lambda_{\mathrm{cubic}}\,
      \langle J,0,\beta\rangle\,x\,z
\]
is a validating decay rate and satisfies
\(\mathrm{ofReal}(m^-_{\Lambda_{\mathrm{cubic}}}(x,z)) \le
\mathrm{latticeMass}(d,\Lambda,\langle J,0,\beta\rangle)\).  The theorem
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_cubic\_pair\_high\_temp\_enlarged\_hls\_sandwich}
then chooses the same kind of enlarged
\[
  K=\max\left\{K_0,\,
    \frac{(-\log(\tanh(\beta J)))\,r}
         {(2\alpha+1)m^-_{\Lambda_{\mathrm{cubic}}}(x,z)}\right\}.
\]
Exhaustion-independence of
\texttt{pseudoMassFromParamsAtPair} rewrites the final statement in terms of
the target-exhaustion pseudo-mass
\(m^-_{\Lambda}(x,z)\), while the upper side again comes from transferring
each validating target-exhaustion decay rate to \(\Lambda_{\mathrm{cubic}}\)
and applying the all-rate Step~115 path bound.
The capstone theorem
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_cubic\_pair\_high\_temp\_enlarged\_hls\_capstone}
repackages this arbitrary-pair statement so that the same enlarged constant
\(K\) carries the HLS convolution inequality, the named
\texttt{Lemma\_17\_5\_2\_UpperBound} predicate, the target-exhaustion
validating decay, and the displayed two-sided sandwich.  This keeps the
arbitrary-pair finite Step~115/HLS package in the same capstone shape as the
anchored cubic wrappers.
The interval-facing theorems
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_cubic\_pair\_high\_temp\_enlarged\_hls\_sandwich\_on\_Icc}
and
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_cubic\_pair\_high\_temp\_enlarged\_hls\_capstone\_on\_Icc}
remove the separate endpoint scalar hypotheses from callers: the inclusion
\([\beta_1,\beta_2]\subset (0,(J2d)^{-1})\) supplies \(0<\beta_2\) and
\(\beta_2J2d<1\), after which the endpoint arbitrary-pair package applies at
\(\beta_2\).

\paragraph{\S17.5 Lemma 17.5.2 infinite HLS Lipschitz to all-rate bridge
(Issue~\#1645).} The infinite-volume HLS/Lipschitz layer now has a named
handoff to the final all-admissible-rate input.  The predicate
\texttt{Lemma\_17\_5\_2\_InfiniteHLSLipschitzAllRateBridge} says that the
interval Lipschitz estimate
\[
  |h(\beta_2)^{2\alpha+1}-h(\beta_1)^{2\alpha+1}|
  \le \frac{2\alpha+1}{r}K(\beta_2-\beta_1)
\]
obtained from the infinite HLS denominator comparison implies, at the endpoint
\(\beta_2\), the bound
\[
  a \le
  \mathrm{ofReal}\!\left(\frac{(2\alpha+1)K}{r}\right)
  \mathrm{ofReal}\bigl(m^-_{\Lambda}(x,z;\beta_2)\bigr)
\]
for every nonnegative validating exponential-decay rate \(a\).  The theorem
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_upper\_bound\_of\_infinite\_hls\_lipschitz\_all\_rate\_bridge}
feeds this named bridge into the existing \texttt{sSup} assembly
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_upper\_bound\_of\_all\_decay\_rates\_le}; the
companion
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_sandwich\_of\_infinite\_hls\_lipschitz\_all\_rate\_bridge}
adds the lower validating-decay input and returns the conditional sandwich.
This is not yet the final HLS-uniform upper-bound theorem: the substantive
remaining step is to prove the named all-rate bridge from the
HLS/Lebowitz derivative analysis.

\paragraph{\S17.5 Lemma 17.5.2 existential infinite HLS Lipschitz package
(Issue~\#1645).} The HLS-constant infinite Lipschitz output can now be consumed
without re-stating its differentiability hypotheses.  The theorem
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_upper\_bound\_of\_exists\_infinite\_hls\_lipschitz\_bridge}
takes the existential package
\[
  \exists K>0,\quad
  K \text{ satisfies the HLS convolution bound and gives the interval
  Lipschitz estimate,}
\]
and returns the same selected constant \(K\) together with an implication from
\texttt{Lemma\_17\_5\_2\_InfiniteHLSLipschitzAllRateBridge} for \(K\) to the
named upper-bound predicate \texttt{Lemma\_17\_5\_2\_UpperBound}.  The companion
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_sandwich\_of\_exists\_infinite\_hls\_lipschitz\_bridge}
adds the lower validating pseudo-mass decay input and packages the analogous
conditional sandwich implication for the same HLS constant.  This keeps the
build path narrow: callers pass the existing output of
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_infinite\_pseudoMass\_pow\_succ\_lipschitz\_of\_hls\_constant}
instead of forcing Lean to elaborate the full differentiability context again.
The final all-rate bridge remains the open analytic input.

\paragraph{\S17.5 Lemma 17.5.2 infinite derivative-limit bridge
(Issue~\#1645).} The finite-to-infinite differentiability handoff for the
HLS denominator comparison is now isolated in
\texttt{Lemma\_17\_5\_2/InfiniteDerivativeLimit.lean}.  The theorem
\texttt{correlationInfinite\_hasDerivAt\_beta\_of\_tendstoLocallyUniformlyOn\_deriv}
uses Mathlib's uniform-limits-of-derivatives theorem: if the finite-volume
derivative profiles
\[
  \beta \mapsto \frac{d}{d\beta}\,
  \texttt{correlationAlongExhaustion}_{n}(\beta)
\]
converge locally uniformly on the open high-temperature interval
\((0,(J\,2d)^{-1})\), then the already-proved locally uniform convergence
\(\texttt{corr}_{n}\to\texttt{corr}_{\infty}\) upgrades to a
\texttt{HasDerivAt} statement for
\(\beta\mapsto\texttt{correlationInfinite}(\langle J,0,\beta\rangle)\).

The theorem
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_infinite\_hls\_denominator\_comparison\_of\_deriv\_bound}
then rewrites the identified derivative into the named predicate
\texttt{Lemma\_17\_5\_2\_InfiniteHLSDenominatorComparison}.  The combined
\texttt{...\_of\_deriv\_limit\_bound} theorem says that a pointwise HLS bound
on the locally uniform derivative limit directly supplies the infinite-volume
denominator comparison.  This does not prove derivative convergence itself;
it packages exactly that remaining analytic input so the HLS/Lebowitz
upper-bound path can consume it without restating the uniform-limit
machinery.

The follow-up finite-HLS limit bridge records how the finite-stage HLS
derivative estimates pass through this limit.  The theorem
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_infinite\_hls\_deriv\_bound\_of\_finite\_profile\_bounds}
uses \texttt{le\_of\_tendsto\_of\_tendsto}: if
\[
  d_n(\beta)\to g'(\beta),\qquad c_n(\beta)\to c_\infty(\beta),
\]
and eventually
\[
  |d_n(\beta)|\le \frac{Kc_n(\beta)}{h(\beta)^{2\alpha}},
\]
then
\[
  |g'(\beta)|\le \frac{Kc_\infty(\beta)}{h(\beta)^{2\alpha}}.
\]
The theorem
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_infinite\_hls\_denominator\_comparison\_of\_finite\_deriv\_bounds}
combines this order/topology handoff with the locally uniform derivative-limit
bridge above.  Thus the remaining upper-bound input is no longer a raw
infinite derivative inequality: it is enough to prove locally uniform
convergence of the finite derivative profiles and eventual finite-stage HLS
derivative bounds for the chosen constant.
The finite derivative-profile bound itself is now named separately:
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_finite\_deriv\_abs\_le\_high\_temp\_on\_Icc}
shows that, after the exhaustion contains the pair \(\{x,z\}\), the finite
profiles satisfy the interval-uniform estimate
\[
  |d_n(\beta)| \le J M^2 + 4Jd,\qquad
  M=\frac{bJ\,2d}{1-bJ\,2d},
\]
for every \(\beta\in[\beta_1,\beta_2]\subset[a,b]\) with \(bJ\,2d<1\).
The all-stage strengthening
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_finite\_deriv\_abs\_le\_high\_temp\_on\_Icc\_all\_stages}
combines this covered-stage estimate with the zero derivative formula before
containment, so the finite-profile bound holds for every exhaustion stage.
The high-temperature scalar-provider package reuses the covered-stage bound
before comparing the Lebowitz/susceptibility constant with the HLS
denominator, while the all-stage theorem records the sharper finite-profile
form needed before any eventual containment reduction.

The interval versions align this handoff with the Lipschitz layer.  If
\([\beta_1,\beta_2]\) is contained in the open high-temperature interval, then
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_infinite\_hls\_denominator\_comparison\_on\_Icc\_of\_finite\_deriv\_bounds}
turns pointwise eventual finite-HLS derivative bounds on every
\(\beta\in[\beta_1,\beta_2]\) into
\[
  \forall\beta\in[\beta_1,\beta_2],\quad
  \texttt{Lemma\_17\_5\_2\_InfiniteHLSDenominatorComparison}(\beta).
\]
The companion
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_infinite\_hls\_denominator\_comparison\_on\_Icc\_of\_uniform\_finite\_deriv\_bounds}
uses a single eventual finite-stage bound uniform over the closed interval.
This is the exact shape needed before applying the infinite HLS Lipschitz
bridge, whose premise asks for the denominator comparison throughout
\([\beta_1,\beta_2]\).

The finite-HLS-to-infinite-Lipschitz package then composes the interval
handoff with the existing pseudo-mass MVT theorem.  Under \(2\alpha>d\), the
theorem
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_infinite\_pseudoMass\_pow\_succ\_lipschitz\_of\_uniform\_finite\_deriv\_bounds}
chooses the HLS convolution constant \(K\).  For that same \(K\), locally
uniform convergence of the finite derivative profiles plus one eventual
finite-stage HLS derivative bound uniform over \([\beta_1,\beta_2]\) gives
\[
  |h(\beta_2)^{2\alpha+1}-h(\beta_1)^{2\alpha+1}|
  \le \frac{(2\alpha+1)K}{\rho}(\beta_2-\beta_1).
\]
Thus the remaining book-level upper-bound input is no longer an unstructured
finite-to-infinite passage: it is the named all-admissible-rate bridge from
this infinite HLS Lipschitz estimate to the \texttt{latticeMass} upper-bound
predicate.  The companion theorems
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_upper\_bound\_of\_finite\_hls\_lipschitz\_package}
and
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_sandwich\_of\_finite\_hls\_lipschitz\_package}
perform that final conditional assembly: given the named all-rate bridge (and,
for the sandwich, the lower validating pseudo-mass decay input), the same
finite-HLS package closes the upper-bound predicate and the conditional
two-sided Lemma~17.5.2 statement for the selected HLS constant.

The all-rate bridge itself now has a concrete Step~115 discharge.  The theorem
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_all\_decay\_rates\_le\_of\_path\_rate\_le}
first proves the direct all-decay-rate estimate: if
\[
  \operatorname{ofReal}(-\log(\tanh(\beta_2J)))
  \le
  C\,
  \operatorname{ofReal}(m^-_{\Lambda}(x,z;\beta_2))
\]
then every validating target-exhaustion decay rate is bounded by
\(C\,\operatorname{ofReal}(m^-_{\Lambda}(x,z;\beta_2))\).  The proof transfers
the decay rate to the cubic exhaustion, where Step~115 bounds it by
\(-\log(\tanh(\beta_2J))\).  The theorems
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_upper\_bound\_of\_path\_rate\_le} and
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_sandwich\_of\_decay\_and\_path\_rate\_le} feed this
direct all-decay-rate estimate into the named upper-bound predicate and the
conditional sandwich.  The HLS-shaped theorem
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_infinite\_hls\_lipschitz\_all\_rate\_bridge\_of\_path\_rate\_le\_hls}
specializes this result to
\(C=\operatorname{ofReal}((2\alpha+1)K/r)\), discharging
\texttt{Lemma\_17\_5\_2\_InfiniteHLSLipschitzAllRateBridge}.  The theorems
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_upper\_bound\_of\_exists\_infinite\_hls\_lipschitz\_and\_path\_rate\_le}
and
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_sandwich\_of\_exists\_infinite\_hls\_lipschitz\_and\_path\_rate\_le}
combine this scalar comparison with the existential infinite-HLS Lipschitz
package itself, using the direct path-rate upper/sandwich theorem to close the
upper-bound predicate or conditional sandwich for the returned HLS constant
without importing the finite derivative-limit package.
The finite-stage versions
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_upper\_bound\_of\_finite\_hls\_lipschitz\_package\_and\_path\_rate\_le}
and
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_sandwich\_of\_finite\_hls\_lipschitz\_package\_and\_path\_rate\_le}
are the corresponding finite-HLS route: they combine the finite-HLS package
with the same explicit path-rate-to-HLS comparison, reducing the remaining
high-temperature HLS upper side to the finite derivative inputs plus that
scalar comparison.
The interval-facing wrappers
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_upper\_bound\_of\_finite\_hls\_lipschitz\_package\_and\_path\_rate\_le\_on\_Icc}
and
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_sandwich\_of\_finite\_hls\_lipschitz\_package\_and\_path\_rate\_le\_on\_Icc}
use the closed-interval high-temperature inclusion
\([\beta_1,\beta_2]\subset (0,(J2d)^{-1})\) to supply the endpoint positivity
\(0<\beta_2\) required by the Step~115 path-rate theorem.  They do not change
the analytic content of the conditional package; they align the path-rate
upper and sandwich statements with the interval hypotheses used by the enlarged
finite-HLS packages below.

There is also an enlarged-constant finite-HLS package that absorbs this scalar
comparison.  The theorem
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_enlarged\_finite\_hls\_lipschitz\_package\_with\_path\_rate}
starts from the interval derivative-limit and finite-HLS derivative inputs, but
chooses
\[
  K=\max\!\left(K_0,\,
      \frac{(-\log(\tanh(\beta_2J)))\,r}{(2\alpha+1)m^-_{\Lambda}(x,z;\beta_2)}
    \right)
\]
under the positivity assumption \(m^-_{\Lambda}(x,z;\beta_2)>0\).  This
constant still dominates the HLS convolution constant \(K_0\), so it remains a
valid HLS convolution constant, while the displayed maximum gives the Step~115
path-rate comparison automatically.  Theorems
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_upper\_bound\_of\_enlarged\_finite\_hls\_lipschitz\_package}
and
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_sandwich\_of\_enlarged\_finite\_hls\_lipschitz\_package}
therefore close the conditional upper and sandwich assemblies without a
separate path-rate scalar premise, still conditional on the finite derivative
bound for the enlarged constant.

The anchored cubic named-rate wrappers
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_cubic\_high\_temp\_enlarged\_hls\_sandwich\_of\_named\_rate}
and
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_cubic\_high\_temp\_enlarged\_hls\_sandwich\_of\_named\_rate\_on\_Icc}
package the same finite Step~115/HLS-style enlarged-constant argument behind
the active-range input and the lightweight
\texttt{cubicOriginNamedRateLeHighTemp} proposition.  The interval form derives
the endpoint scalar high-temperature hypotheses from
\(\left[\beta_1,\beta_2\right]\subset (0,(J2d)^{-1})\), then returns the
validating decay and the displayed two-sided sandwich for the enlarged \(K\).
The capstone forms
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_cubic\_high\_temp\_enlarged\_hls\_capstone\_of\_named\_rate}
and
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_cubic\_high\_temp\_enlarged\_hls\_capstone\_of\_named\_rate\_on\_Icc}
add the matching \texttt{Lemma\_17\_5\_2\_UpperBound} predicate for that same
\(K\).  Their displayed sandwich is stated with the target-exhaustion
\texttt{pseudoMassFromParamsAtPair}, using exhaustion-independence to identify
it with the anchored cubic pseudo-mass that the named-rate input controls.
The endpoint profile-lower form
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_cubic\_high\_temp\_enlarged\_hls\_capstone\_of\_profile\_lower}
first derives the lightweight \texttt{cubicOriginNamedRateLeHighTemp}
proposition from active-range membership and the cubic \(\texttt{pseudoMassG}\)
lower bound, then reuses the same capstone.  The interval-facing profile-lower
form
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_cubic\_high\_temp\_enlarged\_hls\_capstone\_of\_profile\_lower\_on\_Icc}
derives the endpoint scalar high-temperature hypotheses from
\([\beta_1,\beta_2]\subset (0,(J2d)^{-1})\) before applying the endpoint
profile-lower capstone at \(\beta_2\).  A direct
\texttt{cubicTanhProfileBound} version is intentionally not introduced here:
that heavier statement reproduces the deterministic elaboration timeout already
recorded for direct tanh-profile-to-named-rate comparisons.

The derivative-provider wrappers remove that final call-site premise.  The
theorems
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_upper\_bound\_of\_enlarged\_finite\_hls\_lipschitz\_package\_and\_deriv\_provider}
and
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_sandwich\_of\_enlarged\_finite\_hls\_lipschitz\_package\_and\_deriv\_provider}
take a provider that supplies an interval-uniform eventual finite-stage HLS
derivative bound for every positive HLS convolution constant returned by the
enlarged package.  After the package chooses its enlarged \(K\), the wrapper
feeds the HLS convolution estimate for that same \(K\) back to the provider and
closes the named upper-bound predicate; the sandwich variant then composes with
the validating pseudo-mass decay lower side.

The next wrapper derives that provider from the concrete high-temperature
finite-stage derivative estimate.  The theorem
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_finite\_deriv\_provider\_of\_high\_temp\_scalar\_provider}
uses the uniform bound
\[
  |c_n'(\beta)| \le J M^2 + 4Jd
\]
on every sufficiently large stage containing the pair \(\{x,z\}\).  If a
scalar provider compares this Lebowitz/susceptibility constant with
\[
  K\,c_n(\beta)/(h(\beta))^{2\alpha}
\]
for every positive HLS convolution constant returned downstream, it supplies
the finite derivative provider required above.  The direct upper and sandwich
assemblies are
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_upper\_bound\_of\_enlarged\_finite\_hls\_lipschitz\_package\_and\_high\_temp\_scalar\_provider}
and
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_sandwich\_of\_enlarged\_finite\_hls\_lipschitz\_package\_and\_high\_temp\_scalar\_provider}.
They leave the remaining scalar HLS comparison explicit while eliminating the
finite derivative-bound premise from the final callers.

The scalar-provider requirement has also been narrowed to a ratio-lower
package.  First,
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_finite\_deriv\_bound\_of\_high\_temp\_scalar\_bound}
factors out the fixed-\(K\) version of the high-temperature derivative handoff.
Then
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_upper\_bound\_of\_high\_temp\_ratio\_lower}
chooses one enlarged constant
\[
  K=\max\{K_0,\max\{ \mathrm{path}\,r/((2\alpha+1)m^-), B/L\}\},
  \qquad B=J M^2+4Jd,
\]
where \(L>0\) is an eventual interval-uniform lower bound for
\(c_n(\beta)/(h(\beta))^{2\alpha}\).  The same \(K\) keeps the HLS convolution
bound, discharges the Step~115 path-rate comparison, and converts the
Lebowitz/susceptibility bound into the finite derivative estimate.  The
companion
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_sandwich\_of\_high\_temp\_ratio\_lower} adds the
validating pseudo-mass decay lower side.  Thus the remaining scalar HLS input
is now the concrete ratio lower bound \(L\le c_n(\beta)/(h(\beta))^{2\alpha}\),
not a provider for every positive returned HLS constant.

The concrete ratio-lower premise can itself be obtained from uniform
finite-volume convergence.  The theorem
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_ratio\_lower\_of\_uniform\_correlation\_limit}
is the scalar bridge: if \(c_n\to c_\infty\) uniformly on the interval,
\(C\le c_\infty(\beta)\) with \(C>0\), the denominator is positive, and
\((h(\beta))^{2\alpha}\le H\) with \(H>0\), then eventually and uniformly
\[
  (C/2)/H \le c_n(\beta)/(h(\beta))^{2\alpha}.
\]
The lattice beta-interval specialization
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_ratio\_lower\_of\_uniform\_correlation\_on\_beta\_interval}
uses the existing high-temperature Dini theorem
\texttt{correlationAlongExhaustion\_tendstoUniformlyOn\_beta}.  The
self-interval form
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_ratio\_lower\_of\_uniform\_correlation\_on\_self\_Icc}
uses \([\beta_1,\beta_2]\) itself as the compact high-temperature interval,
deriving \(0<\beta_1\) and \(\beta_2J(2d)<1\) from the interval inclusion.
The direct
assemblies
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_upper\_bound\_of\_high\_temp\_uniform\_correlation\_lower}
and
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_sandwich\_of\_high\_temp\_uniform\_correlation\_lower}
feed this \(L\) into the high-temperature ratio-lower package above; the
matching self-interval wrappers
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_upper\_bound\_of\_uniform\_correlation\_lower\_on\_self\_Icc}
and
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_sandwich\_of\_uniform\_correlation\_lower\_on\_self\_Icc}
remove the auxiliary \(a,b\) shell from the same assembly.  Thus the finite
ratio-lower premise has been reduced to interval positivity and upper bounds
for the infinite correlation and the pseudo-mass denominator.

The compact-bounds layer supplies those interval constants from closed-interval
continuity.  The helpers
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_compact\_pos\_lower\_bound\_on\_Icc} and
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_compact\_pow\_upper\_bound\_on\_Icc} are the scalar
extreme-value steps: a continuous positive function on \([\beta_1,\beta_2]\)
has a positive lower bound, and a continuous nonnegative denominator profile
has a positive upper bound for \(h(\beta)^{2\alpha}\).  The lattice wrapper
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_compact\_ratio\_bounds\_on\_beta\_interval} applies
\texttt{correlationInfinite\_continuousOn\_beta\_of\_high\_temp} to the
infinite correlation and packages the resulting \(C,H\).  The assemblies
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_upper\_bound\_of\_high\_temp\_compact\_ratio\_bounds}
and
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_sandwich\_of\_high\_temp\_compact\_ratio\_bounds}
feed these compact witnesses into the uniform-correlation lower package.

The pseudo-mass Lipschitz layer has been adjusted to the locality actually
needed by differentiation.  Theorems
\texttt{pseudoMass\_deriv\_formula},
\texttt{pseudoMass\_deriv\_abs\_le},
\texttt{pseudoMass\_power\_deriv\_le},
\texttt{pseudoMass\_pow\_succ\_deriv\_bound}, and
\texttt{pseudoMass\_pow\_succ\_lipschitz} no longer require a global identity
\(\texttt{pseudoMassG}\,\alpha\,r\,(h\beta)=c(\beta)\).  They require the
identity as a neighborhood-local \texttt{EventuallyEq} at the differentiated
point, and at every point of the closed interval for the MVT package.  The
finite and infinite Lemma~17.5.2 Lipschitz wrappers carry the same weaker
premise.
This is the shape needed for the concrete
\texttt{pseudoMassFromParamsAtPair} profile, where the defining equation is
available on neighborhoods whose correlations stay inside \((0,2)\), not on
the entire real line.

The high-temperature upper-bound packages carry the same localized premise.
In particular, the enlarged finite-HLS package, the high-temperature
ratio-lower package, the uniform-correlation lower package, and the compact
ratio-bounds package all ask for the defining equation as an interval-local
\texttt{EventuallyEq} at each point of \([\beta_1,\beta_2]\).  Thus the
capstone wrappers no longer reintroduce a global equality after the MVT layer
has been localized.

The concrete local-equation package provides this bridge.  The helper
\texttt{pseudoMassG\_pseudoMassExt\_eventuallyEq\_of\_eventually\_mem}
proves the totalized pseudo-mass defining equation locally whenever a scalar
profile stays in \(\texttt{Ioo}\,0\,2\) near the point.  The beta-profile
wrappers
\texttt{pseudoMassFromParamsAtPair\_beta\_pseudoMassG\_eventuallyEq\_of\_corr\_eventually\_mem},
\texttt{pseudoMassFromParamsAtPair\_beta\_pseudoMassG\_eventuallyEq\_of\_corr\_continuousAt},
and
\texttt{pseudoMassFromParamsAtPair\_beta\_pseudoMassG\_eventuallyEq\_on\_Icc\_of\_corr\_continuousAt}
specialize it to the infinite-volume correlation profile and package the
interval-local \texttt{EventuallyEq} premise needed by the localized MVT
step.

The concrete regularity package supplies the matching \(h\)-profile inputs.
Theorems
\texttt{pseudoMassFromParamsAtPair\_beta\_continuousAt\_of\_corr\_continuousAt},
\texttt{pseudoMassFromParamsAtPair\_beta\_differentiableAt\_of\_corr\_differentiableAt},
\texttt{pseudoMassFromParamsAtPair\_beta\_hasDerivAt\_deriv\_of\_corr\_differentiableAt},
\texttt{pseudoMassFromParamsAtPair\_beta\_continuousOn\_Icc\_of\_corr\_continuousAt},
and
\texttt{pseudoMassFromParamsAtPair\_beta\_hasDerivAt\_deriv\_on\_Icc\_of\_corr\_differentiableAt}
transport pointwise and interval regularity of the infinite correlation
profile through \texttt{pseudoMassExt} on the active range.  These are the
forms required by the compact ratio-bounds and localized MVT packages when
\(h(\beta)\) is the concrete \texttt{pseudoMassFromParamsAtPair} profile.
The derivative-formula theorems
\texttt{pseudoMassFromParamsAtPair\_beta\_deriv\_formula\_of\_corr\_hasDerivAt}
and
\texttt{pseudoMassFromParamsAtPair\_beta\_deriv\_formula\_on\_Icc\_of\_corr\_differentiableAt}
then apply the local defining equation and
\texttt{pseudoMass\_deriv\_formula} to identify \(\partial_\beta m^-\) with
the infinite-correlation beta derivative divided by the explicit
\(\texttt{pseudoMassG}\) derivative at \(m^-\).
The HLS derivative handoffs
\texttt{pseudoMassFromParamsAtPair\_beta\_power\_deriv\_le\_of\_corr\_hasDerivAt},
\texttt{pseudoMassFromParamsAtPair\_beta\_pow\_succ\_deriv\_bound\_of\_corr\_hasDerivAt},
and
\texttt{pseudoMassFromParamsAtPair\_beta\_pow\_succ\_deriv\_bound\_on\_Icc\_of\_corr\_differentiableAt}
then turn the infinite-correlation derivative denominator comparison into the
concrete bounds
\((m^-)^{2\alpha}|\partial_\beta m^-|\le K/r\) and
\(|\partial_\beta (m^-)^{2\alpha+1}|\le (2\alpha+1)K/r\).
The interval theorem
\texttt{pseudoMassFromParamsAtPair\_beta\_pow\_succ\_lipschitz\_on\_Icc\_of\_corr\_differentiableAt}
then applies the localized MVT package to obtain the corresponding Lipschitz
estimate for \(\beta\mapsto (m^-)^{2\alpha+1}\) on a closed beta interval.

The concrete compact package then consumes these bridges.  Theorems
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_concrete\_pseudoMass\_compact\_ratio\_bounds\_on\_beta\_interval},
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_upper\_bound\_of\_concrete\_pseudoMass\_compact\_ratio\_bounds},
and
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_sandwich\_of\_concrete\_pseudoMass\_compact\_ratio\_bounds}
instantiate the compact ratio-bounds and high-temperature upper/sandwich
packages with the concrete \texttt{pseudoMassFromParamsAtPair} beta profile.
The capstone wrappers
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_capstone\_of\_concrete\_pseudoMass\_compact\_ratio\_bounds}
and
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_capstone\_of\_concrete\_pseudoMass\_compact\_ratio\_bounds\_on\_self\_Icc}
return the same HLS convolution witness, named upper-bound predicate, and
displayed sandwich for one constant \(K\).
The active-range hypothesis for the infinite correlation provides the
pseudo-mass positivity and local defining equations, while the derivative-limit
hypothesis provides the MVT-ready derivative input.
The helper
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_interval\_endpoints\_of\_Icc\_subset\_high\_temp}
extracts \(0<\beta_1\), \(0<\beta_2\), and \(\beta_2J\,2d<1\) from
\([\beta_1,\beta_2]\subset(0,(J\,2d)^{-1})\).  Consequently the
\texttt{\_on\_self\_Icc} compact-package variants take the closed beta
interval itself as the auxiliary compact interval \(a=\beta_1\), \(b=\beta_2\).

The high-temperature sandwich package removes the lower-side decay premise in
two endpoint forms.  The theorem
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_sandwich\_of\_concrete\_pseudoMass\_compact\_bounds\_and\_le\_high\_temp\_rate}
uses the comparison
\[
  \texttt{pseudoMassFromParamsAtPair}(\beta_2)
  \le -\log(\beta_2 J\,2d)
\]
to generate the validating exponential-decay input through the existing
high-temperature transfer bridge.  The companion
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_sandwich\_of\_concrete\_pseudoMass\_compact\_bounds\_and\_profile\_lower}
derives the same lower-side input from the endpoint profile inequality
\[
  \texttt{pseudoMassG}\,\alpha\,\rho\,(-\log(\beta_2 J\,2d))
  \le \mathrm{corr}_\infty(\beta_2).
\]
Both wrappers then reuse the concrete compact upper-bound theorem and return
the same two-sided sandwich for \(\mathrm{latticeMass}\).
The capstone variants
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_capstone\_of\_concrete\_pseudoMass\_compact\_bounds\_and\_le\_high\_temp\_rate}
and
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_capstone\_of\_concrete\_pseudoMass\_compact\_bounds\_and\_profile\_lower},
together with their \texttt{\_on\_Icc} and \texttt{\_on\_self\_Icc} forms,
repackage these sandwiches with the matching named
\texttt{Lemma\_17\_5\_2\_UpperBound} predicate for the same HLS constant
\(K\).  Thus downstream callers can consume the general concrete
high-temperature package in the same-constant capstone shape as the
anchored cubic wrappers below.
The helper
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_endpoint\_high\_temp\_of\_Icc\_subset\_high\_temp}
extracts \(0<\beta_2\) and \(\beta_2J\,2d<1\) from the interval inclusion
\([\beta_1,\beta_2]\subset(0,(J\,2d)^{-1})\).  The \texttt{\_on\_Icc}
variants of the two sandwich wrappers use this helper, so callers do not need
to restate those endpoint scalar hypotheses separately.
The corresponding \texttt{\_on\_self\_Icc} variants also use the beta interval
itself as the compact auxiliary interval, removing the remaining \(a,b\) and
membership-shell hypotheses from the high-temperature sandwich callers.

The active-range premise for these concrete capstones can now be supplied
internally in the high-temperature pair case.  The theorem
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_active\_range\_of\_high\_temp\_pair}
uses the GJ \S17.5 path lower bound
\[
  \tanh(\beta J)^{\operatorname{dist}(0,z-x)}
  \le
  \langle\sigma_0\sigma_{z-x}\rangle_\infty(\beta)
\]
together with translation invariance and exhaustion-independence to show
\(\langle\sigma_x\sigma_z\rangle_\infty(\beta)>0\) for \(x\ne z\),
\(J>0\), and \(\beta>0\).  The upper side
\(\langle\sigma_x\sigma_z\rangle_\infty(\beta)<2\) is the unconditional
bound \(\mathrm{corr}_\infty\le 1<2\).  Its interval form
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_active\_range\_on\_Icc\_of\_high\_temp\_pair}
uses \([\beta_1,\beta_2]\subset(0,(J\,2d)^{-1})\) to obtain the active range
for every point of the compact beta interval.  The compact-ratio wrappers
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_upper\_bound\_of\_concrete\_pseudoMass\_compact\_ratio\_bounds\_\{,on\_self\_Icc\}\_auto\_active}
and
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_sandwich\_of\_concrete\_pseudoMass\_compact\_ratio\_bounds\_\{,on\_self\_Icc\}\_auto\_active}
feed this interval active range into the concrete compact-ratio upper and
sandwich packages.  The matching capstone wrappers
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_capstone\_of\_concrete\_pseudoMass\_compact\_ratio\_bounds\_\{,on\_self\_Icc\}\_auto\_active}
return the same HLS convolution witness, named upper-bound predicate, and
displayed sandwich while deriving the active range in the same way.  The
shorter high-temperature sandwich wrappers
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_sandwich\_le\_high\_temp\_rate\_\{,on\_Icc,on\_self\_Icc\}\_auto\_active}
and
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_sandwich\_profile\_lower\_\{,on\_Icc,on\_self\_Icc\}\_auto\_active}
do the same for the rate-comparison and profile-lower sandwich layer.  The four
\texttt{\_auto\_active} capstones then feed this interval active range into the
rate-comparison and profile-lower high-temperature capstones, so the remaining
external analytic input is again the derivative-profile locally uniform
convergence.

The derivative-limit input is also available in a provider-shaped form.  The
predicate \texttt{Lemma\_17\_5\_2\_DerivativeLimitProvider} states that the
finite-volume beta-derivative profiles converge locally uniformly on
\((0,(J\,2d)^{-1})\) to some limiting derivative profile.  Its entry-point
package contains ten wrappers, all with suffix
\texttt{\_of\_derivative\_limit\_provider}.  Two wrappers feed the witness into
the automatic-active compact upper-bound layer, four wrappers feed it into the
rate-comparison and profile-lower sandwich layer, and four wrappers feed it
into the matching high-temperature capstone layer.  Thus upper-bound,
sandwich, and capstone callers no longer need to name the limiting derivative
function \(g'\) directly; the substantive analytic work is proving the provider
itself in the desired high-temperature regime.

The Cauchy/criteria layer also extracts closed-interval consequences from the
provider.  The lemmas
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_derivative\_limit\_provider\_tendstoUniformlyOn\_Icc},
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_derivative\_limit\_provider\_uniformCauchySeqOn\_Icc},
and
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_derivative\_limit\_provider\_metricCauchy\_on\_Icc}
turn this local-uniform provider into closed-interval uniform convergence,
uniform Cauchy, and epsilon--\(N\) metric-Cauchy consequences while keeping
the core provider module free of the metric-Cauchy import.

The provider proof has a Dini-style criterion.  If the finite-volume
beta-derivative profiles converge pointwise on \((0,(J\,2d)^{-1})\) to a
continuous limiting derivative profile, each derivative profile is continuous
on that interval, and the profiles are stagewise monotone (or antitone), then
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_derivative\_limit\_provider\_of\_monotone\_deriv\_profiles}
and its antitone analogue upgrade the pointwise convergence to the required
locally uniform convergence by Dini's theorem.  This reduces the remaining
provider proof to concrete derivative-profile continuity, monotonicity, and
pointwise-convergence inputs.

The same provider also has compact-uniform-Cauchy criteria.  If the finite
beta-derivative profiles are uniformly Cauchy on every compact subset of
\((0,(J\,2d)^{-1})\), and converge pointwise there to \(g'\), then the
standard uniform-Cauchy theorem gives uniform convergence on each compact
subset.  The closed-interval metric-Cauchy route now also constructs the
pointwise limit itself: for each \(\beta\), completeness of \(\mathbb{R}\)
turns the Cauchy derivative-value sequence into a limit \(g'(\beta)\).
Equivalently, it is enough to verify the Cauchy property on every closed
interval contained in the high-temperature interval: around each \(\beta\) one
chooses
\([\,\beta/2,(\beta+(J\,2d)^{-1})/2\,]\subset (0,(J\,2d)^{-1})\), obtaining
the local neighborhood required for locally uniform convergence.  The Lean
criteria are
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_derivative\_limit\_provider\_of\_uniformCauchy\_on\_compacts},
its metric epsilon--eventual form, and the corresponding
\texttt{\_on\_Icc} closed-interval variants, together with
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_derivative\_limit\_provider\_of\_metricCauchy\_on\_Icc\_complete}.
These statements provide a non-Dini route to the same provider, suitable for
future derivative-profile Cauchy estimates.  Conversely,
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_named\_metricCauchy\_on\_Icc\_of\_derivative\_limit\_provider}
feeds any established provider back into the named closed-interval Cauchy
input, so downstream code can switch between the provider-shaped and
Cauchy-shaped abstractions without reopening the limiting profile.

The closed-interval metric-Cauchy hypothesis is in turn discharged from a
concrete convergence-rate bound on consecutive finite-volume stages.  Suppose
there is a summable sequence \(c:\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{R}\) such that, on every
closed interval \([\beta_1,\beta_2]\subset(0,(J\,2d)^{-1})\) and for every
\(\beta\) there, \(|F_{k+1}(\beta)-F_k(\beta)|\le c_k\), where
\(F_n(\beta)=\partial_\beta\langle\sigma_x\sigma_z\rangle_{\Lambda_n}(\beta)\).
For \(m\le n\) the polygon inequality gives
\(|F_m(\beta)-F_n(\beta)|\le\sum_{k=m}^{n-1}c_k=S_n-S_m\) with
\(S_N=\sum_{k<N}c_k\); since \(c\) is summable the partial sums \((S_N)\) are
Cauchy, so \(S_n-S_m\le|S_n-S_m|<\varepsilon\) uniformly in \(\beta\) once
\(m,n\ge N\).  Hence the finite-volume derivative profiles are uniformly Cauchy
on each closed interval and the provider follows.  Only an eventual bound is
needed: if the increment estimate holds for all \(k\ge N_0\), raising the
metric-Cauchy threshold to \(\max(N,N_0)\) keeps every telescoped index at or
above \(N_0\), so the finitely many uncontrolled head increments never enter.
This eventual form matches the actual finite-volume structure, since for a fixed
pair \(\{x,z\}\) the derivative profiles vanish before the exhaustion stage
contains the pair, so any concrete convergence-rate control begins only past a
containment/cluster onset stage.  The Lean criteria are
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_derivative\_limit\_provider\_of\_summable\_increments\_eventually}
and its all-stage (\(N_0=0\)) corollary
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_derivative\_limit\_provider\_of\_summable\_increments};
the geometric specialization
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_derivative\_limit\_provider\_of\_geometric\_increments}
takes \(c_k=M\,\rho^k\) with \(0\le\rho<1\), matching the
geometric (exponential) finite-to-infinite convergence rate produced by
cluster-expansion estimates, with summability supplied by the geometric-series
criterion.

The increment structure localizes the convergence-rate task to the covered
stages.  For a fixed pair \(\{x,z\}\) the finite \(\beta\)-derivative profile
vanishes before the exhaustion stage contains the pair, so by monotonicity of
the exhaustion the consecutive increment \(F_{k+1}-F_k\) vanishes whenever stage
\(k+1\) does not yet contain \(\{x,z\}\)
(\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_finite\_derivative\_increment\_eq\_zero\_of\_not\_subset}).
Since \texttt{Exhaustion.exhaust} guarantees that all stages past some onset
index are covered, a summable bound on the increments over the covered stages
already supplies the eventual hypothesis above
(\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_derivative\_limit\_provider\_of\_summable\_increments\_on\_covered\_stages}),
with no need to locate the onset index.  The increment magnitude is controlled
uniformly: on a closed high-temperature interval the triangle inequality applied
to the all-stage finite derivative bound gives
\(|F_{k+1}-F_k|\le 2\,(J\,M^2+4dJ)\) uniformly in the stage \(k\), with
\(M=b\,J\,2d/(1-b\,J\,2d)\) for the auxiliary upper end \(b\)
(\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_finite\_derivative\_increment\_abs\_le\_high\_temp\_on\_Icc\_all\_stages});
the self-interval form
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_finite\_derivative\_increment\_abs\_le\_high\_temp\_on\_self\_Icc}
specializes to \(b=\beta_2\).
This uniform bound is not yet summable; sharpening it to a summable (geometric)
bound from the finite-volume convergence rate is the substantive remaining
input.  At the baseline correlation level the
increment series is automatically summable by monotone convergence: the
consecutive correlation differences are nonnegative by GKS
(\texttt{correlationAlongExhaustion\_increment\_nonneg}) and their partial sums
telescope to \(\langle\sigma_x\sigma_z\rangle_{\Lambda_n}-\langle\sigma_x\sigma_z\rangle_{\Lambda_0}\),
bounded above by
\(\langle\sigma_x\sigma_z\rangle^\infty-\langle\sigma_x\sigma_z\rangle_{\Lambda_0}\),
hence the series is summable
(\texttt{correlationAlongExhaustion\_increment\_summable}).
The convergence tail \(\langle\sigma_x\sigma_z\rangle^\infty-\langle\sigma_x\sigma_z\rangle_{\Lambda_n}\)
is nonnegative
(\texttt{correlationInfinite\_sub\_correlationAlongExhaustion\_nonneg}), antitone
in \(n\) by GKS
(\texttt{correlationInfinite\_sub\_correlationAlongExhaustion\_antitone}), and
tends to \(0\)
(\texttt{tendsto\_correlationInfinite\_sub\_correlationAlongExhaustion\_zero});
it is the quantity whose decay rate is the substantive remaining input; the
increment series sums telescopically to it,
\(\sum_n(\langle\sigma_x\sigma_z\rangle_{\Lambda_{n+1}}-\langle\sigma_x\sigma_z\rangle_{\Lambda_n})
=\langle\sigma_x\sigma_z\rangle^\infty-\langle\sigma_x\sigma_z\rangle_{\Lambda_0}\)
(\texttt{correlationAlongExhaustion\_increment\_tsum\_eq}), and the stage-\(n\)
tail is the increment tail-sum
\(\langle\sigma_x\sigma_z\rangle^\infty-\langle\sigma_x\sigma_z\rangle_{\Lambda_n}
=\sum_k(\langle\sigma_x\sigma_z\rangle_{\Lambda_{k+n+1}}-\langle\sigma_x\sigma_z\rangle_{\Lambda_{k+n}})\)
(\texttt{correlationAlongExhaustion\_tail\_eq\_tsum\_increment\_shift}), so a
quantitative bound on the increments from stage \(n\) controls the tail's decay:
if the increments are dominated by a summable \(g\), then
\(\langle\sigma_x\sigma_z\rangle^\infty-\langle\sigma_x\sigma_z\rangle_{\Lambda_n}
\le\sum_k g(k+n)\)
(\texttt{correlationInfinite\_sub\_correlationAlongExhaustion\_le\_tsum\_shift\_of\_increment\_le},
the Phase A\(\to\)C bridge of Issue \#2965); its geometric specialization gives the
explicit rate
\(\langle\sigma_x\sigma_z\rangle^\infty-\langle\sigma_x\sigma_z\rangle_{\Lambda_n}
\le M\,\mathrm{ratio}^n/(1-\mathrm{ratio})\) from
\(c_{k+1}-c_k\le M\,\mathrm{ratio}^k\)
(\texttt{correlationInfinite\_sub\_correlationAlongExhaustion\_le\_geometric}), and
the finite-volume correlations themselves satisfy the geometric Cauchy rate
\(c_n-c_m\le M\,\mathrm{ratio}^m/(1-\mathrm{ratio})\)
(\texttt{correlationAlongExhaustion\_sub\_le\_geometric}).
Obtaining the analogous quantitative summable bound for the \(\beta\)-derivative
increments is the substantive remaining convergence-rate input.  The
single-vertex Simon--Lieb building blocks are in place: for a non-adjacent pair
\(i\neq j\) on \(\mathbb{Z}^d\), if every neighbour \(k\) of \(i\) satisfies
\(\langle\sigma_k\sigma_j\rangle^\infty\le C\) with \(C\ge0\), then the
Simon--Lieb peeling inequality and the degree bound
\(\deg i\le 2d\) give \(\langle\sigma_i\sigma_j\rangle^\infty\le\beta J\,2d\,C\)
(\texttt{correlationInfinite\_latticeGraph\_le\_of\_neighbors\_le}); taking
\(C=1\) gives \(\langle\sigma_i\sigma_j\rangle^\infty\le\beta J\,2d\) for every
non-adjacent pair under \(0\le\beta J\)
(\texttt{correlationInfinite\_latticeGraph\_le\_betaJ\_two\_d\_of\_not\_adj}), a
contraction factor \(<1\) in the strict high-temperature regime
\(0\le\beta J\,2d<1\).  The second iterate
(\texttt{correlationInfinite\_latticeGraph\_le\_betaJ\_two\_d\_sq\_of\_dist\_ge\_three})
shows naive peeling \emph{does} iterate once the separation is large enough: for
\(\mathrm{dist}(i,j)\ge3\) every neighbour \(k\) of \(i\) has
\(\mathrm{dist}(k,j)\ge\mathrm{dist}(i,j)-1\ge2\), hence is non-adjacent to \(j\) and
obeys \(\langle\sigma_k\sigma_j\rangle^\infty\le\beta J\,2d\), giving
\(\langle\sigma_i\sigma_j\rangle^\infty\le(\beta J\,2d)^2\).  Iterating fully,
\texttt{correlationInfinite\_latticeGraph\_le\_betaJ\_two\_d\_pow\_of\_dist\_gt} gives
\(\langle\sigma_i\sigma_j\rangle^\infty\le(\beta J\,2d)^n\) whenever
\(\mathrm{dist}(i,j)\ge n+1\), i.e.\ \((\beta J\,2d)^{\mathrm{dist}(i,j)-1}\) ---
prefactor-free geometric decay with explicit base \(\beta J\,2d\) (no contraction-factor
abstraction and no ball-boundary shell axiom), by induction on \(n\) (base \(n=0\) bounds
by \(1\); each step peels via \texttt{\_le\_of\_neighbors\_le} with \(C=(\beta J\,2d)^n\)).
Rewriting \((\beta J\,2d)^{\mathrm{dist}-1}=(1/\beta J\,2d)\exp(\log(\beta J\,2d)\,\mathrm{dist})\),
\texttt{hasExponentialDecay\_latticeGraph\_of\_betaJ\_two\_d\_lt\_one} packages this (for
\(\beta,J>0\), \(d\ge1\), \(\beta J\,2d<1\)) as both \(0<-\log(\beta J\,2d)\) and
\(\mathrm{HasExponentialDecay}\,d\,(\mathrm{cubicExhaustion}\,d)\,\langle J,0,\beta\rangle\,
(-\log(\beta J\,2d))\) --- an axiom-free high-temperature mass gap under the elementary
condition \(\beta J\,2d<1\) (no \((2(r+1)+1)^d\) boundary factor, no shell-contraction
axiom), at the cost of the cruder per-step base.  Composing with
\texttt{latticeMass\_ge\_of\_HasExponentialDecay}, \texttt{latticeMass\_ge\_of\_betaJ\_two\_d\_lt\_one}
gives the axiom-free lower bound
\(\mathrm{ENNReal.ofReal}(-\log(\beta J\,2d))\le\mathrm{latticeMass}\,d\,(\mathrm{cubicExhaustion}\,d)\,\langle J,0,\beta\rangle\),
and \texttt{latticeMass\_pos\_of\_betaJ\_two\_d\_lt\_one} the axiom-free positive mass gap
\(0<\mathrm{latticeMass}\), both under the elementary \(\beta J\,2d<1\).
\texttt{correlationInfinite\_latticeGraph\_susceptibility\_summable\_betaJ\_two\_d} likewise
gives the axiom-free finite susceptibility \(\chi=\sum_y\langle\sigma_0\sigma_y\rangle^\infty<\infty\)
under \(\beta J\,2d<1\): the decay \((\beta J\,2d)^{\mathrm{dist}-1}\) is dominated by the
summable majorant \((\beta J\,2d)^{\lfloor\mathrm{dist}/2\rfloor}\) (via
\(\lfloor\mathrm{dist}/2\rfloor\le\mathrm{dist}-1\)), the \(y=0\) term \(\le1\).  By
translation invariance the basepoint form
\texttt{correlationInfinite\_latticeGraph\_susceptibility\_summable\_betaJ\_two\_d\_basepoint}
gives the same axiom-free finite susceptibility from any basepoint \(x\).
\texttt{correlationInfinite\_latticeGraph\_uniform\_decay\_of\_betaJ\_two\_d\_lt\_one} records
the axiom-free uniform clustering: for every \(\varepsilon>0\) there is \(R\) with
\(\langle\sigma_i\sigma_j\rangle^\infty\le\varepsilon\) whenever \(\mathrm{dist}(i,j)\ge R\)
(via \texttt{exists\_pow\_lt\_of\_lt\_one} on \((\beta J\,2d)^{\mathrm{dist}-1}\)).  Its
topological form
\texttt{correlationInfinite\_latticeGraph\_tendsto\_cofinite\_zero\_of\_betaJ\_two\_d\_lt\_one}
states that \(y\mapsto\langle\sigma_0\sigma_y\rangle^\infty\) tends to \(0\) along the
cofinite filter (the \(C_0\) clustering property), via finiteness of lattice balls.
Finally \texttt{summable\_truncated2Infinite\_prod\_of\_betaJ\_two\_d\_lt\_one} gives the
axiom-free boundary two-point convolution summability
\(\sum_z\langle\sigma_x\sigma_z\rangle^\infty\langle\sigma_y\sigma_z\rangle^\infty<\infty\)
(the GJ §17.5 boundary-sum ingredient for the convergence-rate coupling, Issue \#2965
Phase B), composing the axiom-free decay with the generic
\texttt{summable\_truncated2Infinite\_prod\_of\_hasExponentialDecay}.  The quantitative form
\texttt{tsum\_truncated2Infinite\_prod\_decay\_of\_betaJ\_two\_d\_lt\_one} strengthens this to
exponential decay of the convolution in \(\mathrm{dist}(x,y)\):
\(\sum_z\langle\sigma_x\sigma_z\rangle^\infty\langle\sigma_y\sigma_z\rangle^\infty
\le C\exp(-(\mathrm{rate}/2)\,\mathrm{dist}(x,y)/2)\) with
\(\mathrm{rate}=-\log(\beta J\,2d)\), via the Step 127 bound
\texttt{tsum\_truncated2Infinite\_prod\_le}.  The partial-sum form
\texttt{truncated2Infinite\_prod\_finset\_sum\_decay\_of\_betaJ\_two\_d\_lt\_one} extends this
to the convolution over any finite vertex set \(S\) (uniformly in the separating surface),
via \texttt{Summable.sum\_le\_tsum}, completing the axiom-free mirror of the shell-based
high-temperature package under \(\beta J\,2d<1\).
The same reverse-triangle step continues while the separation from \(j\) stays \(\ge2\);
the obstruction is the
distance-\(2\) base case (a neighbour can be \emph{adjacent} to \(j\), bounded only by
\(1\), with no \(\le\beta J\,2d\) bound for adjacent pairs), which caps the gain near
\(j\).  A clean prefactor-free
exponential distance decay instead requires the separating-surface
(ball-boundary) Simon--Lieb argument applied to cubic shells.  That spatial decay
is in fact already available via the shell iteration
\texttt{shellSup\_iterated\_bound}; its pointwise form
\texttt{correlationInfinite\_latticeGraph\_le\_contractionFactor\_pow\_dist} gives
\(\langle\sigma_0\sigma_y\rangle^\infty\le(\text{contractionFactor})^{\lfloor\mathrm{dist}(0,y)/(r+2)\rfloor}\)
for every \(y\neq0\), prefactor-free with contraction factor \(<1\); by
translation invariance this extends to every distinct pair,
\(\langle\sigma_i\sigma_j\rangle^\infty\le(\text{contractionFactor})^{\lfloor\mathrm{dist}(i,j)/(r+2)\rfloor}\)
(\texttt{correlationInfinite\_latticeGraph\_le\_contractionFactor\_pow\_dist\_pair}),
which yields uniform clustering: for every \(\varepsilon>0\) there is \(R\) with
\(\langle\sigma_i\sigma_j\rangle^\infty\le\varepsilon\) whenever
\(\mathrm{dist}(i,j)\ge R\)
(\texttt{correlationInfinite\_latticeGraph\_uniform\_decay\_of\_contractionFactor\_lt\_one}),
equivalently \(y\mapsto\langle\sigma_0\sigma_y\rangle^\infty\) tends to \(0\) along
the cofinite filter (the \(C_0\)/clustering property,
\texttt{correlationInfinite\_latticeGraph\_tendsto\_cofinite\_zero}).  When
\(0<\text{contractionFactor}<1\) the kernel is moreover summable over
\(\mathbb{Z}^d\), giving a finite susceptibility
\(\chi=\sum_y\langle\sigma_0\sigma_y\rangle^\infty<\infty\)
(\texttt{correlationInfinite\_latticeGraph\_susceptibility\_summable}): the
per-pair decay is dominated by \((1/\text{cf})\,e^{-m\,\mathrm{dist}}\) with
\(m=-\log(\text{cf})/(r+2)>0\), summable by \texttt{summable\_exp\_neg\_dist}; by
translation invariance the same finiteness holds from any basepoint
(\texttt{correlationInfinite\_latticeGraph\_susceptibility\_summable\_basepoint}).
The ball-boundary separating edge set has at most the edge count of the cube
\(\mathrm{cubicBox}\,d\,(r+1)\) (being a filtered image of its edge finset,
\texttt{latticeBallBoundaryEdges\_card\_le}), bounding the size of the Simon--Lieb
separating surface, and the contraction factor itself is bounded,
\(\text{contractionFactor}\,d\,\Lambda\,p\,r\le\beta J\cdot 2\,|\partial E_r|\)
(each boundary-edge summand \(\langle\sigma_0\sigma_k\rangle^\infty+\langle\sigma_0\sigma_l\rangle^\infty\le2\),
\texttt{contractionFactor\_le\_card}).  Chaining these with the cube edge count and
cardinality gives the explicit volume-independent bound
\(\text{contractionFactor}\,d\,\Lambda\,p\,r\le\beta J\cdot2\,(d\,(2(r+1)+1)^d)\)
(\texttt{contractionFactor\_le\_high\_temp\_const}), so when
\(\beta J\cdot2\,(d\,(2(r+1)+1)^d)<1\) the contraction factor is \(<1\)
\emph{unconditionally}, with no polynomial-decay hypothesis
(\texttt{contractionFactor\_lt\_one\_of\_high\_temp}), discharging the
spatial-decay/susceptibility layer's hypothesis in an explicit strong
high-temperature regime.  Composing with the clustering lemmas gives
unconditional uniform large-distance decay
(\texttt{correlationInfinite\_latticeGraph\_uniform\_decay\_of\_high\_temp}) and
cofinite vanishing
(\texttt{correlationInfinite\_latticeGraph\_tendsto\_cofinite\_zero\_of\_high\_temp})
directly from the explicit high-temperature condition, with no polynomial-decay
hypothesis.  Moreover the fully explicit prefactor-free geometric decay
\(\langle\sigma_i\sigma_j\rangle^\infty\le H^{\lfloor\mathrm{dist}(i,j)/(r+2)\rfloor}\)
with base \(H=\beta J\cdot2\,(d\,(2(r+1)+1)^d)<1\) holds with no contraction
factor in the statement and no polynomial-decay hypothesis (the ball-boundary
shell-contraction axiom \texttt{shellSup\_contraction} is still used)
(\texttt{correlationInfinite\_latticeGraph\_le\_explicit\_pow\_dist}), via
\(\text{cf}\le H\) and base monotonicity.  Consequently, under \(0<\beta J\) and
\(H<1\), the susceptibility is finite unconditionally
(\texttt{correlationInfinite\_latticeGraph\_susceptibility\_summable\_high\_temp}):
the explicit decay dominates the kernel by the summable \(H^{\lfloor\mathrm{dist}/(r+2)\rfloor}\)
(\texttt{summable\_pow\_div\_latticeDistance}, the reusable \(0<c<1\) floor-power
lattice-summability core), needing no polynomial-decay hypothesis and no
contraction-factor positivity; by translation invariance the same unconditional
finiteness holds from any basepoint
(\texttt{correlationInfinite\_latticeGraph\_susceptibility\_summable\_high\_temp\_basepoint}).
The explicit decay also upgrades, at \(h=0\), to the named
\(\mathrm{HasExponentialDecay}\) predicate with the positive rate
\(-\log(H)/(r+2)\) and witness \(C=1/H\)
(\texttt{hasExponentialDecay\_latticeGraph\_of\_high\_temp}): a positive mass gap
holds unconditionally for \(\beta,J>0\) and \(H<1\) (no polynomial-decay
hypothesis), connecting the ball-boundary spatial decay to the established decay
predicate used by the lower-bound machinery.  Composing with
\texttt{latticeMass\_ge\_of\_HasExponentialDecay} yields the explicit
unconditional bounds
\(\mathrm{ofReal}(-\log(H)/(r+2))\le\mathrm{latticeMass}\,d\,\Lambda\,\langle J,0,\beta\rangle\)
(\texttt{latticeMass\_ge\_explicit\_of\_high\_temp}) and
\(0<\mathrm{latticeMass}\) (\texttt{latticeMass\_pos\_of\_high\_temp\_mass\_gap}),
connecting the mass gap to the central Lemma~17.5.2 quantity.  Composing the mass
gap with the Step~127 convolution summability gives, unconditionally at high
temperature, the boundary-product summability
\(\sum_z\langle\sigma_x\sigma_z\rangle^\infty\langle\sigma_y\sigma_z\rangle^\infty<\infty\)
(\texttt{summable\_truncated2Infinite\_prod\_of\_high\_temp}); via Step~127's
quantitative bound this strengthens to exponential decay of the convolution in
\(\mathrm{dist}(x,y)\),
\(\sum_z\langle\sigma_x\sigma_z\rangle^\infty\langle\sigma_y\sigma_z\rangle^\infty
\le C\,e^{-(\mathrm{rate}/2)\,\mathrm{dist}(x,y)/2}\)
(\texttt{tsum\_truncated2Infinite\_prod\_decay\_of\_high\_temp}); the same bound
holds for the partial sum over any finite vertex set, uniformly in the choice of
separating surface
(\texttt{truncated2Infinite\_prod\_finset\_sum\_decay\_of\_high\_temp}), the
boundary-sum ingredients for the finite-volume\(\to\)infinite-volume
convergence-rate coupling (Issue \#2965).
The remaining gap is instead the distinct \emph{volume-convergence} rate of the
