finite-volume \(\beta\)-derivative increments, related to this spatial decay by
the boundary-effect estimate.  Its geometric foundation is in place: a vertex lying in the stage-\(n+1\) cube but not the
stage-\(n\) cube has a coordinate of absolute value exactly \(n+1\)
(\texttt{exists\_coord\_natAbs\_eq\_of\_mem\_cubicBox\_succ\_not\_mem}), so for a
reference point \(p\) in the radius-\(R\) cube and a fresh stage-\(n+1\) vertex
\(w\) with \(R\le n\), the \(\ell^1\) lattice distance obeys
\(n+1-R\le\mathrm{latticeDistance}(p,w)\)
(\texttt{latticeDistance\_ge\_of\_mem\_cubicBox\_succ\_not\_mem}).  A vertex adjacent to a fresh vertex stays at distance at least \(n-R\)
(\texttt{latticeDistance\_ge\_of\_adj\_mem\_cubicBox\_succ\_not\_mem}), since
adjacency is unit \(\ell^1\)-distance, so the bound carries to the edges touching
the boundary.  Fresh exhaustion vertices and their incident edges thus recede
from any fixed pair at unit speed in the exhaustion stage, which is the
boundary-distance growth driving the (still to be formalized) exponential decay
of the increment contributions.  The cardinality side is also in place: the
stage-\(n\) cube has \((2n+1)^d\) points (\texttt{card\_cubicBox}), so the fresh
boundary set has at most \((2n+3)^d\) vertices
(\texttt{card\_cubicBox\_sdiff\_succ\_le}), and this polynomial growth is
geometrically summable, \(\sum_n (2n+3)^d\rho^n<\infty\) for \(0\le\rho<1\)
(\texttt{summable\_cubicBox\_boundary\_card\_mul\_geometric}).  This is the
summability skeleton---polynomial boundary growth against geometric decay---for
the eventual increment bound.

These pieces assemble into an end-to-end conditional capstone for the
upper-bound side of Lemma~17.5.2.  Assuming a geometric decay bound
\(|F_{k+1}(\beta)-F_k(\beta)|\le M\,\rho^k\) with \(0\le\rho<1\) over the covered
exhaustion stages, the geometric series is summable, so the covered-stage
criterion builds the derivative-limit provider
(\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_derivative\_limit\_provider\_of\_geometric\_increments\_on\_covered\_stages});
feeding the provider into the concrete compact-ratio infinite-HLS upper-bound
assembly then yields the named \(\mathrm{latticeMass}\) upper bound at the right
endpoint \(\beta_2\)
(\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_upper\_bound\_of\_geometric\_increments\_on\_covered\_stages}).
This pins the entire remaining analytic task of the upper-bound side to a single
quantitative estimate---a geometric (summable) convergence-rate bound on the
finite-volume \(\beta\)-derivative increments---with all remaining hypotheses,
besides the discrete HLS dimension condition \(2\alpha>d\), the dimension
condition \(1\le d\), and the distinct-pair condition \(x\neq z\), being
positivity and high-temperature range conditions.  Adjoining a validating
endpoint pseudo-mass exponential-decay hypothesis upgrades this to the two-sided
\(\mathrm{latticeMass}\) sandwich \(m^-(\beta_2)\le m(\beta_2)\le C\,m^-(\beta_2)\)
(\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_sandwich\_of\_geometric\_increments\_on\_covered\_stages}
and \texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_capstone\_of\_geometric\_increments\_on\_covered\_stages}).
The fully-concrete variants replace that abstract decay hypothesis by the
scalar condition \(m^-(\beta_2)\le-\log(\beta_2 J\,2d)\), which validates the
endpoint pseudo-mass as a decay rate, so both sides of the sandwich are driven by
concrete scalar inputs---the geometric \(\beta\)-derivative increment decay
(upper) and the pseudo-mass high-temperature rate bound (lower)
(\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_sandwich\_of\_geometric\_increments\_on\_covered\_stages\_and\_pseudoMass\_le\_rate}
and its capstone counterpart).  The polynomial-prefactor forms take the realistic
increment bound \(|F_{k+1}-F_k|\le M\,(2k+3)^d\rho^k\) carrying the
boundary-cardinality prefactor \((2k+3)^d\); since the prefactored geometric
sequence is summable, the provider and the full upper bound / sandwich still
follow (\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_upper\_bound\_of\_poly\_geometric\_increments\_on\_covered\_stages}
and \texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_capstone\_of\_poly\_geometric\_increments\_on\_covered\_stages}).
This matches the shape a boundary-distance decay estimate would produce: the
fresh-vertex count times a per-vertex exponential decay.  The fully-concrete
realistic-form entry point
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_capstone\_of\_poly\_geometric\_increments\_on\_covered\_stages\_and\_pseudoMass\_le\_rate}
further discharges the lower side via \(m^-(\beta_2)\le-\log(\beta_2 J\,2d)\), so
the entire two-sided sandwich follows from two concrete scalar inputs in exactly
the boundary-prefactored geometric form.

The finite derivative-profile formula and continuity input are now discharged.
The theorem
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_finite\_derivative\_profile\_eq\_beta\_edge\_sum}
states that, once \(\{x,z\}\) is contained in the stage volume, the beta
derivative of the finite two-point function is the explicit finite Lebowitz
edge sum over the induced graph.  The companion theorem
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_finite\_derivative\_profile\_eq\_zero\_of\_not\_subset}
covers the pre-containment stages, where the along-exhaustion correlation is
identically zero and its beta derivative is zero.  These formulas feed
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_finite\_deriv\_abs\_le\_high\_temp\_on\_Icc\_all\_stages},
which proves the closed-interval high-temperature absolute derivative bound
for every exhaustion stage: covered stages use the finite Lebowitz and
susceptibility estimate, while uncovered stages use the zero derivative
formula.  Thus the finite-profile bound itself no longer needs an eventual
containment restriction.  With a derivative-limit provider, this all-stage
bound passes to the limiting derivative profile by
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_derivative\_limit\_profile\_abs\_le\_high\_temp\_on\_Icc}:
pointwise convergence from the locally uniform provider and
\texttt{le\_of\_tendsto} transport the finite bound to the limit.  The theorem
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_correlationInfinite\_deriv\_abs\_le\_high\_temp\_on\_Icc}
then identifies the limiting profile with the beta derivative of the
infinite-volume two-point function and gives the same high-temperature absolute
derivative bound for \(\partial_\beta\langle\sigma_x\sigma_z\rangle_\infty\)
on the closed interval.  The same formulas also feed
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_finite\_derivative\_profile\_continuous\_beta}, which
proves continuity of the finite derivative profile at every exhaustion stage.
The limiting-profile continuity theorems
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_derivative\_limit\_profile\_continuousOn\_high\_temp}
and
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_derivative\_limit\_profile\_continuousOn\_Icc}
then use locally uniform and uniform convergence of the finite derivative
profiles to pass this continuity to the provider witness on the open
high-temperature region and on each closed subinterval.  The theorem
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_correlationInfinite\_deriv\_continuousOn\_high\_temp}
then identifies that continuous provider witness pointwise with the beta
derivative of the infinite-volume two-point function, proving continuity of the
infinite beta derivative on the open high-temperature region.  The regularity
theorems
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_correlationInfinite\_differentiableOn\_high\_temp}
and
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_correlationInfinite\_contDiffOn\_one\_high\_temp}
combine the provider-based derivative existence with this derivative
continuity to prove differentiability and \(C^1\) beta regularity of the
infinite-volume two-point function throughout the open high-temperature
region.  The strengthened
monotone and antitone Dini criteria feed this continuity in automatically,
leaving only the stagewise order direction, pointwise convergence, and
continuity of the limiting derivative profile as external provider inputs.

The provider is also connected one layer lower, at the infinite derivative and
infinite-HLS bridge interface.  Thirty-one entry points feed
\texttt{Lemma\_17\_5\_2\_DerivativeLimitProvider} into thermodynamic-limit
beta differentiability, pointwise and interval infinite HLS denominator
comparisons from finite derivative bounds, the HLS-denominator-comparison to
infinite-Lipschitz package, the concrete pseudo-mass specialization of that
package, fixed-constant concrete infinite-HLS Lipschitz and upper-bound bridges,
a high-temperature ratio-lower package that returns both the concrete interval
infinite-HLS denominator comparisons and endpoint path-rate comparison for one
enlarged HLS constant, ratio-lower-to-upper-bound and
ratio-lower-to-sandwich wrappers, uniform-infinite-correlation-lower
upper/sandwich/capstone wrappers and their self-interval variants,
compact-ratio-bounds provider
upper/sandwich/capstone wrappers and their self-interval variants,
concrete infinite-HLS/path-rate upper-bound,
sandwich, and capstone packages, the
finite-HLS-to-infinite Lipschitz package, and the enlarged upper/sandwich
packages.  This keeps the bridge layer aligned with
the provider-shaped capstone API: callers moving from finite derivative bounds,
the infinite HLS denominator comparison, or the path-rate all-rate bridge into
the infinite-HLS pipeline no longer need to name the limiting derivative
profile \(g'\).

The compact-Cauchy criteria are also wired directly to that bridge layer.
\texttt{DerivativeLimitProviderCauchyHLS.lean} packages nineteen entry points
that
first construct \texttt{Lemma\_17\_5\_2\_DerivativeLimitProvider} from
compact-interval metric Cauchy control alone, using completeness of
\(\mathbb{R}\) to construct the pointwise derivative-profile limit, then feed
the provider into the interval infinite-HLS comparison,
the HLS-denominator-comparison to infinite-Lipschitz packages,
concrete uniform-correlation-lower upper/sandwich/capstone wrappers on the self
interval, concrete compact-ratio upper/sandwich/capstone wrappers and their
self-interval variants, concrete path-rate upper/sandwich/capstone packages,
finite-HLS-to-infinite Lipschitz package, path-rate enlarged package, and the
enlarged upper/sandwich assemblies.  Thus a future concrete Cauchy estimate
for the beta-derivative profiles can be consumed by the HLS-uniform capstone
path without separately naming the intermediate provider witness.

The Dini criteria are wired to the same infinite-HLS bridge layer.
\texttt{DerivativeLimitProviderDiniHLS.lean} packages nineteen entry points
that
first construct \texttt{Lemma\_17\_5\_2\_DerivativeLimitProvider} from the
named Dini-order derivative-profile input, pointwise convergence, and
continuity of the limiting derivative profile, then feed that provider into the
interval infinite-HLS comparison, the HLS-denominator-comparison to
infinite-Lipschitz packages, concrete uniform-correlation-lower
upper/sandwich/capstone wrappers on the self interval, concrete compact-ratio
upper/sandwich/capstone
wrappers and their self-interval variants, concrete path-rate
upper/sandwich/capstone packages,
finite-HLS-to-infinite Lipschitz package, path-rate enlarged package, and
enlarged upper/sandwich assemblies.  This makes the Dini route directly
consumable by the HLS-uniform capstone path while leaving the stagewise order,
pointwise convergence, limiting-profile continuity, and finite derivative/HLS
inputs visible.

The same provider is now connected to the larger finite-HLS assembly layer.
Twenty-two entry points feed \texttt{Lemma\_17\_5\_2\_DerivativeLimitProvider}
into the concrete pseudo-mass finite-derivative-provider upper/sandwich
wrappers, the concrete finite-derivative-provider rate/profile capstones,
direct abstract finite-derivative-provider, scalar-provider, ratio-lower,
uniform-correlation-lower upper/sandwich/capstones and their self-interval variants,
compact-ratio-bounds upper/sandwich wrappers,
compact-ratio capstone wrappers, and self-interval compact-ratio
upper/sandwich/capstone wrappers.
This extends the provider-shaped interface below the concrete capstones:
callers of the enlarged finite-HLS chain also no longer need to name the
limiting derivative profile \(g'\), while the remaining analytic work is
unchanged.

The compact-Cauchy inputs are also named at the finite-HLS assembly boundary.
\texttt{DerivativeProfileInputs.lean} names the compact-interval metric Cauchy
hypothesis and keeps the pointwise derivative-profile limit hypothesis for
legacy and Dini-facing callers.  \texttt{CauchyProvider.lean} proves the
complete-metric Cauchy provider helper without importing downstream HLS
assembly layers.  \texttt{CauchyFiniteHLS.lean} then feeds that provider into the
concrete pseudo-mass finite-derivative-provider, direct abstract
finite-derivative-provider, scalar-provider, ratio-lower,
uniform-correlation-lower upper/sandwich/capstones and their self-interval variants,
compact-ratio-bounds upper/sandwich assemblies,
compact-ratio capstone assemblies, and self-interval compact-ratio
upper/sandwich/capstone assemblies,
as well as the concrete finite-derivative-provider rate/profile capstones.
This keeps the concrete Cauchy estimate as the remaining analytic input while
making it directly consumable by the finite-HLS capstone wrappers.

The Dini inputs are wired to the same finite-HLS assembly boundary.
\texttt{DiniProvider.lean} names the stagewise monotone-or-antitone
derivative-profile input and packages it with pointwise convergence plus
continuity of the limiting derivative profile as a derivative-limit provider.
\texttt{DiniFiniteHLS.lean} then feeds that provider into the concrete
pseudo-mass finite-derivative-provider, direct abstract
finite-derivative-provider, scalar-provider, ratio-lower,
uniform-correlation-lower upper/sandwich/capstones and their self-interval variants,
compact-ratio-bounds upper/sandwich assemblies,
compact-ratio capstone assemblies, and self-interval compact-ratio
upper/sandwich/capstone assemblies,
as well as the concrete finite-derivative-provider rate/profile capstones.
This gives the finite-HLS layer the same direct Dini-shaped API already
available through the provider-shaped and Cauchy-shaped entry points.

The same named Cauchy inputs are now wired to the concrete capstone interface.
\texttt{CauchyConcreteCapstone.lean} passes the compact-interval metric Cauchy
hypothesis through the complete-metric provider helper and into ten concrete
automatic-active entry points: compact upper bounds, rate-comparison
sandwiches, profile-lower sandwiches, and the matching high-temperature
capstones.  Thus downstream callers can work directly with the Cauchy-shaped
analytic hypothesis while keeping the endpoint rate or profile lower input
visible.

The Dini route is wired to the same concrete capstone interface.
\texttt{DiniConcreteCapstone.lean} feeds the named Dini provider into the same
ten automatic-active upper, sandwich, and capstone wrappers.  This makes the
Dini-shaped analytic hypotheses directly consumable by the concrete capstone
layer while keeping the remaining stagewise order and endpoint rate/profile
inputs explicit.

Finally, the anchored cubic self-interval package connects these concrete
wrappers to the existing cubic inputs.  For the upper-bound side, theorem
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_cubic\_origin\_upper\_bound\_on\_self\_Icc}
specializes the concrete self-interval upper-bound package to the cubic
exhaustion and anchored pair \(\{0,z\}\).  The companion
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_cubic\_origin\_latticeMass\_le\_on\_self\_Icc}
unfolds the named \texttt{Lemma\_17\_5\_2\_UpperBound} predicate into the
displayed inequality
\[
  \mathrm{latticeMass}(\beta_2)
  \le
  \texttt{ofReal}\!\left(\frac{(2\alpha+1)K}{\rho}\right)\,
  \texttt{ofReal}\!\left(m^{-}(\beta_2)\right).
\]
The interval tanh-profile forms
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_cubic\_origin\_upper\_bound\_of\_cubicTanhProfileBound\_on\_Icc}
and
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_cubic\_origin\_latticeMass\_le\_of\_cubicTanhProfileBound\_on\_Icc}
derive the needed active-range hypothesis from pointwise
\texttt{cubicTanhProfileBound} on \([\beta_1,\beta_2]\) before applying the
cubic self-interval upper-bound wrapper.

For the sandwich side, theorems
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_cubic\_origin\_sandwich\_of\_named\_rate\_on\_self\_Icc},
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_cubic\_origin\_sandwich\_of\_profile\_lower\_on\_self\_Icc},
and
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_cubic\_origin\_sandwich\_of\_cubicTanhProfileBound\_on\_self\_Icc}
specialize to the cubic exhaustion and anchored pair \(\{0,z\}\).  They accept,
respectively, the named proposition \texttt{cubicOriginNamedRateLeHighTemp}, the
endpoint cubic \texttt{pseudoMassG} profile lower bound, or the named
\texttt{cubicTanhProfileBound} predicate, while leaving the interval active-range
and derivative-limit inputs explicit.
The interval form
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_cubic\_origin\_sandwich\_of\_cubicTanhProfileBound\_on\_Icc}
goes one step further: pointwise \texttt{cubicTanhProfileBound} on
\([\beta_1,\beta_2]\) supplies the whole active-range hypothesis through
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_high\_temp\_scalar\_of\_mem\_Icc\_subset\_high\_temp}
for the scalar high-temperature bounds and then
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_cubic\_origin\_active\_range\_of\_cubicTanhProfileBound\_on\_Icc},
so the remaining external analytic input is the derivative-limit hypothesis.

The capstone bundle wrappers
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_cubic\_origin\_capstone\_of\_named\_rate\_on\_self\_Icc},
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_cubic\_origin\_capstone\_of\_profile\_lower\_on\_self\_Icc},
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_cubic\_origin\_capstone\_of\_cubicTanhProfileBound\_on\_self\_Icc},
and
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_cubic\_origin\_capstone\_of\_cubicTanhProfileBound\_on\_Icc}
return the named upper-bound predicate and the displayed two-sided sandwich for
the same HLS constant \(K\).  This avoids requiring downstream users to run the
upper and lower/sandwich assemblies separately and then compare their witnesses.

\paragraph{\S5.4 Prop 5.4.2 infinite-volume Peierls bound (liminf form)
(PR~\#1643).} Adds the genuine infinite-volume lift of GJ Prop 5.4.2:
under the per-stage hypotheses of \texttt{prop\_5\_4\_2\_along\_exhaustion},
the canonical $\infty$-vol $+$-BC quantity
\texttt{plusGibbsExpectationLiminf} $G \Lambda \langle J, 0, \beta\rangle B
(\lambda n\,\sigma.\,\mathrm{sign}(\sigma\,i_n))$ satisfies the
sandwich
$0 \le 1 - \mathrm{plusGibbsExpectationLiminf} \le e^{-c\beta}$.
Theorems
\texttt{prop\_5\_4\_2\_plusGibbsExpectationLiminf\_lower\_bound}
(via \texttt{Filter.liminf\_le\_limsup} chain through
\texttt{Filter.limsup\_le\_of\_le} on the per-stage
\texttt{plusGibbsExp}$_n \le 1$) and the full sandwich
\texttt{prop\_5\_4\_2\_plusGibbsExpectationLiminf} combining the
existing upper bound (PR~\#1099) with the new lower bound.

\paragraph{\texttt{partitionFunctionAlongExhaustion} /
\texttt{freeEnergyAlongExhaustion} joint AnalyticAt + AnalyticOnNhd
wrappers (along-ex + $\mathbb{Z}^d$)
(\S17.5--\S17.6, PR~\#1642).} 8 new pointwise / global analytic
wrappers, each a thin instantiation of the existing $\Lambda$-layer
\texttt{partitionFunction\LeanLambda\_*\_joint} /
\texttt{freeEnergy\LeanLambda\_*\_joint} at
$\Lambda := \Lambda.\mathrm{volume}\,n$. The along-exhaustion wrappers
now live in
\texttt{AmbientLattice/SpecialCases/JointAnalyticity.lean}, and the
$\mathbb{Z}^d$ wrappers live in
\texttt{Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/JointAnalyticity.lean}
(moved from the legacy modules in PR~\#1860).

\paragraph{Joint AnalyticAt + AnalyticOnNhd wrappers across
all 4 layers (\S17.5--\S17.6, PR~\#1641).} Adds joint (in
$(\beta, J, h)$) AnalyticAt and AnalyticOnNhd wrappers for
\texttt{magnetization}, \texttt{susceptibility}, and along-ex
\texttt{correlation} across abstract, $\Lambda$, along-exhaustion,
and $\mathbb{Z}^d$ concrete layers. 24 new theorems. The
susceptibility joint AnalyticAt case is proved as
\texttt{Finset.analyticAt\_fun\_sum} over the
\texttt{truncated2} summands. The along-exhaustion wrappers now live
in \texttt{AmbientLattice/SpecialCases/JointAnalyticity.lean}, and the
$\mathbb{Z}^d$ wrappers live in
\texttt{Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/JointAnalyticity.lean}
(moved from the legacy modules in PR~\#1860).

\paragraph{Joint ContinuousAt + DifferentiableAt wrappers across
all 4 layers (\S17.5--\S17.6, PR~\#1640).} Adds joint pointwise
(in $(\beta, J, h)$) ContinuousAt and DifferentiableAt wrappers
for \texttt{correlation}, \texttt{magnetization}, and
\texttt{susceptibility} across abstract, $\Lambda$,
along-exhaustion, and $\mathbb{Z}^d$ concrete layers. 30 new
theorems, each derived from PR~\#1639 via
\texttt{.continuousAt} / \texttt{.differentiableAt}. The
along-exhaustion and $\mathbb{Z}^d$ wrapper layers now live in
\texttt{AmbientLattice/SpecialCases/JointRegularity.lean} and
\texttt{Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/JointRegularity.lean}
(moved from the legacy modules in PR~\#1859). PR~\#2097 further carves
the 12 \texttt{*\_continuousAt\_joint} /
\texttt{*\_differentiableAt\_joint} concrete wrappers out into
\texttt{Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/JointRegularityPointwise.lean}.
PR~\#2098 further carves the 6 along-exhaustion
\texttt{*\_continuous\_joint} / \texttt{*\_differentiable\_joint}
concrete wrappers out into
\texttt{Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/JointRegularityAlongEx.lean}.

\paragraph{Joint Continuous + Differentiable wrappers across
all 4 layers
(\S17.5--\S17.6, PR~\#1639).} Adds joint (in $(\beta, J, h)$)
Continuous and Differentiable wrappers for
\texttt{magnetization}, \texttt{susceptibility}, and
\texttt{correlation} across abstract, $\Lambda$, along-exhaustion,
and $\mathbb{Z}^d$ concrete layers. 26 new theorems total:
abstract \texttt{magnetization\_/susceptibility\_\{continuous,
differentiable\}\_joint} (4); $\Lambda$ counterparts (4); along-ex
general-$G$ \texttt{correlation/magnetization/
susceptibility\_AlongExhaustion\_\{continuous,
differentiable\}\_joint(\_gen)} (6); $\mathbb{Z}^d$ concrete
$\Lambda$- and along-ex-layer
\texttt{*\_latticeGraph\_\{continuous, differentiable\}\_joint}
(12). The susceptibility joint case is proved as a finite sum of
\texttt{truncated2 = corr(\{i,j\}) - corr(\{i\})·corr(\{j\})},
each Continuous / Differentiable joint via
\texttt{correlation\_\{continuous, differentiable\}\_joint}. The
along-exhaustion and $\mathbb{Z}^d$ wrapper layers now live in
\texttt{AmbientLattice/SpecialCases/JointRegularity.lean} and
\texttt{Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/JointRegularity.lean}
(moved from the legacy modules in PR~\#1859).

\paragraph{$\mathbb{Z}^d$ concrete
\texttt{partitionFunctionAlongExhaustion} /
\texttt{freeEnergyAlongExhaustion} ContinuousAt +
DifferentiableAt wrappers

beta\_general\_h, J\_general\_h, h, joint\}} (12 thms) and

pointwise wrappers at $G := \texttt{IsingModel.latticeGraph}\,d$,
lifting the ambient versions from PR~\#1636.

ContinuousAt + DifferentiableAt wrappers

\texttt{correlationAlongExhaustion\_\{continuousAt,

\paragraph{\texttt{susceptibilityAlongExhaustion} \texttt{HasDerivAt}
wrappers (ambient + $\mathbb{Z}^d$ concrete)
(\S17.5--\S17.6, PR~\#1634).} Adds \texttt{HasDerivAt} along-ex
wrappers for \texttt{susceptibilityAlongExhaustion} in all four
parameter directions. Ambient (general $G$, split across
\texttt{BetaDerivative.lean} / \texttt{JDerivative.lean} /

(4 thms). 8 new wrappers in existence form
$\exists d \in \mathbb{R},\,\mathrm{HasDerivAt}\,f\,d\,x$. The
ambient layer uses \texttt{\_gen} suffix to avoid potential clash
with $\mathbb{Z}^d$-specialized variants.

\paragraph{\texttt{magnetizationAlongExhaustion} $\beta$-direction
+ general-$G$ \texttt{susceptibilityAlongExhaustion}
Continuous/Differentiable wrappers

(\texttt{SpecialCases.lean}):

differentiable\}\_beta} (general $h$, 2) and

d)} baked in. $\mathbb{Z}^d$ concrete
(\texttt{LatticeGraphCorrelation.lean}):
\texttt{magnetizationAlongExhaustion\_latticeGraph\_\{continuous,
differentiable\}\_beta} (2). 12 new wrappers.

\texttt{freeEnergyAlongExhaustion} Continuous/Differentiable

differentiable\}\_\{beta, field, J\}} (6). 12 new thin
instantiations of the corresponding
\texttt{freeEnergy\LeanLambda\_\{continuous, differentiable\}\_*} at
$\Lambda := \Lambda.\mathrm{volume}\,n$.

\paragraph{Along-exhaustion + $\mathbb{Z}^d$ concrete
\texttt{partitionFunctionAlongExhaustion} /
\texttt{freeEnergyAlongExhaustion} \texttt{HasDerivAt} wrappers
(\S17.5--\S17.6, PR~\#1631).} Specializes the $\Lambda$-layer

(PRs~\#1623, \#1624) at $\Lambda := \Lambda.\mathrm{volume}\,n$.
Along-ex layer:

field\}} (3),

J, field\}} (3) split across

FieldDerivative\}.lean}.
$\mathbb{Z}^d$ concrete:
\texttt{partitionFunctionAlongExhaustion\_latticeGraph\_hasDerivAt\_*}
(3) and
\texttt{freeEnergyAlongExhaustion\_latticeGraph\_hasDerivAt\_*}
(3) in
\texttt{Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation.lean}.
12 new theorems in existence form
$\exists c \in \mathbb{R},\,\mathrm{HasDerivAt}\,f\,c\,x$.

\paragraph{$\mathbb{Z}^d$ concrete (\texttt{latticeGraph} $d$)
along-exhaustion \texttt{HasDerivAt} wrappers
(\S17.5--\S17.6, PR~\#1630).} Direct instantiations at
$G := \texttt{IsingModel.latticeGraph}\,d$ of the
along-exhaustion \texttt{hasDerivAt} family
(\texttt{AmbientLattice/\{BetaDerivative, JDerivative,
FieldDerivative\}.lean}; PR~\#1628 + earlier):
\texttt{correlationAlongExhaustion\_latticeGraph\_hasDerivAt\_*}
(4) and
\texttt{magnetizationAlongExhaustion\_latticeGraph\_hasDerivAt\_*}
(4) for $\beta$ (h $=$ 0 and general h), $J$, and $h$ directions.
8 new theorems in existence form
$\exists c \in \mathbb{R},\,\mathrm{HasDerivAt}\,f\,c\,x$.

\paragraph{$\mathbb{Z}^d$ concrete (\texttt{latticeGraph} $d$)
$\Lambda$-layer \texttt{HasDerivAt} wrappers
(\S17.5--\S17.6, PR~\#1629).} Direct instantiations at
$G := \texttt{IsingModel.latticeGraph}\,d$ of the
$\Lambda$-layer \texttt{hasDerivAt} family
(PRs~\#1619, \#1623--\#1628):
\texttt{hasDerivAt\_correlation\LeanLambda\_latticeGraph\_*} (4),
\texttt{hasDerivAt\_freeEnergy\LeanLambda\_latticeGraph\_*} (3),
\texttt{hasDerivAt\_partitionFunction\LeanLambda\_latticeGraph\_*} (3),
\texttt{hasDerivAt\_boltzmannWeight\LeanLambda\_latticeGraph\_*} (3),
\texttt{magnetization\LeanLambda\_latticeGraph\_hasDerivAt\_*} (4),
\texttt{susceptibility\LeanLambda\_latticeGraph\_hasDerivAt\_*} (4).
21 new theorems in existence form
$\exists c \in \mathbb{R},\,\mathrm{HasDerivAt}\,f\,c\,x$, since
the explicit derivative formulas at the $\mathbb{Z}^d$ concrete
layer would be long and unwieldy; consumers needing the explicit
formula call \texttt{Ambient.hasDerivAt\_*\LeanLambda\_*} directly.

\paragraph{\texttt{correlationAlongExhaustion} /
\texttt{magnetizationAlongExhaustion} HasDerivAt in $J$ / $h$
/ $\beta$ along-exhaustion layer
(\S17.5--\S17.6, PR~\#1628).} Adds the missing
\texttt{hasDerivAt} directions for the along-exhaustion layer.
New files
\texttt{IsingModel/AmbientLattice/JDerivative.lean} and
\texttt{IsingModel/AmbientLattice/FieldDerivative.lean}
(mirroring \texttt{BetaDerivative.lean}):
\texttt{correlationAlongExhaustion\_hasDerivAt\_\{J, field\}},
\texttt{magnetizationAlongExhaustion\_hasDerivAt\_\{J, field\}}.
$\beta$-direction wrappers in
\texttt{BetaDerivative.lean}:
\texttt{magnetizationAlongExhaustion\_hasDerivAt\_beta} (at $h = 0$),
\texttt{magnetizationAlongExhaustion\_hasDerivAt\_beta\_general\_h\_gen}.
6 new theorems with existence-form derivative
$\exists d,\,\mathrm{HasDerivAt}\,f\,d\,x$; subset case lifts to
finite-volume \texttt{hasDerivAt\_correlation\_*}, non-subset is
constant zero. The corresponding \texttt{Continuous*} /
\texttt{Differentiable*} along-ex wrappers already exist in
\texttt{BetaDerivative.lean} under the \texttt{\_gen} suffix.

\paragraph{\texttt{magnetization} / \texttt{susceptibility}
HasDerivAt in $h$ + \texttt{magnetization} HasDerivAt in $\beta$
at $h = 0$, abstract + $\Lambda$ layers
(\S17.5--\S17.6, PR~\#1627).} Completes the §17.5--§17.6
single-site \texttt{hasDerivAt} suite at the $\Lambda$ layer
(the \texttt{\_J} and \texttt{\_beta\_general\_h} directions
were added in PR~\#1619; this PR adds the \texttt{\_field} and
\texttt{\_beta} ($h = 0$) directions). Abstract layer
(\texttt{PhaseTransition.lean}):
\texttt{magnetization\_hasDerivAt\_field}
($\partial_h \langle\sigma_i\rangle = \beta \cdot
(\langle\sigma_i \cdot M\rangle - \langle\sigma_i\rangle
\langle M\rangle)$),
\texttt{magnetization\_hasDerivAt\_beta} (at $h = 0$, edge-sum
form), \texttt{susceptibility\_hasDerivAt\_field} (sum-of-
\texttt{truncated2} h-derivatives via
\texttt{truncated2\_hasDerivAt\_field}). $\Lambda$-layer
(\texttt{AmbientLattice/Defs.lean}):
\texttt{magnetization\LeanLambda\_hasDerivAt\_field},
\texttt{magnetization\LeanLambda\_hasDerivAt\_beta} (at $h = 0$),
\texttt{susceptibility\LeanLambda\_hasDerivAt\_field}. 6 new theorems
(3 abstract + 3 thin $\Lambda$ wrappers); standard
thermodynamic-derivative identities (Glimm--Jaffe §17.5 / §17.6).

\paragraph{\texttt{hasDerivAt\_correlation\LeanLambda} in $\beta / J / h$
at the $\Lambda$-layer (\S17.5--\S17.6, \S4.6, PR~\#1626).}
Lifts four explicit-derivative correlation abstracts to the
$\Lambda$ layer with covariance-form derivatives:
\texttt{hasDerivAt\_correlation\LeanLambda\_beta} (at $h = 0$),
\texttt{\_beta\_general\_h},
\texttt{\_J}, \texttt{\_field}. 4 new thin wrappers.

\paragraph{\texttt{hasDerivAt\_boltzmannWeight\LeanLambda} in $\beta / J / h$
at the $\Lambda$-layer (\S17.5--\S17.6, \S4.6, PR~\#1625).}
Lifts the three per-configuration Boltzmann-weight derivative
abstracts to the $\Lambda$ layer:
\texttt{hasDerivAt\_boltzmannWeight\LeanLambda\_beta},
\texttt{\_J}, \texttt{\_field}. Building blocks for the
partition-function / free-energy variants (PR~\#1623, \#1624).
3 new thin wrappers.

\paragraph{\texttt{hasDerivAt\_partitionFunction\LeanLambda} in $\beta / J
/ h$ at the $\Lambda$-layer (\S17.5--\S17.6, \S4.6, PR~\#1624).}
Lifts three explicit-derivative abstracts to the $\Lambda$
layer: \texttt{hasDerivAt\_partitionFunction\LeanLambda\_beta}
($\partial_\beta Z = \sum_\sigma -H \cdot w$),
\texttt{\_J} ($\partial_J Z = \sum_\sigma \beta \cdot \sum_e
\mathrm{edgeSpin} \cdot w$), and \texttt{\_field}
($\partial_h Z = \sum_\sigma \beta \cdot M \cdot w$). 3 new thin
wrappers.

\paragraph{\texttt{hasDerivAt\_freeEnergy\LeanLambda} in $\beta / J / h$ at
the $\Lambda$-layer (\S17.5--\S17.6, \S4.6, PR~\#1623).} Lifts
three explicit-derivative thermodynamic-identity abstracts to
the $\Lambda$ layer:
\texttt{hasDerivAt\_freeEnergy\LeanLambda\_beta\_general\_h}
($\partial_\beta f = (|\!\uparrow\!\Lambda|)^{-1} \cdot \langle
-H\rangle$),
\texttt{hasDerivAt\_freeEnergy\LeanLambda\_J}
($\partial_J f = (|\!\uparrow\!\Lambda|)^{-1} \cdot \langle
\beta \cdot \sum_e \mathrm{edgeSpin}\rangle$), and
\texttt{hasDerivAt\_freeEnergy\LeanLambda\_field}
($\partial_h f = (|\!\uparrow\!\Lambda|)^{-1} \cdot \langle
\beta \cdot M\rangle$). 3 new thin wrappers.

\paragraph{\texttt{correlation\LeanLambda} / \texttt{susceptibility\LeanLambda}
ContinuousAt + DifferentiableAt at the $\Lambda$-layer
(\S17.5--\S17.6, PR~\#1622).} Point-wise versions
complementing the whole-$\mathbb{R}$ \texttt{Continuous} /
\texttt{Differentiable} wrappers (PR~\#1620, \#1616):
\texttt{correlation\LeanLambda\_continuousAt\_beta},
\texttt{\_continuousAt\_field},
\texttt{\_differentiableAt\_field},
\texttt{susceptibility\LeanLambda\_continuousAt\_beta},
\texttt{\_differentiableAt\_beta},
\texttt{\_continuousAt\_field},
\texttt{\_differentiableAt\_field}. 7 new thin wrappers.

\paragraph{\texttt{correlation\LeanLambda} Continuous + Differentiable in
$\beta / h / J$, $\Lambda$-layer (\S17.5--\S17.6, PR~\#1620).}
Lifts eight correlation regularity abstracts to the $\Lambda$
layer:
\texttt{correlation\LeanLambda\_\{continuous, differentiable\}\_beta} (at
$h = 0$),
\texttt{\_\{continuous, differentiable\}\_beta\_general\_h},
\texttt{\_\{continuous, differentiable\}\_field}, and
\texttt{\_\{continuous, differentiable\}\_J}. 8 new thin
direct-instantiation wrappers; along-exhaustion versions
already exist in
\texttt{Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/Inequalities.lean} and
\texttt{AmbientLattice/BetaDerivative.lean}.

\paragraph{\texttt{susceptibility} / \texttt{magnetization}
HasDerivAt in $\beta/J$, $\Lambda$-layer (\S17.5--\S17.6,
PR~\#1619).} Lifts five HasDerivAt abstracts to the $\Lambda$
layer with explicit derivative formulas:
\texttt{susceptibility\LeanLambda\_hasDerivAt\_beta} (at $h = 0$),
\texttt{\_hasDerivAt\_beta\_general\_h},
\texttt{\_hasDerivAt\_J} (sum over $j : \uparrow\!\Lambda$ of
\texttt{deriv (truncated2 \ldots) \ldots}),
\texttt{magnetization\LeanLambda\_hasDerivAt\_J} (sum over edges of
\texttt{(inducedGraph G $\Lambda$).edgeFinset}), and
\texttt{magnetization\LeanLambda\_hasDerivAt\_beta\_general\_h}. 5 new
thin direct-instantiation wrappers; explicit derivative
formulas are inherently tied to the ambient site set, so the
along-exhaustion and $\mathbb{Z}^d$ versions are deferred until
a use case requires them.

\paragraph{\texttt{susceptibility} parameter-direction convergent
($\beta/h/J \to \infty$), all volume layers (\S5.3, PR~\#1618).}
Lifts three convergence abstracts to all 4 volume layers:
\texttt{susceptibility\_convergent\_beta} ($\beta \to \infty$ at
$J, h \ge 0$),
\texttt{\_convergent\_h} ($h \to \infty$ at $J \ge 0$,
$\beta > 0$), \texttt{\_convergent\_J} ($J \to \infty$ at
$h \ge 0$, $\beta > 0$). 12 new thin direct-instantiation
wrappers (3 abstracts $\times$ 4 layers). The along-ex
wrappers use `\_param` suffix to distinguish from the existing
volume-direction
\texttt{susceptibilityAlongExhaustion\_convergent}, and case-split
on $i \in \Lambda.\mathrm{volume}\ n$ (witness $L = 0$ when not
in volume).

\paragraph{\texttt{magnetization} parameter-direction convergent
($\beta/h/J \to \infty$), $\Lambda$ + along-ex + $\mathbb{Z}^d$
along-ex (\S5.3, PR~\#1617).} Lifts three convergence abstracts
to the missing volume layers ($\mathbb{Z}^d$ $\Lambda$-direct
versions already exist in
\texttt{correlation}$\Lambda \ldots \{i\}$ form):
\texttt{magnetization\_convergent\_beta} ($\beta \to \infty$ at
$J, h \ge 0$),
\texttt{\_convergent\_h} ($h \to \infty$ at $J \ge 0$, $\beta >
0$), \texttt{\_convergent\_J} ($J \to \infty$ at $h \ge 0$,
$\beta > 0$). 9 new thin direct-instantiation wrappers (3
abstracts $\times$ 3 missing layers). All return
$\exists L,\ \mathrm{Tendsto}\ (\ldots\ \mathrm{atTop})\ (\mathrm{nhds}\ L)$.
The along-ex wrappers case-split on
$i \in \Lambda.\mathrm{volume}\ n$: when $i \in$, the value
reduces to the $\Lambda$ wrap; otherwise it is constantly $0$
(witness $L = 0$).

\paragraph{\texttt{susceptibility} Continuous + Differentiable in
$h$ / $J$, $\Lambda$ and $\mathbb{Z}^d$ $\Lambda$ layers (\S17.5--\S17.6,
PR~\#1616).} Lifts four \texttt{susceptibility} regularity
abstracts to the $\Lambda$ and $\mathbb{Z}^d$ $\Lambda$ layers
(along-ex $\mathbb{Z}^d$ wraps already exist in
\texttt{Inequalities.lean} under the unprefixed name):
\texttt{\_continuous\_field}, \texttt{\_differentiable\_field}
(in $h$), \texttt{\_continuous\_J},
\texttt{\_differentiable\_J} (in $J$). 8 new thin
direct-instantiation wrappers (4 abstracts $\times$ 2 missing
layers).

\paragraph{\texttt{magnetization} Continuous + Differentiable in
$h$ / $J$, all volume layers (\S17.5--\S17.6, PR~\#1615).} Lifts
four \texttt{magnetization} regularity abstracts to the four
volume layers (\textit{Lambda}, along-ex, $\mathbb{Z}^d$ \textit{Lambda},
$\mathbb{Z}^d$ along-ex):
\texttt{\_continuous\_field}, \texttt{\_differentiable\_field}
(in $h$),
\texttt{\_continuous\_J}, \texttt{\_differentiable\_J} (in $J$).
16 new thin direct-instantiation wrappers (4 abstracts $\times$
4 layers). The along-ex wrappers case-split on
$i \in \Lambda.\mathrm{volume}\ n$: when $i \in$, the value
reduces to the \textit{Lambda} wrap; otherwise it is constantly $0$.

\paragraph{Mayer filter-connected $n=0/1/2$ + $\varepsilon^n$
expansion + \texttt{mayerPartialSum\_analyticOnNhd}, all volume
layers (\S18.5, PR~\#1613).} Lifts five Mayer / $\varepsilon(t)$
lemmas to the four volume layers (\textit{Lambda}, along-ex,
$\mathbb{Z}^d$ \textit{Lambda}, $\mathbb{Z}^d$ along-ex):
\texttt{mayerPartialSum\_analyticOnNhd}
(\texttt{AnalyticOnNhd ℝ \_ Set.univ}),
\texttt{vdPolymerFamilies\_sum\_minus\_one\_pow}
($\varepsilon(t)^k$ as multi-$\Gamma$ piFinset sum),
\texttt{mayerExpansionTerm\_filter\_connected\_zero}
(filter at $n=0$ is $\emptyset$),
\texttt{\_filter\_connected\_one} (filter at $n=1$ equals full
piFinset), and \texttt{\_two\_filter\_connected\_eq\_incompat}
(filter-connected $\Leftrightarrow$ pair-incompatible at $n=2$).
20 new thin direct-instantiation wrappers (5 abstracts $\times$
4 layers). The longest $\mathbb{Z}^d$ along-ex name
abbreviates \texttt{\_filter\_connected\_eq\_incompat} to
\texttt{\_filter\_conn\_eq\_incompat} to fit under the
100-character linter budget.

\paragraph{\S18.4--\S18.6 capstones ($Z$ formula +
\texttt{freeEnergy} decomposition + log $2$ / $N=1$ corollaries),
all volume layers (PR~\#1612).} Lifts six capstone formulas to the
four volume layers (\textit{Lambda}, along-ex, $\mathbb{Z}^d$
\textit{Lambda}, $\mathbb{Z}^d$ along-ex):
\texttt{partitionFunction\_high\_temp\_expansion\_h\_zero\_polymer\_family}
(\S18.4 main: $Z = 2^{|\iota|} \cdot \cosh^{|E|} \cdot
\sum_\Gamma \prod \tanh^{|P|}$),
\texttt{\_closed\_evenSubgraphs} (FV (3.45) form),
\texttt{freeEnergy\_eq\_polymerFreeEnergy} and
\texttt{\_ferromagnetic} (\S18.6 decomposition $f = \log 2 +
(|E|/|\iota|) \cdot \log \cosh + \mathrm{pFE} / |\iota|$),
\texttt{freeEnergy\_eq\_log\_two\_at\_betaJ\_zero} ($f = \log 2$
at $\beta J = 0$), and \texttt{mayerPartialSum\_one\_at\_one}
($\mathrm{mayerPartialSum}\,G\,1\,1 = |\mathrm{allPolymers}\,G|$).
24 new thin direct-instantiation wrappers (6 abstracts $\times$
4 layers). The hypothesis $0 < |\iota|$ at the Lambda / along-ex
layers is discharged via \texttt{Finset.Nonempty.fintype\_card\_coe\_pos}.
The $\mathbb{Z}^d$ along-ex ferromagnetic wrap uses the
abbreviated \texttt{\_ferro} suffix in
\texttt{freeEnergyAlongExhaustion\_latticeGraph\_eq\_polymerFreeEnergy\_ferro}
to fit under the 100-character linter budget.

\paragraph{\texttt{partitionFunction}
Continuous/Differentiable per-direction at general $h$ +

to all volume layers (\S18.6, PR~\#1611).} Lifts eight abstract

$\mathbb{Z}^d$ layers (Lambda-layer wraps already exist via PRs

\texttt{\_differentiable\_h}, plus
\texttt{freeEnergy\_continuous\_joint} and
\texttt{\_differentiable\_joint}. 24 new thin
direct-instantiation wrappers (8 abstracts $\times$ 3 missing
layers).

\paragraph{\texttt{partitionFunction} joint
Continuous/Differentiable + general-$h$ analyticAt, all volume
layers (\S18.6, PR~\#1610).} Lifts five abstract
\texttt{partitionFunction} regularity theorems to the four
volume layers (\textit{Lambda}, along-ex, $\mathbb{Z}^d$ \textit{Lambda},
$\mathbb{Z}^d$ along-ex):
\texttt{\_continuous\_joint},
\texttt{\_differentiable\_joint},
\texttt{\_analyticAt\_beta\_general\_h},
\texttt{\_analyticAt\_J\_general\_h}, and

wrappers (5 abstracts $\times$ 4 layers). Complements the
existing joint analyticity (PR~\#1535, joint analyticAt) and
$h$-zero regularity (PR~\#1608) wrappers.

\paragraph{\texttt{freeEnergy} per-direction analyticAt +
analyticOnNhd, all volume layers (\S18.6, PR~\#1609).} Lifts
ten abstract \texttt{freeEnergy} per-direction analyticity
theorems to the four volume layers (\textit{Lambda}, along-ex,
$\mathbb{Z}^d$ \textit{Lambda}, $\mathbb{Z}^d$ along-ex):
\texttt{\_analyticAt\_beta\_h\_zero},
\texttt{\_analyticAt\_J\_h\_zero},
\texttt{\_analyticOnNhd\_beta\_h\_zero},
\texttt{\_analyticOnNhd\_J\_h\_zero},
\texttt{\_analyticAt\_beta\_general\_h},
\texttt{\_analyticAt\_J\_general\_h},
\texttt{\_analyticAt\_h},
\texttt{\_analyticOnNhd\_beta\_general\_h},
\texttt{\_analyticOnNhd\_J\_general\_h}, and
\texttt{\_analyticOnNhd\_h}. 40 new thin
direct-instantiation wrappers (10 abstracts $\times$ 4 layers).
Complements the existing joint-direction
\texttt{freeEnergy\LeanLambda\_analyticAt\_joint} (PR~\#1535) at the same
volume layers.

\paragraph{\texttt{partitionFunction} \texttt{h\_zero} regularity,
all volume layers (\S18.6, PR~\#1608).} Lifts eight abstract
\texttt{partitionFunction\_*\_h\_zero} regularity theorems to the
four volume layers (\textit{Lambda}, along-ex, $\mathbb{Z}^d$
\textit{Lambda}, $\mathbb{Z}^d$ along-ex):
\texttt{\_continuous\_beta\_h\_zero},
\texttt{\_continuous\_J\_h\_zero},
\texttt{\_differentiable\_beta\_h\_zero},
\texttt{\_differentiable\_J\_h\_zero},
\texttt{\_analyticAt\_beta\_h\_zero},
\texttt{\_analyticAt\_J\_h\_zero},
\texttt{\_analyticOnNhd\_beta\_h\_zero}, and
\texttt{\_analyticOnNhd\_J\_h\_zero}. 32 new thin
direct-instantiation wrappers (8 abstracts $\times$ 4 layers).
These provide the polymer-expansion-derived h-zero regularity of
$Z(J, 0, \beta)$ in $\beta / J$ at every volume layer, alongside
the existing general-$h$ wrappers. The along-exhaustion and concrete
$\mathbb{Z}^d$ wrappers from this family now live in the matching
\texttt{PartitionFunctionRegularity.lean} child modules under
\path{AmbientLattice/SpecialCases} and
\path{Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation}; the $\Lambda$-layer wrappers
live in
\path{IsingModel/AmbientLattice/AnalyticityLambdaSection186.lean}
(narrowed out of \texttt{Analyticity.lean} in PR~\#1992).

The along-exhaustion and concrete $\mathbb{Z}^d$ joint/general-$h$
\texttt{partitionFunction} wrappers now live in the matching
\texttt{PartitionFunctionGeneralAnalyticity.lean} child modules; the
$\Lambda$-layer wrappers live in
\path{IsingModel/AmbientLattice/AnalyticityLambdaSection186.lean}
(narrowed out of \texttt{Analyticity.lean} in PR~\#1992).

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.vdPolymerFamilies\_sum\_continuous\_complex} /
\texttt{\_differentiable\_complex} / \texttt{\_analyticAt\_complex} (polymer-family sum
complex extension, Issue \#3054)]
The polymer-family sum
\(t\mapsto\sum_{\Gamma}\prod_{P\in\Gamma} t^{|P|}\), viewed as a function
\(\mathbb{C}\to\mathbb{C}\), is \texttt{Continuous}, \texttt{Differentiable}~\(\mathbb{C}\),
and \texttt{AnalyticAt}~\(\mathbb{C}\) at every \(t\in\mathbb{C}\) (a polynomial in \(t\),
hence entire over \(\mathbb{C}\)).  The induction helper
\texttt{analyticAt\_prod\_pow\_complex} mirrors \texttt{analyticAt\_prod\_pow}.
First foundational step for extending the cluster expansion to a complex disc in
\(\beta\)/\(J\), en route to the volume-uniform \(Z_{\mathbb{C}}\) lower bound for the
Lemma 17.5.2 \texttt{hZ} provider (Issue \#3044).
\texttt{analyticAt\_complex\_tanh} (project-local helper, Issue \#3054) gives
\(\mathrm{AnalyticAt}\,\mathbb{C}\,\mathrm{tanh}\,z\) whenever
\(\cosh z\ne0\), via \(\mathrm{tanh}=\sinh/\cosh\) and \texttt{AnalyticAt.div} of the entire
\texttt{Complex.sinh}, \texttt{Complex.cosh} (the complex-\(\tanh\) analyticity for the
substitution \(t=\mathrm{tanh}((\beta J:\mathbb{C}))\) in the polymer expansion).
\texttt{vdPolymerFamilies\_sum\_tanh\_analyticAt\_complex\_beta} / \texttt{\_J}
(Issue \#3054, complex analogue of
\texttt{vdPolymerFamilies\_sum\_tanh\_analyticAt\_beta} / \texttt{\_J}) chain-rule-compose
the polymer-sum complex analyticity with \texttt{analyticAt\_complex\_tanh} and the
entire linear factor \(\beta\mapsto\beta J\) (resp.\ \(J\mapsto\beta J\)), exhibiting
\(\beta'\mapsto\sum_{\Gamma}\prod_{P\in\Gamma}(\mathrm{tanh}(\beta'J))^{|P|}\)
(resp.\ \(J'\mapsto\sum_{\Gamma}\prod_{P\in\Gamma}(\mathrm{tanh}(\beta J'))^{|P|}\))
as complex-analytic at every \(\beta\) (resp.\ \(J\)) with
\(\cosh(\beta J)\ne0\).
\texttt{vdPolymerFamilies\_sum\_complex\_at\_zero} (Issue \#3054, mirror of
\texttt{vdPolymerFamilies\_sum\_at\_zero}) gives the constant term
\(\sum_\Gamma\prod_P(0:\mathbb{C})^{|P|}=1\) — only the empty family
\(\Gamma=\emptyset\) contributes, providing the foundational point for local
non-vanishing of the polymer expansion in a complex disc.
\texttt{vdPolymerFamilies\_sum\_tanh\_complex\_at\_zero\_beta} (Issue \#3054)
evaluates the \texttt{tanh}-substituted complex polymer-family sum at
\(\beta=0\): equals \(1\) via \(\tanh 0=0\) and the previous lemma.
\texttt{vdPolymerFamilies\_sum\_tanh\_complex\_eventually\_ne\_zero\_at\_zero\_beta}
(Issue \#3054) combines this with the chain-rule complex analyticity
(\texttt{vdPolymerFamilies\_sum\_tanh\_analyticAt\_complex\_beta} at
\(\cosh0=1\ne0\)) and \texttt{ContinuousAt.eventually\_ne} to give: the
\texttt{tanh}-substituted polymer-family sum is non-zero in a complex
neighborhood of \(\beta=0\).
\texttt{vdPolymerFamilies\_sum\_tanh\_complex\_ne\_zero\_on\_ball\_at\_zero\_beta}
(Issue \#3054) upgrades the \(\forall^\flat\) form to a quantitative ball:
\(\exists r>0,\forall\beta\in B(0,r),\ \dots\ne0\), via
\texttt{Metric.eventually\_nhds\_iff\_ball}.
\texttt{vdPolymerFamilies\_sum\_tanh\_complex\_norm\_ge\_eps\_on\_closedBall\_at\_zero\_beta}
(Issue \#3054) strengthens the ball-form to a uniform lower bound
\(\|\cdot\|\ge\varepsilon>0\) on a closed sub-ball: shrink the radius so that both
\(\cosh(\beta J)\ne0\) (for \(\tanh\) continuity) and the polymer-\(\tanh\) sum
is non-zero throughout; on the resulting compact closed ball, the continuous
norm function attains a positive minimum (\texttt{IsCompact.exists\_isMinOn}).
The radius \(r\) and \(\varepsilon\) depend on \(G\) and \(J\); volume-uniformity
is the next step toward the volume-uniform \(Z_{\mathbb{C}}\) lower bound
(Issue \#3044).
The four \(J\)-direction analogues
\texttt{vdPolymerFamilies\_sum\_tanh\_complex\_\{at\_zero,eventually\_ne\_zero\_at\_zero,ne\_zero\_on\_ball\_at\_zero,norm\_ge\_eps\_on\_closedBall\_at\_zero\}\_J}
(Issue \#3054) mirror the \(\beta\) variants with \(\beta\) fixed and \(J\) the
variable (cosh continuity uses \texttt{continuous\_const.mul continuous\_id}).
\texttt{partitionFunctionComplex\_high\_temp\_expansion\_h\_zero\_polymer\_family\_at\_real}
(Issue \#3054, \texttt{ComplexAnalyticity/HighTempExpansion.lean}) is the complex
\(Z\) formula at real parameters \(J,\beta\) (coerced to \(\mathbb{C}\)):
\(Z_{\mathbb{C}}(G,\uparrow J,0,\uparrow\beta)
=2^{|\iota|}\,\cosh(\uparrow\beta\uparrow J)^{|E|}\,
\sum_\Gamma\prod_P\tanh(\uparrow\beta\uparrow J)^{|P|}\).
Cast of \texttt{partitionFunction\_high\_temp\_expansion\_h\_zero\_polymer\_family}
via \texttt{partitionFunction\_ofReal\_eq\_partitionFunctionComplex} and
\texttt{Complex.ofReal\_\{cosh,tanh\}}. Analytic-continuation seed for the
general \((J,\beta)\in\mathbb{C}\times\mathbb{C}\) complex identity.
\texttt{partitionFunctionComplex\_high\_temp\_expansion\_h\_zero\_polymer\_family\_near\_zero\_beta}
(Issue \#3054, same file) upgrades to a complex \(\beta\) neighborhood of \(0\)
(for fixed real \(J\)): proof via the identity theorem
\texttt{AnalyticOnNhd.eqOn\_of\_preconnected\_of\_frequently\_eq} on a small
open disc \texttt{Metric.ball 0 r} where \(\cosh(\beta J)\ne0\); LHS entire,
RHS analytic via the \(\beta\)-chain rule from Issue \#3054; accumulation
through real points \((1/(n+1):\mathbb{R}):\mathbb{C}\to0\) using the at-real
seed.
\texttt{partitionFunctionComplex\_norm\_ge\_eps\_on\_closedBall\_at\_zero\_beta\_real\_J}
(Issue \#3054, same file) composes the identity with the polymer-\(\tanh\) sum
closed-ball \(\varepsilon\)-bound and \(\cosh\) compactness to give a
quantitative complex-\(Z\) lower bound on a closed ball around \(\beta=0\):
\(\exists r>0,\varepsilon>0,\forall\beta\in\overline{B}(0,r),\,
\varepsilon\le\|Z_{\mathbb{C}}(G,\uparrow J,0,\beta)\|\). The per-fixed-volume
version of the Lemma 17.5.2 \texttt{hZ} provider via the cluster-expansion
route; both \(r\) and \(\varepsilon\) depend on \(G\) and \(J\). Volume-uniformity
remains the research-level open hard core (Issue \#3044).
\texttt{Ambient.partitionFunctionComplexAlongExhaustion\_norm\_ge\_eps\_on\_closedBall\_at\_zero\_beta\_real\_J}
(Issue \#3054, \texttt{AmbientComplexAnalyticity/HighTempExpansion.lean}) is the
per-stage \(\Lambda\)-layer lift via \texttt{partitionFunctionComplexAlongExhaustion\_apply}:
for each exhaustion stage \(n\), the closed-ball lower bound holds for
\(Z_{\mathbb{C}}{}_{\Lambda_n}(\uparrow J, 0, \beta)\) (per-stage
\(r_n,\varepsilon_n\) depending on the induced subgraph; cross-stage uniformity
is the volume-uniform open problem).
\texttt{Ambient.partitionFunctionComplexAlongEx\_norm\_ge\_eps\_closedBall\_zero\_beta\_realJ\_lattice}
(Issue \#3054,
\texttt{Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/ComplexZLowerBoundLatticeGraph.lean})
specializes to \(G=\texttt{latticeGraph}\,d\) (\(\mathbb{Z}^d\) concrete case).
\texttt{partitionFunctionComplex\_high\_temp\_expansion\_h\_zero\_polymer\_family\_near\_zero\_J}
(Issue \#3054, \texttt{ComplexAnalyticity/HighTempExpansion.lean}) is the
\(J\)-direction analogue: for fixed real \(\beta\), the complex high-temperature
expansion holds in a complex neighborhood of \(J=0\) (identity theorem on
\texttt{Metric.ball 0 r} where \(\cosh((\beta:\mathbb{C})J)\ne0\); accumulation
through real points using the at-real seed).
\textbf{Volume-uniform \texttt{hZ} conditional reduction} (Issue \#3054,
\texttt{AmbientComplexAnalyticity/VolumeUniformHZ.lean}): defines two
structural inputs
\texttt{VolumeUniformZComplexIdentity} (single radius \(r\) for the identity
to hold at every stage) and \texttt{VolumeUniformComplexHTBound} (single
\((r,\varepsilon)\) lower bound on the polymer-expansion RHS at every stage),
and proves \texttt{volume\_uniform\_hZ\_provider\_of\_HT\_bound\_and\_identity}
to derive the volume-uniform \(Z_{\mathbb{C}}\) lower bound across all stages.
\texttt{volume\_uniform\_Z\_ne\_zero\_of\_HT\_bound\_and\_identity} converts the
\(\|\cdot\|\ge\varepsilon>0\) form to \(Z_{\mathbb{C}}\ne0\) (via
\texttt{norm\_pos\_iff}), and
\texttt{partitionFunctionComplex\_inducedGraph\_ne\_zero\_on\_ball\_at\_zero\_of\_volume\_uniform}
unfolds the along-exhaustion wrapper to
\texttt{partitionFunctionComplex (inducedGraph G ($\Lambda$.volume n))
($J{:}\mathbb{C}$) 0 $\beta$} \(\ne0\) — the exact shape required by the
\texttt{hZk}/\texttt{hZk1} hypotheses of
\texttt{dist\_deriv\_correlationAlongExhaustion\_le\_of\_complex\_circle\_bound}
(PR \#3032) for the Lemma 17.5.2 capstone, specialised to the centred disc at
\(\beta=0\).
\textbf{Lemma 17.5.2 CE-route conditional capstone at \(\beta=0\)} (Issue
\#3054,
\texttt{Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/Lemma\_17\_5\_2/CEConditionalCapstone.lean}):
\texttt{dist\_deriv\_correlationAlongExhaustion\_le\_at\_zero\_beta\_of\_volume\_uniform}
composes the volume-uniform \(Z_{\mathbb{C}}\ne0\) bridge with PR \#3032's
capstone-coordinate conditional reduction. For each covered stage \(k\), given
a complex circle bound \(B\), the consecutive \(\beta\)-derivative increment
\(d(\partial_\beta c_k,\partial_\beta c_{k+1})\le B/R\) at \(\beta=0\), with
\(R\) volume-uniform. The remaining open inputs are: (a) the two CE-route
structural \texttt{Prop}s (cluster-expansion convergence in complex,
Issue \#3054); (b) the volume-uniform circle bound \(B\) (complex Simon-Lieb,
Issue \#3044).
\textbf{Generalisation to real \(\beta_0\)} (Issue \#3054):
\texttt{VolumeUniformComplexHTBoundAtReal} /
\texttt{VolumeUniformZComplexIdentityAtReal} and the corresponding
\texttt{volume\_uniform\_hZ\_provider\_at\_real\_beta\_*}/
\texttt{volume\_uniform\_Z\_ne\_zero\_at\_real\_beta\_*}/
\texttt{partitionFunctionComplex\_inducedGraph\_ne\_zero\_on\_ball\_at\_real\_beta\_*}
mirror the \(\beta=0\) chain at an arbitrary real centre \(\beta_0\);
\texttt{dist\_deriv\_correlationAlongExhaustion\_le\_at\_real\_beta\_of\_volume\_uniform}
is the CE-route conditional capstone at general real \(\beta_0\), feeding the
Lemma 17.5.2 increment infrastructure at any high-temperature reference point.
\textbf{Icc-uniform geometric-increment bundle} (Issue \#3054,
\texttt{CEConditionalCapstone.lean}):
\texttt{CERouteIccGeometricIncrement} \(\Lambda\,J\,x\,z\,M\,\mathrm{ratio}\)
packages, for every \(\beta\) in a high-temperature closed interval and every
covered stage \(k\), a single radius \(R\), ne-zero hypotheses for stages \(k\)
and \(k+1\) on \(\overline{B}((\beta{:}\mathbb{R}){:}\mathbb{C}, R)\), and a
circle bound \(B\) on the sphere with \(B/R\le M\cdot\mathrm{ratio}^k\).
\texttt{hincr\_of\_CERouteIccGeometricIncrement} converts the bundle into the
\texttt{hincr} shape of
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_derivative\_limit\_provider\_of\_geometric\_increments\_on\_covered\_stages}
(the increment hypothesis of the existing Lemma 17.5.2 capstone), via direct
application of \texttt{dist\_deriv\_correlationAlongExhaustion\_le\_of\_complex\_circle\_bound}
(PR \#3032) per \((\beta,k)\).
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_derivative\_limit\_provider\_of\_CERouteIccGeometricIncrement}
is the \textbf{end-to-end CE-route Lemma 17.5.2 derivative-limit provider}:
directly composes the bundle with
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_derivative\_limit\_provider\_of\_geometric\_increments\_on\_covered\_stages}
to deliver \texttt{Lemma\_17\_5\_2\_DerivativeLimitProvider}\(\,\Lambda\,J\,x\,z\).
The Fintype instance binders were dropped to use the global noncomputable
instance from \texttt{LatticeBoundaryBED.lean} (the consumer relies on global
resolution; instance-coherence requires the same path).
\textbf{CE-route \(\to\) Lemma 17.5.2 final-capstone wrappers} (Issue \#3054):
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_upper\_bound\_of\_CERouteIccGeometricIncrement}
delivers the named upper-bound predicate directly from the CE-route bundle via
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_upper\_bound\_of\_geometric\_increments\_on\_covered\_stages};
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_sandwich\_of\_CERouteIccGeometricIncrement} additionally
consumes a validating endpoint pseudo-mass exponential-decay hypothesis and
returns the displayed two-sided \(\overline{m}^-\le m \le C\,\overline{m}^-\)
sandwich for one HLS constant.
\textbf{Structural bridge: CE-route Props (per-\(\beta\)) + Icc-uniform circle
assembler \(\to\) bundle} (Issue \#3054):
\texttt{CERouteIccGeometricIncrement\_of\_Props\_and\_circle} composes the
CE-route volume-uniform Props (available for every \(\beta\in[\beta_1,\beta_2]\))
with an Icc-uniform geometric circle assembler that, given a ne-zero disc
radius \(R_0\) derived from the Props at \(\beta\), supplies a sub-radius
\(R\le R_0\) and a circle bound \(B\) on the sphere with
\(B/R\le M\cdot\mathrm{ratio}^k\). Threads the per-\(\beta\) ne-zero hypothesis
from
\texttt{partitionFunctionComplex\_inducedGraph\_ne\_zero\_on\_ball\_at\_real\_beta\_of\_volume\_uniform}
(PR \#3072) into the bundle's per-stage ne-zero slots.
\textbf{One-step Props \(\to\) final-capstone wrappers} (Issue \#3054):
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_upper\_bound\_of\_CERouteProps\_and\_circle} and
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_sandwich\_of\_CERouteProps\_and\_circle} compose the
bridge with the upper-bound/sandwich consumers, delivering the named
\texttt{Lemma\_17\_5\_2\_UpperBound} predicate (resp. the displayed two-sided
sandwich) directly from the per-\(\beta\) CE-route Props and the circle
assembler (and, for the sandwich, the validating endpoint pseudo-mass decay).
\textbf{Cauchy-route Q-input bundle mirror} (Issue \#3054):
\texttt{CERouteIccGeometricIncrement\_of\_Q\_and\_circle} is the alternative
bundle constructor that accepts the same shape of inputs but allows the user
to derive the per-stage ne-zero hypotheses from a second-moment Q-bound via
\texttt{partitionFunctionComplex\_ne\_zero\_of\_second\_moment\_bound\_and\_smallness}
(PR \#3048 + \texttt{norm\_pos\_iff}). This makes the second-moment /
Cauchy-style route an equally valid entry point to the same CE-route bundle.
\textbf{One-step Cauchy Q-circle \(\to\) final-capstone wrappers} (Issue \#3054):
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_upper\_bound\_of\_Q\_and\_circle} and
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_sandwich\_of\_Q\_and\_circle} mirror the CE-route Props
one-step wrappers, allowing a single Q-circle assembler (with ne-zero parts
assembled from Q-bounds) to deliver the named upper-bound predicate or the
displayed two-sided sandwich directly.
\textbf{Unconditional per-fixed-volume Cauchy Q-bound} (Issue \#3054,
\texttt{ComplexAnalyticity/SecondMomentBounds.lean}):
\texttt{hamiltonian\_sq\_le} gives the trivial pointwise bound
\(H(\sigma;G,p)^2\le(|J||E|+|h||\iota|)^2\) via \texttt{hamiltonian\_abs\_le};
\texttt{second\_moment\_bound\_trivial} upgrades to the Boltzmann-weighted form
\(\sum_\sigma\exp(-\beta_{\mathrm{re}}H)\,H^2\le(|J||E|+|h||\iota|)^2\,Z_{\mathbb{R}}\);
\texttt{partitionFunctionComplex\_ne\_zero\_of\_im\_lt\_h\_zero} composes with
PR \#3048 to give an unconditional per-fixed-volume \(Z_{\mathbb{C}}\ne0\) on a
complex disc of explicit radius \(\sqrt{2}/(|J||E|)\) at \(h=0\).
\texttt{partitionFunctionComplex\_ne\_zero\_on\_closedBall\_h\_zero\_at\_zero}
packages the explicit form: for
\(r\cdot(|J||E|)<\sqrt{2}\), \(Z_{\mathbb{C}}\ne0\) on the entire
\(\overline{B}(0,r)\) — the exact \texttt{hZk}/\texttt{hZk1} shape required by
PR \#3032 with explicit unconditional radius. Closes the Cauchy-route at the
per-fixed-volume level unconditionally; volume-uniform requires sharper
connected-correlation Q-bounds (cluster expansion).
\textbf{Unconditional per-stage dist composition} (Issue \#3054):
\texttt{dist\_deriv\_correlationAlongExhaustion\_le\_at\_zero\_beta\_unconditional}
directly composes the unconditional ne-zero with PR \#3032 to give an
unconditional per-stage
\(d(\partial_\beta c_k,\partial_\beta c_{k+1})\le B/r\) at \(\beta=0\) — only
conditional on the user-supplied circle bound \(B\) on
\(\partial B((0:\mathbb{R}):\mathbb{C},r)\). The explicit smallness
\(r\cdot(|J||E_k|)<\sqrt{2}\) (and same for stage \(k+1\)) is \textbf{unconditional}.
\textbf{General-\(\beta_0\) ne-zero} (Issue \#3054):
\texttt{partitionFunctionComplex\_ne\_zero\_on\_closedBall\_h\_zero\_at\_real\_beta}
generalises PR \#3081 to a closedBall centred at any real \(\beta_0\); the
smallness on \(\beta.\mathrm{im}\) is independent of \(\beta.\mathrm{re}\), so
shifting the centre is free.
\textbf{Canonical trivial-Q radius} (Issue \#3054):
\texttt{trivialQRadius}\(\,G\,J:=\sqrt{2}/(|J||E|+1)\) is the canonical
positive explicit radius always satisfying the trivial-Q smallness, with
\texttt{trivialQRadius\_pos} / \texttt{trivialQRadius\_smallness} witnesses;
\texttt{partitionFunctionComplex\_ne\_zero\_on\_trivialQRadius\_closedBall\_h\_zero\_\{at\_zero,at\_real\_beta\}}
provide unconditional \(Z_{\mathbb{C}}\ne0\) on the canonical disc, centred at
\(0\) or any real \(\beta_0\).
\textbf{Auto-assembling Cauchy bundle constructor}:
\texttt{CERouteIccGeometricIncrement\_of\_trivial\_Q\_smallness\_h\_zero} uses
the general-\(\beta_0\) ne-zero to auto-supply the per-stage
\texttt{hZk}/\texttt{hZk1} parts of the bundle from explicit
\(r\cdot(|J||E_k|)<\sqrt{2}\) smallness conditions; the user only supplies
\((r,B,\text{smallness},\text{sphere bound})\) — \textbf{no cluster expansion
required for ne-zero}.
\textbf{One-step Lemma 17.5.2 from trivial-Q smallness + circle} (Issue \#3054):
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_upper\_bound\_of\_trivial\_Q\_smallness\_h\_zero} and
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_sandwich\_of\_trivial\_Q\_smallness\_h\_zero} directly
compose the auto-assembling bundle with the final-capstone wrappers, delivering
\texttt{Lemma\_17\_5\_2\_UpperBound} / two-sided sandwich
\textbf{fully unconditional in the ne-zero direction}; only the Simon-Lieb
sphere circle bound \(B\) (Issue \#3044) remains as an external input.
\textbf{Auto-radius via canonical pair-stage trivial-Q radius} (Issue \#3054):
\texttt{canonicalTrivialQRadiusPair}\(\Lambda\,J\,k:=\min(\texttt{trivialQRadius}\,G_k\,J,\texttt{trivialQRadius}\,G_{k+1}\,J)\)
satisfies smallness for both stages \(k\) and \(k+1\) simultaneously;
\texttt{CERouteIccGeometricIncrement\_of\_canonical\_radius\_circle}
auto-supplies the radius and smallness from this canonical choice.
\textbf{One-step canonical-radius Lemma 17.5.2 wrappers} (Issue \#3054):
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_upper\_bound\_of\_canonical\_radius\_circle} and
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_sandwich\_of\_canonical\_radius\_circle} compose the
auto-radius bundle constructor with the upper-bound/sandwich consumers; the
user supplies only the canonical-radius sphere circle bound \(B\) (and, for
sandwich, the endpoint pseudo-mass decay).
\textbf{Sphere circle bound via direct triangle inequality} (Issue \#3054):
\texttt{sphere\_circle\_bound\_of\_real\_inc\_and\_lipschitz} supplies
\(\|\mathrm{corr}_{\mathbb{C}}\,G_k(w)-\mathrm{corr}_{\mathbb{C}}\,G_{k+1}(w)\|
\le R_{\mathrm{inc}}+(C_k+C_{k+1})\,r\) from a real-axis value increment bound
\(R_{\mathrm{inc}}\) on \([\beta_0-r,\beta_0+r]\) and per-stage Lipschitz
hypotheses \(C_k,C_{k+1}\). Bypasses the vertex-type incompatibility that
prevents direct use of
\texttt{correlationComplex\_diff\_norm\_le\_real\_diff\_plus\_lipschitz}
(PR \#3050) by manual triangle inequality + per-graph real-axis cast
(\texttt{correlation\_ofReal\_eq\_correlationComplex} with
\((0{:}\mathbb{R}){:}\mathbb{C}\to(0{:}\mathbb{C})\) simp normalisation).
\textbf{Canonical-radius bundle from \(R_{\mathrm{inc}}\) + Lipschitz} (Issue
\#3054):
\texttt{CERouteIccGeometricIncrement\_of\_canonical\_radius\_R\_inc\_lipschitz}
composes the sphere bridge with the canonical-radius bundle constructor; the
user supplies only the Cauchy-route mathematical inputs
\((R_{\mathrm{inc}},C_k,C_{k+1})\) with
\((R_{\mathrm{inc}}+(C_k+C_{k+1})r)/r\le M\cdot\mathrm{ratio}^k\), real-axis
increment bound, and per-stage Lipschitz — no smallness, no ne-zero, no
sphere bound.
\textbf{One-step \(R_{\mathrm{inc}}\) + Lipschitz Lemma 17.5.2 wrappers} (Issue
\#3054):
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_upper\_bound\_of\_R\_inc\_lipschitz} and
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_sandwich\_of\_R\_inc\_lipschitz} compose this bundle
constructor with the upper-bound/sandwich consumers; the entry point to
Lemma 17.5.2 endpoints is now directly the Cauchy-route mathematical inputs
per \((\beta, k)\) without any structural witness construction.
\textbf{Uniform-C bundle constructor} (Issue \#3054):
\texttt{CERouteIccGeometricIncrement\_of\_canonical\_radius\_R\_inc\_uniform\_C}
is the convenience specialisation where a single \(C\ge0\) bounds both
stages' Lipschitz (\(C_k=C_{k+1}=C\)); the user supplies \((R_{\mathrm{inc}},C)\)
instead of \((R_{\mathrm{inc}},C_k,C_{k+1})\).
\textbf{One-step uniform-C Lemma 17.5.2 wrappers} (Issue \#3054):
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_upper\_bound\_of\_R\_inc\_uniform\_C} and
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_sandwich\_of\_R\_inc\_uniform\_C} compose this with
the upper-bound/sandwich consumers, giving the simplest entry to Lemma 17.5.2
endpoints — only per-\((\beta,k)\) \((R_{\mathrm{inc}},C)\) inputs (plus
\texttt{hdecay} for sandwich).
\textbf{Explicit \texttt{latticeGraph} lower bound for the canonical radius}
(Issue \#3054):
\texttt{trivialQRadius\_inducedLatticeGraph\_lower\_bound} gives
\(\texttt{trivialQRadius}\,G\,J\ge\sqrt{2}/(|J|\,d\,|\Lambda|+1)\) via
\texttt{inducedLatticeGraph\_card\_edgeFinset\_le} (\(|E|\le d|\Lambda|\)).
\texttt{canonicalTrivialQRadiusPair\_lower\_bound\_volume\_succ} gives the
per-stage analogue
\(\ge\sqrt{2}/(|J|\,d\,|\Lambda_{k+1}|+1)\) via exhaustion monotonicity.
\textbf{Sequence-form bundle constructor} (Issue \#3054):
\texttt{CERouteIccGeometricIncrement\_of\_canonical\_radius\_sequence} is the
convenience specialisation where \(R_{\mathrm{inc}}, C:\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{R}\)
are sequences depending only on the stage \(k\) — closes the per-\((\beta,k)\)
existential automatically.
\textbf{Sequence-form one-step Lemma 17.5.2 wrappers} (Issue \#3054):
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_upper\_bound\_of\_sequence} and
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_sandwich\_of\_sequence} compose the sequence-form
bundle constructor with the upper-bound/sandwich consumers; the user supplies
the constants as sequences directly.
\textbf{Geometric-form bundle constructor} (Issue \#3054):
\texttt{CERouteIccGeometricIncrement\_of\_canonical\_radius\_geometric}
further specialises to \(R_{\mathrm{inc},k}:=M_R\rho_R^k\) (geometric) and
\(C_k:=C\) (constant).
\textbf{Geometric-form one-step Lemma 17.5.2 wrappers} (Issue \#3054):
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_upper\_bound\_of\_geometric} and
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_sandwich\_of\_geometric} deliver the named
upper-bound predicate / two-sided sandwich directly from the parametric form
\((M_R, \rho_R, C)\) plus the corresponding bounds.
\textbf{Poly-geometric CE-route bundle family} (Issue \#3054):
\texttt{CERouteIccPolyGeometricIncrement} +
\texttt{hincr\_of\_CERouteIccPolyGeometricIncrement} +
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_\{upper\_bound,capstone\}\_of\_CERouteIccPolyGeometricIncrement}
use the realistic boundary-prefactor form \(B/r\le M\cdot(2k+3)^d\cdot\mathrm{ratio}^k\)
matching the cubic real-axis increment.
\textbf{Poly-geometric Q / canonical-radius constructors} (Issue \#3054):
\texttt{CERouteIccPolyGeometricIncrement\_of\_Q\_and\_circle} (Cauchy mirror),
\texttt{...of\_trivial\_Q\_smallness\_h\_zero} (auto-supplies ne-zero via
trivial-Q), and \texttt{...of\_canonical\_radius\_circle} (auto-radius)
mirror the regular-geometric variants for the poly·geometric form.
\textbf{Poly-geometric R\_inc + Lipschitz constructor} (Issue \#3054):
\texttt{CERouteIccPolyGeometricIncrement\_of\_canonical\_radius\_R\_inc\_lipschitz}
mirrors the regular-geometric \texttt{...\_R\_inc\_lipschitz} for the
poly\(\cdot\)geometric prefactor form
\(B/r\le M\cdot(2k+3)^d\cdot\mathrm{ratio}^k\); only the Cauchy-route
inputs \((R_{\mathrm{inc}}, C_k, C_{k+1})\) per \((\beta, k)\) are
required, sphere circle bound auto-supplied via the bridge.
\textbf{End-to-end poly-geometric R\_inc + Lipschitz wrappers} (Issue \#3054):
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_\{upper\_bound,capstone\}\_of\_R\_inc\_lipschitz\_poly\_geometric}
compose the bundle constructor with the poly-geometric consumers, yielding
\texttt{Lemma\_17\_5\_2\_UpperBound} / two-sided sandwich directly from the
per-\((\beta, k)\) Cauchy-route mathematical inputs.
\textbf{Poly-geometric uniform-C constructor + end-to-end wrappers} (Issue \#3054):
\texttt{CERouteIccPolyGeometricIncrement\_of\_canonical\_radius\_R\_inc\_uniform\_C}
specialises to single \(C\ge 0\) Lipschitz; user supplies \((R_{\mathrm{inc}}, C)\)
with \((R_{\mathrm{inc}} + 2Cr)/r \le M(2k+3)^d\mathrm{ratio}^k\).
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_\{upper\_bound,capstone\}\_of\_R\_inc\_uniform\_C\_poly\_geometric}
compose with the poly-geometric consumers --- the simplest entry to Lemma 17.5.2.
\textbf{Poly-geometric sequence-form constructor + end-to-end wrappers} (Issue \#3054):
\texttt{CERouteIccPolyGeometricIncrement\_of\_canonical\_radius\_sequence}
specialises with \(R_{\mathrm{inc}}, C : \mathbb{N} \to \mathbb{R}\) sequences.
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_\{upper\_bound,capstone\}\_of\_sequence\_poly\_geometric}
compose with the poly-geometric consumers.
\textbf{Poly-geometric geometric-form constructor + end-to-end wrappers} (Issue \#3054):
\texttt{CERouteIccPolyGeometricIncrement\_of\_canonical\_radius\_geometric}
specialises further to \(R_{\mathrm{inc}, k} = M_R \rho_R^k\), \(C\) constant.
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_\{upper\_bound,capstone\}\_of\_geometric\_poly\_geometric}
compose with the poly-geometric consumers --- simplest parametric API.
\textbf{Fully-concrete CE-route capstones via \(\mathrm{pseudoMass} \le -\log(\beta_2 J \cdot 2d)\)}
(Issue \#3054):
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_capstone\_of\_CERouteIcc\{,Poly\}GeometricIncrement\_and\_pseudoMass\_le\_rate}
compose the CE-route bundles with the
\texttt{...\_and\_pseudoMass\_le\_rate} consumers (Issue \#2931). Both sides of
the Lemma 17.5.2 sandwich are driven by concrete scalar inputs.
\textbf{Geometric-form fully-concrete capstones} (Issue \#3054):
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_capstone\_of\_geometric\{,\_poly\_geometric\}\_and\_pseudoMass\_le\_rate}
compose the geometric-form bundle constructors with the fully-concrete capstones,
giving Lemma 17.5.2 sandwich directly from \((M_R, \rho_R, C) + h_{le}\).
\textbf{Sequence-form fully-concrete capstones} (Issue \#3054):
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_capstone\_of\_sequence\{,\_poly\_geometric\}\_and\_pseudoMass\_le\_rate}
same with sequences \(R_{\mathrm{inc}}, C : \mathbb{N} \to \mathbb{R}\).
\textbf{Uniform-C fully-concrete capstones} (Issue \#3054):
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_capstone\_of\_R\_inc\_uniform\_C\{,\_poly\_geometric\}\_and\_pseudoMass\_le\_rate}
per-\((\beta, k)\) \((R_{\mathrm{inc}}, C)\) with single Lipschitz \(C\).
\textbf{R\_inc + Lipschitz fully-concrete capstones} (Issue \#3054):
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_capstone\_of\_R\_inc\_lipschitz\{,\_poly\_geometric\}\_and\_pseudoMass\_le\_rate}
general per-stage \((R_{\mathrm{inc}}, C_k, C_{k+1})\).
\textbf{Canonical-radius circle fully-concrete capstones} (Issue \#3054):
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_capstone\_of\_canonical\_radius\_circle\{,\_poly\_geometric\}\_and\_pseudoMass\_le\_rate}
simplest entry: only per-\((\beta, k)\) sphere circle bound \(B\).
\textbf{Trivial-Q smallness fully-concrete capstones} (Issue \#3054):
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_capstone\_of\_trivial\_Q\_smallness\_h\_zero\{,\_poly\_geometric\}\_and\_pseudoMass\_le\_rate}
per-\((\beta, k)\) \((r, B, \mathrm{smallness}, \mathrm{sphere bound})\).
\textbf{Q-and-circle fully-concrete capstones} (Issue \#3054):
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_capstone\_of\_Q\_and\_circle\{,\_poly\_geometric\}\_and\_pseudoMass\_le\_rate}
Cauchy Q-input entry: per-\((\beta, k)\) \((R, B, \mathrm{closedBall}~Z\neq 0~\mathrm{for}~k\&k+1, \mathrm{sphere bound})\).
\textbf{Abs form of cubic real-axis increment} (Issue \#3054):
\texttt{abs\_correlationAlongExhaustion\_cubic\_succ\_sub\_le\_poly\_pow}
in \texttt{CubicShellDecaySum.lean} gives
\(|c_k - c_{k+1}| \le \beta J \cdot 2d (2(k+1)+1)^d \cdot \mathrm{cf}^{(k+1-R)/(r_0+2)}\)
via ferromagnetic monotonicity.
\textbf{Cubic abs in direct correlation form} (Issue \#3054):
\texttt{abs\_correlation\_inducedGraph\_cubic\_succ\_sub\_le\_poly\_pow}
rewrites the same bound in the direct
\texttt{correlation (inducedGraph (latticeGraph d) (cubicBox d \_)) \(\langle\)J, 0, \(\beta\rangle\) ...}
shape required by the \(h_{\mathrm{real\_inc}}\) slots of the poly-geometric
CE-route bundle constructors.
\textbf{High-temperature simplification} (Issue \#3054):
\texttt{abs\_correlation\_inducedGraph\_cubic\_succ\_sub\_le\_poly\_pow\_high\_temp}
absorbs the \(\beta J\cdot 2d\) prefactor under \(\beta J\cdot 2d \le 1\),
leaving the clean \((2k+3)^d \cdot \mathrm{cf}^{(k+1-R)/(r_0+2)}\) form.
\textbf{Adjacent vertex in next cubicBox} (Issue \#3054):
\texttt{cubicBox\_succ\_of\_latticeGraph\_adj} gives
\(r \in \mathrm{cubicBox}_R \wedge \mathrm{adj}(r, y) \Rightarrow y \in \mathrm{cubicBox}_{R+1}\).
Combinatorial building block for separation discharge.
\textbf{Separation hypothesis discharge from \(R + 1 \le k\)} (Issue \#3054):
\texttt{not\_sym2\_mem\_straddle\_of\_cubicBox\_R\_succ\_le\_k} and
\texttt{hsep\_of\_cubicBox\_R\_succ\_le\_k} automatically discharge the
\(\mathrm{hsep}\) separation hypothesis required by the cubic per-stage
increment bounds, given only \(r, s \in \mathrm{cubicBox}_R\) and \(R + 1 \le k\).
\textbf{Floor-power \(\to\) geometric bound} (Issue \#3054):
\texttt{cf\_pow\_natDiv\_le\_geometric} gives, for \(0 < \mathrm{cf} < 1\)
and \(m \ge 1\), \(\mathrm{cf}^{\lfloor n/m \rfloor} \le (1/\mathrm{cf}) \cdot \rho^n\)
where \(\rho := \mathrm{cf}^{1/m}\), \(0 < \rho < 1\).
Step A of the cubic CE-route plumbing.
\textbf{Cubic abs in clean geometric high-temperature form} (Issue \#3054, A+B composition):
\texttt{abs\_correlation\_inducedGraph\_cubic\_succ\_sub\_le\_geometric\_high\_temp}
gives \(|c_k - c_{k+1}| \le (1/\mathrm{cf}) \cdot (2k+3)^d \cdot \rho_R^{k+1-R}\)
with \(\rho_R := \mathrm{cf}^{1/(r_0+2)} \in (0, 1)\) --- direct poly\(\cdot\)geometric
shape for the \(R_{\mathrm{inc\_seq}}\) input slot.
\textbf{Cubic abs uniformized via \(\mathrm{cf\_max}\)} (Issue \#3054, Step C):
\texttt{abs\_correlation\_inducedGraph\_cubic\_succ\_sub\_le\_uniform\_cf\_max}
replaces \(\mathrm{cf}(\beta_{re})\) by a uniform upper bound, giving
\(R_{\mathrm{inc\_seq}}(k) := (2k+3)^d \cdot \mathrm{cf\_max}^{(k+1-R)/(r_0+2)}\)
--- \(\beta_{re}\)-independent shape for the \(h_{\mathrm{real\_inc}}\) slot.
\textbf{correlationComplex Lipschitz on closedBall} (Issue \#3054, Step D-light):
\texttt{correlationComplex\_lipschitz\_on\_closedBall} gives
\(\|c_{\mathbb{C}}(w) - c_{\mathbb{C}}(b)\| \le C \cdot \|w - b\|\) for
\(w, b \in \overline{B(\beta_0, R)}\), given \(Z_{\mathbb{C}} \ne 0\) on the
closed disc and an explicit deriv bound \(C\). Direct
\(h_{\mathrm{lip}}\)-slot provider.
\textbf{Cubic abs uniform geometric high-temperature} (Issue \#3054, Step A + Step C):
\texttt{abs\_correlation\_inducedGraph\_cubic\_succ\_sub\_le\_uniform\_geometric\_high\_temp}
gives \(|c_k - c_{k+1}| \le (1/\mathrm{cf\_max}) \cdot (2k+3)^d \cdot \rho_{R,\max}^{k+1-R}\)
with \(\rho_{R,\max} := \mathrm{cf\_max}^{1/(r_0+2)} \in (0, 1)\)
--- \(\beta_{re}\)-independent and fully geometric.
\textbf{CE-route scope clarification + direct increment API}
(refactor Direction 1+3 per codex strategic review, PRs \#3125--\#3126):
the CE-route bundles are documented as structural bridges from per-\((\beta, k)\)
complex circle bound to summable increment, not per-fixed-volume capstones.
\texttt{CERouteIccDirect\{,Poly\}GeometricIncrement} and the
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_\{upper\_bound,capstone\}\_of\_CERouteIccDirect\{,Poly\}GeometricIncrement}
pass-throughs to \texttt{IncrementCapstone.lean} provide the explicit
direct-increment entry point for users not going through Cauchy.
Both inputs remain open at the volume-uniform level (complex cluster-expansion
convergence is research-level).
\end{theorem}

The along-exhaustion and concrete $\mathbb{Z}^d$
\texttt{partitionFunction}/\texttt{freeEnergy} Continuous and Differentiable
wrappers now live in matching \texttt{PartitionFreeEnergyRegularity.lean}
child modules; the $\Lambda$-layer wrappers remain in
\path{IsingModel/AmbientLattice/Analyticity.lean}.

The along-exhaustion and concrete $\mathbb{Z}^d$ high-temperature
\texttt{partitionFunction}/\texttt{freeEnergy} capstone wrappers now live in
matching \texttt{HighTemperatureCapstones.lean} child modules; the
$\Lambda$-layer wrappers live in
\path{IsingModel/AmbientLattice/AnalyticityLambdaCapstones.lean}
(narrowed out of \texttt{Analyticity.lean} in PR~\#1993).

The along-exhaustion and concrete $\mathbb{Z}^d$ Mayer filter-connected and
epsilon-power wrappers now live in matching
\texttt{MayerFilterConnected.lean} child modules; the $\Lambda$-layer wrappers
live in
\path{IsingModel/AmbientLattice/AnalyticityLambdaCapstones.lean}
(narrowed out of \texttt{Analyticity.lean} in PR~\#1993).

The along-exhaustion and concrete $\mathbb{Z}^d$ magnetization regularity
wrappers now live in matching \texttt{MagnetizationRegularity.lean} child
modules; the $\Lambda$-layer wrappers remain in
\path{IsingModel/AmbientLattice/Defs.lean}.

The along-exhaustion and concrete $\mathbb{Z}^d$ magnetization convergence
wrappers now live in matching \texttt{MagnetizationConvergence.lean} child
modules; the general $\Lambda$-layer wrappers remain in
\path{IsingModel/AmbientLattice/Defs.lean}, and the concrete $\Lambda$-direct
wrappers remain in the legacy concrete module.

The along-exhaustion and concrete $\mathbb{Z}^d$ susceptibility convergence
wrappers now live in matching \texttt{SusceptibilityConvergence.lean} child
modules; the general $\Lambda$-layer wrappers remain in
\path{IsingModel/AmbientLattice/Defs.lean}, and the concrete $\Lambda$-direct
wrappers remain in the legacy concrete module.

\path{IsingModel/Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/TwoPointMagnetizationMonotone.lean}

and \texttt{truncated4Infinite\_latticeGraph\_swap\_\{ij,jk,kl\}}
symmetry wrappers now live in

$\mathbb{Z}^d$ \texttt{truncated3/4TwoPoint} trivial-slice wrappers
(\texttt{truncated3TwoPoint\_h\_zero\_of\_distinct},
\texttt{\_J\_zero\_of\_distinct}, \texttt{\_beta\_zero},

\path{IsingModel/Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/TwoPointTruncatedTrivialSlices.lean}
(narrowed out of \path{TwoPoint.lean} in PR~\#2028). The 3
$\mathbb{Z}^d$ \texttt{twoPointFunction} trivial-slice wrappers

The corresponding ambient along-exhaustion closed forms and h-symmetry wrappers
remain in \path{IsingModel/AmbientLattice/SpecialCases/FreeEnergy.lean}.
