\texttt{freeEnergyAlongExhaustion\_upper\_bound} (PR \#122).
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{BddAbove\_freeEnergyAlongExhaustion\_range}]
Under \texttt{BoundedEdgeDensity},
$\operatorname{BddAbove}(\operatorname{range}(\texttt{freeEnergyAlongExhaustion}\,G\,\Lambda\,p))$.
for empty $\Lambda_n$, $f_n = 0$ by definition. Hence
$\max\{0, \log 2 + |\beta|(|J|c+|h|)\}$ is a uniform upper bound.
\end{theorem}

\subsection{Cor 4.3.5 (inductive n-point at \texorpdfstring{$h = 0$}{h = 0}) at infinite volume}

Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, \textit{Quantum Physics}, 2nd ed.\ (1987),
\S 4.3 Corollary 4.3.5, p.\ 62.

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{correlationInfinite\_cor\_4\_3\_5\_h0}]
For a ferromagnetic system $(J \ge 0, \beta > 0)$ with $h = 0$,
$S : \texttt{Finset}\,V$, $j, k : V$, $j \notin S$, $k \notin S$, and
$j \ne k$,
\[
\langle \sigma^{\{j, k\} \cup S} \rangle_\infty
  \le \langle \sigma^S \rangle_\infty \langle \sigma^{\{j, k\}} \rangle_\infty
  + \sum_{T \in \mathcal{P}(S)}
    \langle \sigma^{\{j\} \cup T} \rangle_\infty
    \langle \sigma^{\{k\} \cup (S \setminus T)} \rangle_\infty.
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The along-exhaustion left-hand and right-hand sequences satisfy
\texttt{Tendsto}
(\texttt{tendsto\_finset\_sum} + \texttt{Tendsto.mul/add}).
For each $n$ such that $\{j, k\} \cup S \subseteq \Lambda.\text{volume}\,n$,
apply the finite-volume \texttt{cor\_4\_3\_5\_h0} theorem to
\texttt{inducedGraph}\,$G\,\Lambda(n)$. Use
\texttt{liftFinset\_insert} and \texttt{liftFinset\_sdiff} (PR \#107) for
compound set identifications.
\texttt{Finset.sum\_bij} reindexes $S.powerset \leftrightarrow S'.powerset$
($S' := \texttt{liftFinset}\,S$).
For other $n$, the left-hand side is $0 \le$ the right-hand side, since each
term is nonnegative. Finish with \texttt{le\_of\_tendsto\_of\_tendsto'}.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Edge-set decomposition for disjoint sum graphs (\texttt{SumGraph.lean})}

The file \texttt{IsingModel/SumGraph.lean} records the combinatorial
identities for \texttt{SimpleGraph.sum} ($G \oplus_g H$) that underlie
the super-additivity argument of Glimm--Jaffe \S 4.6 (pp.\ 70ff.):
the free-energy density on a disjoint union of lattices decomposes
additively into the two factor contributions, which in turn rests on
a disjoint splitting of the edge set.

\begin{lemma}[\texttt{SimpleGraph.sum\_eq\_map\_sup}]
For $G : \mathrm{SimpleGraph}\,V$ and $H : \mathrm{SimpleGraph}\,W$,
\[
  G \oplus_g H
    = G.\mathrm{map}(\mathrm{inl}_{V,W})
      \sqcup H.\mathrm{map}(\mathrm{inr}_{V,W}),
\]
where $\mathrm{inl}_{V,W} : V \hookrightarrow V \oplus W$ and
$\mathrm{inr}_{V,W} : W \hookrightarrow V \oplus W$ are the canonical
embeddings.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Case analysis on $a, b \in V \oplus W$: both sides evaluate
identically on \texttt{Adj}.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[\texttt{SimpleGraph.edgeSet\_sum}]
\[
  (G \oplus_g H).E
    = \mathrm{inl}_*(G.E) \,\cup\, \mathrm{inr}_*(H.E),
\]
where $\mathrm{inl}_*, \mathrm{inr}_*$ denote the induced maps on
$\mathrm{Sym}^2$. The two summands are disjoint
(\texttt{disjoint\_sumInlEmb\_sumInrEmb\_edgeSet}).
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Apply \texttt{edgeSet\_sup} and \texttt{edgeSet\_map} to the previous
lemma. Disjointness follows from $\mathrm{inl}\,v \ne \mathrm{inr}\,w$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[\texttt{SimpleGraph.card\_edgeFinset\_sum}]
If $V, W$ are finite and $G, H$ have decidable adjacency, then
\[
  \#(G \oplus_g H).\mathrm{edgeFinset}
    = \#G.\mathrm{edgeFinset} + \#H.\mathrm{edgeFinset}.
\]
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Disjoint union of Finset images. The Finset identity
\texttt{edgeFinset\_sum} is derived from the set-level
\texttt{edgeSet\_sum} by \texttt{Finset.coe\_injective}. Cardinality
then follows from \texttt{Finset.card\_union\_of\_disjoint} combined
with \texttt{Finset.card\_map} applied to the injective
$\mathrm{Embedding.sym2Map}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[\texttt{SimpleGraph.induce\_sum\_map\_sumCompl\_eq\_deleteEdges} (Issue \#2965)]
For a predicate $p$ on the vertices, deleting every edge whose endpoints lie on
different sides of $p$ decomposes $G$ into the disjoint sum of the two induced
subgraphs, up to the relabeling $\mathrm{Equiv.sumCompl}\,p
: \{a\mid p\,a\}\oplus\{a\mid\neg p\,a\}\simeq V$:
\[
  \bigl((G\,\mathrm{induce}\,\{a\mid p\,a\})
    \oplus_g (G\,\mathrm{induce}\,\{a\mid\neg p\,a\})\bigr)
    .\mathrm{map}\,(\mathrm{Equiv.sumCompl}\,p)
  = G.\mathrm{deleteEdges}\,\{e\mid e\text{ straddles }p\}.
\]
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
By edge extensionality: an edge $a\sim b$ survives the deletion iff
$G.\mathrm{Adj}\,a\,b$ and $a,b$ are on the same side of $p$, which is exactly
the adjacency of the transported disjoint sum (the $\mathrm{inl}$/$\mathrm{inr}$
arms of \texttt{SimpleGraph.sum} for same-side pairs, and $\bot$ for straddling
pairs). This is the structural identity behind the component factorization of a
fully separated (bond-deleted) finite-volume Ising system: cutting all bonds
across a partition yields two independent subsystems.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[\texttt{straddle\_not\_mem\_of\_same\_side} /
\texttt{deleteEdges\_straddle\_no\_cross}]
The straddle set contains no same-side edge: if $p\,a \leftrightarrow p\,b$ then
$s(a,b)$ is not deleted (feeds
\texttt{inducedGraph\_deleteEdges\_eq\_of\_not\_internal}). Dually, the
bond-deleted graph has no edge between the two sides: if $p\,a$ and $\neg p\,b$
then $G.\mathrm{deleteEdges}\,\{\text{straddle}\}$ has no $a\sim b$ edge (the
no-cross hypothesis for \texttt{correlation\_inducedGraph\_union\_inl\_of\_no\_cross}
applied to the bond-deleted graph).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Both unfold the straddle membership $s(a,b)\in\{e\mid\neg(p\!\leftrightarrow\!p)(e)\}
\Leftrightarrow \neg(p\,a\leftrightarrow p\,b)$ and the deleteEdges adjacency
$\mathrm{Adj}\wedge s(a,b)\notin\{\dots\}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[\texttt{SimpleGraph.deleteEdges\_filter\_edgeFinset\_eq}]
$G.\mathrm{deleteEdges}\,\uparrow(G.\mathrm{edgeFinset.filter}\,p)
  = G.\mathrm{deleteEdges}\,\{e\mid p\,e\}$: deleting only the \emph{edges}
satisfying $p$ equals deleting every $\mathrm{Sym}^2$ satisfying $p$, since
$\mathrm{deleteEdges}$ ignores non-edges (\texttt{deleteEdges\_eq\_inter\_edgeSet}).
This bridges the $\mathrm{Finset}$-edge-set form of the ball-boundary increment
(which takes $E_0:\mathrm{Finset}$) and the $\mathrm{Set}$-predicate form of the
component-factorization capstone, toward the per-stage exhaustion increment.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
By \texttt{deleteEdges\_eq\_inter\_edgeSet} on the right, then edge extensionality:
$e\in\uparrow(G.\mathrm{edgeFinset.filter}\,p)\Leftrightarrow e\in G.\mathrm{edgeSet}\wedge p\,e
\Leftrightarrow e\in\{e\mid p\,e\}\cap G.\mathrm{edgeSet}$.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Context.} This file is the combinatorial Step~1 toward the
Glimm--Jaffe \S 4.6 super-additivity proof of thermodynamic-limit
convergence of the free-energy density. Subsequent steps
(\texttt{Config}-product equivalence, Hamiltonian splitting,
partition-function multiplicativity, and $\log Z$ super-additivity)
will consume the present lemmas to lift the Fekete-type convergence
of $f_{\Lambda}$ from the current \emph{discretized} subgraph
setting (\texttt{freeEnergy\_convergent\_subgraph}) to the genuine
infinite-volume exhaustion of the ambient lattice framework.

\subsection{Hamiltonian additivity on the disjoint sum graph (\texttt{SumModel.lean})}

With the combinatorial edge-set decomposition from \texttt{SumGraph.lean}
in hand (\S 0099), the Ising Hamiltonian on the disjoint sum graph
splits additively with respect to the \texttt{Sum.elim} decomposition
of configurations.

\begin{lemma}[\texttt{IsingModel.Config.sumEquiv}]
$\mathrm{Config}(\iota \oplus \iota')
  \simeq \mathrm{Config}\,\iota \times \mathrm{Config}\,\iota'$,
given by \texttt{Equiv.sumArrowEquivProdArrow}.
\end{lemma}

\begin{lemma}[\texttt{externalFieldEnergy\_sum}]
\[
  \sum_{i \in \iota \oplus \iota'}
    \mathrm{sign}(\mathrm{Sum.elim}\,\sigma_1\,\sigma_2\,i)
  = \sum_{i \in \iota} \mathrm{sign}(\sigma_1\,i)
    + \sum_{i \in \iota'} \mathrm{sign}(\sigma_2\,i),
\]
proved by \texttt{Fintype.sum\_sum\_type} together with the evaluation
of \texttt{Sum.elim} on \texttt{inl}/\texttt{inr}.
\end{lemma}

\begin{lemma}[\texttt{edgeSpin\_sumInl\_sym2Map} /
\texttt{edgeSpin\_sumInr\_sym2Map}]
For any $e \in \mathrm{Sym}^2\,\iota$,
\[
  \mathrm{edgeSpin}(\mathrm{Sum.elim}\,\sigma_1\,\sigma_2)
    (\mathrm{Sym}^2.\mathrm{map}\,\mathrm{Sum.inl}\,e)
  = \mathrm{edgeSpin}\,\sigma_1\,e,
\]
and analogously for \texttt{Sum.inr}.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proposition}[\texttt{interactionEnergy\_sum}]
For $G, H$ with finite edge sets, $J \in K$, and
$\sigma_1, \sigma_2$ configurations,
\[
  \mathrm{interactionEnergy}(G \oplus_g H)\,J\,(\mathrm{Sum.elim}\,\sigma_1\,\sigma_2)
  = \mathrm{interactionEnergy}\,G\,J\,\sigma_1
    + \mathrm{interactionEnergy}\,H\,J\,\sigma_2.
\]
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Combine the edge-finset decomposition \texttt{edgeFinset\_sum} (PR \#134)
with \texttt{Finset.sum\_union} (using
\texttt{disjoint\_inl\_inr\_edgeFinset} for the disjointness side
condition), then \texttt{Finset.sum\_map} and the per-edge pullbacks
\texttt{edgeSpin\_sumInl\_sym2Map} / \texttt{edgeSpin\_sumInr\_sym2Map}.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{hamiltonian\_sum}]
For $G, H$ with finite edge sets, and parameters $p = (J, h, \beta)$,
\[
  \mathcal{H}_{G \oplus_g H}(p)(\mathrm{Sum.elim}\,\sigma_1\,\sigma_2)
  = \mathcal{H}_G(p)(\sigma_1) + \mathcal{H}_H(p)(\sigma_2).
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
By additivity of each summand in
$\mathcal{H} = \mathrm{interactionEnergy} + \mathrm{externalFieldEnergy}$
(previous proposition and lemma).
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Step 4: partition function multiplicativity.}

\begin{proposition}[\texttt{IsingModel.partitionFunction\_sum}]
For finite lattices $G, H$ and parameters $p = (J, h, \beta)$ over $\mathbb{R}$,
\[
  Z_{G \oplus_g H}(p) = Z_G(p) \cdot Z_H(p).
\]
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Unfolding $Z = \sum_\sigma e^{-\beta \mathcal{H}}$,
\begin{align*}
  Z_{G \oplus_g H}(p)
  &= \sum_{\sigma : \iota \oplus \iota' \to \mathrm{Spin}}
       e^{-\beta \mathcal{H}_{G \oplus_g H}(\sigma)}
     && \text{(\texttt{Equiv.sum\_comp}\,\texttt{Config.sumEquiv.symm})} \\
  &= \sum_{\sigma_1, \sigma_2}
       e^{-\beta (\mathcal{H}_G(\sigma_1) + \mathcal{H}_H(\sigma_2))}
     && \text{(\texttt{Fintype.sum\_prod\_type}, \texttt{hamiltonian\_sum})} \\
  &= \sum_{\sigma_1, \sigma_2}
       e^{-\beta \mathcal{H}_G(\sigma_1)}
       \cdot e^{-\beta \mathcal{H}_H(\sigma_2)}
     && \text{(\texttt{mul\_add}, \texttt{Real.exp\_add})} \\
  &= Z_G(p) \cdot Z_H(p)
     && \text{(\texttt{Finset.sum\_mul\_sum}).}
\end{align*}
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[\texttt{IsingModel.log\_partitionFunction\_sum}]
\[
  \log Z_{G \oplus_g H}(p) = \log Z_G(p) + \log Z_H(p).
\]
Immediate from the proposition and
\texttt{partitionFunction\_pos} / \texttt{Real.log\_mul}.
\end{corollary}

\paragraph{Step 5 preparation: super-additivity inequality.}

\begin{proposition}[\texttt{IsingModel.partitionFunction\_mul\_le\_of\_sum\_le}]
Let $G'$ be any finite simple graph on $\iota \oplus \iota'$
satisfying $G \oplus_g H \le G'$. Then for ferromagnetic parameters
$p$,
\[
  Z_G(p) \cdot Z_H(p) \le Z_{G'}(p).
\]
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
By Step 4, $Z_{G \oplus_g H}(p) = Z_G(p) \cdot Z_H(p)$. The
existing \texttt{partitionFunction\_monotone\_subgraph} (proved in
\texttt{FreeEnergy.lean} via GKS-I) gives
$Z_{G \oplus_g H}(p) \le Z_{G'}(p)$ under ferromagnetic parameters.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[\texttt{IsingModel.log\_partitionFunction\_add\_le\_of\_sum\_le}]
Under the same assumptions,
\[
  \log Z_G(p) + \log Z_H(p) \le \log Z_{G'}(p).
\]
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Positivity of partition functions and \texttt{Real.log\_mul} /
\texttt{Real.log\_le\_log}.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Correlation factorization on the disjoint sum graph (Issue \#2965).}
The same $Z_{G\oplus_g H}=Z_G\cdot Z_H$ factorization, applied to the numerator,
shows that an observable supported on one component is unaffected by the disjoint
other component.

\begin{proposition}[\texttt{IsingModel.correlation\_sum\_inl} /
\texttt{correlation\_sum\_inr}]
For an observable supported on the first component,
\[
  \langle\sigma^{A}\rangle_{G\oplus_g H}
    = \langle\sigma^{A}\rangle_{G}
  \qquad(A\subseteq\iota,\ \text{mapped by }\mathrm{inl}),
\]
and symmetrically $\langle\sigma^{B}\rangle_{G\oplus_g H}=\langle\sigma^{B}\rangle_{H}$
for $B\subseteq\iota'$ mapped by $\mathrm{inr}$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
By \texttt{spinProduct\_map\_inl} the spin product of an $\mathrm{inl}$-supported
observable on $\mathrm{Sum.elim}\,\sigma_1\,\sigma_2$ depends only on $\sigma_1$.
The numerator $\sum_\sigma \sigma^{A}\,e^{-\beta H_{G\oplus_g H}}$ then factors
(via \texttt{Config.sumEquiv} and \texttt{hamiltonian\_sum}) as
$(\sum_{\sigma_1}\sigma^{A}\,e^{-\beta H_G})\cdot Z_H$, and dividing by
$Z_{G\oplus_g H}=Z_G\cdot Z_H$ cancels $Z_H$.
\end{proof}

This is the disjoint-sum factorization step. It is stated for the explicit
disjoint sum $G\oplus_g H$ (a single $\mathrm{inl}$/$\mathrm{inr}$ summand, which
need not itself be connected). Translating the bond-deletion increment
(\texttt{correlation\_sub\_deleteEdges\_le\_derivBound}) into a finite-volume
exhaustion-stage increment additionally requires identifying a separating
\texttt{deleteEdges} graph with such a box/exterior disjoint sum (a graph
isomorphism), which remains a separate step.

\paragraph{Closing Step 5.} Combining this super-additivity with the
uniform upper bound (PR \#122, \#123) and Fekete's lemma produces
convergence of
$f_\Lambda = (\log Z_\Lambda)/|\Lambda|$ along arbitrary
exhaustions of the ambient lattice, completing the proof of
Glimm--Jaffe \S 4.6 Prop 4.6.1. The remaining technical piece is
the type-level identification of an induced graph on
$\Lambda_1 \cup \Lambda_2$ with a supergraph of
$\mathrm{inducedGraph}\,G\,\Lambda_1 \oplus_g \mathrm{inducedGraph}\,G\,\Lambda_2$,
which is the subject of a subsequent PR.

\subsection{Partition function invariance under graph isomorphism
(\texttt{PartitionFunctionIso.lean})}

\begin{proposition}[\texttt{IsingModel.partitionFunction\_map\_equiv}]
Let $e \colon V \simeq W$ be a type equivalence and
$G \colon \mathrm{SimpleGraph}\,V$. Then the pushforward
$G.\mathrm{map}\,e.\mathrm{toEmbedding}$ has the same partition function:
\[
  Z_{G.\mathrm{map}\,e}(p) = Z_G(p).
\]
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Reindex $\sum_{\tau : W \to \mathrm{Spin}} \exp(-\beta \mathcal{H}_{G.\mathrm{map}\,e}(\tau))$ via
\texttt{Equiv.arrowCongr}\,$e$\,\texttt{Equiv.refl}, which puts it in
bijection with $\sum_{\sigma : V \to \mathrm{Spin}} \exp(-\beta \mathcal{H}_G(\sigma))$
using Hamiltonian invariance
\texttt{hamiltonian\_map\_equiv}: for $\sigma = \tau \circ e$,
$\mathcal{H}_{G.\mathrm{map}\,e}(\tau) = \mathcal{H}_G(\sigma)$. The
Hamiltonian invariance itself splits into invariance of the
interaction energy (via \texttt{edgeFinset\_map} + per-edge
spin pullback along $\mathrm{Sym}^2$-map) and invariance of the
external-field energy (\texttt{Fintype.sum\_equiv}).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[\texttt{IsingModel.correlation\_map\_equiv}]
Under the same hypotheses, pushing both the graph and the observable forward
along $e$ leaves the correlation unchanged:
\[
  \langle\sigma^{A.\mathrm{map}\,e}\rangle_{G.\mathrm{map}\,e}(p)
    = \langle\sigma^{A}\rangle_{G}(p).
\]
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
As for $Z$, reindex the numerator via \texttt{Equiv.arrowCongr}; the spin
product transports by \texttt{spinProduct\_map\_equiv}
($\sigma^{A.\mathrm{map}\,e}(\sigma\circ e^{-1})=\sigma^{A}(\sigma)$) and the
Boltzmann weight by \texttt{hamiltonian\_map\_equiv}, while the partition
function is invariant by \texttt{partitionFunction\_map\_equiv}; the
$Z^{-1}$ factors then match.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Purpose.} This is infrastructure for transporting an
\texttt{inducedGraph} on a disjoint Finset union
$\Lambda_1 \cup \Lambda_2$ back to a graph on the disjoint sum
type $\uparrow\Lambda_1 \oplus \uparrow\Lambda_2$
(via \texttt{Equiv.Finset.disjUnionEquiv}), so that the
super-additivity inequality of the previous step applies to
actual finite-volume exhaustions.

\subsection{Super-additivity on Finset disjoint union
(\texttt{AmbientLatticeSum.lean})}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.log\_partitionFunction\_inducedGraph\_disjUnion\_super\_additive}]
For $G \colon \mathrm{SimpleGraph}\,V$, disjoint
$\Lambda_1, \Lambda_2 : \mathrm{Finset}\,V$, and ferromagnetic $p$,
\[
  \log Z_{\mathrm{inducedGraph}\,G\,\Lambda_1}(p)
  + \log Z_{\mathrm{inducedGraph}\,G\,\Lambda_2}(p)
  \le \log Z_{\mathrm{inducedGraph}\,G\,(\Lambda_1 \cup \Lambda_2)}(p).
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Let $e = \mathrm{Equiv.Finset.union}\,\Lambda_1\,\Lambda_2\,\mathrm{hd}
  \colon \uparrow\!\Lambda_1 \oplus \uparrow\!\Lambda_2
     \simeq \uparrow\!(\Lambda_1 \cup \Lambda_2)$.
\begin{align*}
  \log Z_{\mathrm{inducedGraph}\,\Lambda_1}
  + \log Z_{\mathrm{inducedGraph}\,\Lambda_2}
  &= \log Z_{\mathrm{inducedGraph}\,\Lambda_1
      \oplus_g \mathrm{inducedGraph}\,\Lambda_2}
     & \text{(\S 0101 \texttt{log\_partitionFunction\_sum})} \\
  &= \log Z_{(\mathrm{inducedGraph}\,\Lambda_1
      \oplus_g \mathrm{inducedGraph}\,\Lambda_2).\mathrm{map}\,e}
     & \text{(\S 0103 \texttt{log\_partitionFunction\_map\_equiv})} \\
  &\le \log Z_{\mathrm{inducedGraph}\,(\Lambda_1 \cup \Lambda_2)}
     & \text{(ferromagnetic subgraph monotonicity,}
\end{align*}
using the key subgraph relation \texttt{inducedGraph\_sum\_map\_le\_union}:
every edge of $(\mathrm{inducedGraph}\,\Lambda_1
\oplus_g \mathrm{inducedGraph}\,\Lambda_2)\,.\mathrm{map}\,e$ is
either an $\mathrm{inl}$-image edge (both endpoints in $\Lambda_1$)
or an $\mathrm{inr}$-image edge (both in $\Lambda_2$), and in either
case it is an edge of $\mathrm{inducedGraph}\,G\,(\Lambda_1 \cup \Lambda_2)$.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Exact split with no cross edges (Issue \#2965).}
When in addition there is no $G$-edge between $\Lambda_1$ and $\Lambda_2$, the
subgraph relation becomes an equality.

\begin{proposition}[\texttt{IsingModel.inducedGraph\_sum\_map\_eq\_union\_of\_no\_cross}]
For disjoint $\Lambda_1,\Lambda_2$ with $\neg\,G.\mathrm{Adj}\,a\,b$ for all
$a\in\Lambda_1,b\in\Lambda_2$,
\[
  (\mathrm{inducedGraph}\,\Lambda_1\oplus_g\mathrm{inducedGraph}\,\Lambda_2)
    \,.\mathrm{map}\,e
  = \mathrm{inducedGraph}\,G\,(\Lambda_1\cup\Lambda_2).
\]
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The $\le$ direction is \texttt{inducedGraph\_sum\_map\_le\_union}; for $\ge$, an
edge $a\sim b$ of the induced union gives $G.\mathrm{Adj}\,a\,b$ with
$a,b\in\Lambda_1\cup\Lambda_2$, and the no-cross hypothesis rules out the split
cases, so both endpoints lie in the same part and the edge is an
$\mathrm{inl}$/$\mathrm{inr}$-image.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[\texttt{IsingModel.correlation\_inducedGraph\_sum\_map\_inl}]
For an observable $A$ supported on $\Lambda_1$,
\[
  \langle\sigma^{A}\rangle_{(\mathrm{inducedGraph}\,\Lambda_1
    \oplus_g\mathrm{inducedGraph}\,\Lambda_2).\mathrm{map}\,e}
  = \langle\sigma^{A}\rangle_{\mathrm{inducedGraph}\,\Lambda_1},
\]
by \texttt{correlation\_map\_equiv} (iso transport) and
\texttt{correlation\_sum\_inl} (disjoint-sum factorization). Together with the
equality above this is the component-factorization bridge from a fully separated
finite-volume system to the isolated-component correlation.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proposition}[\texttt{IsingModel.correlation\_inducedGraph\_union\_inl\_of\_no\_cross}]
Under the no-cross hypothesis, the same factorization holds directly on the
induced subgraph of the union:
\[
  \langle\sigma^{A}\rangle_{\mathrm{inducedGraph}\,G\,(\Lambda_1\cup\Lambda_2)}
  = \langle\sigma^{A}\rangle_{\mathrm{inducedGraph}\,G\,\Lambda_1}.
\]
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Transport the previous proposition across the graph equality
\texttt{inducedGraph\_sum\_map\_eq\_union\_of\_no\_cross} using
\texttt{correlation\_congr\_of\_eq}: correlation is invariant under graph
equality even across distinct \texttt{Fintype edgeSet} instances, since
$\mathrm{edgeFinset}$ coerces to the instance-free $\mathrm{edgeSet}$. This
absorbs the \texttt{Fintype} motive obstruction that blocks a direct
\texttt{rw}.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[\texttt{IsingModel.inducedGraph\_deleteEdges\_eq\_of\_not\_internal}]
If no edge of the deleted set $D$ has both endpoints in $S$, then
\[
  \mathrm{inducedGraph}\,(G.\mathrm{deleteEdges}\,D)\,S
    = \mathrm{inducedGraph}\,G\,S.
\]
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
An edge of the induced subgraph on $S$ joins two vertices of $S$, hence lies
inside $S$ and is never among $D$ (by hypothesis); so the induce-adjacency is
unchanged by the deletion. This is the Finset route to the bond-deletion
$\to$ isolated-component bridge: deleting the cut edges between $S$ and its
complement leaves the within-$S$ induced subgraph (hence its correlations)
intact, and the bond-deleted graph then has no cross edges, so
\texttt{correlation\_inducedGraph\_union\_inl\_of\_no\_cross} applies to it.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[\texttt{IsingModel.correlation\_induce\_univ}]
Since $G.\mathrm{induce}\,\mathrm{univ}\simeq_g G$ (mathlib \texttt{induceUnivIso},
via \texttt{Equiv.Set.univ}), pushing an observable along that relabeling
preserves the correlation:
\[
  \langle\sigma^{A}\rangle_{G.\mathrm{induce}\,\mathrm{univ}}
    = \langle\sigma^{A.\mathrm{map}\,e}\rangle_{G},\qquad e=\texttt{Equiv.Set.univ}.
\]
This connects the $\mathrm{inducedGraph}\,\_\,\mathrm{univ}$ left side of the
capstone below back to the raw graph $G$ (the form of the ball-boundary
increment), for the per-stage increment assembly.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The graph equality $(G.\mathrm{induce}\,\mathrm{univ}).\mathrm{map}\,e = G$ holds
by edge extensionality (the iso adjacency); then compose
\texttt{correlation\_map\_equiv} with \texttt{correlation\_congr\_of\_eq}.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[\texttt{IsingModel.correlation\_induce\_of\_forall\_mem}]
If $s$ contains every vertex ($\forall x,\ x\in s$), then
$\langle\sigma^{A}\rangle_{G.\mathrm{induce}\,s}
  = \langle\sigma^{A.\mathrm{map}\,e}\rangle_{G}$ with
$e=\texttt{Equiv.subtypeUnivEquiv}$. Generalizes the previous proposition from
$\mathrm{univ}$ to any full set, in particular $s=\uparrow(S\cup S^{c})$ (full by
\texttt{Finset.union\_compl}); this avoids forcing the propositional Finset
equality $S\cup S^{c}=\mathrm{univ}$ at the type level when connecting the
capstone's $\mathrm{inducedGraph}\,\_\,(S\cup S^{c})$ side to the raw bond-deleted
graph in the per-stage-increment assembly. Same proof scheme as
\texttt{correlation\_induce\_univ}.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proposition}[\texttt{IsingModel.correlation\_congr\_all}]
If $G_1=G_2$ then
$\langle\sigma^{A}\rangle_{G_1}=\langle\sigma^{A}\rangle_{G_2}$ regardless of the
vertex \texttt{Fintype} and \texttt{edgeSet Fintype} instances, since
\texttt{Fintype} is a \texttt{Subsingleton} (\texttt{Fintype.subsingleton}):
after \texttt{subst}, \texttt{Subsingleton.elim} equates the residual instances.
Strengthens \texttt{correlation\_congr\_of\_eq} and bridges the
\texttt{Finset.Subtype.fintype} (from \texttt{inducedGraph}) against the
\texttt{Set}-induce vertex \texttt{Fintype}.
\end{proposition}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.correlation\_deleteEdges\_straddle\_eq\_inducedGraph}
(per-stage-increment form)]
For $S\subseteq V$ and an $S$-supported observable $A$,
\[
  \langle\sigma^{A}\rangle_{G.\mathrm{deleteEdges}\,\{\text{straddle }S\}}
    = \langle\sigma^{A}\rangle_{\mathrm{inducedGraph}\,G\,S},
\]
i.e.\ the \emph{raw} bond-deleted graph's correlation of an $S$-internal
observable equals the isolated induced-subgraph correlation. Composes
\texttt{correlation\_induce\_of\_forall\_mem} (on the full set
$\uparrow(S\cup S^{c})$), \texttt{correlation\_congr\_all}, and the
component-factorization capstone below. It pairs with the ball-boundary
increment \texttt{correlation\_sub\_deleteEdges\_le\_derivBound} to give the
numeric per-stage increment
\texttt{correlation\_pair\_sub\_inducedGraph\_le\_derivBound} (below).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proposition}[\texttt{IsingModel.triple\_map\_subtypeUnivEquiv\_eq}]
The triple-mapped $S$-observable equals the raw subtype-inclusion image:
\[
  ((A.\mathrm{map}\,\mathrm{inl}).\mathrm{map}\,\mathrm{union})
    .\mathrm{map}\,\mathrm{subtypeUnivEquiv}
  = A.\mathrm{map}\,\langle\mathrm{Subtype.val},\cdot\rangle.
\]
By \texttt{Finset.map\_map} the three embeddings compose to the inclusion
$\uparrow S\hookrightarrow V$. This identifies the observable of
\texttt{correlation\_deleteEdges\_straddle\_eq\_inducedGraph} with the plain
$V$-vertex observable (e.g.\ $\{r,s\}$ for the pair), supplying the observable
match for the per-stage increment; together with
\texttt{deleteEdges\_filter\_edgeFinset\_eq} and \texttt{correlation\_congr\_all}
the remaining gap to the numeric increment is only the discharge of the
ball-boundary hypotheses for the straddle-edge finset (now carried out in the
theorem below).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proposition}[\texttt{IsingModel.inducedGraph\_induce\_preimage\_map\_eq}]
For $S\subseteq T$, the nested induced subgraph equals the direct one up to the
nested-subtype relabeling $\{x:\uparrow T\mid x.\mathrm{val}\in S\}\simeq\uparrow S$:
\[
  \bigl((\mathrm{inducedGraph}\,G\,T).\mathrm{induce}\,\{x\mid x.\mathrm{val}\in S\}\bigr)
    .\mathrm{map}\,\mathrm{nestedSubtypeEquiv}
  = \mathrm{inducedGraph}\,G\,S.
\]
An edge survives iff its (deep) endpoints are $G$-adjacent and both in $S$. This
is the graph-level foundation for instantiating the per-stage increment on cubic
exhaustion stages $\mathrm{box}_k\subseteq\mathrm{box}_{k+1}$ (Issue \#2965).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proposition}[\texttt{IsingModel.correlation\_inducedGraph\_induce\_preimage}]
The correlation companion: for $S\subseteq T$,
\[
  \langle\sigma^{A.\mathrm{map}\,e^{-1}}\rangle_{(\mathrm{inducedGraph}\,G\,T).\mathrm{induce}\,\{x\mid x.\mathrm{val}\in S\}}
  = \langle\sigma^{A}\rangle_{\mathrm{inducedGraph}\,G\,S},
  \qquad e=\mathrm{nestedSubtypeEquiv}.
\]
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Apply \texttt{correlation\_map\_equiv} to the \emph{direct} graph
$\mathrm{inducedGraph}\,G\,S$ with the inverse relabeling $e^{-1}$ (so the heavy
nested-subtype graph appears only as the map \emph{result}, never as the graph
the lemma operates on, which sidesteps the earlier \texttt{whnf} wall), then
bridge via \texttt{correlation\_congr\_all} and the graph equality
$(\mathrm{inducedGraph}\,G\,S).\mathrm{map}\,e^{-1}
= (\mathrm{inducedGraph}\,G\,T).\mathrm{induce}\,\{x\mid x.\mathrm{val}\in S\}$.
This supplies the $c_k$-side identity for the cubic-exhaustion per-stage
increment.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.correlation\_pair\_sub\_inducedGraph\_le\_derivBound}
(numeric per-stage correlation increment, Issue \#2965 Phase A)]
For $r,s\in S$, $r\neq s$, ferromagnetic parameters at $h=0$, with $r,s$ on no
cut (straddle) edge,
\[
  \langle\sigma_r\sigma_s\rangle_{G}
    - \langle\sigma_r\sigma_s\rangle_{\mathrm{inducedGraph}\,G\,S}
  \le \mathrm{derivBound}\,G\,(G.\mathrm{edgeFinset.filter}\,\mathrm{straddle})\,r\,s,
\]
i.e.\ the full-model pair correlation exceeds the isolated induced-subgraph pair
correlation by at most the ball-boundary $\mathrm{derivBound}$ over the cut edges.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Apply the ball-boundary bond-deletion increment
\texttt{correlation\_sub\_deleteEdges\_le\_derivBound} with
$E_0=G.\mathrm{edgeFinset.filter}\,\mathrm{straddle}$ (its hypotheses: edges are
non-diagonal, $E_0\subseteq G.\mathrm{edgeFinset}$, and the interior hypothesis
that $r,s$ avoid the cut), then rewrite the bond-deleted correlation via
\texttt{correlation\_deleteEdges\_filter\_pair\_eq}: by
\texttt{deleteEdges\_filter\_edgeFinset\_eq} the filtered-finset deletion equals
the straddle-set deletion, \texttt{correlation\_congr\_all} matches the
correlations, and \texttt{triple\_map\_subtypeUnivEquiv\_eq} /
\texttt{pair\_map\_val\_eq} identify the pair observable, so the
component-factorization capstone applies. Marking the straddle predicate
\texttt{@[reducible]} and stating the bridge as a separate lemma keeps the
elaboration within the heartbeat budget.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[\texttt{IsingModel.correlation\_inducedGraph\_nested\_finset}]
For nested finsets $T_1\subseteq T_2$ and an observable $A$ on $\uparrow T_1$,
\[
  \langle\sigma^{A.\mathrm{map}\,e^{-1}}\rangle_{\mathrm{inducedGraph}\,
    (\mathrm{inducedGraph}\,G\,T_2)\,(T_1.\mathrm{subtype}\,(\cdot\in T_2))}
  = \langle\sigma^{A}\rangle_{\mathrm{inducedGraph}\,G\,T_1},
\]
where $e=\mathrm{nestedFinsetEquiv}\colon \uparrow(T_1.\mathrm{subtype}\,(\cdot\in
T_2))\simeq\uparrow T_1$ is the double-subtype relabeling. This is the
concrete-layer companion of \texttt{correlation\_inducedGraph\_induce\_preimage}:
it uses the \texttt{Finset.subtype} slice $T_1.\mathrm{subtype}\,(\cdot\in T_2)$
rather than the $\mathrm{Set}$-predicate $\{x\mid x.\mathrm{val}\in T_1\}$, so it
matches the $S:\mathrm{Finset}\,\uparrow T_2$ region of the single-box increment
directly, sidestepping the $\uparrow S$ versus $\{x\mathbin{/\!/}x.\mathrm{val}\in
T_1\}$ subtype-type mismatch. Same proof scheme: apply
\texttt{correlation\_map\_equiv} to the \emph{direct} graph
$\mathrm{inducedGraph}\,G\,T_1$ with $e^{-1}$, then bridge via
\texttt{correlation\_congr\_all} and the graph equality (an edge survives the
double induce iff its deep endpoints are $G$-adjacent and both in $T_1$).
\end{proposition}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.correlation\_pair\_two\_box\_le\_derivBound}
(two-box per-stage increment, Issue \#2965 Phase A)]
For nested finsets $T_1\subseteq T_2$, a pair $r,s\in T_1$ with $r\neq s$,
ferromagnetic parameters at $h=0$, and $r,s$ on no cut edge of the $T_1$-slice,
\[
  \langle\sigma_r\sigma_s\rangle_{\mathrm{inducedGraph}\,G\,T_2}
    - \langle\sigma_r\sigma_s\rangle_{\mathrm{inducedGraph}\,G\,T_1}
  \le \mathrm{derivBound}\,(\mathrm{inducedGraph}\,G\,T_2)\,
    (\mathrm{filter}\,\mathrm{straddle})\,r\,s,
\]
i.e.\ enlarging the box from $T_1$ to $T_2$ increases the pair correlation by at
most the ball-boundary $\mathrm{derivBound}$ over the cut edges of the slice. This
is the form instantiated on cubic exhaustion stages $\mathrm{box}_k\subseteq
\mathrm{box}_{k+1}$ to bound the per-stage difference $c_{k+1}-c_k$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Apply the single-box increment
\texttt{correlation\_pair\_sub\_inducedGraph\_le\_derivBound} to
$G'=\mathrm{inducedGraph}\,G\,T_2$ with region
$S=T_1.\mathrm{subtype}\,(\cdot\in T_2)$ and pair $\langle r,\_\rangle,
\langle s,\_\rangle$ (in $S$ since their values lie in $T_1$, by
\texttt{Finset.mem\_subtype}). Then identify the inner-box correlation via
\texttt{correlation\_inducedGraph\_nested\_finset}, rewriting the pair observable
along $e^{-1}$ with \texttt{pair\_map\_nestedFinsetEquiv\_symm}.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.Ambient.correlationAlongExhaustion\_cubic\_succ\_sub\_le\_derivBound}
(cubic-exhaustion per-stage increment, Issue \#2965 Phase A)]
For the cubic exhaustion of $\mathrm{latticeGraph}\,d$ and a pair $r,s$ interior to
the stage $\mathrm{box}_k$ (on no cut edge of the $\mathrm{box}_k$-slice),
\[
  c_{k+1}-c_k
  \le \mathrm{derivBound}\,(\mathrm{inducedGraph}\,(\mathrm{latticeGraph}\,d)\,
    \mathrm{box}_{k+1})\,(\mathrm{filter}\,\mathrm{straddle})\,r\,s,
\]
where $c_n=\texttt{correlationAlongExhaustion}\,(\mathrm{latticeGraph}\,d)\,
(\mathrm{cubicExhaustion}\,d)\,p\,\{r,s\}\,n$. This is the correlation-side
per-stage difference bound, one input toward the volume-convergence rate program
of GJ Lemma~17.5.2 (distinct from the $\beta$-derivative provider that program also
requires).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Instantiate the two-box increment
\texttt{correlation\_pair\_two\_box\_le\_derivBound} with
$G=\mathrm{latticeGraph}\,d$, $T_1=\mathrm{box}_k\subseteq T_2=\mathrm{box}_{k+1}$
(nested by \texttt{cubicBox\_mono}). Unfold \texttt{correlationAlongExhaustion} on
both stages: the observable subsets $\{r,s\}\subseteq\mathrm{box}_k\subseteq
\mathrm{box}_{k+1}$ select the \texttt{dite} ``then'' branch, and
\texttt{liftFinset\_pair} identifies the lifted pair observable with the explicit
subtype pair. The edge-set \texttt{Fintype} hypotheses of the abstract increment are
discharged from finiteness of the box subtype (\texttt{Fintype.ofFinite}).
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[\texttt{IsingModel.derivBound\_le\_of\_correlation\_le}
(Phase A$\to$B link, Issue \#2965)]
If a symmetric, nonnegative $c\colon\iota\times\iota\to\mathbb{R}$ dominates every
two-point correlation, $\langle\sigma_a\sigma_b\rangle_G\le c(a,b)$, then
\[
  \mathrm{derivBound}\,G\,E_0\,r\,s
  \le \beta J\sum_{\{k,l\}\in E_0}\bigl(c(r,k)c(s,l)+c(r,l)c(s,k)+c(r,s)c(k,l)\bigr).
\]
Each summand of $\mathrm{derivBound}$ is a sum of three products of nonnegative
correlations (\texttt{gks\_first}), so the products are monotone under the pointwise
bound. This separates the boundary-sum decay step: one may later substitute
$c(a,b)=$ an infinite-volume decay bound (through the finite-volume $\le$
infinite-volume monotonicity
\texttt{correlationAlongExhaustion\_le\_correlationInfinite}) without re-touching the
$\mathrm{derivBound}$ edge-sum algebra, isolating the divergent-prefactor-sensitive
decay analysis downstream.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Unfold $\mathrm{derivBound}$, pull out the nonnegative factor $\beta J$, and compare
the edge sums termwise; on each edge $\{k,l\}$ apply $\mathtt{mul\_le\_mul}$ to each of
the three products using $0\le\langle\cdot\rangle\le c$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.Ambient.derivBound\_inducedGraph\_cubic\_le\_infiniteVolume\_sum}
(cubic-shell \texttt{derivBound} infinite-volume bound, Issue \#2965 Phase A$\to$B)]
For sites $r,s\in\mathrm{box}_n$ and any edge set $E_0$ of
$\mathrm{inducedGraph}\,(\mathrm{latticeGraph}\,d)\,\mathrm{box}_n$,
\[
  \mathrm{derivBound}\,\dots\,E_0\,\langle r\rangle\,\langle s\rangle
  \le \beta J\sum_{\{a,b\}\in E_0}\bigl(g(r,a)g(s,b)+g(r,b)g(s,a)+g(r,s)g(a,b)\bigr),
\]
where $g(x,y)=\texttt{correlationInfinite}\,(\mathrm{latticeGraph}\,d)\,
(\mathrm{cubicExhaustion}\,d)\,p\,\{x,y\}$ is the infinite-volume pair-set
correlation (a two-point function for distinct sites; the $\mathrm{Finset}$ pair
$\{x,y\}$ collapses to a one-site correlation when $x=y$). Combined with the
per-stage increment
\texttt{correlationAlongExhaustion\_cubic\_succ\_sub\_le\_derivBound}, it bounds
$c_{k+1}-c_k$ by an infinite-volume boundary sum.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Instantiate \texttt{derivBound\_le\_of\_correlation\_le} with
$c(\alpha,\beta)=g(\alpha,\beta)$: symmetric by \texttt{Finset.pair\_comm},
nonnegative by \texttt{correlationInfinite\_nonneg}, and dominating the
finite-volume correlations by the bridge
\texttt{correlation\_inducedGraph\_cubic\_le\_correlationInfinite}. The bridge in
turn specialises \texttt{correlationAlongExhaustion\_le\_correlationInfinite}
(unfolding \texttt{correlationAlongExhaustion} on the box, with the observable
subset holding because both endpoints lie in $\mathrm{box}_n$, and matching the
lifted pair via \texttt{liftFinset\_pair}). This isolates the
divergent-prefactor-sensitive spatial-decay analysis (Phase B) downstream.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Tight (diagonal-free) per-stage increment chain, Issue \#2965]
The weak \texttt{derivBound} carries a diagonal term
$\langle\sigma_r\sigma_s\rangle\langle\sigma_k\sigma_l\rangle$ whose shell sum is
$\langle\sigma_r\sigma_s\rangle\cdot O(|\partial\mathrm{box}_k|)$, which
\emph{diverges} with the shell size and so cannot yield a summable/geometric
convergence-rate bound for $c_k$. The entire increment chain is therefore
re-derived with the \emph{tight} ball-boundary
Simon--Lieb bound \texttt{derivBoundTight} (cross products
$\langle\sigma_r\sigma_k\rangle\langle\sigma_s\sigma_l\rangle
+\langle\sigma_r\sigma_l\rangle\langle\sigma_s\sigma_k\rangle$ only, via
\texttt{cor\_4\_3\_3\_scaled}):
\texttt{scaledCorrelation\_one\_sub\_zero\_le\_derivBoundTight}
$\to$ \texttt{correlation\_sub\_deleteEdges\_le\_derivBoundTight}
$\to$ \texttt{correlation\_pair\_sub\_inducedGraph\_le\_derivBoundTight}
$\to$ \texttt{correlation\_pair\_two\_box\_le\_derivBoundTight}
$\to$ \texttt{correlationAlongExhaustion\_cubic\_succ\_sub\_le\_derivBoundTight},
together with \texttt{derivBoundTight\_le\_of\_correlation\_le}
$\to$ \texttt{derivBoundTight\_inducedGraph\_cubic\_le\_infiniteVolume\_sum}. The
net diagonal-free bound is
\[
  c_{k+1}-c_k \le \beta J\sum_{\{a,b\}\in\text{shell}}
    \bigl(g(r,a)g(s,b)+g(r,b)g(s,a)\bigr),
\]
$g$ the infinite-volume correlation, on which the Phase B spatial decay yields a
geometric rate.
\end{remark}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.Ambient.derivBoundTight\_inducedGraph\_cubic\_le\_decay\_sum}
(cubic-shell decay-sum bound, Issue \#2965 Phase B)]
For $d\ge1$, ferromagnetic zero-field parameters ($h=0$), in the
high-temperature regime
$\mathrm{contractionFactor}\,d\,(\mathrm{cubicExhaustion}\,d)\,p\,r_0<1$, and
$r,s\in\mathrm{box}_n$ on no edge of $E_0$,
\[
  \mathrm{derivBoundTight}\,\dots\,E_0\,\langle r\rangle\,\langle s\rangle
  \le \beta J\!\!\sum_{\{a,b\}\in E_0}\!\!\bigl(\mathrm{cf}^{\,d(r,a)/(r_0+2)}\mathrm{cf}^{\,d(s,b)/(r_0+2)}
    +\mathrm{cf}^{\,d(r,b)/(r_0+2)}\mathrm{cf}^{\,d(s,a)/(r_0+2)}\bigr),
\]
where $\mathrm{cf}=\mathrm{contractionFactor}\,d\,(\mathrm{cubicExhaustion}\,d)\,p\,r_0$
and $d(x,y)$ is the $\ell^1$ lattice distance.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Chain the diagonal-free infinite-volume bound
\texttt{derivBoundTight\_inducedGraph\_cubic\_le\_infiniteVolume\_sum} with the
per-pair spatial decay
\texttt{correlationInfinite\_latticeGraph\_le\_contractionFactor\_pow\_dist\_pair}
applied termwise: each factor $g(x,y)\le\mathrm{cf}^{\,d(x,y)/(r_0+2)}$ (distinctness
$r\neq a$, etc.\ from the hypothesis that $r,s$ lie on no $E_0$-edge, via
\texttt{Sym2.mem\_iff}), and the products are monotone since correlations and
$\mathrm{cf}$-powers are nonnegative. The cross-product-only (tight) structure is
what guarantees every factor genuinely decays.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.Ambient.derivBoundTight\_cubic\_shell\_le\_card\_pow}
(geometric per-stage bound, Issue \#2965 Phase B)]
For $d\ge1$, ferromagnetic zero-field parameters, high temperature
($\mathrm{cf}=\mathrm{contractionFactor}\,d\,(\mathrm{cubicExhaustion}\,d)\,p\,r_0<1$),
and a pair $r,s\in\mathrm{box}_R$ with $R\le k$ on no cut edge of the
$\mathrm{box}_k$-slice,
\[
  \mathrm{derivBoundTight}\,(\mathrm{inducedGraph}\,(\mathrm{latticeGraph}\,d)\,
    \mathrm{box}_{k+1})\,(\mathrm{shell})\,\langle r\rangle\,\langle s\rangle
  \le \beta J\cdot\bigl(|\mathrm{shell}|\cdot 2\,\mathrm{cf}^{\,(k+1-R)/(r_0+2)}\bigr).
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Each straddle edge of the $\mathrm{box}_k$-slice has an endpoint outside
$\mathrm{box}_k$ (\texttt{straddle\_fresh\_vertex}), a fresh vertex of
$\mathrm{box}_{k+1}\setminus\mathrm{box}_k$, whose $\ell^1$ distance to $r$ (and to
$s$) is at least $k+1-R$
(\texttt{latticeDistance\_ge\_of\_mem\_cubicBox\_succ\_not\_mem}); hence its decay
factor is $\le\mathrm{cf}^{\,(k+1-R)/(r_0+2)}$
(\texttt{cf\_pow\_fresh\_le}, via \texttt{pow\_le\_pow\_of\_le\_one}) while the
partner factor is $\le1$. Each summand of
\texttt{derivBoundTight\_inducedGraph\_cubic\_le\_decay\_sum} is thus
$\le 2\,\mathrm{cf}^{\,(k+1-R)/(r_0+2)}$; summing the uniform bound over the shell
(\texttt{Finset.sum\_le\_card\_nsmul}) gives the cardinality factor. With
$|\mathrm{shell}|$ polynomial in $k$ and $\mathrm{cf}<1$, the per-stage increment is
summable.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.Ambient.derivBoundTight\_cubic\_shell\_le\_poly\_pow}
(polynomial $\times$ geometric shell bound, Issue \#2965 Phase B)]
Under the hypotheses of the geometric per-stage bound,
\[
  \mathrm{derivBoundTight}\,(\mathrm{inducedGraph}\,(\mathrm{latticeGraph}\,d)\,
    \mathrm{box}_{k+1})\,(\mathrm{shell})\,\langle r\rangle\,\langle s\rangle
  \le \beta J\cdot\bigl(2\,d\,(2(k+1)+1)^d\,\mathrm{cf}^{\,(k+1-R)/(r_0+2)}\bigr),
\]
the $M\,(2k+3)^d\,\mathrm{ratio}^k$ shape required by the volume-convergence-rate
capstone. The shell cardinality bound $|\mathrm{shell}|\le d\,(2(k+1)+1)^d$
(\texttt{cubic\_shell\_card\_le}) follows from the handshake edge count
\texttt{inducedLatticeGraph\_card\_edgeFinset\_le} and \texttt{card\_cubicBox}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Chain \texttt{derivBoundTight\_cubic\_shell\_le\_card\_pow} with
\texttt{cubic\_shell\_card\_le}, bounding $|\mathrm{shell}|\cdot
2\,\mathrm{cf}^{\dots}$ by $d\,(2(k+1)+1)^d\cdot 2\,\mathrm{cf}^{\dots}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.Ambient.correlationAlongExhaustion\_cubic\_succ\_sub\_le\_poly\_pow}
(polynomial $\times$ geometric per-stage correlation increment, Issue \#2965 Phase B)]
Under the same hypotheses, the successive correlation difference along the cubic
exhaustion satisfies
\[
  c_{k+1}-c_k \le \beta J\cdot\bigl(2\,d\,(2(k+1)+1)^d\,\mathrm{cf}^{\,(k+1-R)/(r_0+2)}\bigr),
\]
$c_n=\texttt{correlationAlongExhaustion}\,(\mathrm{latticeGraph}\,d)\,
(\mathrm{cubicExhaustion}\,d)\,p\,\{r,s\}\,n$ — the correlation-side
$M\,(2k+3)^d\,\mathrm{ratio}^k$ per-stage rate.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Compose the tight per-stage increment
\texttt{correlationAlongExhaustion\_cubic\_succ\_sub\_le\_derivBoundTight} with
\texttt{derivBoundTight\_cubic\_shell\_le\_poly\_pow}. The two
$\mathrm{derivBoundTight}$ shell terms now share the canonical
$\mathrm{inducedGraph}\,(\mathrm{latticeGraph}\,d)\,\Lambda$ edge-set instance
(\texttt{LatticeBoundaryBED}): the broad fallback \texttt{Fintype} edge-set instances
in \texttt{CubicPerStageIncrement}/\texttt{CubicShellInfiniteVolumeBound} are given
lower priority so the canonical instance is used for the outer induced lattice
graphs while the fallback still covers the inner \texttt{deleteEdges}/\texttt{sum}
graphs.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.Ambient.derivative\_profile\_cubic\_le\_lebowitz\_tight}
(tight Lebowitz bound for the cubic $\beta$-derivative profile, Issue \#2965 Phase C)]
For $0\le J$, $0<\beta$, distinct sites $x\neq z$ with
$\{x,z\}\subseteq\mathrm{volume}\,n$, the $\beta$-derivative of the finite-volume
two-point profile $c_n$ satisfies
\[
  \partial_\beta c_n \le J\sum_{\{u,v\}\in E}
    \bigl(\langle\sigma_x\sigma_u\rangle\langle\sigma_z\sigma_v\rangle
      +\langle\sigma_x\sigma_v\rangle\langle\sigma_z\sigma_u\rangle\bigr)
    + J\,|\{e\in E : \langle x\rangle\in e\ \lor\ \langle z\rangle\in e\}|,
\]
the error term counting only edges incident to $x$ or $z$ ($O(1)$ in $n$), and the
cross sum being the two-point object governed by the Phase A--B spatial decay.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Identify the profile with the induced-graph correlation of the lifted pair via
\texttt{correlationAlongExhaustion\_of\_subset}, \texttt{correlation$\Lambda$\_apply}
and \texttt{liftFinset\_pair}, then apply
\texttt{correlation\_beta\_deriv\_le\_lebowitz\_tight} to
$\mathrm{inducedGraph}\,(\mathrm{latticeGraph}\,d)\,(\mathrm{volume}\,n)$; the
profile's derivative is the \texttt{HasDerivAt} value, which that lemma bounds.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.Ambient.derivative\_profile\_cubic\_le\_infiniteVolume\_lebowitz}
(infinite-volume Lebowitz bound for the cubic $\beta$-derivative profile, Issue \#2965 Phase C)]
Under the same hypotheses,
\[
  \partial_\beta c_n \le J\sum_{\{u,v\}\in E}
    \bigl(g(x,u)\,g(z,v)+g(x,v)\,g(z,u)\bigr) + J\,|\mathrm{incident}|,
\]
where $g(a,b)=\texttt{correlationInfinite}\,(\mathrm{latticeGraph}\,d)\,
(\mathrm{cubicExhaustion}\,d)\,\langle J,0,\beta\rangle\,\{a,b\}$ is the
infinite-volume pair-set correlation (a two-point function for distinct sites).
Since these cross products decay spatially
(Phase B) and the susceptibility is summable, $F_n$ is controlled by a single
infinite-volume object.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Compose \texttt{derivative\_profile\_cubic\_le\_lebowitz\_tight} with the termwise
finite-volume $\le$ infinite-volume bridge
\texttt{correlation\_inducedGraph\_cubic\_le\_correlationInfinite}; the products are
monotone since the correlations are nonnegative (\texttt{gks\_first},
\texttt{correlationInfinite\_nonneg}). The incident-edge error term is unchanged.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[\texttt{IsingModel.Ambient.ursell\_cubic\_le\_infiniteVolume\_cross}
(per-edge Ursell summand bound, Issue \#2965 Phase C)]
For a non-degenerate edge $\{u,v\}$ of $\mathrm{inducedGraph}\,(\mathrm{latticeGraph}\,d)\,
(\mathrm{volume}\,n)$ with endpoints distinct from the lifted sites
$\langle x\rangle,\langle z\rangle$,
\[
  \langle\sigma^{A\,\triangle\,\{u,v\}}\rangle-\langle\sigma^A\rangle\langle\sigma_u\sigma_v\rangle
    \le g(x,u)\,g(z,v)+g(x,v)\,g(z,u),\qquad A=\{x,z\},
\]
$g$ the infinite-volume two-point function. Composes the finite-volume Lebowitz
summand bound \texttt{summand\_le\_lebowitz\_of\_disjoint} with the termwise
finite $\le$ infinite bridge. This is the per-edge handle for summing the Ursell
terms over the new shell edges of a stage (far from $x,z$, so the cross products
decay) in the $\beta$-derivative increment analysis.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proposition}[\texttt{IsingModel.hasDerivAt\_scaledBoltzmannWeight\_beta} and
\texttt{hasDerivAt\_scaledPartitionFunction\_beta} (Issue \#2965 Phase C, mixed
$\partial_\beta\partial_s$ route)]
The $s$-coupling-scaled Boltzmann weight
$\mathrm{scaledBoltzmannWeight}\,G\,E_0\,\langle J,0,\beta\rangle\,s\,\sigma
=\mathrm{boltzmannWeight}\cdot\exp(-\beta(1-s)J\sum_{E_0}\sigma_e)$ depends on
$\beta$ through both factors; by the product rule its $\beta$-derivative is
$(-H-(1-s)J\sum_e\sigma_e)\cdot\mathrm{scaledBoltzmannWeight}$, $H$ the Hamiltonian.
The scaled partition function's $\beta$-derivative is the termwise sum. These are
the first two pieces of the scaled-correlation $\beta$-derivative chain — the mixed
$\partial_\beta\partial_s$ route expressing the finite-volume $\beta$-derivative
increment as a shell sum (no interior edges, avoiding the stage-edge
correspondence).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proposition}[\texttt{IsingModel.hasDerivAt\_scaledGibbsExpectation\_beta} and
\texttt{hasDerivAt\_scaledCorrelation\_beta} (Issue \#2965 Phase C, chain complete)]
With the per-configuration $\beta$-log-derivative of the weight
$D(\sigma)=-H(\sigma)-(1-s)J\sum_{E_0}\sigma_e$, the quotient rule gives
$\partial_\beta\langle F\rangle_s=\langle F\,D\rangle_s-\langle F\rangle_s\langle
D\rangle_s$, and specialising $F=\sigma^A$,
$\partial_\beta\langle\sigma^A\rangle_s=\langle\sigma^A
D\rangle_s-\langle\sigma^A\rangle_s\langle D\rangle_s$. This completes the
scaled-correlation $\beta$-derivative chain, the inner $\beta$-derivative used to
bound the finite-volume $\beta$-derivative increment
$g_k'(\beta)=\int_0^1\partial_\beta\partial_s\,\mathrm{scaledCorrelation}\,ds$ as a
shell sum.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proposition}[\texttt{IsingModel.hasDerivAt\_scaledCorrelation\_truncated\_beta}
(Issue \#2965 Phase C, mixed route)]
With the $\beta$-log-derivative $D(\sigma)=-H(\sigma)-(1-s)J\sum_{E_0}\sigma_e$, the
$\beta$-derivative of one scaled truncated (Ursell-like) summand is
\[
  \partial_\beta\bigl[\langle\sigma^B\rangle_s-\langle\sigma^A\rangle_s\langle\sigma^C\rangle_s\bigr]
  = \bigl(\langle\sigma^B D\rangle_s-\langle\sigma^B\rangle_s\langle D\rangle_s\bigr)
    -\bigl[(\langle\sigma^A D\rangle_s-\langle\sigma^A\rangle_s\langle D\rangle_s)\langle\sigma^C\rangle_s
      +\langle\sigma^A\rangle_s(\langle\sigma^C D\rangle_s-\langle\sigma^C\rangle_s\langle D\rangle_s)\bigr],
\]
from \texttt{hasDerivAt\_scaledCorrelation\_beta} at $B,A,C$ via the difference and
product rules. Each summand of
$\partial_s\,\mathrm{scaledCorrelation}=\beta J\sum_{E_0}[\langle\sigma^{A\triangle\{u,v\}}\rangle_s
-\langle\sigma^A\rangle_s\langle\sigma_u\sigma_v\rangle_s]$ has this form, so this is
the per-edge building block of the mixed $\partial_\beta\partial_s$ derivative over
the cut shell.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proposition}[\texttt{IsingModel.hasDerivAt\_scaledGibbs\_truncated\_beta}
(Issue \#2965 Phase C, mixed route)]
For arbitrary observables, $\partial_\beta[\langle F_1\rangle_s-\langle
F_2\rangle_s\langle F_3\rangle_s]$ is given by
\texttt{hasDerivAt\_scaledGibbsExpectation\_beta} and the difference/product rules.
Specialised to $F_1=\sigma^A W$, $F_2=\sigma^A$, $F_3=W$ ($W=\sum_{E_0}\sigma_e$) it
is the inner factor of the mixed $\partial_\beta\partial_s$ derivative, since
$\partial_s\,\mathrm{scaledCorrelation}=\beta J(\langle\sigma^A
W\rangle_s-\langle\sigma^A\rangle_s\langle W\rangle_s)$ --- the gibbs-covariance
form avoiding the $\mathrm{Sym2.lift}$ edge sum.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proposition}[\texttt{IsingModel.hasDerivAt\_scaledCorrelation\_increment\_beta}
(Issue \#2965 Phase C)]
The bond-adding increment $g(\beta)=\langle\sigma^A\rangle_{s=1}-\langle\sigma^A\rangle_{s=0}$
(full minus bond-deleted correlation) has $\beta$-derivative the difference of the
two $\beta$-derivatives (\texttt{hasDerivAt\_scaledCorrelation\_beta} at $s=1,0$),
with $D_1=-H$, $D_0=-H-J\sum_{E_0}\sigma_e$:
\[
  g'(\beta)=\bigl[\langle\sigma^A D_1\rangle_1-\langle\sigma^A\rangle_1\langle D_1\rangle_1\bigr]
    -\bigl[\langle\sigma^A D_0\rangle_0-\langle\sigma^A\rangle_0\langle D_0\rangle_0\bigr].
\]
Stated for arbitrary $E_0$; in the per-stage shell application ($E_0=$ cut shell,
plus the $s=0$ bond-deleted identification) this is the explicit $\beta$-derivative
increment $F_{k+1}(\beta)-F_k(\beta)$, whose bound then requires the shell
cancellation of the full-energy $\langle\sigma^A H\rangle$ terms between the two
systems --- the remaining quantitative input of the capstone.
\end{proposition}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.hasDerivAt\_scaledCorrelation\_increment\_beta\_decomposed}
(shell-cancellation decomposition of the $\beta$-derivative increment, Issue \#2965 Phase C)]
The bond-adding increment $g(\beta)=\langle\sigma^A\rangle_{s=1}-\langle\sigma^A\rangle_{s=0}$
has $\beta$-derivative
\[
  g'(\beta) = \bigl[\mathrm{Cov}_0(\sigma^A,H)-\mathrm{Cov}_1(\sigma^A,H)\bigr]
    + J\,\mathrm{Cov}_0\!\Bigl(\sigma^A,\textstyle\sum_{E_0}\sigma_e\Bigr),
\]
$H$ the full Hamiltonian, $\mathrm{Cov}_s$ the scaled covariance. The second term is
localized to the cut set $E_0$ (a per-edge truncated-correlation sum,
\texttt{scaledCovariance\_sum\_right}, bounded by the Part-B shell decay); the first
is the full-vs-bond-deleted ($s=1$ vs $s=0$) coupling difference of the same energy
covariance, whose bulk cancels.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Fold \texttt{hasDerivAt\_scaledCorrelation\_increment\_beta} into covariance form,
apply the endpoint $\beta$-log-derivatives \texttt{betaLogDeriv\_one}
($D_1=-H$), \texttt{betaLogDeriv\_zero} ($D_0=-H-J\sum_{E_0}\sigma_e$), and the
covariance second-observable linearity (\texttt{scaledCovariance\_sub\_right},
\texttt{\_neg\_right}, \texttt{\_const\_mul\_right}); conclude by \texttt{ring}.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.scaledCovariance\_spinProduct\_edgeSpin\_eq\_scaledCorrelation}
and \texttt{\_edgeSpin\_zero\_sub\_one\_eq\_scaledCorrelation} (per-edge covariance as a
scaled-correlation truncated function, Issue \#2965 Phase C)]
For a non-degenerate edge $\{u,v\}$ ($u\ne v$),
\[
  \mathrm{Cov}_s(\sigma^A,\sigma_u\sigma_v)
    = \langle\sigma^{A\triangle\{u,v\}}\rangle_s
      - \langle\sigma^A\rangle_s\,\langle\sigma_u\sigma_v\rangle_s,
\]
using $\sigma_e=\mathrm{spinProduct}\{u,v\}$
(\texttt{edgeSpin\_quot\_eq\_spinProduct'}) and the spin-product fusion
$\sigma^A\sigma^{\{u,v\}}=\sigma^{A\triangle\{u,v\}}$ (\texttt{spinProduct\_mul}).
Substituting at the endpoints $s=0,1$ expresses each coupling-difference summand as
\[
  \mathrm{Cov}_0(\sigma^A,\sigma_u\sigma_v)-\mathrm{Cov}_1(\sigma^A,\sigma_u\sigma_v)
    = \bigl[\langle\sigma^{A\triangle\{u,v\}}\rangle_0
        -\langle\sigma^A\rangle_0\langle\sigma_u\sigma_v\rangle_0\bigr]
      - \bigl[\langle\sigma^{A\triangle\{u,v\}}\rangle_1
        -\langle\sigma^A\rangle_1\langle\sigma_u\sigma_v\rangle_1\bigr].
\]
Since the scaled correlations $\langle\cdot\rangle_1$ (full) and $\langle\cdot\rangle_0$
(bond-deleted) differ by the bond-adding increment over the cut set, this rewrites each
coupling-difference summand in terms of correlation increments, the objects whose
geometric decay is established by the Part-A/B per-stage increment machinery.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.correlationComplex} and
\texttt{correlationComplex\_analyticAt\_beta},
\texttt{correlation\_ofReal\_eq\_correlationComplex} (complex correlation and its
$\beta$-analyticity, Issue \#3026)]
The complex-parameter correlation
\[
  \langle\sigma^A\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}(J,h,\beta)
    := Z_{\mathbb{C}}(J,h,\beta)^{-1}\sum_\sigma \sigma^A\,
        \exp\!\bigl(-\beta\,H_{\mathbb{C}}(\sigma;J,h)\bigr)
\]
is analytic in $\beta$ wherever $Z_{\mathbb{C}}\ne0$ (\texttt{AnalyticAt.inv} of
\texttt{partitionFunctionComplex\_analyticAt\_beta} times the entire numerator
\texttt{gibbsNumeratorComplex\_analyticAt\_beta}), and agrees with the real correlation on
the real axis, $\langle\sigma^A\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}(J,h,\beta)=
\langle\sigma^A\rangle(J,h,\beta)$ (as a real-embedded complex number) for real
parameters (via \texttt{partitionFunction\_ofReal\_eq\_partitionFunctionComplex} and the
numerator analogue). This supplies the complex-analytic extension required by the Cauchy-estimate
derivative bridge: on any $\beta$-neighborhood where the relevant finite-volume
partition functions are nonzero (in particular near the real axis, where the real
$Z>0$), the finite-volume correlation, hence the value increment $c_k-c_{k+1}$,
extends complex-analytically in $\beta$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.hamiltonianComplex\_ofReal\_eq},
\texttt{norm\_exp\_neg\_beta\_hamiltonianComplex}, and the partition-function/correlation
norm bounds (Issue \#3044, foundational complex/real cast)]
At real parameters \(J,h\) the complex Hamiltonian coincides with the real-cast of the real
Hamiltonian: \(H_\mathbb{C}(\sigma;J_\mathbb{R},h_\mathbb{R})=\overline{(H_\mathbb{R}(\sigma))}\)
(\texttt{hamiltonianComplex\_ofReal\_eq}).  Consequently, for any complex \(\beta\),
\[
  \bigl\|\exp(-\beta\,H_\mathbb{C}(\sigma))\bigr\| = \exp(-\beta.\mathrm{re}\,H_\mathbb{R}(\sigma)),
\]
via \texttt{Complex.norm\_exp} and \((-\beta\,\overline{H_\mathbb{R}}).\mathrm{re}=-\beta.\mathrm{re}\,H_\mathbb{R}\).
Sum-of-moduli together with the spin-product norm bound \(\|\sigma^A\|=1\) give
\(\|Z_\mathbb{C}(\beta)\|\le Z_\mathbb{R}(\beta.\mathrm{re})\) and
\(\|N_\mathbb{C}(\beta)\|\le Z_\mathbb{R}(\beta.\mathrm{re})\), hence
\[
  \|\langle\sigma^A\rangle_\mathbb{C}(\beta)\| \le
    \frac{Z_\mathbb{R}(\beta.\mathrm{re})}{\|Z_\mathbb{C}(\beta)\|}.
\]
These are the foundational modulus bounds for the complex Simon--Lieb development; the
denominator's volume-uniform lower bound (cluster expansion / Lee--Yang region) is the
genuine remaining input.  The complementary
\texttt{partitionFunctionComplex\_re\_le\_norm} (trivial \((Z_{\mathbb{C}}).\mathrm{re}\le\|Z_{\mathbb{C}}\|\))
and \texttt{partitionFunctionComplex\_re\_eq} (explicit cosine formula
\(\mathrm{Re}(Z_{\mathbb{C}}(\beta))=\sum_\sigma\exp(-\beta.\mathrm{re}\,H_{\mathbb{R}}(\sigma))\cos(\beta.\mathrm{im}\,H_{\mathbb{R}}(\sigma))\)
via \texttt{Complex.exp\_re} and \texttt{Real.cos\_neg}) supply the lower-bound entry.  The quantitative form
\texttt{partitionFunctionComplex\_re\_ge\_deficit} makes the deficit explicit via the
cosine lower bound \(1-x^2/2\le\cos x\)
(\texttt{Real.one\_sub\_sq\_div\_two\_le\_cos}):
\[
  \mathrm{Re}(Z_{\mathbb{C}}(\beta)) \ge Z_{\mathbb{R}}(\beta.\mathrm{re})
    - \frac{(\beta.\mathrm{im})^2}{2}\sum_\sigma e^{-\beta.\mathrm{re}\,H_{\mathbb{R}}(\sigma)}\,H_{\mathbb{R}}(\sigma)^2.
\]
The deficit is volume-dependent through the second-moment sum (extensive \(H_{\mathbb{R}}\));
volume-uniform control requires cluster-expansion / Mayer-series ingredients.
\texttt{partitionFunctionComplex\_norm\_ge\_of\_second\_moment\_le} and
\texttt{correlationComplex\_norm\_le\_of\_second\_moment\_le} package this into the
conditional reduction: given an upper bound \(Q\) on the second-moment sum and the
smallness \((\beta.\mathrm{im})^2/2\cdot Q<Z_{\mathbb{R}}(\beta.\mathrm{re})\),
\(\|Z_{\mathbb{C}}(\beta)\|\ge Z_{\mathbb{R}}(\beta.\mathrm{re})-(\beta.\mathrm{im})^2/2\cdot Q\)
and \(\|\langle\sigma^A\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}(\beta)\|\le Z_{\mathbb{R}}(\beta.\mathrm{re})/
(Z_{\mathbb{R}}(\beta.\mathrm{re})-(\beta.\mathrm{im})^2/2\cdot Q)\).  The remaining input
is the uniform \(Q\) bound (cluster expansion at high temperature).
\texttt{correlationComplex\_norm\_sub\_le\_of\_norm\_deriv\_le} adds the mean-value
Lipschitz form: on a convex \(s\subseteq\mathbb{C}\) with \(\langle\sigma^A\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}\)
differentiable and \(\|\partial\langle\sigma^A\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}\|\le C\) on \(s\),
\(\|\langle\sigma^A\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}(\beta)-\langle\sigma^A\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}(\beta')\|
\le C\,\|\beta-\beta'\|\); specialising \(\beta'=\uparrow\!\beta.\mathrm{re}\), this gives
the deviation from the real-axis value
\(\|\langle\sigma^A\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}(\beta)-\uparrow\!(\text{real correlation})\|
\le C\,|\beta.\mathrm{im}|\) (the conditional Lipschitz transfer ingredient).
\texttt{correlationComplex\_diff\_norm\_le\_real\_diff\_plus\_lipschitz} combines this with
the triangle inequality through the real-axis values to yield the central connection:
\(\|\langle\sigma^A\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}(G_1;\beta)-\langle\sigma^A\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}(G_2;\beta)\|
\le|\langle\sigma^A\rangle_{\mathbb{R}}(G_1;\beta.\mathrm{re})-\langle\sigma^A\rangle_{\mathbb{R}}(G_2;\beta.\mathrm{re})|
+(C_1+C_2)\,\|\beta-\uparrow\!\beta.\mathrm{re}\|\) ---
combining the real-axis value increment (poly\(\cdot\)geometric under \(\beta J\,2d<1\),
axiom-free) with the Lipschitz transfer.  The end-to-end composition
\texttt{correlationComplex\_diff\_norm\_le\_from\_real\_bound} packages this: given an
explicit real-axis bound \(B_{\text{real}}\) and Lipschitz constants \(C_1,C_2\),
\(\|\langle\sigma^A\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}(G_1;\beta)-\langle\sigma^A\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}(G_2;\beta)\|
\le B_{\text{real}}+(C_1+C_2)\,\|\beta-\uparrow\!\beta.\mathrm{re}\|\) ---
the conditional reduction feeding the \texttt{hB} provider of the Cauchy bridge.
The complementary \texttt{correlationComplex\_norm\_deriv\_le\_of\_norm\_le\_on\_sphere}
(Cauchy-estimate wrapper) gives
\(\|\partial\langle\sigma^A\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}(\beta_0)\|\le M/R\) from
\texttt{DiffContOnCl} + sphere norm bound \(M\), completing the \(C\)-input pipeline:
sphere norm bound (from the second-moment input \(Q\), or directly from a
\(Z_{\mathbb{C}}\) lower bound) + Cauchy estimate yields the Lipschitz constant \(C\) for
the triangle composition.  The direct alternative
\texttt{correlationComplex\_norm\_le\_of\_partition\_norm\_ge} bypasses the second-moment
route: given \(0<z_{\min}\le\|Z_{\mathbb{C}}(\beta)\|\),
\(\|\langle\sigma^A\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}(\beta)\|\le Z_{\mathbb{R}}(\beta.\mathrm{re})/z_{\min}\)
--- the cleanest entry point when a uniform \(Z_{\mathbb{C}}\) lower bound is supplied
(e.g., cluster-expansion analyticity of \(\log Z\) on a complex disc).
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.correlationComplex\_diffContOnCl\_beta} and
\texttt{\_of\_real} (complex correlation is \texttt{DiffContOnCl} on a nonvanishing disc,
Issue \#3026)]
If $Z_{\mathbb{C}}\ne0$ on the closed disc $\overline{B}(\beta_0,R)$ ($R>0$), then
$\langle\sigma^A\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}$ is \texttt{DiffContOnCl}~$\mathbb{C}$ on
$B(\beta_0,R)$ (differentiable on the open disc, continuous up to the boundary) — exactly
the hypothesis of the Cauchy bridge \texttt{abs\_deriv\_le\_of\_complex\_extension}, via
\texttt{correlationComplex\_analyticAt\_beta} at each point of the closed disc and
\texttt{DifferentiableOn.diffContOnCl} ($\overline{B(\beta_0,R)}=\overline{B}(\beta_0,R)$).
The real-axis corollary: at real parameters $p$, since
$Z_{\mathbb{C}}(\uparrow\!p.\beta)=\uparrow\!(Z\,p)\ne0$, by continuity there is $R>0$ with
$\langle\sigma^A\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}$ \texttt{DiffContOnCl} on $B(\uparrow\!p.\beta,R)$ — a
concrete disc on which the Cauchy bridge applies.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.Ambient.dist\_deriv\_correlationAlongExhaustion\_le\_of\_complex\_circle\_bound}
(capstone per-stage $\beta$-derivative increment from complex inputs, Issue \#3026)]
For consecutive covered exhaustion stages $k,k+1$: if both finite-volume complex partition
functions (on the induced graphs over $\Lambda.\mathrm{volume}\,k$ and
$\Lambda.\mathrm{volume}\,(k{+}1)$) are nonzero on the closed disc $\overline{B}(\beta,R)$
($R>0$), and the complex correlation value increment is bounded by $B$ on the boundary
circle $\partial B(\beta,R)$, then
\[
  \mathrm{dist}(\partial_\beta c_k,\partial_\beta c_{k+1}) \le B/R,
  \qquad c_k=\mathrm{correlationAlongExhaustion}\dots k.
\]
Assembles \texttt{deriv\_correlationAlongExhaustion\_eq\_inducedGraph}, the real
$\beta$-derivative \texttt{hasDerivAt\_correlationΛ\_latticeGraph\_beta} (differentiability),
the real-part agreement \texttt{correlation\_ofReal\_eq\_correlationComplex},
\texttt{correlationComplex\_diffContOnCl\_beta} with \texttt{DiffContOnCl.sub}, and the
Cauchy bridge \texttt{dist\_deriv\_le\_of\_complex\_extension}; the edge-set \texttt{Fintype}
instances are pinned to the exhaustion-stage instances to keep resolution coherent. This is
the conditional reduction feeding the capstone $\mathtt{hincr}$: once a stage-uniform
zero-free radius $R$ and a $\mathrm{poly}\cdot\mathrm{geometric}$ boundary bound $B$ are
supplied (the remaining complex/Lee--Yang-region hard core), the full Lemma 17.5.2 upper
bound / sandwich follows.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.Ambient.correlationAlongExhaustion\_eq\_correlation\_inducedGraph}
and \texttt{deriv\_correlationAlongExhaustion\_eq\_inducedGraph} (covered-stage exhaustion
correlation derivative identity, Issue \#3026)]
For a covered stage $A\subseteq\Lambda.\mathrm{volume}\,n$,
\[
  \mathrm{correlationAlongExhaustion}\,G\,\Lambda\,p\,A\,n
    = \langle\sigma^{A}\rangle_{\mathrm{inducedGraph}\,G\,(\Lambda.\mathrm{volume}\,n)},
\]
the $A\subseteq\Lambda.\mathrm{volume}\,n$ branch (\texttt{dif\_pos}) with $\mathrm{correlation}_\Lambda$
definitionally the induced-graph correlation. As the coverage condition is independent of
$\beta$, the identity holds for the $\beta$-family and hence its $\beta$-derivative. This
rewrites the capstone $\mathtt{hincr}$ — phrased with
$\mathrm{deriv}(\mathrm{correlationAlongExhaustion}\dots)$ — into the bare induced-graph
correlation derivatives on the cubic boxes, the form on which
\texttt{dist\_deriv\_correlation\_le\_of\_complex\_circle\_bound} operates.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.Ambient.hasDerivAt\_correlationAlongExhaustion\_of\_hasDerivAt\_inducedGraph}
(\texttt{HasDerivAt} transfer for the covered-stage \texttt{correlationAlongExhaustion}
$\beta$-family, Issue \#3026)]
Companion to \texttt{deriv\_correlationAlongExhaustion\_eq\_inducedGraph}: for a covered
stage $A\subseteq\Lambda.\mathrm{volume}\,n$, if
$\beta'\mapsto\langle\sigma^A\rangle_{\mathrm{inducedGraph}\,G\,(\Lambda.\mathrm{volume}\,n)}^{\langle J,h,\beta'\rangle}$
has \texttt{HasDerivAt} at $\beta$ with derivative $d$, then so does
$\beta'\mapsto\mathrm{correlationAlongExhaustion}\,G\,\Lambda\,\langle J,h,\beta'\rangle\,A\,n$
with the same $d$. Folds the function-level identity through \texttt{HasDerivAt}. Lets
per-stage \texttt{HasDerivAt}-based bounds on the induced graph transfer directly to bounds
on the $\mathrm{correlationAlongExhaustion}$ $\beta$-family — the form the Lemma 17.5.2
capstone consumes.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.Ambient.correlationAlongExhaustion\_latticeGraph\_beta\_deriv\_le}
(Lebowitz $\beta$-derivative bound on \texttt{correlationAlongExhaustion} at $\mathbb{Z}^d$;
GJ \S17.5 Cor.~4.3.3, pp.~311--312; Issue \#2965 Phase C, real-axis route)]
For an exhaustion $\Lambda$ of $\mathbb{Z}^d = \mathrm{Fin}\,d\to\mathbb{Z}$, a covered
pair $\{r,s\}\subseteq\Lambda.\mathrm{volume}\,n$ with $r\ne s$ (taking
$h_r:r\in\Lambda.\mathrm{volume}\,n$, $h_s:s\in\Lambda.\mathrm{volume}\,n$ and
$h_{\mathrm{rs\_sub}}:\{r,s\}\subseteq\Lambda.\mathrm{volume}\,n$), $J\ge 0$ and $\beta>0$,
there exists
$d_{\mathrm{val}}\in\mathbb{R}$ with
$\mathrm{HasDerivAt}\,(\beta'\mapsto\mathrm{correlationAlongExhaustion}\,(\mathrm{latticeGraph}\,d)\,\Lambda\,\langle J,0,\beta'\rangle\,\{r,s\}\,n)\,d_{\mathrm{val}}\,\beta$
and
\[
  d_{\mathrm{val}} \le J\,\sum_{\langle u,v\rangle\in E(G_n)}
       \bigl[c_n(r,u)\,c_n(s,v) + c_n(r,v)\,c_n(s,u)\bigr]
     + J\cdot 4d,
\]
where $G_n=\mathrm{inducedGraph}\,(\mathrm{latticeGraph}\,d)\,(\Lambda.\mathrm{volume}\,n)$ and
$c_n=\langle\sigma_\cdot\,\sigma_\cdot\rangle_{G_n}^{\langle J,0,\beta\rangle}$. Composes
the per-stage induced-graph Lebowitz bound \texttt{inducedLatticeGraph\_beta\_deriv\_le}
(Step 157) with the \texttt{HasDerivAt} transfer
\texttt{hasDerivAt\_correlationAlongExhaustion\_of\_hasDerivAt\_inducedGraph} above, using
\texttt{liftFinset\_pair} to rewrite
$\mathrm{liftFinset}\,\{r,s\}\,h_{\mathrm{rs\_sub}}=\{\langle r,h_r\rangle,\langle s,h_s\rangle\}$.
The per-stage real-axis input for Issue \#2965 Phase C: the alternative route to the Lemma
17.5.2 $\beta$-derivative increment bound bypassing the CE / Cauchy decomposition entirely.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.Ambient.correlationAlongExhaustion\_latticeGraph\_beta\_deriv\_eq\_le}
(closed-form $\mathrm{deriv}$-version of the Lebowitz $\beta$-derivative bound; GJ \S17.5
Cor.~4.3.3, pp.~311--312; Issue \#2965 Phase C)]
Same hypotheses as the existential form, but the conclusion replaces
$\exists d_{\mathrm{val}},\ \mathrm{HasDerivAt}\,\dots\,d_{\mathrm{val}}\,\beta\wedge d_{\mathrm{val}}\le\dots$
with the closed-form
\[
  \mathrm{deriv}\,(\beta'\mapsto\mathrm{correlationAlongExhaustion}\,(\mathrm{latticeGraph}\,d)\,
        \Lambda\,\langle J,0,\beta'\rangle\,\{r,s\}\,n)\,\beta
    \le J\,\sum_{\langle u,v\rangle\in E(G_n)}
       \bigl[c_n(r,u)\,c_n(s,v) + c_n(r,v)\,c_n(s,u)\bigr]
     + J\cdot 4d.
\]
Obtained by \texttt{HasDerivAt.deriv} on the witness from the existential form, letting
downstream consumers use the bound without unpacking the existential.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.Ambient.correlationAlongExhaustion\_latticeGraph\_\{beta,J\}\_deriv\_nonneg}
and \texttt{\_sandwich} (two-sided per-stage $\beta/J$-derivative sandwich; GJ \S17.5 GKS-II
/ Cor.~4.3.3, pp.~311--312; Issue \#2965 Phase C, real-axis Lebowitz route, full sandwich)]
Lower bound $0\le\mathrm{deriv}\,(\beta'\mapsto\mathrm{correlationAlongExhaustion}\dots)\,\beta$
(resp.\ in $J$) for ferromagnetic $h=0$ systems via GKS-II / $\beta$-monotonicity
(resp.\ $J$-monotonicity) of $\mathrm{correlationAlongExhaustion}$, combined with the
unconditional upper bound \texttt{\_le\_susceptibilityAlong\_sq} to give a sandwich
\[
  0 \le \mathrm{deriv}\,(\beta'\mapsto\dots)\,\beta
    \le J\cdot\chi_{\mathrm{along}}^2 + J\cdot 4d
  \qquad\text{(resp.\ }
  0\le \mathrm{deriv}\,(J'\mapsto\dots)\,J
    \le\beta\cdot\chi_{\mathrm{along}}^2+\beta\cdot 4d\text{)}.
\]
Implementation: from $\mathrm{MonotoneOn}\,(\mathrm{Ioi}\,0)$ (via existing
\texttt{correlationAlongExhaustion\_latticeGraph\_monotone\_\{beta,J\}}) plus
\texttt{MonotoneOn.derivWithin\_nonneg} and \texttt{derivWithin\_of\_isOpen} (since
$\mathrm{Ioi}\,0$ is open). The non-negativity holds for any observable $A$ (not only
two-point pairs); the sandwich is for the pair case. Equivalent to
$|\mathrm{deriv}\,\dots|\le\dots$ for ferromagnetic $h=0$ systems with strictly positive
parameters. Precise hypotheses: $\beta$-direction assumes $0\le J,\ 0<\beta$;
$J$-direction assumes $0<J,\ 0<\beta$ (since the proof bounds the derivative as the
inner-point derivWithin on $\mathrm{Ioi}\,0$, needing the differentiation point to be
in the open interior).
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.Ambient.correlationAlongExhaustion\_latticeGraph\_J\_deriv\_le\_susceptibilityInfinite\_sq}
(J-derivative of $\mathrm{correlationAlongExhaustion}$ bounded by
$\beta\cdot\chi_\infty^2 + \beta\cdot 4d$; GJ \S17.5 Cor.~4.3.3, pp.~311--312, + Step 162;
Issue \#2965 Phase C, J-direction parallel of \texttt{\_beta\_deriv\_le\_susceptibilityInfinite\_sq})]
Conditional susceptibility-infinite J-derivative bound: requires \texttt{BddAbove} of the
per-stage susceptibility sequences at $r$, $s$ (holds in the high-temperature region):
\[
  \mathrm{deriv}\,(J'\mapsto\mathrm{correlationAlongExhaustion}\,(\mathrm{latticeGraph}\,d)\,
        \Lambda\,\langle J',0,\beta\rangle\,\{r,s\}\,n)\,J
    \le \beta\cdot\chi_\infty(r)\cdot\chi_\infty(s) + \beta\cdot 4d.
\]
Boundary term $\beta\cdot 4d$ matches the J-direction Lebowitz bound. Completes the
$\beta/J\times\chi_{\mathrm{along}}/\chi_\infty$ matrix. Composes the J-direction deriv-form
with Step 162.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[J-direction parallel: \texttt{hasDerivAt\_correlationAlongExhaustion\_J\_of\_hasDerivAt\_inducedGraph},
\texttt{correlationAlongExhaustion\_latticeGraph\_J\_deriv\_le},
\texttt{\_J\_deriv\_eq\_le}, \texttt{\_J\_deriv\_le\_susceptibilityAlong\_sq}
(GJ \S17.5 Cor.~4.3.3, pp.~311--312; Issue \#2965 Phase C, J-direction)]
Full J-direction analogue of the $\beta$-direction theorems above. The
\texttt{HasDerivAt} transfer in $J$ (companion of the $\beta$ version, in
\texttt{CorrelationAlongExhaustionDeriv.lean}); the per-stage J-derivative Lebowitz bound
\[
  d_{\mathrm{val}}
    \le \beta\,\sum_{\langle u,v\rangle\in E(G_n)}
        \bigl[c_n(r,u)\,c_n(s,v) + c_n(r,v)\,c_n(s,u)\bigr]
      + \beta\cdot 4d,
\]
in existential form, then closed-form $\mathrm{deriv}\,(\dots)\,J\le\dots$; and the
unconditional susceptibility-product bound
$\mathrm{deriv}\,(\dots)\,J\le\beta\cdot\chi_{\mathrm{along},n}(r)\cdot\chi_{\mathrm{along},n}(s)+\beta\cdot 4d$.
The boundary term is $\beta\cdot 4d$ instead of $J\cdot 4d$
(cf.\ \texttt{inducedLatticeGraph\_J\_deriv\_le}, Step 218). Same composition pattern as the
$\beta$-direction: Step 218 plus the J-direction transfer and \texttt{liftFinset\_pair},
then \texttt{HasDerivAt.deriv} and Step 161.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.Ambient.correlationAlongExhaustion\_latticeGraph\_beta\_deriv\_le\_susceptibilityAlong\_sq}
($\beta$-derivative of $\mathrm{correlationAlongExhaustion}$ bounded by
$J\cdot\chi_{\mathrm{along}}^2 + J\cdot 4d$; GJ \S17.5 Cor.~4.3.3, pp.~311--312, + Step 161;
Issue \#2965 Phase C, real-axis Lebowitz route)]
Unconditional susceptibility-product $\beta$-derivative bound: drops the \texttt{BddAbove}
hypotheses of \texttt{\_le\_susceptibilityInfinite\_sq} by using Step 161
(\texttt{inducedLatticeGraph\_leb\_sum\_le\_susc\_along}) — the per-stage Lebowitz cross sum
is bounded by the \emph{per-stage} susceptibility product, which is unconditionally
well-defined:
\[
  \mathrm{deriv}\,(\beta'\mapsto\mathrm{correlationAlongExhaustion}\,(\mathrm{latticeGraph}\,d)\,
        \Lambda\,\langle J,0,\beta'\rangle\,\{r,s\}\,n)\,\beta
    \le J\cdot\chi_{\mathrm{along},n}(r)\cdot\chi_{\mathrm{along},n}(s) + J\cdot 4d.
\]
Same hypotheses as the existential form, \emph{without} any boundedness assumption on the
susceptibility sequences. Composes
\texttt{correlationAlongExhaustion\_latticeGraph\_beta\_deriv\_eq\_le} with Step 161.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.Ambient.correlationAlongExhaustion\_latticeGraph\_beta\_deriv\_le\_susceptibilityInfinite\_sq}
($\beta$-derivative of $\mathrm{correlationAlongExhaustion}$ bounded by
$J\cdot\chi_\infty^2 + J\cdot 4d$; GJ \S17.5 Cor.~4.3.3, pp.~311--312, + Step 162;
Issue \#2965 Phase C)]
Composes the deriv-form Lebowitz bound
\texttt{correlationAlongExhaustion\_latticeGraph\_beta\_deriv\_eq\_le} with Step 162's
infinite-volume susceptibility product bound on the Lebowitz edge sum
(\texttt{inducedLatticeGraph\_leb\_sum\_le\_susceptibilityInfinite}):
\[
  \mathrm{deriv}\,(\beta'\mapsto\mathrm{correlationAlongExhaustion}\,(\mathrm{latticeGraph}\,d)\,
        \Lambda\,\langle J,0,\beta'\rangle\,\{r,s\}\,n)\,\beta
    \le J\cdot\chi_\infty(r)\cdot\chi_\infty(s) + J\cdot 4d.
\]
The susceptibility product bound requires \texttt{BddAbove} of the per-stage susceptibility
sequences at $r$ and $s$ (Step 162's standing hypotheses; in particular holds in the
high-temperature region). The right-hand side is uniform in $n$ — the per-stage real-axis
input for the Phase C convergence-rate argument.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.Ambient.correlationInfinite\_le\_two\_div\_one\_add\_pow\_pseudoMassFromParamsAtPair}
($m^-$ majorant for $\mathrm{correlationInfinite}$; GJ \S17.5 p.~311; Issue \#1645 Step 119
plan Step 5.1)]
For any active pair $(x, z)$ (i.e.\ $\mathrm{correlationInfinite}\,\{x, z\}\in(0, 2)$), the
infinite-volume two-point function is dominated by the rational pseudo-mass majorant:
\[
  \mathrm{correlationInfinite}\,(\mathrm{latticeGraph}\,d)\,\Lambda\,\langle J,0,\beta\rangle\,
      \{x, z\} \le \frac{2}{1 + (m^-\cdot r)^{\alpha}},
\]
where $m^- = \mathtt{pseudoMassFromParamsAtPair}\,h_\alpha\,h_r\,d\,\Lambda\,\langle J,0,\beta\rangle\,x\,z$
and $r>0$ is the pseudo-mass radius parameter. Direct corollary of the defining identity
$\mathtt{pseudoMassG}\,\alpha\,r\,m^- = \mathrm{correlationInfinite}$ (\texttt{pseudoMass\_spec})
composed with the pointwise rational bound
$\mathtt{pseudoMassG}\,\alpha\,r\,t \le 2/(1 + (t\cdot r)^{\alpha})$
(\texttt{pseudoMassG\_le\_two\_div\_one\_add\_pow}, which drops the
$e^{-tr}\le 1$ factor). The $m^-$ majorant used in GJ p.~312 to substitute
$\langle\phi(x)\phi(z)\rangle/A\to 2/(1+(m^-\cdot d(x,z))^\alpha)$ in the proof of Theorem
17.5.1; the first step toward the HLS comparison form $|c'|\le K\cdot c / m^{-2\alpha}$
needed by \texttt{gj\_theorem\_17\_5\_1\_pseudoMass\_pow\_succ\_lipschitz\_on\_Icc}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.one\_div\_mul\_pow\_eq\_one\_div\_pow\_mul\_one\_div\_pow},
\texttt{one\_div\_mul\_pow\_mul\_one\_div\_mul\_pow\_eq}
(polynomial $M$-factor factorization; GJ \S17.5 p.~312; Issue \#1645 Step 119 plan Step 5.4
bridge)]
For $M > 0$, $t > 0$, $\alpha \in \mathbb{N}$:
\[
  \frac{1}{(M\cdot t)^\alpha} = \frac{1}{M^\alpha}\cdot\frac{1}{t^\alpha},
\]
and the pair-product form factors $M^{-2\alpha}$:
\[
  \frac{1}{(M\cdot t_x)^\alpha}\cdot\frac{1}{(M\cdot t_y)^\alpha}
    = \frac{1}{M^{2\alpha}}\cdot\frac{1}{t_x^\alpha}\cdot\frac{1}{t_y^\alpha}.
\]
Algebraic identity (\texttt{mul\_pow} + \texttt{field\_simp}). Reveals the $m^{-2\alpha}$
prefactor matching GJ p.~312's bound on the HLS sum.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.one\_div\_one\_add\_pow\_mul\_one\_div\_one\_add\_pow\_le\_one\_div\_pow\_mul\_one\_div\_pow},
\texttt{two\_div\_one\_add\_pow\_mul\_two\_div\_one\_add\_pow\_le\_two\_div\_pow\_mul\_two\_div\_pow}
(pair-product polynomial-decay bridge; GJ \S17.5 p.~312; Issue \#1645 Step 119 plan Step 5.4
bridge)]
For $M\cdot t_x > 0$, $M\cdot t_y > 0$ and $\alpha\in\mathbb{N}$:
\[
  \frac{1}{1+(M\cdot t_x)^\alpha}\cdot\frac{1}{1+(M\cdot t_y)^\alpha}
    \le \frac{1}{(M\cdot t_x)^\alpha}\cdot\frac{1}{(M\cdot t_y)^\alpha}
    = M^{-2\alpha}\cdot t_x^{-\alpha}\cdot t_y^{-\alpha},
\]
with a scaled-by-2 version. Direct product of two
\texttt{one\_div\_one\_add\_pow\_le\_one\_div\_pow} applications via \texttt{mul\_le\_mul}.
Pair-product form of the polynomial-decay bridge, ready for combining with the pseudo-mass
pair-product majorant (PR \#3155) to produce $m^{-2\alpha}\cdot d_x^{-\alpha}\cdot d_y^{-\alpha}$
form on which the discrete HLS convolution sum bound applies.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.one\_div\_one\_add\_pow\_le\_one\_div\_pow},
\texttt{two\_div\_one\_add\_pow\_le\_two\_div\_pow}
(pointwise polynomial-decay bridge; GJ \S17.5 p.~312; Issue \#1645 Step 119 plan Step 5.4
bridge)]
For $M\cdot t > 0$ (resp.\ $t\cdot r > 0$) and $\alpha \in \mathbb{N}$:
\[
  \frac{1}{1 + (M\cdot t)^\alpha} \le \frac{1}{(M\cdot t)^\alpha}
  \qquad\text{(resp.\ }
  \frac{2}{1 + (t\cdot r)^\alpha} \le \frac{2}{(t\cdot r)^\alpha}\text{)}.
\]
Pointwise bridge from the pseudo-mass majorant form $1/(1+(M\cdot t)^\alpha)$ (PR \#3154 etc.) to
the polynomial-decay form $(M\cdot t)^{-\alpha} = M^{-\alpha}\cdot t^{-\alpha}$ usable in the
discrete HLS sum. The first bridge lemma toward Step 5.4 (HLS sum bound). Proof:
$1 + (M\cdot t)^\alpha \ge (M\cdot t)^\alpha$ and \texttt{one\_div\_le\_one\_div\_of\_le}
(resp.\ \texttt{div\_le\_div\_of\_nonneg\_left}) with \texttt{pow\_pos} positivity.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[Lemma 17.5.2 bridge-to-HLS bundle: Simon-Lieb + adjacent + ferromagnetic chain;
GJ \S17.5 pp.~311--312; Issue \#1645]
Bundled GJ-proposition-size collection in file
\texttt{HLSBridgeFromSimonLieb.lean} consolidating the Step 5.7d-p atomic
composers (\#3175--\#3187):
\begin{itemize}
  \item \texttt{PseudoMassLatticeDistanceBridge\_of\_simonLieb\_smallReg\_adjacent}:
    end-to-end \texttt{PseudoMassLatticeDistanceBridge} from Simon-Lieb
    Lebowitz bound + adjacent exp input + ferromagnetic.
  \item \texttt{tsum\_correlationInfinite\_pair\_product\_le\_const\_of\_simonLieb\_smallReg\_adjacent}:
    HLS sum existential at arbitrary $(x_0, y_0)$.
  \item \texttt{PseudoMassLatticeDistanceBridge\_of\_simonLieb\_trichotomy\_adjacent}
    and
    \texttt{tsum\_correlationInfinite\_pair\_product\_le\_const\_of\_simonLieb\_trichotomy\_adjacent}:
    full adjacent/small/large trichotomy constructors without the uniform
    small-regime premise.
  \item \texttt{tsum\_correlationInfinite\_pair\_product\_zero\_anchor\_le\_const\_of\_simonLieb}:
    HLS sum at the zero anchor.
  \item \texttt{all\_pair\_bound\_of\_simonLieb\_smallReg\_adjacent\_provider},
    \texttt{all\_pair\_active\_of\_betaJ\_pos\_provider}: standalone
    field providers.
  \item \texttt{PseudoMassLatticeDistanceBridge\_of\_simonLieb\_smallReg\_tanh\_adjacent}:
    tanh-input adjacent variant using Step 5.7d's $\tanh^d \le e^{-(M\cdot d)}$.
  \item \texttt{tsum\_correlationInfinite\_pair\_product\_le\_const\_of\_simonLieb\_tanh\_adjacent}:
    HLS sum from the tanh-input variant.
  \item \texttt{PseudoMassLatticeDistanceBridge\_of\_simonLieb\_trichotomy\_tanh\_adjacent}
    and
    \texttt{tsum\_correlationInfinite\_pair\_product\_le\_const\_of\_simonLieb\_trichotomy\_tanh\_adjacent}:
    tanh-adjacent versions of the full trichotomy path.
  \item The fixed-name \texttt{canonical\_*} bridge and HLS entry points now
    route through the full trichotomy path; the old small-regime constructors
    remain available as compatibility helpers.
  \item \texttt{simonLiebTrichotomyBridgeRate} selects
    \[
      M_\ast = \min\left(1,
        \frac{\texttt{simonLiebRate}\,\beta\,J\,d/2}{(\alpha:\mathbb{R})+1}
      \right).
    \]
    In the strict high-temperature regime it proves
    $0 < M_\ast$, $M_\ast \le 1$, and
    $((\alpha:\mathbb{R})+1)M_\ast \le
      \texttt{simonLiebRate}\,\beta\,J\,d/2$.
  \item \texttt{canonical\_bridge\_from\_simonLieb\_selected\_rate\_adjacent},
    \texttt{canonical\_hls\_sum\_selected\_rate},
    \texttt{canonical\_bound\_provider\_selected\_rate}, and
    \texttt{canonical\_hls\_sum\_zero\_anchor\_selected\_rate} use this
    selected $M_\ast$, so callers only provide the adjacent correlation bound
    at $M_\ast$ rather than the scalar witnesses
    $0 < M$, $M \le 1$, and
    $((\alpha:\mathbb{R})+1)M \le \texttt{simonLiebRate}/2$.
