  \item \texttt{canonical\_K\_pos\_from\_hls\_sum\_selected\_rate}
    extracts the positive HLS constant from the selected-rate HLS sum without
    reintroducing those scalar witnesses.
  \item \texttt{simonLiebTanhTrichotomyBridgeRate} selects
    \[
      M_{\tanh} = \min(M_\ast,\texttt{highTempExpRate}\,\beta\,J).
    \]
    It keeps the same positivity, $M_{\tanh} \le 1$, and Simon-Lieb
    rate-gap conclusions, and additionally proves
    $M_{\tanh} \le \texttt{highTempExpRate}\,\beta\,J$.
  \item \texttt{canonical\_bridge\_from\_tanh\_selected\_rate\_adjacent},
    \texttt{canonical\_hls\_sum\_tanh\_selected\_rate},
    \texttt{canonical\_bound\_provider\_tanh\_selected\_rate}, and
    \texttt{canonical\_hls\_sum\_zero\_anchor\_tanh\_selected\_rate} use
    $M_{\tanh}$ for tanh-adjacent inputs, so callers no longer supply
    the extra tanh-to-exp witness
    $M \le \texttt{highTempExpRate}\,\beta\,J$.
  \item \texttt{canonical\_K\_pos\_from\_hls\_sum\_tanh\_selected\_rate}
    is the corresponding tanh-adjacent positive-constant extraction.
\end{itemize}
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.Ambient.PseudoMassLatticeDistanceBridge\_of\_bound\_active}
(direct \texttt{PseudoMassLatticeDistanceBridge} constructor;
GJ \S17.5 pp.~311--312; Issue \#1645 Step 119 plan Step 5.7p)]
Direct convenience constructor of
\texttt{PseudoMassLatticeDistanceBridge} taking:
\begin{itemize}
  \item $M_{\inf} : \mathbb{R}$ with $0 < M_{\inf}$;
  \item $\mathrm{Ferromagnetic}\,\langle J, 0, \beta\rangle$;
  \item all-pair \texttt{bound} (Step 5.7n, \#3185 shape);
  \item all-pair \texttt{active} (Step 5.7o, \#3186 shape).
\end{itemize}
Bypasses the conditional \texttt{cubicTanhProfileBound} family path of
\texttt{PseudoMassLatticeDistanceBridge\_of\_cubicTanh\_family} (\#3172),
matching the natural shape of Step 5.7n / Step 5.7o providers.  After the
all-displacement tanh-profile no-go, this direct bound+active constructor is
the canonical bridge interface once the concrete bound and active providers are
supplied.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.Ambient.correlationInfinite\_pair\_active\_of\_betaJ\_pos}
(all-pair active range from $0 < \beta\cdot J$;
GJ \S17.5 pp.~311--312; Issue \#1645 Step 119 plan Step 5.7o)]
Under $0 \le J$, $0 < \beta$, $0 < \beta\cdot J$, for every distinct pair
$(x, z)$:
$\mathrm{correlationInfinite}\,\{x, z\} \in (0, 2)$.

Proof: $0 < \tanh(\beta\cdot J)^{d(0, z - x)}$ (from $\tanh(\beta\cdot J) > 0$),
the existing tanh-power lower bound
\texttt{twoPointFunction\_ge\_tanh\_betaJ\_pow\_dist}, and translation
invariance give the lower bound. The upper bound is
$\mathrm{correlationInfinite} \le 1 < 2$.

Complements Step 5.7n (\#3185)'s all-pair \texttt{bound} provider,
completing the structural input set for building a concrete
\texttt{PseudoMassLatticeDistanceBridge} value directly from Simon-Lieb
$+$ adjacent analytic inputs (without going through the conditional
\texttt{cubicTanhProfileBound} family).
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.Ambient.pseudoMassFromParamsAtPair\_all\_pair\_simonLieb\_smallReg\_bound}
(all-pair \texttt{bridge.bound} from Step 5.7m;
GJ \S17.5 pp.~311--312; Issue \#1645 Step 119 plan Step 5.7n)]
Composes Step 5.7m (\#3184) with the existing
\texttt{pseudoMassFromParamsAtPair\_lower\_bound\_of\_zero\_anchored} to
produce the all-pair shape
$\forall x \ne z, M \cdot d(x, z) \le \mathrm{pseudoMass} \cdot r$.
Matches the \texttt{bound} field signature of
\texttt{PseudoMassLatticeDistanceBridge}.

This is the final structural step in the Step 5.7 plumbing chain from
Simon-Lieb $+$ adjacent analytic inputs to the bridge's \texttt{bound}
field for the small-$M$ regime.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.Ambient.pseudoMassFromParamsAtPair\_zero\_anchored\_simonLieb\_smallReg\_uniform}
(\texttt{hbase} quantifier composer from Step 5.7l;
GJ \S17.5 pp.~311--312; Issue \#1645 Step 119 plan Step 5.7m)]
Lifts Step 5.7l (\#3183) per-$w$ composer to the universally-quantified
$\forall w \ne 0, M \cdot d(0, w) \le \mathrm{pseudoMass} \cdot r$ shape.
This is the zero-anchored input consumed by
\texttt{pseudoMassFromParamsAtPair\_lower\_bound\_of\_zero\_anchored}
(existing), which lifts to all distinct pairs.

Uniform per-$w \ne 0$ hypotheses: active range, small-$t\cdot r$
constraint $M \cdot d(0, w) \le 1$, and adjacent input
$\mathrm{correlation}\,\{0, w\} \le e^{-M}$ at $\mathrm{dist}(0, w) = 1$.
The small constraint is restrictive (forces $M = 0$ for unbounded $w$
support), but the composer provides clean structural plumbing.

Completes the per-$w$ to $\forall w \ne 0$ lift in the small-$M$ regime.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.Ambient.pseudoMassFromParamsAtPair\_M\_dist\_zero\_le\_simonLieb\_smallReg\_combined}
(combined Simon-Lieb + adjacent \texttt{bridge.bound} composer for $\mathrm{dist} \ge 1$;
GJ \S17.5 pp.~311--312; Issue \#1645 Step 119 plan Step 5.7l)]
Per-$w$ composer dispatching by $\mathrm{dist}(0, w) = 1$ (adjacent) vs
$\ge 2$. Adjacent applies Step 5.7k (\#3182). Non-adjacent applies
Step 5.7j (\#3181, Simon-Lieb direct).

Hypotheses:
$1 \le \alpha$, $0 < r$;
$0 \le \beta\cdot J$, $0 < \beta\cdot J\cdot(2d) \le 1$;
$0 \le M$, $M \le \mathrm{simonLiebRate}(\beta, J, d)/2$;
$w \ne 0$, $M \cdot d(0, w) \le 1$;
active range $\mathrm{correlation}\,\{0, w\} \in (0, 2)$;
adjacent input: $\mathrm{correlation}\,\{0, w\} \le e^{-M}$ whenever
$\mathrm{dist}(0, w) = 1$.
Conclusion: $M \cdot d(0, w) \le \mathrm{pseudoMass} \cdot r$.

Completes the per-$w$ \texttt{bridge.bound} API for $\mathrm{dist} \ge 1$
in the small-$M$ regime with explicit adjacent input.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.Ambient.pseudoMassFromParamsAtPair\_zero\_le\_of\_corr\_le\_exp\_adjacent}
(adjacent dist = 1 specialization of small-regime Step 5.7e;
GJ \S17.5 pp.~311--312; Issue \#1645 Step 119 plan Step 5.7k)]
Specialization of Step 5.7e small-regime (\#3176) to $\mathrm{dist}(0, w) = 1$.
With $M \le 1$, the small-regime constraint $M \cdot d(0, w) \le 1$ is
automatic, and the bound shape collapses to $M \le \mathrm{pseudoMass} \cdot r$.

Hypotheses: $1 \le \alpha$, $0 < r$, $0 \le M$, $M \le 1$,
$\mathrm{dist}(0, w) = 1$, active range
$\mathrm{correlation}\,\{0, w\} \in (0, 2)$, and the adjacent exp bound
$\mathrm{correlation}\,\{0, w\} \le e^{-M}$. Conclusion:
$M \le \mathrm{pseudoMass} \cdot r$.

Complements Step 5.7j (\#3181)'s $\mathrm{dist} \ge 2$ Simon-Lieb composer,
together covering the full $\mathrm{dist} \ge 1$ range in the small-$M$
regime.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.Ambient.pseudoMassFromParamsAtPair\_M\_dist\_zero\_le\_of\_simonLieb\_smallReg}
(Simon-Lieb direct \texttt{bridge.bound} composer in small regime;
GJ \S17.5 pp.~311--312; Issue \#1645 Step 119 plan Step 5.7j)]
Direct per-$w$ atomic composer combining Step 5.7h (\#3179) and Step 5.7e
small-regime (\#3176). Hypotheses:
$1 \le \alpha$, $0 < r$;
$0 \le \beta\cdot J$, $0 < \beta\cdot J\cdot(2d)$, $\beta\cdot J\cdot(2d) \le 1$;
$0 \le M$, $M \le \mathrm{simonLiebRate}(\beta, J, d)/2$;
$2 \le \mathrm{dist}(0, w)$ (excluding adjacent pairs);
$M \cdot d(0, w) \le 1$ (small regime);
$\mathrm{correlationInfinite}\,\{0, w\} \in (0, 2)$. Conclusion:
$M \cdot d(0, w) \le \mathrm{pseudoMassFromParamsAtPair}\,0\,w \cdot r$.

Proof: Step 5.7h gives $\mathrm{correlation} \le
e^{-(\mathrm{simonLiebRate}/2 \cdot d(0,w))}$, then $M \le \mathrm{simonLiebRate}/2$
and $d(0,w) \ge 0$ strengthen to $\mathrm{correlation} \le e^{-(M\cdot d(0,w))}$,
which the small-regime composer
(\texttt{...\_corr\_le\_exp\_smallReg}) consumes.

The adjacent $\mathrm{dist} = 1$ case requires a separate input.  The
large-$M\cdot d$ regime is handled by the next rate-gap absorption composer.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.Ambient.correlationInfinite\_latticeGraph\_le\_exp\_neg\_M\_dist\_div\_pow\_of\_simonLieb\_largeReg}
and
\texttt{IsingModel.Ambient.pseudoMassFromParamsAtPair\_M\_dist\_zero\_le\_of\_simonLieb\_largeReg}
(Simon-Lieb large-regime \texttt{bridge.bound} composer;
GJ \S17.5 pp.~311--312; Issue \#1645 Step 119 plan Step 5.7j large-regime
extension)]
For $2 \le \mathrm{dist}(0,w)$, $1 \le M\cdot d(0,w)$, and
\[
  ((\alpha:\mathbb{R})+1)M \le \mathrm{simonLiebRate}(\beta,J,d)/2,
\]
the Simon--Lieb rate-halved estimate implies the large-regime analytic input
\[
  \mathrm{correlationInfinite}\,\{0,w\}
    \le
    \frac{e^{-M d(0,w)}}{(M d(0,w))^\alpha}.
\]
The corresponding pseudo-mass composer then feeds this input to
\texttt{pseudoMassFromParamsAtPair\_M\_dist\_zero\_le\_of\_corr\_le\_exp\_div\_pow\_largeReg}
and concludes
\[
  M d(0,w)
    \le
    \mathrm{pseudoMassFromParamsAtPair}\,0\,w \cdot r.
\]

The proof uses the elementary estimate $t^\alpha \le e^{\alpha t}$ for
$1 \le t$, with $t=M d(0,w)$.  The hypothesis
$((\alpha:\mathbb{R})+1)M \le \mathrm{simonLiebRate}/2$ gives
$\alpha M \le \mathrm{simonLiebRate}/2 - M$, so multiplying by the
nonnegative distance absorbs the polynomial denominator into the exponential
rate gap.  This is the large-regime replacement for the globally impossible
positive-$M$ small-regime assumption
$\forall w,\; M d(0,w) \le 1$; the next composer combines it with the existing
small-regime and adjacent inputs.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.Ambient.pseudoMassFromParamsAtPair\_M\_dist\_zero\_le\_simonLieb\_trichotomy\_combined},
\texttt{IsingModel.Ambient.pseudoMassFromParamsAtPair\_zero\_anchored\_simonLieb\_trichotomy\_uniform},
\texttt{IsingModel.Ambient.pseudoMassFromParamsAtPair\_all\_pair\_simonLieb\_trichotomy\_bound},
\texttt{IsingModel.Ambient.PseudoMassLatticeDistanceBridge\_of\_simonLieb\_trichotomy\_adjacent}, and
\texttt{IsingModel.Ambient.tsum\_correlationInfinite\_pair\_product\_le\_const\_of\_simonLieb\_trichotomy\_adjacent}
(Simon--Lieb full trichotomy HLS bridge; GJ \S17.5 pp.~311--312; Issue
\#1645 Step 119 plan Step 5.7j full-trichotomy extension)]
For $0 < M$, $M \le 1$, and
\[
  ((\alpha:\mathbb{R})+1)M \le \mathrm{simonLiebRate}(\beta,J,d)/2,
\]
the Simon--Lieb HLS bridge no longer needs the uniform small-regime hypothesis
$\forall w,\; M d(0,w) \le 1$.  For each nonzero displacement $w$, the proof
splits into three cases:
\[
\begin{array}{ll}
\mathrm{dist}(0,w)=1, & \text{use the adjacent input;}\\
2\le \mathrm{dist}(0,w)\ \text{and}\ M d(0,w)\le 1,
  & \text{use the Simon--Lieb small-regime composer;}\\
2\le \mathrm{dist}(0,w)\ \text{and}\ 1\le M d(0,w),
  & \text{use the large-regime rate-gap composer.}
\end{array}
\]
The same case split is lifted first to the uniform zero-anchored bound, then by
translation invariance to all pairs:
\[
  M d(x,z)
    \le
    \mathrm{pseudoMassFromParamsAtPair}\,x\,z \cdot r.
\]
Together with active range from $0<\beta J$, this constructs a
\texttt{PseudoMassLatticeDistanceBridge} and feeds the abstract HLS capstone to
obtain the pair-product sum bound
\[
  \exists K>0,\quad
  \sum_z \langle \sigma_{x_0}\sigma_z\rangle_\infty
         \langle \sigma_{y_0}\sigma_z\rangle_\infty
    \le K.
\]
The consumer layer also provides the tanh-adjacent variants
\texttt{PseudoMassLatticeDistanceBridge\_of\_simonLieb\_trichotomy\_tanh\_adjacent}
and
\texttt{tsum\_correlationInfinite\_pair\_product\_le\_const\_of\_simonLieb\_trichotomy\_tanh\_adjacent}.
The canonical fixed-name entry points
\texttt{canonical\_bridge\_from\_simonLieb\_adjacent},
\texttt{canonical\_hls\_sum}, \texttt{canonical\_bound\_provider},
\texttt{canonical\_bridge\_from\_tanh\_adjacent},
\texttt{canonical\_hls\_sum\_tanh},
\texttt{canonical\_K\_pos\_from\_hls\_sum}, and
\texttt{canonical\_hls\_sum\_zero\_anchor} now route through this trichotomy
path, preserving the old stable names while dropping the uniform
small-regime premise.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.Ambient.pseudoMassFromParamsAtPair\_M\_dist\_zero\_le\_of\_corr\_le\_tanh\_exp\_trichotomy}
(asymmetric tanh / exp \texttt{hbase} composer;
GJ \S17.5 pp.~311--312; Issue \#1645 Step 119 plan Step 5.7i)]
Asymmetric trichotomy composer taking the small-regime input
$\mathrm{correlation}\,\{0,w\} \le \tanh(\beta\cdot J)^{d(0,w)}$ (the
natural shape of cubic-path tanh decay) and the large-regime input
$\mathrm{correlation}\,\{0,w\} \le e^{-(M\cdot d(0,w))} / (M\cdot d(0,w))^\alpha$,
dispatching by case-split on $M\cdot d(0,w) \le 1$ vs $1 < M\cdot d(0,w)$.

In the small regime applies Step 5.7e tanh-input variant
(\texttt{...corr\_le\_tanh\_pow\_smallReg}, \#3176) which internally uses
Step 5.7d (\#3175) to convert tanh form to exp form. In the large regime
applies Step 5.7e large variant
(\texttt{...corr\_le\_exp\_div\_pow\_largeReg}) directly.

Companion to Step 5.7f's exp-only trichotomy composer (\#3177), exploiting
the natural mismatch between tanh-typed small-regime cubic-path estimates
and exp/polynomial-typed large-regime decay arising in GJ \S17.5 analytic
derivations.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.Ambient.correlationInfinite\_latticeGraph\_le\_exp\_neg\_half\_simonLiebRate\_dist\_of\_dist\_ge\_two}
(Simon-Lieb dist $\ge 2$ exp-form with $\mathrm{simonLiebRate}/2$ rate;
GJ \S17.5 pp.~311--312; Issue \#1645 Step 119 plan Step 5.7h)]
Under $0 \le \beta\cdot J$, $0 < \beta\cdot J\cdot(2d)$,
$\beta\cdot J\cdot(2d) \le 1$, and $2 \le \mathrm{dist}(i, j)$:
\[
  \mathrm{correlationInfinite}\,\{i, j\}
    \le e^{-(\mathrm{simonLiebRate}/2)\cdot \mathrm{dist}(i, j)}.
\]
Proof: take $n := \mathrm{dist}(i, j) - 1$, apply
\texttt{...le\_exp\_neg\_simonLiebRate\_pow\_of\_dist\_gt} (Step 5.7g) to
get $\mathrm{correlation} \le e^{-\mathrm{simonLiebRate}\cdot n}$, then use
$\mathrm{dist} - 1 \ge \mathrm{dist}/2$ (valid for $\mathrm{dist} \ge 2$)
together with $\mathrm{simonLiebRate} \ge 0$ (from $\beta\cdot J\cdot(2d) \le 1$) to
strengthen the exponent to $\mathrm{simonLiebRate}/2 \cdot \mathrm{dist}$.

The $\mathrm{dist} = 1$ case is genuinely outside this composer's scope:
Simon-Lieb gives only $\mathrm{correlation} \le 1$ at distance $1$, no
exponential decay. Handling adjacent pairs requires a separate single-step
input, which the full trichotomy bridge above composes with this non-adjacent
result.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.Ambient.simonLiebRate} and
\texttt{IsingModel.Ambient.correlationInfinite\_latticeGraph\_le\_exp\_neg\_simonLiebRate\_pow\_of\_dist\_gt}
(Simon-Lieb exp-form correlation bound;
GJ \S17.5 pp.~311--312; Issue \#1645 Step 119 plan Step 5.7g)]
Define
$\mathrm{simonLiebRate}(\beta, J, d) := -\log(\beta\cdot J\cdot(2d))$.
In the strict high-temperature regime $0 < \beta\cdot J\cdot(2d) < 1$,
$\mathrm{simonLiebRate} > 0$, giving genuine exponential decay.

Under $0 \le \beta\cdot J$ and $0 < \beta\cdot J\cdot(2d)$, for every
$n : \mathbb{N}$ and $i, j \in \mathbb{Z}^d$ with $n + 1 \le \mathrm{dist}(i, j)$:
\[
  \mathrm{correlationInfinite}\,\{i, j\}
    \le e^{-\mathrm{simonLiebRate}\cdot n}.
\]
Direct consequence of
\texttt{correlationInfinite\_latticeGraph\_le\_betaJ\_two\_d\_pow\_of\_dist\_gt}
combined with the exp identity
$(\beta\cdot J\cdot(2d))^n = e^{n\cdot\log(\beta\cdot J\cdot(2d))}
= e^{-\mathrm{simonLiebRate}\cdot n}$
(\texttt{betaJ\_two\_d\_pow\_eq\_exp\_neg\_simonLiebRate\_mul}).

Note: the exponent is $n = \mathrm{dist} - 1$ from peeling, not
$\mathrm{dist}$; converting to $\mathrm{correlation} \le e^{-(M\cdot\mathrm{dist})}$
introduces a constant prefactor $e^{\mathrm{simonLiebRate}} = 1/(\beta\cdot J\cdot 2d)$,
a known artifact of the Simon-Lieb peeling structure (the final
near-neighbour step at distance 1 contributes the base factor 1 rather
than $\beta\cdot J\cdot 2d$).
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.Ambient.pseudoMassFromParamsAtPair\_M\_dist\_zero\_le\_of\_corr\_le\_exp\_trichotomy}
(\texttt{hbase} quantifier composer via trichotomy;
GJ \S17.5 pp.~311--312; Issue \#1645 Step 119 plan Step 5.7f)]
Let $\alpha \ge 1$, $r > 0$, $d : \mathbb{N}$, $\Lambda$ an exhaustion of
$\mathbb{Z}^d$, $p$ Ising parameters, and $0 \le M$. Assume the per-$w$
active range
$\mathrm{correlationInfinite}\,\{0,w\} \in (0, 2)$ for every $w \ne 0$,
together with the two regime-specific analytic-input families:
\begin{itemize}
  \item small: for $w \ne 0$ with $M \cdot d(0, w) \le 1$,
    $\mathrm{correlationInfinite}\,\{0,w\} \le e^{-(M \cdot d(0, w))}$;
  \item large: for $w \ne 0$ with $1 \le M \cdot d(0, w)$,
    $\mathrm{correlationInfinite}\,\{0,w\} \le
    e^{-(M \cdot d(0, w))} / (M \cdot d(0, w))^\alpha$.
\end{itemize}
Then for every $w \ne 0$,
$M \cdot d(0, w) \le \mathrm{pseudoMassFromParamsAtPair}\,0\,w \cdot r$.
Proof: dispatch by case-split on $M \cdot d(0, w) \le 1$ vs $1 < M \cdot d(0, w)$,
applying the corresponding Step 5.7e atomic composer per $w$.

The unified-input variant
\texttt{...\_corr\_le\_exp\_div\_max\_pow} takes the single bound
$\mathrm{correlationInfinite}\,\{0,w\} \le
e^{-(M \cdot d(0, w))} / \max(1, M \cdot d(0, w))^\alpha$,
which automatically equals $e^{-(M \cdot d(0, w))}$ in the small regime
($\max = 1$) and $e^{-(M \cdot d(0, w))} / (M \cdot d(0, w))^\alpha$ in the
large regime ($\max = M \cdot d(0, w)$), removing the need for caller-side
trichotomy.

These composers complete the structural plumbing from analytic-input families
to the $\mathtt{hbase}$ field of
\texttt{PseudoMassLatticeDistanceBridge\_of\_cubicTanh\_family} (\#3172).
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.Ambient.pseudoMassFromParamsAtPair\_M\_dist\_zero\_le\_of\_corr\_le\_*}
(exp / tanh correlation-upper-bound composers for the zero-anchored
\texttt{bridge.bound};
GJ \S17.5 pp.~311--312; Issue \#1645 Step 119 plan Step 5.7e)]
Let $\alpha \ge 1$, $r > 0$, $d : \mathbb{N}$, $\Lambda$ an exhaustion of
$\mathbb{Z}^d$, $p$ Ising parameters, $0 \le M$, and $w \in \mathbb{Z}^d$
with the correlation active range
$\mathrm{correlationInfinite}\,\{0,w\} \in (0, 2)$. Three composers produce
the zero-anchored shape $M \cdot d(0, w) \le
\mathrm{pseudoMassFromParamsAtPair}\,0\,w \cdot r$:
\begin{enumerate}
  \item \texttt{...\_corr\_le\_exp\_smallReg}: under $M \cdot d(0,w) \le 1$
    and $\mathrm{correlationInfinite}\,\{0,w\} \le e^{-(M \cdot d(0,w))}$,
    chain $e^{-(t \cdot r)} \le \mathrm{pseudoMassG}_{\alpha, r}(t)$ (small
    $t \cdot r$, with $t := M \cdot d(0, w)/r$) and \#3173.
  \item \texttt{...\_corr\_le\_exp\_div\_pow\_largeReg}: under
    $1 \le M \cdot d(0,w)$ and
    $\mathrm{correlationInfinite}\,\{0,w\} \le e^{-(M \cdot d(0,w))} /
    (M \cdot d(0,w))^\alpha$,
    chain $e^{-(t \cdot r)} / (t \cdot r)^\alpha \le
    \mathrm{pseudoMassG}_{\alpha, r}(t)$ (large $t \cdot r$, with
    $t := M \cdot d(0, w)/r$) and \#3173.
  \item \texttt{...\_corr\_le\_tanh\_pow\_smallReg}: under
    $0 \le \beta \cdot J$, $M \le \mathrm{highTempExpRate}(\beta, J)$,
    $M \cdot d(0, w) \le 1$, and
    $\mathrm{correlationInfinite}\,\{0,w\} \le \tanh(\beta \cdot J)^{d(0,w)}$,
    chain \#3175 (with $r := 1$, so $\tanh(\beta J)^k \le e^{-(M\cdot k)}$)
    and the small-regime exp composer.
\end{enumerate}
Atomic composers for the $\mathtt{hbase}$ field of
\texttt{PseudoMassLatticeDistanceBridge\_of\_cubicTanh\_family} (\#3172).
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.tanh\_pow\_le\_exp\_neg\_M\_dist\_r\_of\_M\_r\_le\_highTempExpRate}
(cubic-path tanh decay to exp form bridge;
GJ \S17.5 pp.~311--312; Issue \#1645 Step 119 plan Step 5.7d)]
Under $0 \le \beta\cdot J$ and $M\cdot r \le \mathrm{highTempExpRate}(\beta, J)
= -\log(\tanh(\beta\cdot J))$, for every $d \in \mathbb{N}$:
\[
  \tanh(\beta\cdot J)^d \le e^{-(M\cdot d\cdot r)}.
\]
Proof: when $\tanh(\beta\cdot J) > 0$,
$\tanh(\beta\cdot J)^d = e^{d\cdot \log(\tanh(\beta\cdot J))}
= e^{-(\mathrm{highTempExpRate})\cdot d}$,
and the hypothesis gives $M\cdot r \cdot d \le \mathrm{highTempExpRate}\cdot d$, hence
$-\mathrm{highTempExpRate}\cdot d \le -M\cdot r \cdot d$, so the exponential bound
follows by monotonicity. The endpoint $\tanh(\beta\cdot J) = 0$ splits by $d$:
for $d = 0$, $\tanh(\beta\cdot J)^0 = 1 = e^{0}$; for $d > 0$,
$\tanh(\beta\cdot J)^d = 0 \le e^{-(M\cdot d\cdot r)}$. This is the analytic bridge
from cubic-path tanh decay $\mathrm{correlation} \le \tanh(\beta\cdot J)^d$ into
the form $\mathrm{correlation} \le e^{-(M\cdot d\cdot r)}$, combinable with
\#3174's \texttt{pseudoMassG\_ge\_exp\_of\_tr\_le\_one}
(small-$t\cdot r$) or \texttt{pseudoMassG\_ge\_exp\_div\_pow\_of\_tr\_ge\_one}
(large-$t\cdot r$) and the bound reduction of \#3173 to land directly in the
\texttt{bridge.bound} shape.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.pseudoMassG\_ge\_exp\_div\_pow\_of\_tr\_ge\_one}
(large-$t\cdot r$ pseudoMassG lower bound;
GJ \S17.5 pp.~311--312; Issue \#1645 Step 119 plan Step 5.7c)]
For $\alpha \in \mathbb{N}$ and $t\cdot r \ge 1$:
\[
  \frac{e^{-(t\cdot r)}}{(t\cdot r)^\alpha} \le
  \frac{2\,e^{-(t\cdot r)}}{1 + (t\cdot r)^\alpha}
  = \mathrm{pseudoMassG}_{\alpha, r}(t).
\]
Direct from $(t\cdot r)^\alpha \ge 1$ so $1 + (t\cdot r)^\alpha \le 2\,(t\cdot r)^\alpha$,
hence $2 / (1 + (t\cdot r)^\alpha) \ge 1 / (t\cdot r)^\alpha$. Paired with the
small-$t\cdot r$ regime bound \texttt{pseudoMassG\_ge\_exp\_of\_tr\_le\_one}
(\texttt{PseudoMass/Profile.lean}), this gives a useful explicit lower bound on
\texttt{pseudoMassG} in both regimes. The large-$t\cdot r$ form is exactly what
is needed to land a cubic-path tanh decay $c \le \tanh(\beta J)^d$ of the form
$c \le e^{-M\cdot d\cdot r}/(M\cdot d)^\alpha$ into
\texttt{pseudoMass\_ge\_iff\_pseudoMassG\_ge} (\texttt{PseudoMass/Basic.lean})
reverse direction, yielding the \texttt{bridge.bound}-shape via the wrappers of
\#3173.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.Ambient.pseudoMassFromParamsAtPair\_ge\_of\_corr\_le\_pseudoMassG},
\texttt{...\_M\_dist\_zero\_le\_of\_corr\_le\_pseudoMassG}
(\texttt{bridge.bound} reduction from a \texttt{pseudoMassG} upper bound;
GJ \S17.5 pp.~311--312; Issue \#1645 Step 119 plan Step 5.7b)]
If the correlation $\langle\phi(x)\phi(z)\rangle$ lies in the active range
$(0, 2)$ and is dominated by $\mathrm{pseudoMassG}_{\alpha,r}(t)$ for some
$t \ge 0$, then $t \le \mathrm{pseudoMassFromParamsAtPair}(x, z)$. Specialising
to $t = M \cdot d(0, w) / r$ for $M \ge 0$ at the zero-anchored displacement
gives $M \cdot d(0, w) \le \mathrm{pseudoMassFromParamsAtPair}(0, w) \cdot r$,
exactly matching the \texttt{hbase} input of
\texttt{PseudoMassLatticeDistanceBridge\_of\_cubicTanh\_family}
(\#3172). Atomic reduction obtained from
\texttt{pseudoMass\_ge\_iff\_pseudoMassG\_ge} (\texttt{PseudoMass/Basic.lean})
through \texttt{pseudoMassExt\_of\_mem}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.Ambient.PseudoMassLatticeDistanceBridge\_of\_cubicTanh\_family}
(concrete bridge constructor from a \texttt{cubicTanhProfileBound} family;
GJ \S17.5 pp.~311--312; Issue \#1645 Step 119 plan Step 5.7)]
Given the ferromagnetic data $0 \le J$, $0 < \beta$, the high-temperature
constraint $\beta\cdot J\cdot 2d < 1$, positivity inputs $0 < r$, $0 < M_{\inf}$,
a uniform tanh-profile family providing
\texttt{cubicTanhProfileBound} $\alpha\ d\ r\ \beta\ J\ w$ at every nonzero
displacement $w$, and the zero-anchored pseudo-mass lower bound
$M_{\inf}\cdot d(0, w) \le m^-(0, w)\cdot r$ for every nonzero $w$, the
constructor produces the abstract bridge value
\texttt{PseudoMassLatticeDistanceBridge} $h_\alpha\ h_r\ d\ J\ \beta$.
Translation invariance is used to lift the anchored facts to arbitrary distinct
pairs: \texttt{correlationInfinite\_latticeGraph\_pair\_eq\_twoPointFunction}
gives the pair correlation form, and \texttt{latticeDistance\_translate\_eq}
gives the lattice-distance form. The constructed bridge value can be fed
directly into \texttt{tsum\_correlationInfinite\_pair\_product\_le\_HLS\_const}.
This remains a conditional compatibility constructor: the no-go result above
shows that the all-displacement \texttt{cubicTanhProfileBound} family cannot be
obtained in positive dimension from only \(0<r\), \(0<\beta J\), and
\(\beta J\,2d<1\).
The supporting wrappers
\texttt{correlationInfinite\_pair\_eq\_displacement},
\texttt{pseudoMassFromParamsAtPair\_eq\_displacement},
\texttt{correlationInfinite\_pair\_mem\_Ioo\_zero\_two\_of\_cubicTanhProfileBound\_displacement},
\texttt{latticeDistance\_pair\_eq\_displacement},
\texttt{pseudoMassFromParamsAtPair\_lower\_bound\_of\_zero\_anchored}, and
\texttt{correlationInfinite\_pair\_active\_of\_cubicTanhProfileBound\_family}
expose each piece of the lift.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.Ambient.PseudoMassLatticeDistanceBridge},
\texttt{tsum\_correlationInfinite\_pair\_product\_le\_HLS\_const}
(HLS correlation pair-product capstone with bridge abstraction;
GJ \S17.5 p.~312; Issue \#1645 Step 119 plan Step 5.6)]
The structure \texttt{PseudoMassLatticeDistanceBridge} abstracts the
pseudo-mass to lattice-distance lower bound
$M_{\inf}\cdot d(x,z) \le m^-(x,z)\cdot r'$ together with the ferromagnetic
assumption and per-pair active-range membership. Under the hypotheses
$1 \le \alpha$, $0 < r'$, $d < 2\alpha$, given any such bridge,
for any $x_0, y_0 : \mathbb{Z}^d$:
\[
  \exists K > 0,\quad \sum_{z \in \mathbb{Z}^d}
    \langle \phi(x_0)\phi(z)\rangle\cdot\langle \phi(y_0)\phi(z)\rangle
  \le K,
\]
with $K = 4 \cdot (\max(1, (M_{\inf}^\alpha)^{-1})\cdot 2^\alpha)^2 \cdot K_{\mathrm{HLS}}$.
Proof outline: at $z = x_0$ or $z = y_0$ the singleton correlation vanishes by
$\{x, x\} = \{x\}$ and $h = 0$ $Z_2$ symmetry (odd cardinality). For
$z \notin \{x_0, y_0\}$, Step~5.1
(\texttt{correlationInfinite\_le\_two\_div\_one\_add\_pow\_pseudoMassFromParamsAtPair})
gives $\langle\phi(x)\phi(z)\rangle \le 2/(1+(m^-\cdot r')^\alpha)$, then the
bridge bound $m^-\cdot r' \ge M_{\inf}\cdot d$ combined with monotonicity yields
$\le 2/(1+(M_{\inf}\cdot d)^\alpha)$. The pair-product sum is then bounded by
the $U_4$-aligned existential constant
\texttt{discrete\_hls\_pseudoMass\_two\_pair\_convolution\_constant}.
The abstract bridge can be supplied by the conditional
\texttt{PseudoMassLatticeDistanceBridge\_of\_cubicTanh\_family} when its family
inputs are already available.  After the all-displacement tanh-profile no-go,
the positive high-temperature route should instead use the direct Simon--Lieb
trichotomy constructors with their adjacent/bound/active inputs.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.tsum\_two\_div\_pseudoMass\_pair\_product\_le\_const\_pow\_M},
\texttt{discrete\_hls\_pseudoMass\_two\_pair\_convolution\_constant}
(HLS pair tsum $2/(1+\ldots)\cdot 2/(1+\ldots)$ variant for $U_4$ form;
GJ \S17.5 p.~312; Issue \#1645 Step 119 plan Step 5.5c $U_4$-aligned)]
For $d, \alpha \in \mathbb{N}$ with $d < 2\alpha$, $M > 0$, and
$x, y : \mathbb{Z}^d$:
\[
  \sum_{z \in \mathbb{Z}^d}
    \frac{2}{1+(M\cdot d(x,z))^\alpha}\cdot\frac{2}{1+(M\cdot d(y,z))^\alpha}
  \le 4 \cdot \bigl(\max(1, (M^\alpha)^{-1})\cdot 2^\alpha\bigr)^2
      \cdot \sum_{z \in \mathbb{Z}^d} (1 + d(0, z))^{-2\alpha},
\]
and an existential positive constant $K$ form. Scaled-by-4 variant of the
basic $1/(1+\ldots)\cdot 1/(1+\ldots)$ form, matching the
$2/(1+(m^-\cdot r')^\alpha)\cdot 2/(1+(m^-\cdot r')^\alpha)$ shape of
Step~5.3's $|U_4|$ pseudo-mass majorant
(\texttt{truncated4TwoPoint\_abs\_le\_pseudoMass\_majorant\_of\_distinct}, PR \#3156).
The factor $2\cdot 2 = 4$ becomes the scalar prefactor on the squared HLS
constant. Directly composable downstream with the GJ \S17.5 Lemma~17.5.2
derivative-bound pipeline.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.discrete\_hls\_pseudoMass\_convolution\_constant}
(discrete HLS PseudoMass convolution constant; GJ \S17.5 p.~312;
Issue \#1645 Step 119 plan Step 5.5c existential wrapper)]
For $d, \alpha \in \mathbb{N}$ with $d < 2\alpha$, there exists $K \in \mathbb{R}$
with $K > 0$ such that for all $M > 0$ and $x, y : \mathbb{Z}^d$:
\[
  \sum_{z \in \mathbb{Z}^d}
    \frac{1}{1+(M\cdot d(x,z))^\alpha}\cdot\frac{1}{1+(M\cdot d(y,z))^\alpha}
  \le \bigl(\max(1, (M^\alpha)^{-1})\cdot 2^\alpha\bigr)^2 \cdot K.
\]
Packages \texttt{tsum\_pseudoMass\_pair\_product\_le\_const\_pow\_M} with the
explicit positive witness $K = \sum_z (1 + d(0, z))^{-2\alpha}$, paralleling
the real-$\alpha$ \texttt{discrete\_hls\_convolution\_constant}
(\texttt{IsingModel/PolyDecay.lean}). Convenient interface for downstream GJ
\S17.5 Lemma~17.5.2 derivative-bound composition: only the existential positive
bound matters; the corner-sum geometric structure of $K$ is irrelevant.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.tsum\_pseudoMass\_pair\_product\_le\_const\_pow\_M}
(HLS pair tsum bound for PseudoMass form; GJ \S17.5 p.~312;
Issue \#1645 Step 119 plan Step 5.5c capstone)]
For $d, \alpha \in \mathbb{N}$ with $d < 2\alpha$, $M > 0$, and
$x, y : \mathbb{Z}^d$:
\[
  \sum_{z \in \mathbb{Z}^d}
    \frac{1}{1+(M\cdot d(x,z))^\alpha}\cdot\frac{1}{1+(M\cdot d(y,z))^\alpha}
  \le \bigl(\max(1, (M^\alpha)^{-1})\cdot 2^\alpha\bigr)^2
      \cdot \sum_{z \in \mathbb{Z}^d} (1 + d(0, z))^{-2\alpha}.
\]
Composition of the pair pointwise bridge
\texttt{one\_div\_one\_add\_M\_t\_pow\_pair\_le\_const\_sq\_mul\_one\_div\_one\_add\_pow\_pow},
the form bridge \texttt{one\_div\_one\_add\_pow\_eq\_rpow\_neg}
($1/(1+t)^\alpha = (1+t)^{-(\alpha:\mathbb{R})}$ for $t \ge 0$, natural $\alpha$),
and the existing real-$\alpha$ HLS pair-sum bound
\texttt{tsum\_pow\_neg\_conv\_le\_const} (\texttt{IsingModel/PolyDecay.lean}).
Summability of the natural-$\alpha$ LHS is obtained by comparison with the
AM-GM bound $((1+d(x,z))^{-2\alpha}+(1+d(y,z))^{-2\alpha})/2$ (paralleling the
existing PolyDecay proof). The squared prefactor
$(\max(1, (M^\alpha)^{-1})\cdot 2^\alpha)^2$ collapses to $M^{-2\alpha}\cdot 4^\alpha$
(the GJ p.~312 $m^{-2\alpha}$ scaling) when $M\le 1$ and $\alpha\ge 1$, and to
$4^\alpha$ when $M^\alpha\ge 1$. This is the discrete HLS sum step in GJ
p.~312's $|c'|\le K\cdot c\cdot m^{-2\alpha}$ derivation.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.one\_div\_one\_add\_M\_t\_pow\_le\_max\_mul\_one\_div\_one\_add\_t\_pow},
\texttt{one\_div\_one\_add\_t\_pow\_le\_two\_pow\_mul\_one\_div\_one\_add\_pow\_pow},
\texttt{one\_div\_one\_add\_M\_t\_pow\_le\_const\_mul\_one\_div\_one\_add\_pow\_pow},
\texttt{one\_div\_one\_add\_pow\_eq\_rpow\_neg},
\texttt{one\_div\_one\_add\_M\_t\_pow\_pair\_le\_const\_sq\_mul\_one\_div\_one\_add\_pow\_pow}
(HLS pointwise bridges $1/(1+(M\cdot t)^\alpha) \to 1/(1+t)^\alpha$ and pair form;
GJ \S17.5 p.~312; Issue \#1645 Step 119 plan Step 5.5c pointwise)]
For $M > 0$, $t \ge 0$, $\alpha \in \mathbb{N}$:
\[
  \frac{1}{1 + (M\cdot t)^\alpha}
    \le \max(1,\,(M^\alpha)^{-1})\cdot\frac{1}{1 + t^\alpha}
    \le \max(1,\,(M^\alpha)^{-1})\cdot 2^\alpha\cdot\frac{1}{(1+t)^\alpha}.
\]
The $M$-bridge inequality follows by case analysis on $M^\alpha\ge 1$ (max $= 1$,
$1 + t^\alpha \le 1 + M^\alpha t^\alpha$) vs $M^\alpha < 1$ (max $= (M^\alpha)^{-1}$,
$(M^\alpha)^{-1}\cdot(1 + M^\alpha t^\alpha) = (M^\alpha)^{-1} + t^\alpha \ge 1 + t^\alpha$).
The form bridge $1/(1+t^\alpha) \le 2^\alpha\cdot 1/(1+t)^\alpha$ follows by case
analysis on $t \le 1$ ($(1+t)^\alpha \le 2^\alpha\le 2^\alpha(1+t^\alpha)$) vs
$t > 1$ ($(1+t)^\alpha \le (2t)^\alpha = 2^\alpha t^\alpha \le 2^\alpha(1+t^\alpha)$).
The composition prefactor $\max(1, (M^\alpha)^{-1})\cdot 2^\alpha$ collapses to
$M^{-\alpha}\cdot 2^\alpha$ (the GJ p.~312 $m^{-\alpha}$ scaling) when $M\le 1$ and
$\alpha\ge 1$, and to $2^\alpha$ when $M^\alpha\ge 1$. The $(1+t)^{-\alpha}$
polynomial-decay body matches the existing infinite-sum infrastructure
\texttt{tsum\_pow\_neg\_conv\_le\_const} (\texttt{IsingModel/PolyDecay.lean}; Step 130B,
real $\alpha$ form). First pointwise step toward the HLS infinite-sum bound
$\sum_z 1/((1+(m^-\cdot d(x,z))^\alpha)\cdot(1+(m^-\cdot d(y,z))^\alpha))
  \le \mathrm{const}\cdot m^{-2\alpha}$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.Ambient.truncated4TwoPoint\_sum\_abs\_le\_card\_mul\_uniform\_of\_distinct}
(finite-sum $|U_4|$ uniform bound; GJ \S17.5 p.~312; Issue \#1645 Step 119 plan Step 5.5b)]
Under per-$u$ distinctness $\{0, r, s, u\}$ pairwise, per-$u$ active-pair membership
$\mathrm{correlationInfinite}\in(0,2)$ for $\{r,u\}$ and $\{0,u\}$ (and fixed for $\{0,s\}$,
$\{r,s\}$), and a uniform $m_{\mathrm{inf}}$ lower bound on all relevant per-pair
pseudo-masses across $u\in A$:
\[
  \sum_{u\in A} |U_4^\infty(0, r, s, u)|
    \le |A|\cdot\frac{8}{r'^{2\alpha}\cdot m_{\mathrm{inf}}^{2\alpha}}.
\]
The single-term uniform bound (Step 5.5a) is $u$-independent, so the sum reduces to $|A|$
times the constant. Preliminary volume-uniform sum bound; the finer HLS sum with per-$z$
polynomial decay refines this (Step 5.5c).
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.Ambient.truncated4TwoPoint\_abs\_le\_pseudoMass\_uniform\_lower\_of\_distinct}
($|U_4^\infty|$ uniform $m^-_{\mathrm{inf}}$ bound; GJ \S4.3 Cor 4.3.3 + \S17.5 p.~312; Issue \#1645
Step 119 plan Step 5.5a)]
Given a uniform positive lower bound $m_{\mathrm{inf}}$ on the 4 per-pair pseudo-masses
$m^-_{0s}, m^-_{ru}, m^-_{0u}, m^-_{rs}$ with $0 < m_{\mathrm{inf}}$:
\[
  |U_4^\infty(0, r, s, u)| \le \frac{8}{r'^{2\alpha}\cdot m_{\mathrm{inf}}^{2\alpha}}.
\]
Each per-pair $1/m^{-\alpha}$ is bounded by $1/m_{\mathrm{inf}}^\alpha$ via
\texttt{pow\_le\_pow\_left}$_0$ and \texttt{one\_div\_le\_one\_div\_of\_le}; the 2
same-shape terms sum to give factor 8. The GJ p.~312 form ready for the HLS sum step.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.Ambient.truncated4TwoPoint\_abs\_le\_pseudoMass\_polynomial\_factored\_of\_distinct}
($|U_4^\infty|$ factored polynomial form; GJ \S4.3 Cor 4.3.3 + \S17.5 p.~312; Issue \#1645
Step 119 plan Step 5.4)]
Algebraic rewrite of PR \#3159 using the $M^{-2\alpha}$ factorization (PR \#3160). For
ferromagnetic h=0 with pairwise-distinct $\{0, r, s, u\}$, all 4 pair correlations active,
all 4 pseudo-masses strictly positive:
\[
  |U_4^\infty(0, r, s, u)|
    \le \frac{4}{r'^{2\alpha}}\cdot\frac{1}{m^-_{0s}{}^{\alpha}\,m^-_{ru}{}^{\alpha}}
      + \frac{4}{r'^{2\alpha}}\cdot\frac{1}{m^-_{0u}{}^{\alpha}\,m^-_{rs}{}^{\alpha}}.
\]
Exposes the common $r'^{-2\alpha}$ prefactor and per-pseudoMass $m^{-\alpha}$ decay
separately, the form GJ uses to extract the $m^{-2\alpha}$ prefactor in the HLS sum step.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.Ambient.truncated4TwoPoint\_abs\_le\_pseudoMass\_polynomial\_of\_distinct}
($|U_4^\infty|$ bounded by pseudoMass polynomial decay form; GJ \S4.3 Cor 4.3.3 + \S17.5 p.~312;
Issue \#1645 Step 119 plan Step 5.4 capstone)]
Compose PR \#3156 (pseudoMass pair-product majorant) with PR \#3158 (pair-product polynomial
decay bridge) twice via \texttt{add\_le\_add}. For ferromagnetic h=0 with pairwise-distinct
$\{0, r, s, u\}$, all 4 pair correlations active in $(0, 2)$, and all 4 pseudo-masses
strictly positive:
\[
  |U_4^\infty(0, r, s, u)|
    \le \frac{2}{(m^-_{0s}\cdot r')^\alpha}\cdot\frac{2}{(m^-_{ru}\cdot r')^\alpha}
      + \frac{2}{(m^-_{0u}\cdot r')^\alpha}\cdot\frac{2}{(m^-_{rs}\cdot r')^\alpha}.
\]
The polynomial-decay form ready for the discrete-HLS sum step
(\texttt{tsum\_pow\_neg\_conv\_le\_const}, Step 130B) over $z$ in the GJ p.~312 derivation.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.Ambient.truncated4TwoPoint\_abs\_le\_pseudoMass\_majorant\_of\_distinct}
($|U_4^\infty|$ bounded by pseudo-mass pair-product majorant; GJ \S4.3 Cor 4.3.3 + \S17.5 p.~312;
Issue \#1645 Step 119 plan Step 5.3)]
For ferromagnetic $\langle J, 0, \beta\rangle$ on $\mathbb{Z}^d$ with pairwise-distinct
$\{0, r, s, u\}$ and all four pair correlations active in $(0, 2)$:
\[
  |U_4^\infty(0, r, s, u)|
    \le \frac{2}{1+(m^-_{0s}\cdot r')^\alpha}\cdot\frac{2}{1+(m^-_{ru}\cdot r')^\alpha}
      + \frac{2}{1+(m^-_{0u}\cdot r')^\alpha}\cdot\frac{2}{1+(m^-_{rs}\cdot r')^\alpha}.
\]
Direct composition of the Lebowitz IIIb absolute-value bound
\texttt{truncated4TwoPoint\_abs\_le\_pair\_correlations\_of\_distinct} (Step 5; PR \#3152) with
the single-pair $m^-$ majorant
\texttt{correlationInfinite\_le\_two\_div\_one\_add\_pow\_pseudoMassFromParamsAtPair} (Step 5.1;
PR \#3154) applied to each of the 4 pair correlations, followed by \texttt{mul\_le\_mul} for
each cross product and \texttt{add\_le\_add} for the sum. The GJ p.~312 numerator bound in
the form ready for the HLS sum over $z$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.Ambient.correlationInfinite\_pair\_product\_le\_four\_div\_one\_add\_pow\_pseudoMassFromParamsAtPair}
($m^-$ pair-product majorant; GJ \S17.5 p.~312; Issue \#1645 Step 119 plan Step 5.2)]
For two active pairs $(x, z)$ and $(y, z)$,
\[
  \langle\sigma_x\sigma_z\rangle\cdot\langle\sigma_y\sigma_z\rangle
    \le \frac{2}{1+(m^-_{xz}\cdot r)^\alpha}\cdot\frac{2}{1+(m^-_{yz}\cdot r)^\alpha}.
\]
Direct corollary of the single-pair $m^-$ majorant
\texttt{correlationInfinite\_le\_two\_div\_one\_add\_pow\_pseudoMassFromParamsAtPair}
applied twice via \texttt{mul\_le\_mul} with GKS-I non-negativity. The pair-product form
used in GJ p.~312 inside $\sum_z\langle\phi(x_0)\phi(z)\rangle\langle\phi(y_0)\phi(z)\rangle$
— the Lebowitz IIIb cross-product term whose sum gives the HLS comparison
$|c'|\le K\cdot c/m^{-2\alpha}$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.Ambient.dist\_deriv\_correlation\_le\_of\_complex\_circle\_bound}
(derivative-increment bridge from a complex circle bound, Issue \#3026)]
For two finite-volume Ising correlations on graphs $G_1,G_2$ at real parameters $J,h$ and a
real inverse temperature $\beta$, if both complex partition functions are nonzero on the
closed disc $\overline{B}(\beta,R)$ ($R>0$) and the complex correlation difference is bounded
by $B$ on the boundary circle $\partial B(\beta,R)$, then
\[
  \mathrm{dist}\bigl(\partial_\beta\langle\sigma^A\rangle_{G_1},
    \partial_\beta\langle\sigma^A\rangle_{G_2}\bigr) \le B/R.
\]
Assembles \texttt{correlationComplex\_diffContOnCl\_beta} (with \texttt{DiffContOnCl.sub}),
the real-part agreement \texttt{correlation\_ofReal\_eq\_correlationComplex},
differentiability from \texttt{correlationComplex\_analyticAt\_beta}, and the Cauchy bridge
\texttt{dist\_deriv\_le\_of\_complex\_extension}. This reduces the capstone $\mathtt{hincr}$
to its remaining \emph{complex} inputs: a zero-free closed disc $\overline{B}(\beta,R)$ of a
usable radius $R$ (uniform across the stages; real-axis positivity gives only local,
graph-dependent zero-freeness) together with a boundary-circle bound $B$ on the value
increment (a complex / Lee--Yang-region Simon--Lieb estimate).
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.Ambient.abs\_deriv\_le\_of\_complex\_extension} and
\texttt{dist\_deriv\_le\_of\_complex\_extension} (Cauchy-estimate derivative bridge, Issue \#3026)]
If a real function $g:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}$ is the real part of a complex-analytic
extension $G:\mathbb{C}\to\mathbb{C}$ on the disc $B(\beta,R)$, continuous up to the
boundary, with $\|G\|\le C$ on the circle $\partial B(\beta,R)$, then
\[
  |g'(\beta)| \le C/R.
\]
The complex derivative $G'(\beta)$ exists, its real part is $g'(\beta)$
(\texttt{HasDerivAt.real\_of\_complex}), and $|(G'(\beta)).\mathrm{re}|\le\|G'(\beta)\|\le
C/R$ by \texttt{Complex.abs\_re\_le\_norm} and Cauchy's estimate
\texttt{Complex.norm\_deriv\_le\_of\_forall\_mem\_sphere\_norm\_le}. The capstone-shaped
corollary gives $\mathrm{dist}(f'(\beta),h'(\beta))\le C/R$ when $f-h$ has such an
extension. This is the analytic device of the \emph{Cauchy-estimate route} to the
capstone $\mathtt{hincr}$: the $\beta$-derivative increment
$\mathrm{dist}(\partial_\beta c_k,\partial_\beta c_{k+1})=|\partial_\beta(c_k-c_{k+1})|$.
Applying the bridge still requires a \emph{complex} uniform bound $C$ on the value
increment $c_k-c_{k+1}$ over the boundary circle $\partial B(\beta,R)$ (a subsequent step;
the real-axis value-increment estimate alone does not suffice for Cauchy); given such a
complex bound the lemma yields the \emph{derivative}-increment bound $C/R$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.scaledCovariance\_edgeSpin\_zero\_sub\_one\_eq\_increment\_combination}
(coupling-difference summand as a correlation-increment combination, Issue \#2965 Phase C)]
Writing $\Delta(S):=\langle\sigma^S\rangle_0-\langle\sigma^S\rangle_1$ for the (negated)
bond-adding correlation increment over the cut set, each per-edge coupling-difference
summand decomposes as
\[
  \mathrm{Cov}_0(\sigma^A,\sigma_u\sigma_v)-\mathrm{Cov}_1(\sigma^A,\sigma_u\sigma_v)
    = \Delta(A\triangle\{u,v\}) - \Delta(A)\,\langle\sigma_u\sigma_v\rangle_0
      - \langle\sigma^A\rangle_1\,\Delta(\{u,v\}).
\]
Pure algebra (\texttt{ring}) from
\texttt{scaledCovariance\_edgeSpin\_zero\_sub\_one\_eq\_scaledCorrelation} by adding and
subtracting $\langle\sigma^A\rangle_1\langle\sigma_u\sigma_v\rangle_0$. The correlation
coefficients $\langle\sigma_u\sigma_v\rangle_0,\langle\sigma^A\rangle_1$ are bounded by
$1$, so this reduces the coupling-difference bound to bounding the three correlation
increments $\Delta(A\triangle\{u,v\}),\Delta(A),\Delta(\{u,v\})$ — each the bond-adding
increment over the cut set, controlled by the Part-A/B per-stage increment-decay
machinery.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.scaledCovariance\_spinProduct\_hamiltonian\_eq\_neg\_J\_edge\_sum}
and \texttt{\_coupling\_difference\_eq\_neg\_J\_edge\_sum} (energy covariance as a per-edge
sum, Issue \#2965 Phase C, $h=0$)]
For zero external field the Hamiltonian is the pure interaction energy
$H=-J\sum_{e\in\mathrm{edges}}\sigma_e$, so
\[
  \mathrm{Cov}_s(\sigma^A,H) = -J\sum_{e\in\mathrm{edges}}\mathrm{Cov}_s(\sigma^A,\sigma_e),
\]
and subtracting at the endpoints $s=0$ and $s=1$ (in the intended bond-deletion
application $s=0$ is the bond-deleted graph and $s=1$ the full graph) gives the
coupling difference
\[
  \mathrm{Cov}_0(\sigma^A,H)-\mathrm{Cov}_1(\sigma^A,H)
    = -J\sum_{e\in\mathrm{edges}}\bigl[\mathrm{Cov}_0(\sigma^A,\sigma_e)
        -\mathrm{Cov}_1(\sigma^A,\sigma_e)\bigr].
\]
Pure second-observable linearity (\texttt{scaledCovariance\_const\_mul\_right},
\texttt{\_sum\_right}, \texttt{Finset.sum\_sub\_distrib}). This rewrites the hard core of
the $\beta$-derivative increment decomposition as a sum over all edges of per-edge
covariance differences; in that application the bulk contributions (edges far from the
cut shell) are expected to cancel between $s=0$ and $s=1$, leaving a shell-localized
contribution (a later quantitative step, not established by these linearity lemmas).
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.scaledCovariance\_zero\_edgeSpin\_le\_lebowitz}
and \texttt{\_full} (per-edge shell covariance bounds, Issue \#2965 Phase C)]
For ferromagnetic $h=0$ and four distinct sites $x,z,u,v$, the $s=0$ summand of the
localized shell term satisfies
\[
  \mathrm{Cov}_0\!\bigl(\sigma^{\{x,z\}},\sigma_u\sigma_v\bigr)
    \le \langle\sigma_x\sigma_u\rangle_{\mathrm{bd}}\langle\sigma_z\sigma_v\rangle_{\mathrm{bd}}
      + \langle\sigma_x\sigma_v\rangle_{\mathrm{bd}}\langle\sigma_z\sigma_u\rangle_{\mathrm{bd}}
    \le \langle\sigma_x\sigma_u\rangle_G\langle\sigma_z\sigma_v\rangle_G
      + \langle\sigma_x\sigma_v\rangle_G\langle\sigma_z\sigma_u\rangle_G,
\]
where $\mathrm{bd}=G\setminus E_0$ is the bond-deleted graph. The first inequality is
the Lebowitz / $\mathrm{cor}\,4.3.3$ bound applied on the bond-deleted graph (via
\texttt{scaledCovariance\_zero\_edgeSpin\_eq\_bondDeleted\_ursell}); the second is GKS
bond-monotonicity \texttt{correlation\_deleteEdges\_le} ($\langle\cdot\rangle_{\mathrm{bd}}
\le\langle\cdot\rangle_G$, products of nonnegative correlations monotone). This can later
be combined, in the high-temperature cubic shell setting (contraction factor
$\mathrm{cf}<1$, fresh-shell geometry), with the infinite-volume correlation decay to
make the localized shell term $J\sum_{e\in E_0}\mathrm{Cov}_0(\sigma^{\{x,z\}},\sigma_e)$
geometric using the correlation-side Part-B machinery, without requiring Part B on the
bond-deleted graph.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.Ambient.scaledCovariance\_zero\_edgeSpin\_le\_infiniteVolume\_cross}
(cubic shell covariance bounded by the infinite-volume Lebowitz cross, Issue \#2965 Phase C)]
On the induced lattice graph over the cubic box $(\mathrm{cubicExhaustion}\,d).\mathrm{volume}\,n$,
for ferromagnetic $h=0$ and four distinct box vertices $x,z,u,v$,
\[
  \mathrm{Cov}_0\!\bigl(\sigma^{\{x,z\}},\sigma_u\sigma_v\bigr)
    \le g\{x,u\}\,g\{z,v\} + g\{x,v\}\,g\{z,u\},
\]
$g=\mathrm{correlationInfinite}$. Composes the abstract full-graph bound
\texttt{scaledCovariance\_zero\_edgeSpin\_le\_lebowitz\_full} with the
finite-to-infinite bridge \texttt{correlation\_inducedGraph\_cubic\_le\_correlationInfinite}
(applied to each of the four cross factors, nonnegativity from \texttt{gks\_first} /
\texttt{correlationInfinite\_nonneg}), mirroring the correlation-side
\texttt{ursell\_cubic\_le\_infiniteVolume\_cross}. For a cut edge $\{u,v\}$ straddling the
shell a fresh endpoint carries the infinite-volume spatial decay, so summing over the
cubic shell gives a geometric bound on the localized shell term
$J\sum_{e\in E_0}\mathrm{Cov}_0(\sigma^{\{x,z\}},\sigma_e)$, reusing the correlation-side
Part-B machinery.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.Ambient.scaledCovariance\_shell\_sum\_le\_card\_pow}
(geometric bound on the cubic shell covariance sum, Issue \#2965 Phase C)]
For $d\ge1$, ferromagnetic $h=0$, high temperature ($\mathrm{cf}<1$), interior sites
$x,z\in\mathrm{box}_R$ ($R\le k$, $x\ne z$) on no cut edge of the shell,
\[
  \sum_{e\in\mathrm{shell}}\mathrm{Cov}_0\!\bigl(\sigma^{\{x,z\}},\sigma_e\bigr)
    \le |\mathrm{shell}|\cdot 2\,\mathrm{cf}^{\lfloor(k+1-R)/(r_0+2)\rfloor}.
\]
Sums the per-edge bound
\texttt{scaledCovariance\_zero\_edgeSpin\_le\_infiniteVolume\_cross} over the cut shell:
each straddle edge has a fresh endpoint (\texttt{straddle\_fresh\_vertex}) carrying the
geometric infinite-volume decay (\texttt{correlationInfinite\_fresh\_le}), the partner
factor $\le1$. This is the shell (Part-B-type) contribution to the per-stage
$\beta$-derivative increment bound, mirroring the correlation-side
\texttt{ursell\_shell\_sum\_le\_card\_pow}; combined with
\texttt{scaledCovariance\_sum\_right} it bounds the localized shell term
$J\,\mathrm{Cov}_0(\sigma^A,\sum_{E_0}\sigma_e)$ of the increment decomposition.
\end{theorem}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.Ambient.ursell\_shell\_sum\_le\_card\_pow}
(geometric bound on the new-shell-edge Ursell sum, Issue \#2965 Phase C, Part B)]
For $d\ge1$, ferromagnetic $h=0$, high temperature ($\mathrm{cf}<1$), interior
sites $x,z\in\mathrm{box}_R$ ($R\le k$, $x\neq z$) on no cut edge of the
$\mathrm{box}_k$-slice,
\[
  \sum_{e\in\mathrm{shell}}\mathrm{Ursell}^{(k+1)}_e
    \le |\mathrm{shell}|\cdot 2\,\mathrm{cf}^{\,(k+1-R)/(r_0+2)}.
\]
Each straddle edge has a fresh endpoint
(\texttt{straddle\_fresh\_vertex}) whose infinite-volume correlation to $x,z$ decays
as $\mathrm{cf}^{\,(k+1-R)/(r_0+2)}$ (\texttt{correlationInfinite\_fresh\_le}), the
partner factor being $\le1$; \texttt{ursell\_cubic\_le\_infiniteVolume\_cross} bounds
each Ursell summand by the infinite-volume cross product. This is the Part B (shell)
contribution to the per-stage $\beta$-derivative increment, mirroring the
correlation-side \texttt{derivBoundTight\_cubic\_shell\_le\_card\_pow}.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proposition}[\texttt{IsingModel.Ambient.lemma\_17\_5\_2\_finite\_derivative\_increment\_eq}
(explicit $\beta$-derivative increment on covered stages, Issue \#2965 Phase C)]
For consecutive stages $k\subseteq k+1$ both containing $\{x,z\}$,
\[
  F_{k+1}(\beta)-F_k(\beta)
    = J\!\!\sum_{e\in E(\mathrm{box}_{k+1})}\!\!\mathrm{Ursell}^{(k+1)}_e
      - J\!\!\sum_{e\in E(\mathrm{box}_k)}\!\!\mathrm{Ursell}^{(k)}_e,
\]
where $\mathrm{Ursell}_e=\langle\sigma^{A\,\triangle\,\{u,v\}}\rangle
-\langle\sigma^A\rangle\langle\sigma_u\sigma_v\rangle$ ($A=\{x,z\}$). Immediate from
\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_finite\_derivative\_profile\_eq\_beta\_edge\_sum} at both
stages (here $E(\cdot)$ is over $\Lambda.\mathrm{volume}\,k$ and
$\Lambda.\mathrm{volume}\,(k+1)$ for an arbitrary exhaustion $\Lambda$). The
explicit starting point for the per-stage increment bound: the difference splits
algebraically into edges new to stage $k+1$ and edges shared with stage $k$. For
the cubic exhaustion the new edges form a shell far from $\{x,z\}$ (decaying Ursell)
and the shared interior Ursell terms differ only by the inter-stage correlation
differences — the intended later specialization.
\end{proposition}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{IsingModel.correlation\_inducedGraph\_deleteEdges\_union\_inl}
(Phase A component-factorization capstone, Finset route)]
For a region $S\subseteq W$, deleting the cut edges between $S$ and its complement
gives, for any $S$-supported observable $A$,
\[
  \langle\sigma^{A}\rangle_{\mathrm{inducedGraph}\,(G.\mathrm{deleteEdges}\,\{\text{straddle }S\})\,(S\cup S^{c})}
    = \langle\sigma^{A}\rangle_{\mathrm{inducedGraph}\,G\,S},
\]
i.e.\ the bond-deleted model's correlation of an $S$-internal observable equals
the correlation in the isolated induced subgraph on $S$ of the \emph{original}
model.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Compose \texttt{correlation\_inducedGraph\_union\_inl\_of\_no\_cross} (the
bond-deleted graph has no cross edges, by \texttt{deleteEdges\_straddle\_no\_cross})
with \texttt{correlation\_congr\_of\_eq} of
\texttt{inducedGraph\_deleteEdges\_eq\_of\_not\_internal} (deleting the cut leaves
the within-$S$ induced subgraph unchanged, by
\texttt{straddle\_not\_mem\_of\_same\_side}). This realizes the component
factorization via the Finset/\texttt{inducedGraph} route, sidestepping the
\texttt{Equiv.sumCompl} instance pathology. Combined with the ball-boundary
bond-deletion increment \texttt{correlation\_sub\_deleteEdges\_le\_derivBound}, it
yields the per-stage exhaustion increment bound (Issue \#2965, Phase A).
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Closing Prop 4.6.1.} Combined with the uniform upper
bound (PR \#122, \#123), Fekete's lemma now yields
convergence of $f_\Lambda = (\log Z_\Lambda)/|\Lambda|$ along
arbitrary exhaustions of the ambient lattice; this is the
Prop 4.6.1 body in the next PR.

\begin{theorem}[GJ \S 4.6 Prop 4.6.1, disjoint-tower \(+\)
\texttt{BoundedEdgeDensity} form; PR \#203]
For a graph $G : \mathrm{SimpleGraph}\, V$ with an exhaustion
$\Lambda : \mathrm{Exhaustion}\, V$, Ising parameters $p$ with
bounded edge density along $\Lambda$, and the disjoint-tower
hypotheses
\[
  |\Lambda_{m+n}| = |\Lambda_m| + |\Lambda_n|, \quad
  \log Z_{\Lambda_m} + \log Z_{\Lambda_n}
    \le \log Z_{\Lambda_{m+n}}, \quad
  |\Lambda_1| \neq 0,
\]
the exhaustion free-energy density $f_{\Lambda_n}
= (\log Z_{\Lambda_n})/|\Lambda_n|$ converges to the
infinite-volume free energy $f_\infty$:
\[
  \lim_{n\to\infty} f_{\Lambda_n}(p) = f_\infty(p).
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
This is a strict relaxation of the base theorem
\texttt{freeEnergyAlongExhaustion\_tendsto\_of\_superadditive}
(which requires in addition the explicit hypothesis
$\mathrm{BddAbove}\,(\mathrm{range}\,(f_\Lambda))$).
The \texttt{BoundedEdgeDensity} hypothesis yields that
$\mathrm{BddAbove}$ bound via
\texttt{BddAbove\_freeEnergyAlongExhaustion\_range}: if every
$\Lambda_n$ has at most $c\,|\Lambda_n|$ edges, then each
$f_{\Lambda_n}$ is bounded by
$\log 2 + |\beta|(|J|c + |h|)$ (for nonempty stages, combined with
$0$ on empty stages via the $\max$ with $0$).
The remaining proof steps are as in the base theorem: apply
mathlib's \texttt{Subadditive.tendsto\_lim} to
$u_n := -\log Z_{\Lambda_n}$, translate via $|\Lambda_n| =
n\cdot|\Lambda_1|$ (from \texttt{hcard\_add}) to obtain
$f_{\Lambda_n} = -u_n/(n\,|\Lambda_1|)$, and identify the limit
with $f_\infty = \limsup f_{\Lambda_n}$ via
\texttt{freeEnergyInfinite\_eq\_of\_tendsto}. Reference:
Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe} \S 4.6 Prop 4.6.1, p.~68.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Translation-invariance scaffolding for GJ \S 4.6
Prop 4.6.1 (PRs \#220--\#224).}
A multi-PR programme toward dropping the disjoint-tower hypotheses
of \texttt{freeEnergyAlongExhaustion\_tendsto\_of\_disjoint\_tower}
(PR \#203). Completed steps:
\begin{enumerate}
\item \texttt{Ambient.IsTranslationInvariant G}: a
  \texttt{SimpleGraph V} is translation invariant under an
  \texttt{AddAction T V} if the edge relation is preserved
  ($G.Adj\,(t +_v u)\,(t +_v v) \leftrightarrow G.Adj\,u\,v$).
  Trivial instances for $\bot$ and $\top$.
\item \texttt{vaddFinset t A := A.image (...)} using the map
  $u \mapsto t +_v u$, with
  \texttt{vaddFinset\_card}, \texttt{vaddFinset\_zero},
  \texttt{vaddFinset\_disjoint\_of\_disjoint}.
\item \texttt{TranslationInvariantExhaustion T V}
  (\texttt{extends Exhaustion V}) with fields
  \texttt{shift} of type $\mathbb N \to T$, \texttt{shift\_zero},
  \texttt{volume\_zero}, \texttt{volume\_succ}
  ($\Lambda_{n+1} = \Lambda_n \cup (\mathrm{shift}\,n
  +_v \Lambda_1)$),
  \texttt{disjoint\_shift}. Gives automatic
  \texttt{volume\_card\_add}: $|\Lambda_{m+n}| = |\Lambda_m| +
  |\Lambda_n|$.
\item \texttt{disjointTowerHypotheses\_of\_translationInvariant}:
  given a \texttt{TranslationInvariantExhaustion} + user-supplied
  \texttt{hsuper} + \texttt{hcard\_one}, assemble a full
  \texttt{DisjointTowerHypotheses} record.
\item \texttt{freeEnergyAlongExhaustion\_tendsto\_of\_translationInvariant}:
  combined with \texttt{BoundedEdgeDensity}, yields the Fekete
  convergence to \texttt{freeEnergyInfinite}.
\end{enumerate}
\emph{Remaining}: derive \texttt{hsuper} from log-$Z$ translation
invariance. Requires Ising-Hamiltonian translation equivariance
(separate multi-PR step).
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe} \S 4.6 Prop 4.6.1,
p.~68.

\begin{theorem}[Finset-based susceptibility at $J = 0$ closed form;
PR \#231]
For the repo-level Finset-based susceptibility
$\chi := \sum_j U_2(i,j)$, where $U_2(i,j) = \langle
\sigma^{\{i,j\}}\rangle - \langle\sigma^{\{i\}}\rangle
\langle\sigma^{\{j\}}\rangle$,
for any ambient graph $G$ with finite $\iota$ and any
$h, \beta \in \mathbb{R}$,
\[
  \chi^{\langle 0, h, \beta\rangle}_G(i)
  = \tanh(\beta h) \cdot \big(1 - \tanh(\beta h)\big).
\]
(Caveat: this differs from the physics response-function
$dM/dh = \beta(1-t^2)$ at the diagonal, because the Finset
collapse $\{i,i\} = \{i\}$ gives
$\langle\sigma^{\{i\}}\rangle - \langle\sigma^{\{i\}}\rangle^2
= t - t^2$ rather than the physics
$\langle\sigma_i^2\rangle - \langle\sigma_i\rangle^2 = 1 - t^2$.)
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
$\chi(i) := \sum_j U_2(i,j)$. For $j \neq i$,
\texttt{truncated2\_J\_zero\_of\_ne} (PR \#207) gives summand zero.
For $j = i$, the Finset $\{i,i\}$ collapses to $\{i\}$, so
$U_2(i,i) = \langle \sigma^{\{i\}}\rangle -
\langle \sigma^{\{i\}}\rangle^2 = t - t^2$ with $t = \tanh(\beta h)$
(\texttt{correlation\_J\_zero} + card$\,\{i\}=1$). Factor to
$t(1-t)$. Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe} \S 4.1
(non-interacting slice); \S 5.1 pp.~76--77 (susceptibility).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Finite-volume magnetization at $J = 0$
$= \tanh(\beta h)$; PR \#219]
For any graph $G$ with finite $\iota$, any $h, \beta \in \mathbb{R}$,
and any site $i$, the finite-volume magnetization satisfies
$M^{\langle 0, h, \beta\rangle}_G(i) = \tanh(\beta h)$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
$\mathrm{magnetization}(p; i) := \mathrm{correlation}(p; \{i\})$.
Apply the finite-volume closed form
$\mathrm{correlation}(\langle 0, h, \beta\rangle; A)
= \tanh(\beta h)^{|A|}$ (\texttt{correlation\_J\_zero}) with
$A = \{i\}$: $|A| = 1$, so the power reduces to
$\tanh(\beta h)$.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe} \S 4.1
(non-interacting $J = 0$ slice); \S 5.1 pp.~76--77
(magnetization).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[$\infty$-vol magnetization at $J = 0$
ferromagnetic = $\tanh(\beta h)$; PR \#218]
For a ferromagnetic parameter $\langle 0, h, \beta \rangle$ and
any graph $G$ with exhaustion $\Lambda$, the infinite-volume
magnetization at a site $i$ is
\[
  M^\infty(G, \Lambda; \langle 0, h, \beta\rangle; i)
  = \tanh(\beta h).
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
$\mathrm{magnetizationInfinite}(p; i) :=
\mathrm{correlationInfinite}(p; \{i\})$. Apply the closed form
$\mathrm{correlationInfinite}(\langle 0, h, \beta\rangle; A) =
\tanh(\beta h)^{|A|}$ (PR \#210) with $A = \{i\}$, so
$|A| = 1$ and the power reduces to $\tanh(\beta h)$.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe} \S 4.1
(non-interacting $J = 0$ slice; $\beta$ is unrestricted here, so
this is distinct from the infinite-temperature $\beta = 0$
slice); \S 5.1 pp.~76--77 (magnetization).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Lebowitz 4-point $J = 0$ closed form
$-2\,t^4$ (finite and $\infty$-volume); PR \#215]
For any ambient graph $G$, any $h, \beta \in \mathbb{R}$, and
pairwise distinct sites $i, j, k, l$,
\[
  U_4^{\text{Lebowitz}}(G, \langle 0,h,\beta\rangle; i,j,k,l)
  = -2\,\tanh(\beta h)^4.
\]
The same formula holds for the infinite-volume Lebowitz 4-point
under a ferromagnetic hypothesis.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
At $J = 0$,
$\langle\sigma^A\rangle_{\langle 0,h,\beta\rangle}
= \tanh(\beta h)^{|A|}$
by \texttt{correlation\_J\_zero} (finite volume) or
\texttt{correlationInfinite\_J\_zero} (ferromagnetic
infinite volume, PR \#210). With pairwise distinct
$i, j, k, l$ the seven Finset cardinals are
$4$ (once) and $2$ (six times), so
\[
  U_4 = t^4 - 3(t^2 \cdot t^2) = t^4 - 3\,t^4 = -2\,t^4,
  \quad t := \tanh(\beta h).
\]
Note $-2\,t^4 \le 0$ unconditionally, consistent with
$U_4 \le 0$ (Glimm--Jaffe Cor. 4.3.3).
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe} \S 5.1 pp.~72--74
(cluster context); \S 4.3 Cor. 4.3.3 / Lebowitz.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Lebowitz 4-point $\beta = 0$ vanishing (finite and
$\infty$-volume); PR \#214]
For any ambient graph $G$, any $J, h \in \mathbb{R}$, any sites
$i, j, k, l$:
$U_4^{\text{Lebowitz}}(G, \langle J,h,0\rangle; i,j,k,l) = 0$
in finite volume, and
$U_4^{\text{Lebowitz}}(\infty; i,j,k,l) = 0$ at infinite volume
along any exhaustion.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
At $\beta = 0$ every Boltzmann weight is $1$, so
$\langle \sigma^A \rangle = 0$ for any nonempty Finset
$A \subseteq \iota$
(\texttt{correlation\_beta\_zero\_vanish\_of\_nonempty\_A}). The
Lebowitz 4-point
$U_4 = \langle \sigma^{\{i,j,k,l\}}\rangle
- \langle\sigma^{\{i,j\}}\rangle\langle\sigma^{\{k,l\}}\rangle
- \langle\sigma^{\{i,k\}}\rangle\langle\sigma^{\{j,l\}}\rangle
- \langle\sigma^{\{i,l\}}\rangle\langle\sigma^{\{j,k\}}\rangle$
has all seven Finset correlations over nonempty subsets (each
contains at least one supplied site), so every term is $0$ and
$U_4 = 0$.

$\infty$-volume version is identical with
\texttt{correlationInfinite\_beta\_zero\_vanish\_of\_nonempty\_A}
in place of the finite-volume lemma.

Note: at $J = 0$ the Lebowitz 4-point is $-2\,t^4$ where
$t = \tanh(\beta h)$ for pairwise-distinct sites, which is
non-zero when $\beta h \neq 0$, so the $J = 0$ slice does
\emph{not} vanish.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe} \S 5.1 pp.~72--74
(cluster context); \S 4.3 Cor. 4.3.3 / Lebowitz.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[$\infty$-vol truncated-3 trivial slices; PR \#212]
For a ferromagnetic $\langle 0, h, \beta\rangle$ and pairwise
distinct $i, j, k$, the $\infty$-vol Ursell 3-point function
$\langle \sigma_i; \sigma_j; \sigma_k \rangle_\infty^{\langle
0,h,\beta\rangle}$ vanishes. For $\langle J, h, 0\rangle$ and any
sites, the $\infty$-vol Ursell 3-point function also vanishes.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
$J = 0$: Substitute
$\langle \sigma^A \rangle_\infty = \tanh(\beta h)^{|A|}$ (the PR \#210
closed form) into each of the seven Finset correlations, use
$|\{i,j,k\}| = 3, |\{i\}|=|\{j\}|=|\{k\}|=1,
|\{i,j\}|=|\{j,k\}|=|\{i,k\}|=2$, and reduce the Ursell
combination to $t^3 - 3\cdot t^3 + 2\cdot t^3 = 0$ with
$t := \tanh(\beta h)$.

$\beta = 0$: Every Finset correlation in the Ursell expression is
over a nonempty Finset, so the PR \#211 vanishing
$\langle \sigma^A \rangle_\infty^{\langle J,h,0\rangle} = 0$
forces each term to zero.

Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe} \S 5.1 pp.~72--74
(cluster context); \S 4.3 (Ursell / GHS).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[$\infty$-vol $\beta = 0$ correlation vanishing and
truncated-2 zero; PR \#211]
For any ambient graph $G$, any exhaustion $\Lambda :
\mathrm{Exhaustion}\,V$, any $J, h \in \mathbb{R}$, and any
nonempty $A \subseteq V$,
\[
  \langle \sigma^A \rangle_\infty^{G,\Lambda,\langle J,h,0\rangle}
  = 0.
\]
In particular, for any sites $i, j : V$ (distinct or not),
$\langle \sigma_i; \sigma_j \rangle_\infty^{G,\Lambda,\langle
J,h,0\rangle} = 0$.
No ferromagnetic hypothesis is required.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
At $\beta = 0$ every Boltzmann weight equals $1$, so
\texttt{correlation\_beta\_zero\_vanish\_of\_nonempty\_A} gives
finite-volume vanishing on any nonempty Finset. Pulling this to
the along-exhaustion sequence: when $A \subseteq \Lambda.volume\,n$
the lifted subset is still nonempty (via \texttt{liftFinset\_card})
and the finite-volume lemma applies; when
$A \not\subseteq \Lambda.volume\,n$ the
$\mathrm{correlationAlongExhaustion}$ is $0$ by definition. Hence
the sequence is pointwise zero, so
$\mathrm{correlationInfinite} = \bigsqcup_n 0 = 0$.
The truncated-2 vanishing follows by substitution at
$\{i,j\}, \{i\}, \{j\}$ — all three are nonempty, and $i = j$ is
handled by the Finset collapse $\{i,j\} = \{i\}$.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe} \S 5.1 pp.~72--74
(cluster context); \S 4.1 (infinite-temperature slice).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[$\infty$-vol $J = 0$ correlation closed form and
truncated-2 vanishing; PR \#210]
For a ferromagnetic parameter $\langle 0, h, \beta \rangle$
(i.e. $h \ge 0$, $0 < \beta$; the strict-$\beta$ condition comes
from \texttt{Ferromagnetic.hβ}), any graph $G :
\mathrm{SimpleGraph}\, V$ and exhaustion $\Lambda :
\mathrm{Exhaustion}\, V$, and any finite $A \subseteq V$,
\[
  \langle \sigma^A \rangle_\infty^{G,\Lambda,\langle 0,h,\beta\rangle}
  = \tanh(\beta h)^{|A|}.
\]
For distinct $i \neq j$ the infinite-volume truncated 2-point
function vanishes:
$\langle \sigma_i; \sigma_j \rangle_\infty^{G,\Lambda,\langle 0,h,\beta\rangle}
= 0$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
By the finite-volume closed form $\langle \sigma^A
\rangle_\Lambda^{\langle 0,h,\beta\rangle} = \tanh(\beta h)^{|A|}$
(see \texttt{correlation\_J\_zero}), when $A \subseteq \Lambda.volume\,n$
we have
$\text{correlationAlongExhaustion}\,G\,\Lambda\,\langle 0,h,\beta\rangle\,A\,n
= \tanh(\beta h)^{|A|}$
(the Finset lift preserves cardinality). By $\Lambda.\mathrm{exhaust}$,
this holds for all sufficiently large $n$, so the sequence is
eventually constant at $\tanh(\beta h)^{|A|}$. On the other hand,
by \texttt{correlationAlongExhaustion\_tendsto\_ciSup}
(ferromagnetic, monotone + bounded), the sequence also tends to
$\mathrm{correlationInfinite}\,G\,\Lambda\,\langle 0,h,\beta\rangle\,A$.
Uniqueness of limits gives the claimed closed form.
The truncated-2 vanishing is immediate by substitution at
$\{i,j\}, \{i\}, \{j\}$ and the cardinalities $2, 1, 1$.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe} \S 5.1 pp.~72--74
(cluster property context); \S 4.1 (infinite-temperature slice).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Trivial-slice vanishing of the truncated 3-point
(Ursell) function; PR \#209]
For any ambient graph $G$, any $h, \beta \in \mathbb{R}$, and
pairwise distinct sites $i, j, k$,
$\langle \sigma_i; \sigma_j; \sigma_k
\rangle_{G,\langle 0,h,\beta\rangle} = 0$.
For any $J, h \in \mathbb{R}$ and any sites $i, j, k$ — distinctness
is not required, since repeated indices collapse at the Finset
level inside the Lean definition of \texttt{truncated3}, paralleling
\texttt{truncated2\_beta\_zero} —
$\langle \sigma_i; \sigma_j; \sigma_k
\rangle_{G,\langle J,h,0\rangle} = 0$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
$J = 0$ case. By \texttt{correlation\_J\_zero},
$\langle \sigma^A \rangle_{\langle 0,h,\beta\rangle} =
\tanh(\beta h)^{|A|}$. With $t := \tanh(\beta h)$ and pairwise
distinct $i, j, k$ one has $|\{i,j,k\}| = 3$ and each pair has
card $2$. The Ursell combination becomes
\[
  t^3 - 3\,(t \cdot t^2) + 2\,t^3
  = t^3 - 3\,t^3 + 2\,t^3 = 0.
\]

$\beta = 0$ case. By
\texttt{correlation\_beta\_zero\_vanish\_of\_nonempty\_A}, every
nonempty-subset correlation in the Ursell combination vanishes,
so the linear combination is $0$.

Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe} \S 5.1 pp.~72--74
(cluster property context); \S 4.3 (Ursell functions / GHS).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Infinite-temperature ($\beta=0$) vanishing of the
truncated 2-point function; PR \#208]
For any ambient graph $G$, any $J, h \in \mathbb{R}$, and any sites
$i, j : \iota$ (distinct or not),
\[
  \langle \sigma_i; \sigma_j \rangle_{G,\langle J,h,0\rangle} = 0.
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
At $\beta = 0$, the Boltzmann weight $\exp(-\beta H) \equiv 1$, so
$\langle \sigma^A \rangle_{G,\langle J,h,0\rangle}$ is the uniform
spin average. By
\texttt{correlation\_beta\_zero\_vanish\_of\_nonempty\_A}, this
vanishes on any nonempty $A$. Hence each of
$\langle\sigma^{\{i,j\}}\rangle$,
$\langle\sigma^{\{i\}}\rangle$, $\langle\sigma^{\{j\}}\rangle$
is $0$, so the truncated value is $0 - 0 \cdot 0 = 0$.
Note that unlike the $J = 0$ slice, here no $i \neq j$ hypothesis
is required: if $i = j$ the Finset first term
$\langle\sigma^{\{i,j\}}\rangle$ collapses to
$\langle\sigma^{\{i\}}\rangle$ (not the physics product
$\langle\sigma_i\sigma_i\rangle = 1$) and again vanishes at
$\beta = 0$.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe} \S 5.1,
pp.~72--74 (cluster property context); §4.1 infinite-temperature
slice of the correlation function is the direct source of
\texttt{correlation\_beta\_zero\_vanish\_of\_nonempty\_A}.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Non-interacting ($J=0$) factorisation of the
truncated 2-point function; PR \#207]
For any ambient graph $G : \mathrm{SimpleGraph}\, \iota$ with
$\iota$ finite, any $h, \beta \in \mathbb{R}$, and any distinct
sites $i \neq j$ in $\iota$,
\[
  \langle \sigma_i; \sigma_j \rangle_{G,\langle 0,h,\beta\rangle}
  = \langle \sigma_i \sigma_j \rangle - \langle \sigma_i\rangle
  \langle \sigma_j \rangle = 0.
\]
No distance / separation hypothesis is needed: the
factorisation is identically true for any two distinct sites.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
By \texttt{correlation\_J\_zero}, for any subset
$A \subseteq \iota$,
$\langle \sigma^A \rangle_{G,\langle 0,h,\beta\rangle} =
\tanh(\beta h)^{|A|}$.
With $i \neq j$ one has $|\{i,j\}| = 2$ and $|\{i\}| = |\{j\}| = 1$,
so
\[
  \langle \sigma^{\{i,j\}} \rangle = \tanh(\beta h)^{2}, \qquad
  \langle \sigma^{\{i\}} \rangle\langle \sigma^{\{j\}}\rangle
  = \tanh(\beta h)^{1} \cdot \tanh(\beta h)^{1}
  = \tanh(\beta h)^{2},
\]
and the difference vanishes.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe} \S 5.1, pp.~72--74
(cluster property context). This is the trivial non-interacting
slice of the cluster property discussion; note that $\beta$ is
arbitrary, so this is distinct from the high-temperature
($\beta$ small) regime. Non-zero-$J$ decay at large separation
in pure phases remains follow-up work.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[GJ \S 4.6 Prop 4.6.1, log-linear-card builder and
$\beta = 0$ instance; PR \#206]
(i) If $\log Z_\Lambda = |\Lambda| \cdot c$ for all stages, then the
super-additivity field of
\texttt{DisjointTowerHypotheses} is automatic under
\texttt{hcard\_add} — this is the generic builder
\texttt{DisjointTowerHypotheses.of\_log\_linear\_card}.

(ii) At $\beta = 0$ one has $Z_\Lambda = 2^{|\Lambda|}$, hence
$\log Z_\Lambda = |\Lambda| \cdot \log 2$, fitting the
log-linear pattern. So
\texttt{DisjointTowerHypotheses.of\_beta\_zero} and the Fekete
corollary
\texttt{freeEnergyAlongExhaustion\_beta\_zero\_tendsto\_of\_hcard\_add}
follow immediately, parallel to the $J = 0$ case.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
(i) Super-additivity reduces to
$(|\Lambda_m| + |\Lambda_n|) \cdot c \le |\Lambda_{m+n}| \cdot c$;
under \texttt{hcard\_add} the two sides are \emph{equal}, so the
proof does not rely on any sign assumption on $c$ — it is a pure
equality reduction, not a monotonicity argument.
(ii) \texttt{IsingModel.partitionFunction\_beta\_zero} +
\texttt{card\_config\_eq\_two\_pow} + \texttt{Real.log\_pow} give
$\log Z_\Lambda = |\Lambda| \cdot \log 2$. The rest is
\texttt{of\_log\_linear\_card} applied with $c = \log 2$.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe} \S 4.6 Prop 4.6.1, p.~68.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[GJ \S 4.6 Prop 4.6.1, concrete $J = 0$ instance; PR \#205]
At $J = 0$, with bounded edge density along $\Lambda$, a
cardinality-additive exhaustion
($|\Lambda_{m+n}| = |\Lambda_m| + |\Lambda_n|$) with
$|\Lambda_1| \neq 0$, the exhaustion free-energy density
converges to the infinite-volume free energy:
\[
  f_{\Lambda_n}(0, h, \beta) \longrightarrow f_\infty(0, h, \beta).
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
At $J = 0$, \texttt{IsingModel.partitionFunction\_J\_zero} gives
$Z_\Lambda = (2\cosh(\beta h))^{|\Lambda|}$, so
$\log Z_\Lambda = |\Lambda| \cdot \log(2\cosh(\beta h))$. The
\texttt{DisjointTowerHypotheses} record's super-additivity field
becomes
\[
  (|\Lambda_m| + |\Lambda_n|) \log(2\cosh(\beta h))
  \le |\Lambda_{m+n}| \log(2\cosh(\beta h)),
\]
which holds with equality under \texttt{hcard\_add}. Combined with
\texttt{BoundedEdgeDensity}, the bundled Fekete theorem
\texttt{freeEnergyAlongExhaustion\_tendsto\_of\_disjointTowerHypotheses}
closes the proof.  Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe} \S 4.6
Prop 4.6.1, p.~68. This is the first concrete
\texttt{DisjointTowerHypotheses} instance; non-$J\!=\!0$ instances
require translation invariance and remain follow-up.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[GJ \S 4.6 Prop 4.6.1,
\texttt{DisjointTowerHypotheses} bundle; PR \#204]
The three structural hypotheses $|\Lambda_{m+n}| = |\Lambda_m|
+ |\Lambda_n|$, $\log Z_{\Lambda_m} + \log Z_{\Lambda_n} \le
\log Z_{\Lambda_{m+n}}$, and $|\Lambda_1| \neq 0$ are packaged
as a single \texttt{DisjointTowerHypotheses} record. Given
\texttt{BoundedEdgeDensity}$\,G\,\Lambda$ and such a record,
$f_{\Lambda_n}(p) \to f_\infty(p)$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Definitional unpack of the
\texttt{DisjointTowerHypotheses} record into the three
hypotheses of
\texttt{freeEnergyAlongExhaustion\_tendsto\_of\_disjoint\_tower}.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe} \S 4.6 Prop 4.6.1,
p.~68. This is an API-site convenience wrapper; no new
mathematical content.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Direct $\limsup$ corollary of GJ \S 5.4 Prop 5.4.2
along an exhaustion; PR \#213]
Under the same per-stage hypotheses as PR \#202, the
$\limsup_n (1 - \langle \sigma_{i_n} \rangle_{+}^{G_n, B_n})$ along
\texttt{atTop} is bounded above by $\exp(-c\beta)$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
From the per-stage bound of PR \#202
($\texttt{prop\_5\_4\_2\_along\_exhaustion}$): at every $n$,
$0 \le 1 - \langle \sigma_{i_n} \rangle_{+}^{G_n, B_n} \le
\exp(-c\beta)$. Apply mathlib's
\texttt{Filter.limsup\_le\_of\_le}: the eventually-uniform upper
bound $\exp(-c\beta)$ holds pointwise, and the
\(\texttt{IsCoboundedUnder}~(\cdot \le \cdot)\) side condition is
satisfied by the pointwise lower bound $0$
(\texttt{isCoboundedUnder\_le\_of\_eventually\_le} with $x = 0$).
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe} \S 5.4 Prop 5.4.2,
p.~83. This is a direct $\limsup$ corollary; strengthening to a
genuine infinite-volume $+$-BC expectation \emph{inequality}
remains follow-up work.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Corollary/wrapper of GJ \S 5.4 Prop 5.4.2 along an
exhaustion (per-stage); PR \#202]
For a graph $G : \mathrm{SimpleGraph}\, V$ with an exhaustion
$\Lambda : \mathrm{Ambient.Exhaustion}\, V$, suppose that for every
stage $n$ the induced graph $G_n := \mathrm{Ambient.inducedGraph}\,G\,(\Lambda_n)$
is preconnected, $B_n \subseteq \Lambda_n$ is a non-empty set of
$+$-boundary-condition sites,
$i_n \in \Lambda_n$, and the common exponential bound
\[
  2 \cdot 2^{\#\Lambda_n} \exp(-2\beta J) \le \exp(-c\beta)
\]
holds uniformly in $n$. Then, for $J, \beta > 0$ and $h = 0$, the
Peierls bound
\[
  0 \;\le\; 1 - \langle \sigma_{i_n} \rangle_{+}^{G_n, B_n}
  \;\le\; \exp(-c\beta)
\]
holds at every $n$.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Direct application of \texttt{prop\_5\_4\_2\_self\_contained}
(Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe} \S 5.4 p.~83) at each stage $n$;
the hypotheses at stage $n$ are the finite-volume hypotheses of
that theorem specialised to $G_n$, $B_n$, $i_n$. This is a
scaffolding step: subsequent PRs on the same branch will pick a
canonical $(B_n, i_n)$ from the ambient geometry and take the
$\limsup$ / $\liminf$ to recover the genuine infinite-volume
bound $0 \le 1 - \langle\sigma_i\rangle_+^\infty \le \exp(-c\beta)$
(Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe} \S 5.4 p.~83).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Groundwork for GJ \S 4.6 Prop 4.6.1 — concrete
translation-invariant lattice; PR \#244]
For every $d : \mathbb{N}$, the Ising lattice graph
$\texttt{latticeGraph}\ d : \operatorname{SimpleGraph}(\mathrm{Fin}\ d \to \mathbb{Z})$
(edges between points at $\ell^1$-distance $1$) is invariant under
the canonical self-action of $\mathrm{Fin}\ d \to \mathbb{Z}$ on
itself:
\[
  \texttt{Ambient.IsTranslationInvariant}\ (\mathrm{Fin}\ d \to \mathbb{Z})\ (\texttt{latticeGraph}\ d).
\]
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
On the self-action, $t +_v u = t + u$ pointwise, hence
$(t +_v u) - (t +_v v) = u - v$ in every coordinate. The defining
ℓ¹ condition $\sum_i |x_i - y_i| = 1$ is therefore preserved in
both directions.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Remark.} This is the first non-trivial
\texttt{IsTranslationInvariant} instance — earlier examples
$(\bot, \top)$ are structurally trivial (no edges / all pairs).
The lattice graph $\texttt{latticeGraph}\ d$ is the natural ambient
graph behind the \S 4.6 Prop 4.6.1 translation-invariant exhaustion
programme. The natural two-sided cubic exhaustion
$\Lambda_n = \{x : \mathrm{Fin}\ d \to \mathbb{Z} \mid \forall i,\ -n \le x_i \le n\}$
does not fit the current
\texttt{TranslationInvariantExhaustion} structure, which demands
that each stage differ from the previous by a single translated base
block (\texttt{volume\_succ}) with \texttt{shift} an additive
homomorphism $\mathbb{N} \to T$. Assembling a compatible concrete
exhaustion requires either relaxing the single-block constraint or
altering the \texttt{Exhaustion} interface, and is deferred to a
subsequent PR.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe} \S 4.6 Prop 4.6.1, p.~68.

\begin{theorem}[GJ \S 4.6 concrete: cubic \texttt{Ambient.Exhaustion} on
$\mathbb{Z}^d$; PR \#245]
For every $d : \mathbb{N}$, the \emph{cubic box}
\[
  \mathrm{cubicBox}\ d\ n
  := \mathrm{Fintype.piFinset}\ (\lambda \_\ \Rightarrow\
    \mathrm{Finset.Icc}\ (-(n:\mathbb{Z}))\ n)
\]
determines an \texttt{Ambient.Exhaustion} of the integer lattice
$\mathrm{Fin}\ d \to \mathbb{Z}$ via
$\mathrm{cubicExhaustion}\ d .\mathrm{volume} := \mathrm{cubicBox}\ d$,
together with the monotonicity lemma $m \le n \Rightarrow
\mathrm{cubicBox}\ d\ m \subseteq \mathrm{cubicBox}\ d\ n$ and the
exhaust lemma: every finite $A \subset \mathbb{Z}^d$ is contained in
some $\mathrm{cubicBox}\ d\ N$ where $N$ is the maximum absolute
coordinate across points of $A$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}[Exhaust lemma]
Let $S := \{|a_i|^{\mathrm{nat}} : a \in A,\ i : \mathrm{Fin}\ d\}$
as a \texttt{Finset $\mathbb{N}$}. In the non-degenerate case
$A \ne \emptyset$ and $d \ne 0$, $S$ is non-empty and we take
$N := \mathrm{Finset.max'}\ S$. For any $n \ge N$ and $a \in A$,
$i : \mathrm{Fin}\ d$, we have $|a_i|^{\mathrm{nat}} \le N \le n$,
so $|a_i| \le n$ in $\mathbb{Z}$, hence $-n \le a_i \le n$ and
$a \in \mathrm{cubicBox}\ d\ n$. In the degenerate case $S = \emptyset$
(equivalent to $A = \emptyset \vee d = 0$), take $N := 0$; the
membership goal $A \subseteq \mathrm{cubicBox}\ d\ n$ reduces,
under the assumption $a \in A$, to producing an element of $S$ (from
any $i : \mathrm{Fin}\ d$ supplied by $\mathrm{mem\_cubicBox}$'s
intro), contradicting $S = \emptyset$.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Remark.} This is the first concrete
\texttt{Ambient.Exhaustion} on the physical integer lattice — prior
instances exist only on abstract $V$. It makes
$\mathrm{correlationInfinite}\ (\mathrm{latticeGraph}\ d)\
(\mathrm{cubicExhaustion}\ d)\ p\ A$ an explicit object. A matching
\texttt{TranslationInvariantExhaustion} is not provided (structural
obstruction noted above); bounded-edge-density for
$\mathrm{latticeGraph}\ d$ along this exhaustion is deferred to a
subsequent PR.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe} \S 4.6, p.~67.

\begin{theorem}[Bounded edge density for $\mathrm{latticeGraph}\,d$
on cubic boxes; PR \#246]
For every $d : \mathbb{N}$,
\[
  \mathrm{Ambient.BoundedEdgeDensity}\ (\mathrm{IsingModel.latticeGraph}\,d)\
  (\mathrm{Ambient.cubicExhaustion}\,d)
\]
holds with constant $c = d$: $|E(\mathrm{latticeGraph}\,d[\Lambda_n])| \le
d \cdot |\Lambda_n|$ where $\Lambda_n = \mathrm{cubicBox}\,d\,n$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
\emph{Vertex degree bound.} For every vertex $v : \mathrm{Fin}\,d \to \mathbb{Z}$,
the neighbours in $\mathrm{latticeGraph}\,d$ lie in the $2d$-element set
$\{\mathrm{Function.update}\,v\,i\,(v_i \pm 1) : i : \mathrm{Fin}\,d\}$
(formalised as \texttt{latticeNeighborEnum}). The ℓ¹-distance-$1$ condition
$\sum_j |v_j - w_j| = 1$ forces exactly one coordinate $i$ to differ from $v$,
with $|v_i - w_i| = 1$. Restricting to $w \in \Lambda_n$ only lowers the
degree, so $\deg_{\mathrm{induced}}(v) \le 2d$ for every $v \in \Lambda_n$.

\emph{Handshake.} By the degree-sum formula
$\sum_{v \in \Lambda_n} \deg(v) = 2|E|$, we get
$2|E| \le 2d |\Lambda_n|$, hence $|E| \le d |\Lambda_n|$ (divide by $2$).
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Remark.}
Combined with $\mathrm{cubicExhaustion}\,d$ (PR~\#245), this closes the
concrete-ambient-lattice infrastructure for GJ \S 4.6 modulo the still
missing super-additivity ingredient (a compatible
$\mathrm{TranslationInvariantExhaustion}$). The per-stage
$\mathrm{freeEnergyAlongExhaustion}$ bound
$\le \log 2 + |\beta|(|J|\cdot d + |h|)$ now has an explicit constant.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe \cite{glimm-jaffe} \S 4.6, p.~67.

\begin{theorem}[Concrete $\mathbb{Z}^d$ Ising free-energy sandwich; PR \#247]
For every $d : \mathbb{N}$, ferromagnetic parameters $0 \le J$,
$0 \le h$, $0 < \beta$, and any stage $n$ with $(\mathrm{cubicExhaustion}\,d)
.\mathrm{volume}\,n$ non-empty,
\[
  \log 2
    \le \mathrm{freeEnergyAlongExhaustion}\ (\mathrm{latticeGraph}\,d)\
        (\mathrm{cubicExhaustion}\,d)\ \langle J, h, \beta\rangle\ n
    \le \log 2 + |\beta| \cdot (|J| \cdot d + |h|).
\]
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
The lower bound is the ferromagnetic, nonempty-stage specialisation
of $\mathrm{freeEnergyAlongExhaustion\_ge\_log\_two}$ in
$\texttt{AmbientLattice.lean}$. The upper bound applies
$\mathrm{freeEnergyAlongExhaustion\_le\_uniform\_upper\_bound}$ with
the concrete edge-density constant $c = d$ furnished by
$\mathrm{inducedLatticeGraph\_card\_edgeFinset\_le}$ (PR~\#246).
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Remark.}
Capstone for the concrete $\mathbb{Z}^d$ infrastructure developed
across PRs~\#244 ($\mathrm{IsTranslationInvariant}$),
\#245 ($\mathrm{cubicExhaustion}\,d$), and \#246
($\mathrm{BoundedEdgeDensity}$ with $c = d$). The Fekete convergence
$f_n \to f_\infty$ still requires a compatible
$\mathrm{TranslationInvariantExhaustion}$ on $\mathbb{Z}^d$, deferred
pending a structure refactor.

\begin{theorem}[Shifted exhaustion; PR \#249]
Let $T$ be an additive group acting on $V$ (with $\mathrm{DecidableEq}\,V$).
For every $\mathrm{Ambient.Exhaustion}\,V\ \Lambda$ and $t : T$,
\[
  \Lambda.\mathrm{shift}\,t : \mathrm{Ambient.Exhaustion}\,V,
  \quad (\Lambda.\mathrm{shift}\,t).\mathrm{volume}\,n := t +_v \Lambda.\mathrm{volume}\,n,
\]
inherits monotonicity and the exhaust property; moreover
$|(\Lambda.\mathrm{shift}\,t).\mathrm{volume}\,n| = |\Lambda.\mathrm{volume}\,n|$
and $\Lambda.\mathrm{shift}\,0 = \Lambda$ pointwise on volumes.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
