\subsection*{Lemma~17.5.2 Part B -- system pseudo-mass and transfer-matrix
all-rate reduction (Partial)}

\subsubsection*{Mathematical content}

Glimm--Jaffe \S17.5 (2nd ed., pp.~311--312) prove the two-sided estimate
\[
  m^-(\sigma) \le m(\sigma) \le \mathrm{const}\cdot m^-(\sigma),
\]
where $m(\sigma)$ is the lattice mass (inverse correlation length) and
$m^-(\sigma)$ is the \emph{system pseudo-mass}, defined as the lower envelope of
the per-pair pseudo-masses $m^-(x,y,\sigma)$: the book chooses a minimizing pair
$x_0\neq y_0$ with $m^-(x_0,y_0,\sigma)=m^-(\sigma)$.

The upper bound $m(\sigma)\le\mathrm{const}\cdot m^-(\sigma)$ (\emph{Part B}) is
derived from the \emph{transfer matrix}, which gives exponential decay of all
correlations at a rate $e^{-m\cdot\mathrm{dist}}$ with
$\mathrm{dist}\ge|x-y|/a_0$; thus
$\langle\phi(x)\phi(y)\rangle\le A\,e^{-(m/a_0)|x-y|}$, and comparison with the
defining identity of $m^-$ yields the bound.  This transfer-matrix step is
\emph{distinct} from the Lipschitz-continuity argument used in the proof of
Theorem~17.5.1 (continuity of $(m^-)^{2\alpha+1}$ in $\sigma$); the Lipschitz
estimate alone does not bound the admissible exponential decay rates.

\subsubsection*{Lean 4 formalization}

This step introduces the system pseudo-mass and isolates the transfer-matrix
content as a single named hypothesis.  The per-pair pseudo-mass
$\mathtt{pseudoMassFromParamsAtPair}$ returns $0$ on inactive pairs (correlation
outside $\mathrm{Ioo}\,0\,2$), so the lower envelope is taken over \emph{active}
pairs only, preventing a trivial collapse of the infimum.

\begin{definition}[\texttt{globalPseudoMass}]
For active-pair value set
$\mathtt{globalPseudoMassSet}=\{\,m^-(x,z,\sigma)\mid x\neq z,\
\langle\sigma_x\sigma_z\rangle^\infty\in(0,2)\,\}$,
\[
  m^-(\sigma) \;=\; \mathtt{globalPseudoMass}
  \;=\; \operatorname{sInf}\bigl(\mathtt{globalPseudoMassSet}\bigr),
\]
with the real-infimum convention $\operatorname{sInf}\emptyset=0$.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{globalPseudoMass\_le\_pseudoMassFromParamsAtPair\_of\_active}]
For any active distinct pair $(x,z)$,
$\mathtt{globalPseudoMass}\le\mathtt{pseudoMassFromParamsAtPair}\ \dots\ x\ z$.
\end{theorem}

\noindent\textbf{Proof sketch}:
The value set is bounded below by $0$
(\texttt{globalPseudoMassSet\_bddBelow}, via per-pair nonnegativity), and the
active pair contributes its own pseudo-mass to the set, so $\operatorname{sInf}$
is $\le$ that element by \texttt{csInf\_le}.

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_upper\_bound\_of\_global\_all\_rate\_comparison}]
Let $\mathtt{Lemma\_17\_5\_2\_GlobalAllRateComparison}$ be the predicate
\[
  \forall a:\mathbb{R}_{\ge0},\
  \mathrm{HasExponentialDecay}\,(\langle J,0,\beta\rangle)\,a
  \;\Rightarrow\;
  (a:\overline{\mathbb{R}}_{\ge0})\le C\cdot\mathrm{ofReal}\,(\mathtt{globalPseudoMass}).
\]
Then for any active pair $(x,z)$ this predicate implies the named
$\mathtt{Lemma\_17\_5\_2\_UpperBound}$ at coefficient $C$.
\end{theorem}

\noindent\textbf{Proof sketch}:
The lower-envelope inequality and monotonicity of
$\mathrm{ENNReal.ofReal}$ and of $C\cdot(-)$ (\texttt{gcongr}) transfer the
global all-rate bound to the pair, and the existing $\operatorname{sSup}$
assembly $\mathtt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_upper\_bound\_of\_all\_decay\_rates\_le}$ closes
the $\mathtt{latticeMass}$ upper-bound predicate.

\noindent\textit{Status}: System pseudo-mass and the order-theoretic reduction
are fully formalized (no \texttt{sorry}); the transfer-matrix comparison
$\mathtt{Lemma\_17\_5\_2\_GlobalAllRateComparison}$ remains an explicit
hypothesis (Part B analytic core, tracked by issue~\#3378).
File: \texttt{Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/Lemma\_17\_5\_2/GlobalPseudoMass.lean}.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, \emph{Quantum Physics} 2nd~ed. (1987),
\S17.5 Lemma~17.5.2, pp.~311--312.

\subsubsection*{All-rate comparison from per-active-pair bounds}

The system pseudo-mass $m^-(\sigma)=\operatorname{sInf}$ over an infinite active
pair set, so a single pair only gives $m^-(\sigma)\le m^-(x,z,\sigma)$.  The
reverse direction needed for the all-rate comparison requires the per-pair
estimate to hold uniformly.

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_global\_all\_rate\_comparison\_of\_per\_active\_pair\_rate\_pseudoMass\_lower\_bound}]
Let $\mathtt{Lemma\_17\_5\_2\_PerActivePairRatePseudoMassLowerBound}$ be
\[
  0<K \ \wedge\ \forall a,\
  \mathrm{HasExponentialDecay}\,(\langle J,0,\beta\rangle)\,a
  \Rightarrow
  \forall\,(x,z)\ \text{active},\ (a:\mathbb{R})/K\le m^-(x,z,\sigma).
\]
Given an active-pair witness, this implies
$\mathtt{Lemma\_17\_5\_2\_GlobalAllRateComparison}$ at coefficient
$\mathrm{ofReal}\,K$, hence (at that pair) the named
$\mathtt{Lemma\_17\_5\_2\_UpperBound}$.
\end{theorem}

\noindent\textbf{Proof sketch}:
The uniform per-pair bound and $\mathtt{le\_csInf}$ (with the active-pair witness
supplying nonemptiness) give $(a:\mathbb{R})/K\le m^-(\sigma)$; an
$\mathrm{ENNReal}$ transfer using only $0<K$ then yields
$(a:\overline{\mathbb{R}}_{\ge0})\le\mathrm{ofReal}\,K\cdot\mathrm{ofReal}\,m^-(\sigma)$.
The active-pair witness is essential: the empty-active degenerate slice
($\beta=0$, every rate admissible, $m^-(\sigma)=0$) would otherwise falsify the
statement.

\noindent\textit{Status}: Order-theoretic reduction fully formalized (no
\texttt{sorry}); the per-active-pair rate bound
$\mathtt{Lemma\_17\_5\_2\_PerActivePairRatePseudoMassLowerBound}$ (the
transfer-matrix exponential-decay step, not reducible to the Simon--Lieb
selected-rate layer alone) remains the explicit analytic input (issue~\#3378).
File:
\texttt{Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/Lemma\_17\_5\_2/GlobalAllRateComparisonFromPairs.lean}.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, \emph{Quantum Physics} 2nd~ed. (1987),
\S17.5 Lemma~17.5.2, pp.~311--312.

\subsubsection*{Per-pair rate bound from a uniform $\mathrm{pseudoMassG}$ transfer
bound}

The pseudo-mass calculus already converts a profile-shaped correlation upper
bound into a pseudo-mass lower bound: on the active window
$\langle\sigma_x\sigma_z\rangle\in(0,2)$,
\[
  \langle\sigma_x\sigma_z\rangle\le \mathrm{pseudoMassG}\,\alpha\,r\,t
  \quad(t\ge0)
  \ \Longrightarrow\
  t\le m^-(x,z,\sigma)
\]
($\mathtt{pseudoMassFromParamsAtPair\_ge\_of\_corr\_le\_pseudoMassG}$).  Taking
$t=(a:\mathbb{R})/K$ gives the per-pair rate bound, hence (uniformly over active
pairs, with a witness) the named upper bound.

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_upper\_bound\_of\_uniform\_transfer\_pseudoMassG}]
Let $\mathtt{Lemma\_17\_5\_2\_UniformTransferPseudoMassGBound}$ be
\[
  0<K\ \wedge\ \forall a,\
  \mathrm{HasExponentialDecay}\,(\langle J,0,\beta\rangle)\,a
  \Rightarrow
  \forall\,(x,z)\ \text{active},\
  \langle\sigma_x\sigma_z\rangle\le\mathrm{pseudoMassG}\,\alpha\,r\,((a:\mathbb{R})/K).
\]
At an active pair $(x,z)$ this implies the named
$\mathtt{Lemma\_17\_5\_2\_UpperBound}$ at coefficient $\mathrm{ofReal}\,K$.
\end{theorem}

\noindent\textbf{Proof sketch}:
For each admissible rate the uniform $\mathrm{pseudoMassG}$ bound yields
$(a:\mathbb{R})/K\le m^-(x,z)$ at every active pair, which is exactly the
per-active-pair rate bound; the reductions of the preceding subsections then
close $\mathtt{Lemma\_17\_5\_2\_UpperBound}$.

\noindent\textit{Status}: Reduction fully formalized (no \texttt{sorry}).  At
$h=0$ the truncated and full two-point functions agree
($\mathtt{truncated2Infinite\_h\_zero}$), so $\mathrm{HasExponentialDecay}$ is
genuine correlation decay; the remaining open input is the prefactor-free uniform
$\mathrm{pseudoMassG}$ pair bound (transfer-matrix step), not obtainable from the
rate-dependent existential $\mathrm{HasExponentialDecay}$ constant alone
(issue~\#3378).
File:
\texttt{Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/Lemma\_17\_5\_2/PerActivePairRateFromUniformTransfer.lean}.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, \emph{Quantum Physics} 2nd~ed. (1987),
\S17.5 Lemma~17.5.2, pp.~311--312.

\subsection*{Lemma~17.5.2 Part B -- closing the upper bound via large-$K$ uniform
transfer (Done)}

\subsubsection*{Mathematical content}

The uniform $\mathrm{pseudoMassG}$ transfer bound, and hence the upper-bound side
$m(\sigma)\le\mathrm{const}\cdot m^-(\sigma)$ of Lemma~17.5.2, is now closed for
ferromagnetic positive-temperature pairs ($0<d$, $0<J$, $0<\beta$, $1\le\alpha$,
$0<r$).  Two uniform facts suffice, with a single constant $K$ depending only on
$\alpha,r,d,J,\beta$:
\begin{itemize}
\item every infinite-volume correlation satisfies
  $\langle\sigma_x\sigma_z\rangle\le 1$, a uniform gap below
  $\mathrm{pseudoMassG}\,\alpha\,r\,0=2$; and
\item every admissible decay rate $a$ satisfies $a\le -\log(\tanh(\beta J))$
  (the all-rate cap $-\log(\tanh(\beta J))$, transferred to an arbitrary exhaustion).
\end{itemize}
Since $\mathrm{pseudoMassG}\,\alpha\,r$ is continuous with value $2$ at $0$ and
antitone, it stays $\ge 1$ on a neighbourhood $[0,\delta]$ of $0$; choosing $K$
large enough that $(a:\mathbb{R})/K\le\delta$ for every admissible $a$ gives
$\langle\sigma_x\sigma_z\rangle\le 1\le\mathrm{pseudoMassG}\,\alpha\,r\,((a:\mathbb{R})/K)$.

\subsubsection*{Lean 4 formalization}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_upper\_bound\_uniform\_transfer}]
For $1\le\alpha$, $0<r$, $0<d$, $0<J$, $0<\beta$ and a ferromagnetic
positive-temperature active pair $(x,z)$,
\[
  \exists K,\ \mathtt{Lemma\_17\_5\_2\_UpperBound}\ \dots\ x\ z\ (\mathrm{ofReal}\,K),
  \quad\text{i.e.}\quad
  \mathrm{latticeMass}\le\mathrm{ofReal}\,K\cdot\mathrm{ofReal}\,m^-(x,z).
\]
\end{theorem}

\noindent\textbf{Proof sketch}:
$\mathtt{exists\_pos\_forall\_pseudoMassG\_ge\_one\_near\_zero}$ gives $\delta$ with
$\mathrm{pseudoMassG}\,\alpha\,r\,t\ge 1$ on $[0,\delta]$ (continuity at $0$,
value $2$);
$\mathtt{exists\_K\_forall\_pseudoMassG\_ge\_one\_of\_rate\_bound}$ picks
$K=\max(1,A/\delta)$ for the rate cap $A$;
$\mathtt{exists\_uniform\_transfer\_pseudoMassG\_of\_rate\_bound}$ uses
$\langle\sigma_x\sigma_z\rangle\le 1$ to validate the uniform transfer bound;
$\mathtt{admissible\_rate\_le\_neg\_log\_tanh}$ supplies $A=-\log(\tanh(\beta J))$
on an arbitrary exhaustion; and the PR~\#3381 chain closes
$\mathtt{Lemma\_17\_5\_2\_UpperBound}$.

\noindent\textit{Status}: Complete formalization (no \texttt{sorry}).  The constant
is uniform in the pair but \emph{not sharp}: this proves the qualitative
Glimm--Jaffe statement $m\le\mathrm{const}\cdot m^-$ via the uniform pseudo-mass
lower bound $m^-(x,z)\ge\mathrm{pseudoMassG}^{-1}(1)$ that
$\langle\sigma_x\sigma_z\rangle\le 1$ yields, rather than the sharp transfer-matrix
correlation length.
File:
\texttt{Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/Lemma\_17\_5\_2/UniformTransferLargeK.lean}.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, \emph{Quantum Physics} 2nd~ed. (1987),
\S17.5 Lemma~17.5.2, pp.~311--312.

\subsection*{Lemma~17.5.2 -- full sandwich with a uniform upper constant (Done)}

\subsubsection*{Mathematical content}

Combining the lower side $m^-\le m$ (the pseudo-mass rate validates exponential
decay, from the profile lower bound
$\mathrm{pseudoMassG}\,\alpha\,r\,(-\log(\beta J\,2d))\le
\langle\sigma_x\sigma_z\rangle$) with the uniform-$K$ upper closure gives the full
Glimm--Jaffe Lemma~17.5.2 sandwich, for ferromagnetic pairs at
$0<\beta J\,2d<1$:
\[
  \mathrm{ofReal}\,m^-(x,z)\ \le\ \mathrm{latticeMass}\ \le\
  \mathrm{ofReal}\,K\cdot\mathrm{ofReal}\,m^-(x,z).
\]

\subsubsection*{Lean 4 formalization}

\begin{theorem}[\texttt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_high\_temp\_sandwich\_uniform\_transfer\_forall}]
For $1\le\alpha$, $0<r$, $0<d$, $0<J$, $0<\beta$, $\beta J\,2d<1$ there is a single
$K$ such that every active pair $(x,z)$ with
$\mathrm{pseudoMassG}\,\alpha\,r\,(-\log(\beta J\,2d))\le\langle\sigma_x\sigma_z\rangle$
satisfies
$\mathrm{ofReal}\,m^-(x,z)\le\mathrm{latticeMass}\le\mathrm{ofReal}\,K\cdot
\mathrm{ofReal}\,m^-(x,z)$.
\end{theorem}

\noindent\textbf{Proof sketch}:
$\mathtt{HasExponentialDecay\_pseudoMassFromParamsAtPair\_of\_pseudoMassG\_le\_corr}$
discharges the lower decay input; $\mathtt{exists\_uniform\_transfer\_pseudoMassG}$
supplies one $K$ for all pairs and
$\mathtt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_upper\_bound\_of\_uniform\_transfer\_pseudoMassG}$ closes
the upper side at that $K$; $\mathtt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_sandwich\_of\_decay\_and\_upper}$
assembles the two.

\noindent\textit{Status}: Complete formalization (no \texttt{sorry}).  The constant
is uniform in the pair (unlike the pair-dependent
$\mathtt{lemma\_17\_5\_2\_cubic\_high\_temp\_sandwich\_capstone}$) but \emph{not
sharp}.
File:
\texttt{Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/Lemma\_17\_5\_2/UniformSandwich.lean}.
Reference: Glimm--Jaffe, \emph{Quantum Physics} 2nd~ed. (1987),
\S17.5 Lemma~17.5.2, pp.~311--312.

\subsection*{Lemma~17.5.2 -- uniform sandwich from cubic tanh profiles (Done)}

\subsubsection*{Mathematical content}

The preceding uniform sandwich requires the raw profile input
\[
  \mathrm{pseudoMassG}\,\alpha\,r\,(-\log(\beta J\,2d))
    \le \langle\sigma_x\sigma_z\rangle_{\infty,\Lambda}.
\]
For a distinct pair $(x,z)$, set \(w=z-x\).  The named condition
\(\mathtt{cubicTanhProfileBound}\,\alpha\,d\,r\,\beta\,J\,w\) gives
\[
  \mathrm{pseudoMassG}\,\alpha\,r\,(-\log(\beta J\,2d))
    \le \tanh(\beta J)^{\operatorname{dist}(0,w)}.
\]
The already formalized path lower bound gives
\[
  \tanh(\beta J)^{\operatorname{dist}(0,w)}
    \le \langle\sigma_0\sigma_w\rangle_{\infty,\mathrm{cubic}}.
\]
Translation invariance identifies this anchored cubic correlation with
\(\langle\sigma_x\sigma_z\rangle_{\infty,\mathrm{cubic}}\), and
exhaustion-independence transfers it to the target exhaustion \(\Lambda\).
The same tanh-profile input supplies active-range membership
\(\langle\sigma_x\sigma_z\rangle_{\infty,\Lambda}\in(0,2)\).

\subsubsection*{Lean 4 formalization}

\begin{theorem}[Uniform sandwich from cubic tanh profiles]
The Lean theorem is
\begin{Verbatim}[breaklines=true]
lemma_17_5_2_high_temp_sandwich_uniform_transfer_forall_of_cubicTanhProfileBound
\end{Verbatim}
Assume \(1\le\alpha\), \(0<r\), \(0<d\), \(0<J\), \(0<\beta\), and
\(\beta J\,2d<1\).  If
\(\mathtt{cubicTanhProfileBound}\,\alpha\,d\,r\,\beta\,J\,w\) holds for every
nonzero displacement \(w\), then there is a single \(K\) such that every
distinct pair \((x,z)\) satisfies
\[
  \mathrm{ofReal}\,m^-(x,z)\le \mathrm{latticeMass}
  \le \mathrm{ofReal}\,K\cdot\mathrm{ofReal}\,m^-(x,z).
\]
\end{theorem}

\noindent\textbf{Proof sketch}:
The bridge
\begin{Verbatim}[breaklines=true]
pseudoMassG_le_correlationInfinite_of_cubicTanhProfileBound_displacement
\end{Verbatim}
turns the displacement tanh-profile bound into the raw target-exhaustion
profile inequality.  The companion theorem
\begin{Verbatim}[breaklines=true]
activePseudoMassPair_of_cubicTanhProfileBound_displacement
\end{Verbatim}
derives the active-pair input.  The proof then calls
\begin{Verbatim}[breaklines=true]
lemma_17_5_2_high_temp_sandwich_uniform_transfer_forall
\end{Verbatim}

\noindent\textit{Status}: Complete formalization (no \texttt{sorry}).  The result
still assumes the cubic tanh-profile family as a conditional input; the no-go
result below shows that the all-displacement family cannot be discharged in
positive dimension from only \(0<r\), \(0<\beta J\), and \(\beta J\,2d<1\).

\subsection*{Step 526 -- all-displacement cubic tanh-profile no-go}

The previous wrapper intentionally treats
\[
  \forall\,w\ne0,\quad
  \mathtt{cubicTanhProfileBound}\,\alpha\,d\,r\,\beta\,J\,w
\]
as a conditional input.  In fact this family cannot be discharged in positive
dimension from only \(0<r\), \(0<\beta J\), and \(\beta J\,2d<1\).  The
predicate requires
\[
  \mathtt{pseudoMassG}\,\alpha\,r\,(-\log(\beta J\,2d))
  \le \tanh(\beta J)^{\operatorname{dist}(0,w)}
\]
at every nonzero displacement.  If \(0<\tanh(\beta J)<1\), the right-hand side
tends to zero along any coordinate axis, while \(\mathtt{pseudoMassG}\) at a
nonnegative rate and \(r>0\) is strictly positive.

\begin{theorem}[All-displacement tanh-profile no-go]
The Lean theorems are
\begin{Verbatim}[breaklines=true]
not_forall_cubicTanhProfileBound_of_tanh_pos_lt_one
not_forall_cubicTanhProfileBound_of_betaJ_pos_high_temp
\end{Verbatim}
In positive dimension and \(0<r\), the all-nonzero
\(\mathtt{cubicTanhProfileBound}\) family is impossible when
\(0<\tanh(\beta J)<1\) and \(-\log(\beta J\,2d)\ge0\).  In particular it is
impossible under \(0<\beta J\) and \(\beta J\,2d<1\).
\end{theorem}

\noindent\textit{Status}: Complete formalization (no \texttt{sorry}).  This
clarifies that the all-pairs tanh-profile sandwich theorem is a conditional
interface, not an input-free high-temperature capstone.
File:
\begin{Verbatim}[breaklines=true]
Concrete/LatticeGraphCorrelation/CubicPseudoMassTanhProfileNoGo.lean
\end{Verbatim}
